“In the Caitanya-caritamrta (Antya 4.226) there is a verse that describes the writings of Sril Rupa Gosvami: Srila Rupa Gosvami compiled 100,000 verses, beginning with the book Dana-Keli-Kaumudi. In all these scriptures, he elaborately explained the transcendental mellows of the activities of Vrndavna.

This one act drama describes the very celebrated pastime of Krsna’s efforts to extract a toll from Radha and her companions as they carried butter for the performance of a sacrifice being performed in the forest of Vrndavana. Of course, it cannot be emphasized enough that such pastimes are only to be relished by devotees who have thoroughly understood Lord Krsna’s position as the Supreme Personality of Godhead by carefully studying the first nine cantos of the Srimad-Bhagavatam.”

# Dāna-keli-kaumudī (VCT)

## Introduction

### Verse 1

*antaḥ-smeratayojjvalā jala-kaṇa-vyākīrṇa-pakṣmāṅkurā*  
*kiñcitpāṭalitāñcalā rasikatotsiktā puraḥ kuñcatī*  
*ruddhāyāḥ pathi mādhavena madhura-vyābhugna-tārottarā*  
*rādhāyāḥ kila-kiñcita-stavakinī dṛṣṭiḥ śriyaṁ vaḥ kriyāt*

**Пословный перевод:**

*antaḥ—*inside; *smeratayā—*with smiling; *ujjvalā—*resplendent; *jala-kaṇa—*with teardrops; *vyākīrṇa—*filled; *pakṣma—*of the eyelashes; *aṅkurā—*the shoots; *kiñcit—*slight; *pāṭalitā—*with pinkness; *añcalā—*corners; *rasikatā—*with the desire to taste *rasa*;*utsiktā—*overflowing; *puraḥ—*in front; *kuñcatī—*being contracted; *ruddhāyāḥ—*who was stopped; *pathi—*on the path; *mādhavena—*by Mādhava; *madhura—*charming; *vyābhugna—*crooked; *tāra—*due to the eyes; *uttarā—*topmost; *rādhāyāḥ—*of Rādhā; *kila-kiñcita—*the ecstatic state called *kila-kiñcita*; *stavakinī—*which makes into a bouquet; *dṛṣṭiḥ—*eyes; *śriyam—*the wealth (of *prema*); *vaḥ—*to you all; *kriyāt—*may it create.

**Перевод:**

**When Mādhava stood in front of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, blocking Her way, She squinted, and teardrops overlaid Her stamenlike eyelashes. At the same time, Her eyes, dazzling with a concealed smile, overflowed with amorous desire. Those eyes with pink corners looked sideways and were most charming, slightly revealing the bouquet of Her kila-kiñcita ecstasy. May Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī’s eyes bestow the wealth of prema upon all of you.**

**Комментарий:**

Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī begins his *‘Mahatī-ṭīkā’* with the following verse:

*dāna-keli-kalau lupta-dharma-maryādayor bhaje*  
*rādhā-mādhavayoḥ kāma-lobha-dambha-madānṛtam*

**“While quarreling during the tax collecting pastime, Rādhā and Mādhava lost Their sense of moral boundaries. I worship Their transcendental lust, greed, pride, excitement, and cunning.”**

He glorifies the author as well: “Śrī Rūpa is the crest jewel of *rasika* devotees and the kingof all poets. He presented the mostexalted topics in his dramas like the *Vidagdha-mādhava* and accepted the vow of constantly and diligently distributing the ever delightful ambrosia of Rādhā-Mādhava’s pastimes. To cheer up his dear friend, the chief of the devotees who are in love with *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, Śrī Rūpa composed the drama *Dāna-keli-kaumudī*, which is like a wave of nectar.”

In the first opening verse of the *Dāna-keli-kaumudī*, Rūpa Gosvāmī prays for the benediction of wealth (*‘śriyam’*)—pure love for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa—to all the readers. *‘Śriyam’* can also mean “beauty,” which here would mean “beauty of the ecstatic symptoms like being stunned and trembling, which are caused by *prema*.” It is nothing less than this unparalleled and eternal wealth of *prema*—our real and most essential need—or the beauty of its symptoms, that the author wishes us to attain by reading this drama. And it is neither Kṛṣṇa, nor even Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, but Her eyes at the moment when She experiences a special kind of ecstatic emotional turmoil, *kila-kiñcita,* which are the bestower of this benediction. Also, the fact that the bouquet of Rādhā’s *kila-kiñcita* glance bestows *prema*, signifies that this glance is like a wish-fulfilling creeper.

As described by Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī in *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta (Madhya 14.169-170)*, *kila-kiñcita* is one of the twenty special ecstatic moods that stir to waves the ocean of Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental happiness and enchant His mind. Rūpa Gosvāmī defines *kila-kiñcita* as follows:

*garvābhilāṣa-rudita-smitāsūya-bhaya-krudhām*  
*saṅkarī-karaṇaṁ harṣād ucyate kila-kiñcitam*

**“When pride, desire, weeping, smiling, resentment, fear, and anger are combined as a result of joy, and the mixture of these feelings is called** ***kila-kiñcita*****.”**

This special *bhāva* arises in Rādhārāṇī when Kṛṣṇa, disguised as a tax collector, blocks Her way, or acting as a gardener, stops Her from picking flowers, or touches Her body by force in front of Her gopī friend. The *Dāna-keli-kaumudī* describes particularly the pastime of Kṛṣṇa’s trying to collect a toll from the *gopīs* at the foot of Govardhana hill.

Viśvanātha Cakravartī describes how all the emotions comprising *kila-kiñcita-bhāva* are revealed in Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī’s eyes. The smile concealed in them showed Her joy; squinting—Her fear; tears on Her eyelashes—crying that results from concealing one’s real feelings; passion that filled Her eyes—Her amorous desire; the eyes’ charming appearance—Her pride; glancing sideways—Her resentment; and finally, the pink color in the eye corners express both Her smiling and anger (Because pink is the combination of white and red—the colors that in this context represent smiling and anger respectively).

The comparison of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī’s *kila-kiñcita* glance to a bouquet is very appropriate. Just as a bouquet is an aesthetic combination of flowers of very different shapes and colors, *kila-kiñcita* is an exquisite mix of transcendental emotions that are very different in their character. Taking into consideration the poetic device known as *śleṣa*, or double meaning, the same verse could be understood as a visual description of the bouquet of Rādhārāṇī’s *kila-kiñcita* glance. “…The inner portion (*antaḥ*) of this resplendent bouquet had new blossoms (*smeratayā*), and the stamens of its flowers (*pakṣmāṅkurā*) were overlaid with drops of nectar (*jala-kaṇa*). The front portion made of the bouquet was made of buds (*puraḥ kuñcatī*), and its sides were slightly pink (*kiñcit-pāṭalitāñcalā*). Overflowing with elegance (*rasikatotsiktā*), this bouquet appeared most beautiful (*uttarā*) by assuming charming curved shape (*madhura-vyābhugnatā-rā*)…”

### Verse 2

*vibhur api kalayan sadābhivṛddhiṁ*  
*gurur api gaurava-caryayā vihīnaḥ*  
*muhur upacita-vakrimāpi śuddho*  
*jayati mura-dviṣi rādhikānurāgaḥ*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vibhuḥ—*all-pervading; *api—*although; *kalayan—*making; *sadā—*constantly; *abhivṛddhim—*increase in all respects; *guruḥ—*grave; *api—*although; *gaurava—*of courtesy; *caryayā—*by the conduct; *vihīnaḥ—*bereft; *muhuḥ—*again and again; *upacita—*accumulated; *vakrimā—*crookedness; *api—*although; *śuddhaḥ—*pure; *jayati—*may it be glorious; *mura-dviṣi—*for the enemy of the demon Mura; *rādhikā—*of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī; *anurāgaḥ—*ever fresh love.

**Перевод:**

**All glories to Rādhikā’s ever-fresh love for Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the enemy of the Mura demon! Even though all-pervading, Her love constantly increases in all respects; even though profound, it doesn’t care for courtesy at all; even though beset with duplicity again and again, it is pure.**

**Комментарий:**

In the second opening verse Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī glorifies the mysterious *anurāga* of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, which is the focus of the part of the play we entitled…. *Anurāga* is defined by Rūpa Gosvāmī:

*sadānubhūtam api yaḥ kuryān nava-navaṁ priyam*  
*rāgo bhavan nava-navaḥ so ’nurāga itīryate*

**“*****Anurāga*** **is such love which makes a beloved to appear ever-fresh, even though one perceives a beloved at every moment. In this way,** ***anurāga*** **is love that is experienced as if for the first time.”**

Rūpa Gosvāmī describes the contradictory nature of *anurāga* by first saying, “Even though all-pervading, Her \[Rādhārāṇī’s\] love constantly increases in all respects.” (*‘vibhur api kalayan sadābhivṛddhim’*) Viśvanātha Cakravartī’s briefly comments that the love of Śrī Rādhā for Kṛṣṇa is all-pervading, because it is a manifestation of *cit-śakti* (spiritual potency).

This all-pervasiveness can be understood in the sense that a devotee sees everything in existence as directly related to the Lord. Moreover, the Lord then personally appears in everything within one’s vision:

*sarva-bhūteṣu yaḥ paśyed*  
*bhagavad-bhāvam ātmanaḥ*  
*bhūtāni bhagavaty ātmany*  
*eṣa bhāgavatottamaḥ*

**“The most advanced devotee sees within everything the soul of all souls, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Consequently he sees systematically everything in relation to the Supreme Lord and understands that everything that exists is eternally situated within the Lord.”**

*sthāvara-jaṅgama dekhe, nā dekhe tāra mūrti*  
*sarvatra haya nija iṣṭa-deva-sphūrti*

**“The** ***mahā-bhāgavata*****, the advanced devotee, certainly sees everything mobile and immobile, but he does not exactly see their forms. Rather, everywhere he immediately sees manifest the form of the Supreme Lord.”**

*paraspara veṇu-gīte haraye cetana*  
*mora bhrame tamālere kare āliṅgana*  
*kṛṣṇa-āliṅgana pāinu, janama saphale*  
*kṛṣṇa-sukhe magna rahe vṛkṣa kari’ kole*

**“The flutelike murmur of the bamboos rubbing against one another steals Rādhārāṇī’s consciousness, for She thinks it to be the sound of My flute. And She embraces a** ***tamāla*** **tree, mistaking it for Me. ‘I have gotten the embrace of Śrī Kṛṣṇa,’ She thinks, ‘so now My life is fulfilled.’ Thus She remains immersed in pleasing Kṛṣṇa, taking the tree in Her arms.”**

If Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī’s love is all-pervading, how could it continue to increase? Viśvanātha Cakravartī resolves this first seeming contradiction by referring to *Vedānta-sūtra* 2.1.33: *‘loka-vat tu līlā-kaivalyam’*—“But as is also seen in the ordinary world, this is simply His pastime.”

This *sūtra* establishes that the Lord’s activities in relation to the creation are nothing else but His pleasure pastimes (*līlā-kaivalyam*) and can be compared to some degree to playful and carefree activities of ordinary people (*loka-vat*)—like dancing of a drunkard or a child. In other words, the Lord’s purpose behind His activities is pleasure only, uninhibited by any ulterior motive—He plays for the sake of playing, which is tasting *rasa* in the company of His devotees. In the same way, Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī loves Kṛṣṇa for the sake of loving Him, bringing Him pleasure. Her love, unconstricted by any goal outside of itself, knows no other course but to constantly and inconceivably increase.

The verse then goes on to point out the second contradiction of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī’s *anurāga*: “Even though profound, it doesn’t care for courtesy at all.” (*‘gurur api gaurava-caryayā vihīnaḥ’*) In other words, even though Her love is ‘*guru*’ (“heavy, important, grave”), Rādhā doesn’t always express it in a respectful or favorable to Kṛṣṇa manner, as somebody might expect. Instead, putting aside the manners of a polite lover, She often treats Kṛṣṇa with apparent neglect or resentment. According to *Mahaṭī-ṭīkā*, this is because Her *anurāga* arises from the sweet amorous affection permeated with the sense of possessiveness in relation to Kṛṣṇa (*mamatā*).

The third ostensible paradox is stated in the third line of the verse: “Although beset with duplicity again and again, it is pure.” (*‘muhur upacita-vakrimāpi śuddho’*) Viśvanātha Cakravartī reconciles this in two ways. He says that Rādhā’s love is pure because it is a special, essential manifestation of *śuddha-sattva* (the combination of *saṁvit* (awareness) and *hlādinī* (pleasure) parts of the Lord’s internal potency), and in the sense that it is free of any ulterior motive.

This means that despite being full of contrary conduct, which would ordinarily be a sign of false ego, Rādhā’s love is free of any material discrepancy, composed totally of spiritual energy. After all, if Rādhā’s refractoriness resulted from the three modes of material nature how could it attract Kṛṣṇa? Her love is thus free from any mundane quality by default, regardless of the way it expresses itself. This is because for its existence, Rādhā’s love does not dependent on anything but the happiness of the beloved. So, as Rādhā brings Kṛṣṇa pleasure with Her amicable behavior, She does the same with Her crooked speech, angry rebukes, or even temporary abandonement.

The Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava ācāryas have analyzed Rādhā’s love for Kṛṣṇa and concluded that it excels the love of all other devotees in depth and intensity. They unanimously accept Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī to be the best devoteeand therefore capable of pleasing Kṛṣṇa to the highest degree. Śrī Rādhā’s love is a puzzle inconceivable even for Kṛṣṇa. To understand Her love, Kṛṣṇa had no other means than to assume the complexion and sentiment of Śrī Rādhā in His appearance as Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

## The Prologue

### Verse 3-4

*(nāndy-ante sūtradhāraḥ:)alam ati-vistareṇa.*  
*(samantād avalokya) hanta! kathaṁ madīya-nāndī-candrikā-sandīpita-bhāva-bandhurā nandīśvara-girer upatyakāyāṁ ghūrṇate satāṁ maṇḍalī.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nāndī—*of the *nāndī* verses; *ante—*at the end; *sūtradhāraḥ—*the play director; *alam—*enough; *ati-vistareṇa—*of excessive elaboration. *samantāt—*around; *avalokya—*looking; *hanta—*just see; *katham—*how; *madīya—*my; *nāndī—*of the invocation; *candrikā—*by the moonlight; *sandīpita—*inflamed; *bhāva—*ecstatic emotion; *bandhurā—*beautiful; *nandīśvara-gireḥ—*of the Nandīśvara hill; *upatyakāyām—*at the foot; *ghūrṇate—*stirring; *satām—*of the devotees; *maṇḍalī—*the assembly.

**Перевод:**

**(Sūtradhāra, having completed the singing of the nāndī verses) Enough of this elaborate praise.**

**(He looks around.) Just see the devotees! Beautiful in ecstatic love inflamed by the moonlight of my invocation verses, they are stirring in rapture at the foot of the Nandīśvara hill.**

**Комментарий:**

The two opening verses of the *Dāna-keli-kaumudī* are *nāndī-ślokas*. *Nāndī* (the *maṅgalācaraṇa* of a dramatic play) refers to the glorification verses recited at the beginning of a drama to bestow benediction, offer glorification, and define the subject of a drama. *Nāndī* is called so, because it brings happiness (*nanda*) to the person glorified and the audience. The purpose of *nāndī* is to dispel any possible obstacles to the successful performance of the drama and to capture the attention of the audience.

In the *nāndī* of the *Dāna-keli-kaumudī* the benediction was bestowed in the first verse with the words *‘śriyaṁ vaḥ kriyāt’* (“May \[Rādhā’s glance\] bestow the wealth of *prema* upon all of you”); glorification was offered with the words *‘jayati…rādhikānurāgaḥ’* in the second verse (“All glories to Rādhikā’s *anurāga*”); the subject matter of the drama—the toll pastime—was defined in the first verse: *‘ruddhāyāḥ pathi mādhavena’* (“\[of Rādhā\] who was blocked by Mādhava on Her path”) and also alluded toby *‘kila-kiñcita-stavakinī’* (“\[Rādhā’s\] bouquet-like glance of *kila-kiñcita*”). This is the analysis of Surendranatha Shastri.

A sūtradhāra is a learned stage director in traditional Sanskrit drama. As the name suggests, he holds (*dhāra*) the “thread” (*sūtra*), or essence, of *nātya. Nāṭya* is an art that combines acting, dancing, and instrumental music. These three elements are present in a performance of a traditional Sanskrit play. A sūtradhāra must be learned in the science of *nāṭya* and its artistic application to make the play successful. Another possible reason for the name sūtradhara is because the stage director is like a puppeteer and the actors are like puppets that move at his command. Traditionally, he himself appears on the stage in the beginning of a play, and, while ringing a bell, sings *nāndī* verses to introduce the play to the audience.

Viśvanātha Cakravartī says that comparing the *nāndī* verses to the moonlight suggests that the ecstatic love of the assembled devotees is like an ocean that swells by the influence of the full moon.

### Verse 5

*(punar avekṣya)*  
*bhaktaḥ ko’pi tanos tanoti pulakair nṛtyann ihotphullatāṁ*  
*śuṣyan ko’pi cirād vivarṇa-vadano dhatte vidīrṇaṁ manaḥ*  
*garjan dhāvati ko’pi vindati patan ko’py eṣa niṣpandatām*  
*udyaty acyuta-vibhrame gatir abhūt kā stheyasām apy asau*

**Пословный перевод:**

*punaḥ—*again; *avekṣya—*watching; *bhaktaḥ—*a devotee; *kaḥ api—*certain; *tanoḥ—*of the body; *tanoti—*expands; *pulakaiḥ—*by the hairs standing on end; *nṛtyan—*dancing; *iha—*here; *utphullatām—*the bloated condition; *śuṣyan—*drying up; *kaḥ api—*some (devotee); *cirāt—*for a long time; *vivarṇa-vadanaḥ—*with a pale face; *dhatte—*has; *vidīrṇam—*torn to pieces; *manaḥ—*his mind; *garjan—*roaring; *dhāvati—*runs;*kaḥ api—*some (devotee);*vindati—*reaches; *patan—*falling down; *kaḥ api—*some (devotee);*eṣaḥ—*this; *niṣpandatām—*motionless state; *udyati—*when appears; *acyuta-vibhrame—*a pastime of Acyuta; *gatiḥ—*the condition; *abhūt—*arose; *kā—*what; *stheyasām—*of those who are very composed; *api—*even; *asau—*that.

**Перевод:**

**(Looking more) While dancing, one devotee increases the size of his body with his hairs standing on end. Another devotee stays for a long time with a parched mouth and pale face—his mind is shattered. Someone else is running around and roaring, and someone has fallen on the ground and became motionless. What is this ecstatic state that has come upon even the very composed devotees, as they hear a pastime of Lord Acyuta?**

**Комментарий:**

Viśvanātha Cakravartī provides the following explanation on this verse. The first devotee described here has his hairs standing on end out of sudden joy from hearing the *nāndī* verses that alluded to the pastime of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. The second devotee has his mouth dried up and face withered due to feeling himself low and dejected. This is the result of his extreme longing. The third devotee, roaring with pride and joy, runs aroundbecause he hasseen the Lord in his heart and has all of a sudden realized himeslf to be an associate of the Lord. The fourth devotee has fallen on the ground and become motionless because the bliss obtained from seeing the Lord has stupefied and bewildered him. From this it is understood that each devotee mentioned here possesses a higher degree of *prema* than the one mentioned before him.

Viśvanātha Cakravartī gives this verse an alternative explanation, which isbased on the concept presented in the *Vaiṣṇava-toṣaṇī*. He explains that there are two types of *prema*: in one confidence is predominant (*viśrambha-pradhāna-prema*), and in the other longing is predominant (*utkaṇṭha-pradhāna-prema*). Accordingly, there are two groups of devotees: one relishes*viśrambha-pradhāna-prema* and the other *utkaṇṭha-pradhāna-prema*. Since confidence and longing are contradictory moods, they cannot simultaneously appear in any devotee from either group, even though they may be caused by the same stimuli (*uddīpana-vibhava*). Devotees who relish*viśrambha-pradhāna-prema* exhibit ecstatic symptoms like hairs standing on end, dancing, roaring, and running; Devotees who relish*utkaṇṭha-pradhāna-prema* exhibit symptoms like drying of the mouth, becoming pale, falling to the ground, and fainting. The degree to which these symptoms are manifest depends on the intensity of the devotee’s direct contact with the Lord.

All these ecstatic statesare indescribable, and the sūtradhāra is wonderstruck at their influence on even the very self-controlled and usually calm devotees. This verse shows the caliber of devotees who are about to watch the performance of the *Dāna-keli-kaumudī*—they all are highly advanced. Just hearing the prologue describingRādhārāṇī’s glance and Her pure love brings about uncontrollable ecstatic symptoms in their bodies. One can only imagine the degree to which the ocean of their love will swell at witnessing the actual performance of *Dāna-keli-līlā* that is just about to begin.

One has to be spiritually qualified to be properly absorbed in hearing such confidential pastimes of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, watching a performance of them, or enacting a drama about them. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī repeatedly warned all practicing devotees against prematurely dwelling on the subject of *mādhurya-rasa*: *‘ jaḍa-bhāva nā chāḍile rasika to’ haya nā’—*“If materialistic emotions are not entirely rejected, one can never become a true *rasika*.” If sex desire is still present in one’s heart, one will not derive much benefit from such dwelling. The conjugal pastimes of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, even though spotlessly pure and transcendental, will repeatedly remind of mundane lusty affairs to those whose hearts are not sufficiently purified. If one then continues with such superficial hearing, it will only increase one’s desire for gross sense enjoyment. Libertinous *prākṛta-sahajiyās* are the practical example of this. As the proverb goes, “Fools rush in where angels dare not.” Therefore, if one feels agitatedwhile reading certain passages describing the forms or activities of the divine couple, one should refrain from focusing one’s attention there. Rather, one should concentrate on other pastimes of the Lord in order to make one’s heart qualified for hearing the descriptions of *mādhurya-līlā*. *‘Anartha-nivṛtti-kāle rūpe jaḍa dekhe nā, anartha-nivṛtti-kāle guṇe jaḍa bujhe nā, anartha-nivṛtti-kāle jaḍa līlā seve nā’—*“After the *anarthas* are removed, material attributes are no longer seen in the transcendental forms of Kṛṣṇa, material attributes are no longer perceived in His pure qualities, and one never serves mundane pastimes (i.e. engages in material activities).”

### Verse 6-7

*(kṣaṇaṁ vimṛśya) ām, abhijñātam. nidānaṁ sādhiṣṭha-prema-kadamba-kādambarāṇām āḍambaro ‘yam. yataḥ,*  
*gabhīro ‘py aśrāntaṁ duradhigama-pāro ‘pi nitarām*  
*ahāryāṁ maryādāṁ dadhad api harer āspadam api*  
*satāṁ stomaḥ premaṇy udayati samagre sthagayituṁ*  
*vikāraṁ na sphāraṁ jala-nidhir ivendau prabhavati*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kṣaṇam—*for a moment; *vimṛśya—*considering; *ām—*yes; *abhijñātam—*I understand; *nidānam—*the reason; *sādhiṣṭha—*the most mature; *prema—*of *prema*; *kadamba—*the multitude; *kādambarāṇām—*of the intoxicating beverage; *āḍambaraḥ—*theintensity; *ayam—*this. *yataḥ—*since; *gabhīraḥ—*deep; *apy—*although; *aśrāntam—*tirelessly; *duradhigama—*difficult to reach; *pāraḥ—*whose shore; *api—*although; *nitarām—*moreover; *ahāryām—*natural; *maryādām—*boundary; *dadhat—*maintaining; *api—*although; *hareḥ—*of Lord Hari; *āspadam—*the seat; *api—*although; *satām—*of the devotees; *stomaḥ—*the multitude; *premaṇi—*prema topmost divine love; *udayati—*when it arises; *samagre—*complete; *sthagayitum—*to stop; *vikāram—*the transformations; *na—*not; *sphāram—*expanded; *jala-nidhiḥ—*an ocean; *iva—*like; *indau—*when the moon (is present); *prabhavati—*able;

**Перевод:**

**(Thinking for a moment) Oh yes, I see. The reason for this transcendental madness is the highly intoxicating beverage of the intense and most mature varieties of prema: Even though the ocean is always very deep and by far unsurpassable, even though it is the resting place of the Lord and never crosses its natural boundaries, it cannot remain still in the presence of the full moon. Similarly, even though the devotees are always very grave and unfathomable, and even though they naturally respect the boundaries of proper conduct and are themselves the abode of the Lord, when the most mature love of God arises in their hearts, they cannot suppress their pervasive transformations.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse is quoted in *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* 2.4.270 in relation to one’s heart becoming strongly agitated with various *vyabhicāri-bhāvas* (transient ecstatic states) when one’s *sthāyi-bhāva* (permanent relationship with the Lord) becomes very prominent. Hearing the sūtradhara’s two opening verses, the devotees who have gathered to watch the performance are deeply affected. From this we can understand that their *sthāyi-bhāva* is that of conjugal love (*mādhurya-rati*), and it was reinforced by hearing the glories of Rādhā’s *anurāga*. Below is Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī’s commentary to this verse.

Just as the ocean is very deep, the perfect devotees are naturally very grave, and this in itself is the reason why their topmost love for the Lord is for the most time concealed. Like the ocean, which always stays within its boundaries, the shores, such perfect devotees, although it is not natural to them, restrain themselves to the limits of accepted norms of behavior in order to avoid becoming presumptuous. As the ocean is immeasurable, the devotees are unfathomable on account of their countless virtues. This is another reason why their *prema* stays hidden. Whenever a devotee exhibits some transcendental quality, common people cannot see it, for due to its spiritual nature people’s vision gets covered. The fact that the devotee is the abode of the Lord is also a reason for the devotee’s *prema* remaining unnoticed. In other words, because the Lord is in his heart all the time, his *prema* usually doesn’t explode in external transformations. One may ask why the ocean, even though the abode of the Lord, swells in tide when it sees the full moon? The answer is that the ocean swells with joy because its son, the moon, assists the Lord in His pastime of resting by spreading its rays all around.

### Verse 8-9

*(punar nibhālya) tatrāpi viśva-vilakṣaṇā sā nirbharam ati-mohinī keli-caryā (iti mūrdānam ādhunvan sa-dhairyam)*  
*premorjitā narma-vivāda-goṣṭhī*  
*gopendra-sūnoḥ saha rādhayāsau*  
*haṁsān api śrotra-taṭīm avāptā*  
*śuddhāmṛtād apy abhito ruṇaddhi*

**Пословный перевод:**

*punaḥ—*again; *nibhālya—*observing; *tatra—*in that matter; *api—*even; *viśva—*from the world; *vilakṣaṇā—*different; *sā—*this; *nirbharam—*extremely; *ati-mohinī—*bewildering beyond limit; *keli-caryā—*playful behavior; *iti—*thus; *mūrdānam—*his head; *ādhunvan—*shaking; *sa-dhairyam—*soberly. *prema—*by *prema*; *ūrjitā—*enlivened; *narma—*with jokes; *vivāda—*and with arguments; *goṣṭhī—*the conversation; *gopa*-*indra—*of the king of the cowherds; *sūnoḥ—*of the son; *saha—*with; *rādhayā—*Rādhā; *asau—*that; *haṁsān—*swans; *api—*even; *śrotra—*of the ears; *taṭīm—*the border; *avāptā—*reaching; *śuddha—*pure; *amṛtāt—*from nectar; *api—*even; *abhitaḥ—*on all sides; *ruṇaddhi—*blocks.

**Перевод:**

**(Looking again) Still, this uncommon playful behavior is bewildering beyond any limit.**

**(Shaking his head, says soberly:) The joking dispute that took place between the cowherd prince Kṛṣṇa and Rādhā during the toll pastime is animated by pure transcendental love. When that dispute even slightly reaches the ears of the swanlike self-satisfied souls, it prevents even them from tasting the pure nectar of the happiness found in the impersonal Brahman.**

**Комментарий:**

Based on the conventional meaning of the sūtradhāra’s gesture (*mūrdhānam ādhunvan*—shaking the head), Surendranath Shastri clarifies the reason behind the sūtradhāra’s behavior as follows. Just by looking at the gathering of devotees absorbed in relishing *kṛṣṇa-prema*, the sūtradhāra felt himself being swept under the sway of their ecstasy. But remembering his responsibility of conducting the performance that is just about to begin, he composes himself by shaking his head. Sober, sūtradhāra then proceeds to justify the wild ecstasy of the pure devotees in front of him by describing the transcendental potency of the toll pastime of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa*—*“If this is how even the impersonalists react upon hearing the toll pastime, then what would happen to the pure devotees if they hear it?”

All pastimes of Rādhā and Kṛṣna, or the Supreme Person and His internal pleasure potency, are absolutely pure. There is not even a slightest tinge of material contamination in them. So, even the swanlike self-satisfied souls, who have no attraction to anything material whatsoever, cannot resist the overwhelming and compelling sweetness of those pastimes, if by some great fortune they happen to hear of them even a little. Such lucky impersonalists give up the path of meditation on the impersonal Brahman, for they find the unlimitedly variegated transcendental pastimes of the Lord and His devotees incomparably sweeter and more attractive.

In the present verse, the supreme attractiveness of the toll pastime is highlighted even more vividly with the word *‘śrotra-taṭī’* (“ear-border”). It implies that even if the self-satisfied souls absorbed in Brahman may not listen to the narration of the toll pastime deliberately, it will still capture their consciousness. Śrīla Śukadeva Gosvāmī became attracted to the personal feature of the Lord in that way. Vyāsadeva, Śukadeva’s father, wanted to make his son a *vaiṣṇava*. He sent his disciples to wait on the road, and as Śukadeva passed by, the disciples recited some verses from the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that described the Lord’s exalted personal qualities. Having unintentionally heard those verses, Śukadeva Gosvāmī was no more interested in thinking of the impersonal Brahman. By that hearing he was totally plunged into the ocean of love of Kṛṣṇa.

Through the mouth of the sūtradhāra, Rūpa Gosvāmī points out the transcendental value and spotless purity of *Dāna-keli-līlā* at its outset. In this way he reminds us that we, aspiring transcendentalists, should treat this highly exalted subject matter with utmost regard and adoration.

### Verse 10

*(praviśya naṭaḥ sānandam)*  
*avagaṇita-sandhi-bhūmā*  
*nāṭya-kaleyaṁ baliṣṭha-saptāṅgā*  
*parama-suvṛtti-yugāḍhyā*  
*vara-rājya-śrīr iva sphurati*

**Пословный перевод:**

*praviśya—*entering; *naṭaḥ—*the actor; *sa-ānandam—*joyfully; *avagaṇita—*disregarded; *sandhi—*of joints; *bhūmā—*where there is a multitude; *nāṭya-kalā—*the art of dramatic performance; *iyam—*this; *baliṣṭha—*the strongest; *sapta-aṅgā—*where there are seven parts; *parama—*the topmost; *su-vṛtti—*of good styles; *yuga—*by the pair; *āḍhyā—*endowed; *vara—*best; *rājya—*of the kingdom; *śrīḥ—*opulence; *iva—*like; *sphurati—*appears.

**Перевод:**

**(Actor entering the stage, says cheerfully:) The ingenuity of this drama shines like the glory of a great empire.Its plot omits many phases, its seven sub-elements are most prominent, and it is endowed with the two best elegant demeanors.**

**Комментарий:**

A sūtradhāra is always accompanied on the stage by an actor, his assistant.

In Sanskrit drama there is a method to increase the eagerness of the audience for watching the performance. It is called *prarocanā*, and it is accomplished by glorifying things related to the drama, such as the place and time of the plot, the subject of the drama, its author, and the audience. Rūpa Gosvāmī has has tempted us so far with the description of the prototype audience of *Dāna-keli-kaumudī*—the highest order of *rasika-bhaktas*; and with the place of its original performance—the foot of Nandīśvara hill, which is situated in Vraja. The present verse introduces us to and glorifies the exquisite structure of *Dāna-keli-kaumudī*. For the detailed elaboration on this verse please refer to the Appendix 1—“The dramatical analysis of *Dāna-keli-kaumudī*.”

The actor compares the ingenuity of *bhāṇikā* to the opulence, or the glory of a great empire. This parallel is drawn naturally, for dramatic terms mentioned in this verse are also employed in politics. Thus, as Viśvanātha Cakravartī explains, *sandhi* is defined in politics as an affectionate and friendly joining of one ruler with another for the purpose of achieving his own ends. Just a mighty emperor doesn’t care much to ally with other rulers, a *bhāṇikā* play doesn’t care to include the number of twists (*sandhis*) in its plot. Also, just as the seven sub-elements (*sandhy-aṅgas*) in *bhāṇikā*’s plot are very prominent, the seven constituents (*aṅgas*) of a big kingdom—the ruler, ministers, friends, treasury, subjects, jail, and the army force—are very firm. Finally, a truly great kingdom is rich (*āḍhyā*), and its dealings are always endowed (*yuk*) with utmost nobility (*parama-suvṛtti*).

### Verse 11

*(sūtradhāraḥ:) bhos tāṇḍavācāryapāṇḍitya-pāraṁgata, saṁyag abhijñātam. yad eṣa niyogena suhṛdām uparūpaka-bhidāṁ dāna-keli-kaumudīṁ nāma bhāṇikām abhinetum udyato ’smi, tad atra nijābhiṣṭa-daivatānusmaraṇa-maṅgalam ācareyam.*  
*(ity añjaliṁ kṛtvā)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhoḥ—*O; *tāṇḍava—*of dancing; *ācārya—*O preceptor; *pāṇḍitya—*of expertise; *pāram-gata—*who has reached the limit; *saṁyak—*properly; *abhijñātam—*you have understood; *yad—*because; *eṣaḥ—*this; *niyogena—*on order; *suhṛdām—*of my friends; *uparūpaka-bhidām—*among the minor types of drama; *dāna-keli-kaumudīṁ—Dāna-keli-kaumudī*; *nāma—*by the name; *bhāṇikām—*a kind of one-act play; *abhinetum—*to direct; *udyataḥ—*ready;*asmi—*I am;*tad—*so; *atra—*here; *nija—*my own; *abhīṣṭa—*worshipable; *daivata—*Deity; *anusmaraṇa—*of remembrance; *maṅgalam—*the auspiciousness; *ācareyam—*I should perform; *iti—*thus; *añjalim kṛtvā—*folding his hands.

**Перевод:**

**Sūtradhāra: O teacher of Tāṇḍava dance, you have reached the limits of learning.You got it right:Now, on the order of my friends, I am getting ready to stage the small one-act play entitled ‘Dāna-keli-kaumudī.’I should therefore invoke auspiciousness for this endeavor by remembering My worshipable Lord.**

**(Folding his hands, he says the following)**

**Комментарий:**

Sūtradhāra addresses the actor as *tāṇḍava-ācārya*, or a teacher of *tāṇḍava* dance. *Tāṇḍava* is a particular dance performed as an auspicious introduction to a dramatic performance. Originally the dance of Lord Śiva, it was added to the performance procedure on the request of its founder. Bharata Muni describes it at length in *Nāṭya-śāstra.*

### Verse 12

*nāmākṛṣṭa-rasajñaḥ*  
*śīlenoddīpayan sadā nandam*  
*nija-rūpotsava-dāyī*  
*sanātanātmā prabhur jayati*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nāma—*by His name; *ākṛṣṭa—*attracted; *rasa-jñaḥ—*the knowers of *rasa*; *śīlena—*by His character; *uddīpayan—*illuminating; *sadā—*always; *nandam—*Nanda, the King of Vraja; *nija—*His own; *rūpa—*by beauty; *utsava—*a festival; *dāyī—*providing; *sanātana—*eternal; *ātmā—*His self; *prabhuḥ—*our master; *jayati—*may He be glorious.

**Перевод:**

**All glories to the eternal Lord, who brings about the festival with His own beauty! Attracting the knowers of transcendental mellows with His holy name, He always gladdens Nanda Mahārāja with His dealings.**

**Комментарий:**

In this verse Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, through the mouth of the sūtradhāra, offers glorification to His worshipable Deity, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the son of Nanda Mahārāja. If we consider double meanings of the words and splitting of the syllables in this verse, it could be understood as a glorification of Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī, Rūpa Gosvāmī’s elder brother, whom he considered his spiritual master. Thus, alternatively, the verse could be understood as follows: “All glories to our master Sanātana, who is our life and soul (*ātmā*). His tongue (*rasajñaḥ*) is attracted by the Lord’s holy name, he kindles transcendental joy (*sad ānandam*) in the hearts of devotees by his character, and creates a festival for his dear Rūpa (*nija-rūpa*).” *‘Nāmākṛṣṭa-rasajñaḥ’* can also describe Sanātana Gosvāmī as “he, who attracts *rasika* devotees with his name.”

Rūpa Gosvāmī glorifies Sanātana Gosvāmī in the beginning of his other works in a similar fashion—by composing a verse with the word *‘sanātana’* in it.

### Verse 13

*naṭaḥ: bhāva, paśya paśya! gāndhāra-grāma-guros tava gāndharva-vidyā-prabandhena kuraṅga-dharmam upalambhitā rasajña-ratna-maṇḍalī nātmānam apy anusandhātum asau kṣamate!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhāva—*o learned one; *paśya paśya—*look, look; *gāndhāra-grāma —*of the musical scale beginning from the third note; *guroḥ—*of the teacher; *tava—*your; *gāndharva-vidyā—*in the science of *gāndharva* music (the kind of music that includes notes, rhythm, and words); *prabandhena—*due to the song; *kuraṅga—*of a deer; *dharmam—*the nature; *upalambhitā—*which has attained; *rasa-jña-ratna—*of jewel-like knowers of *rasa*; *maṇḍalī—*the assembly; *na—*not; *ātmānam—*itself; *api—*even; *anusandhātum—*to recognize; *asau—*this; *kṣamate—*is able.

**Перевод:**

**Actor: O learned teacher of the ‘gāndhāra’ scale, look, look! Because of your musical invocation, the best knowers of transcendental mellows are stunned just like deer. They are not even aware of themselves!**

**Комментарий:**

Indian traditional texts on the theory of music explain that there are three *grāmas*, or systematic arrangements of musical notes (*svaras*) and microtones (*śrutis*) according to the basic, or tonic note and intervals between the sounds. Thus there is *ṣaḍja-grāma*, *madhyama-grāma*, and *gāndhāra-grāma*, rooted in their respective tonics*—ṣaḍja (ṣa)*, *madhyama (ma)*, and *gāndhāra (ga)*. These *grāmas* are also mentioned in Vāyu, Agni, and Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇas.

*Saṅgīta-makaranda*, written by a person named Nārada, explains that although *gāndhāra-grāma* is practiced in *svarga-loka*, or heavenly planets, it still can be theoretically explained as an arrangement where *ga* has four *śrutis* (intervals), *ma* three, *pa* three, *dha* three, *ni* four, *ṣa* three, and *ṛ* two. It sums up to twenty-two *śrutis* per octave in total, as is in the other two *grāmas*. It is also stated that human beings cannot sing in *gāndhāra-grāma* owing to its both too high and too low pitch, though it could have been practiced on earth in ancient times. The presiding deity of *gāndhāra-grāma* is Maheśvara, or lord Śiva, its time of the year is rainy season, and its time of the day is afternoon.

### Verse 14

*(sūtradhāraḥ)*  
*prakaṭita-lalitālaṅkṛta-*  
*gāndharveyaṁ mahā-vidyā*  
*nāndī-mukhī na hi kathaṁ*  
*rasikendrānandinī bhavitā*

**Пословный перевод:**

*prakaṭita—*expressed; *lalita—*graceful; *alaṅkṛta—*decorated with musical ornaments; *gāndharvā—*performed by singing; *iyam—*this; *mahā-vidyā—*great magical incantation; *nāndī-mukhī—*the invocation verses at its outset; *na—*not; *hi—*indeed; *katham—*why; *rasika-indra—*to the foremost among the conoisseurs of *rasa*; *ānandinī—*the source of joy; *bhavitā—*will become;

**Перевод:**

**Sūtradhāra: Wouldn’t this great magical incantation, begun with nāndī verses and sung with graceful musical ornaments, bring joy to the foremost conoisseurs of rasa?**

**Комментарий:**

With his rhetoric question the sūtradhāra states that the magical incantation of *Dāna-keli-kaumudī* will for sure gladden the topmost *rasika* devotees.

Taking into account that the words *lalitā*, *gāndharvā*, and *nāndīmukhī* are also the names of the main characters of *Dāna-keli-kaumudī*, this verse could be alternatively understood as an introduction to the plot of the drama: “Wouldn’t vastly learned (*mahā-vidyā*) Nāndīmukhī bring joy to the foremost conoisseur of *rasa*, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, by revealing (*prakaṭita*) to Him Śrī Rādhā decorated by Lalitā-sakhī?”

### Verse 15-16

*(nepathye) sādhu, bhoḥ kuśīlavācārya, tathyaṁ kathayasi. yad adya nāndīmukhī gāndharvikām āvedya rasika-vṛnda-mauler vrajendra-nandanasya ciram ānandāya bhavitrī.*

*(sūtradhāraḥ) kathaṁ vana-devīyaṁ vṛndā subalena sārdham ita evābhivartate? tad atra nāṭye naṭān niyoktum itaḥ prayāva (iti niṣkrāntau. prastāvanā.)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nepathye—*from behind the scene; *sādhu—*well said; *bhoḥ—*my dear; *kuśīlava—*of actors; *ācārya—*o preceptor; *tathyam—*the truth; *kathayasi—*you speak; *yat—*because; *adya—*today; *nāndīmukhī—*Nāndīmukhī; *gāndharvikām—*about Gāndharvikā (Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī); *āvedya—*having informed; *rasika-vṛnda—*of all the of conoisseurs of *rasa*; *mauleḥ—*of the chief; *vraja-indra-nandanasya—*of the son of the king of Vraja; *ciram—*for a long time; *ānandāya—*for His happiness; *bhavitrī—*will be; *sūtradhāraḥ*—stage director; *katham—*why; *vana-devī—*forest goddess; *iyam—*this; *vṛndā—*Vṛndā; *subalena sārdham—*with Subala; *itaḥ—*here; *eva—*only; *abhivartate—*is approaching; *tat—*therefore; *atra—*in this; *nāṭye—*in drama; *naṭān—*the actors; *niyoktum—*to engage; *itaḥ—*from here; *prayāva—*let two of us leave; *iti—*thus; *niṣkrāntau—*the two exit; *prastāvanā*—the prelude.

**Перевод:**

**voice from behind the scene: Well said, my dear teacher of acting, you speak the truth: today Nandīmukhī has brought long-lasting happiness to King Nanda’s son, the best conoisseur of rasa, by relating to Him the news of Gāndharvikā \[Rādhārāṇī\].**

**Sūtradhāra: Isn’tthe forest goddess Vṛndā is coming to this very spot along with Subala? Let us then leave to engage the actors in the play.**

**(The sūtradhāra and the actor exit. End of the prelude.)**

**Комментарий:**

As sūtradhāra suspects, the voice from behind the scene belongs to Vṛndā Devī, the goddess of Vṛndāvana forest. She paraphrases the last verse spoken by the sūtradhāra beginning *‘prakaṭita-lalitā-…’*, taking up its alternative, confidential meaning.

The type of prelude, or *prastāvanā*, when one of the play characters enters by repeating a statement of sūtradhāra or the meaning of his statement, is called *kathodghāṭa*. *Kathodghāṭa* is specifically appropriate if a drama is about love. Using this dramatic device, Rūpa Gosvāmī transports us at this point to the transcendental reality of the *dāna-keli-līlā.*

## The Interlude

### Verse 17

*(tataḥ praviśati subalena samaṁ saṅkathayantī vṛndā)*  
*(vrndā ‘sādhu bhoḥ, kuśīlavācārya’ ti paṭhitvā) subala, katham etayāpi maṅgala-vārtayā tvam utphulla-mukho nābhilakṣyase?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tataḥ—*then; *praviśati—*enters; *subalena samam—*with Subala; *saṅkathayantī—*conversing;*ṛā*ṛā*sādhu—*well done; *bhoḥ—*my dear; *kuśīlava-ācārya—* teacher of acting; *iti—*the words beginning with; *paṭhitvā—*reciting; *subala—*O Subala; *katham—*how; *etayā—*due to this; *api—*even; *maṅgala-vārtayā—*auspicious news; *tvam—*you; *utphulla-mukhaḥ—*with blossoming face; *na—*do not; *abhilakṣyase—*appear.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā (enters the stage, conversing with Subala. After repeating sūtradhara’s words beginning “Well said, my dear teacher of acting,” she says): O Subala, why does even this auspicious news not light up your face with joy?**

### Verse 18

*(subalaḥ:) vṛnde, imassa pasaṅgassa visesa viṇṇāṇa suṇṇadāe muddho via jādo hmi, tā phuḍaṁ kahijjau.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vṛnde—*o Vṛnda; *imassa—*of this; *pasaṅgassa—*incident; *visesa—*specific; *viṇṇāṇa—*of understanding; *suṇṇadāe—*by the absence; *muddho—*bewildered; *via—*as if; *jādo—*became; *hmi—*I; *tā—*therefore; *phuḍam—*clearly; *kahijjau—*please speak.

**Перевод:**

**Subala: O Vṛndā, please speak more clearly. I know nothing about this incident with Nāndīmukhī and so am bewildered.**

### Verse 19

*(vṛndā:) adya rādhā sakhībhir maṇḍita-sanīḍā govinda-kuṇḍa-rodhasi makha-maṇḍape gurūṇāṁ abhyanujñayā haiyaṅgavīnaṁ vikretuṁ abhikramiṣyati tad āvedayituṁ nāndīmukhī sāndīpaner mātur upadeśena mukundam upalabdhā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*adya—*today; *rādhā—*Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī; *sakhībhiḥ—*with her girlfriends; *maṇḍita—*graced; *sanīḍā—*whose proximity; *govinda-kuṇḍa—*of Govinda Kuṇḍa; *rodhasi—*at the bank; *makha-maṇḍape—*on the sacrificial ground; *gurūṇām—*of the elders; *abhyanujñayā—*with the permission; *haiyaṅgavīnam—*ghee prepared from yesterday’s milk; *vikretum—*to sell; *abhikramiṣyati—*will come near; *tad—*this; *āvedayitum—*to inform; *nāndīmukhī—*Nandīmukhī; *sāndīpaneḥ—*of Sandīpani Muni; *mātuḥ—*of the mother; *upadeśena—*on the order; *mukundam—*Mukunda; *upalabdhā—*has approached*.*

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: Today Rādhārāṇī, beautifully surrounded by Her girlfriends, will pass nearby on Her way to the sacrificial ground on the shore of Govinda-kuṇḍa. With the permission of Her superiors, She goes there to sell fresh ghee. On the order of Paurṇamāsī, Nandīmukhī has approached Mukunda to inform Him about this.**

**Комментарий:**

Here Vṛndā reveals to Subala what news Nāndīmukhī has delivered to Kṛṣṇa. In the verse 14 sūtradhāra merely hinted at this. The day on which this tax pastime occurred is the full-moon day of Vaiśākha month (April-May). This is clear from the text 523.

### Verse 20

*(subalaḥ sānandam) vunde, esā nihila-māhurī varīasī rāhiā kahaṁ ettha lahuammi atthe guru-aṇehiṁ aṇuṇṇādā?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-ānandam—*with joy; *vunde—*o Vṛnda; *esā—*this; *nihila—*complete; *māhurī—*in sweetness; *varīasī—*the most excellent; *rāhiā—*Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī; *kaham—*how; *ettha—*in this; *lahuammi—*inferior; *atthe—*purpose; *guru-aṇehiṁ—*by Her elders; *aṇuṇṇādā—*is allowed.

**Перевод:**

**Subala (joyfully): O Vṛnda, how is that this most charming girl Rādhikā has been allowed by Her elders to do this inferior work?**

**Комментарий:**

Since Rādhā is the daughter of Vṛṣabhānu, a *vaiśya* king, She does not have to personally perform works such as selling ghee. Now Vṛndā will explain to Subala the reason for Rādhā’s doing this.

### Verse 21

*(vṛndā:)*  
*yad-ahani havanīyaṁ hāri haiyaṅgavīnaṁ*  
*svayam idam upahāryaṁ go-duhām aṅganābhiḥ*  
*upaharaṇa-karīṇām apy abhīṣṭārtha-siddhir*  
*munibhir abhihitāsya prakriyeyaṁ makhasya*

**Пословный перевод:**

*yat-ahani—*on which day; *havanīyam—*is to be offered in the sacrifice; *hāri—*delightful; *haiyaṅgavīnam—*fresh ghee; *svayam—*directly; *idam—*this; *upahāryam—*must be presented; *go-duhām—*of the cowherds; *aṅganābhiḥ—*by youngladies; *upaharaṇa-karīṇām—*of those who bring it; *api—*and; *abhīṣṭa-artha—*of whatever they desire; *siddhiḥ—*the achievement; *munibhiḥ—*by thesages; *abhihitā—*declared; *asya—*of this (sacrifice); *prakriyā—*the procedure; *iyam—*this; *makhasya—*of the sacrifice.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: Because on the day of this sacrifice on which fine, fresh ghee needs to be offered in the fire, young cowherd ladies must themselves bring it. And the girls who bring it, so the sages have declared, will obtain whatever they desire. Such is the standard procedure for this sacrifice.**

**Комментарий:**

What the gopīs are going to bring is *‘hayaṅgavīna*,*’* or clarified butter (ghee) prepared from the previous day’s milk or yoghurt. Vṛndā explains to Subala that Rādhikā is not doing an inferior work. It is a sacrificial procedure which will fulfull the desires of the cowherd girls who follow it.

### Verse 22-23

*(subalaḥ:)*  
*eriso so kassa mahantassa maho?*  
*(vṛndā:)*  
*sugṛhīta-nāmadheyasyānaka-dundubheḥ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*eriso—*such as; *so—*that; *kassa—*of which; *mahantassa—*great person; *maho—*sacrifice; *vṛndā—*Vṛndā speaks; *sugṛhīta—*which is uttered for aupiciousness; *nāmadheyasya—*whose name; *ānaka-dundubheḥ—*of Ānaka-dundubhi (Vasudeva).

**Перевод:**

**Subala: Who is that great person performing this sacrifice?**

**Vṛndā: He who is known by the auspicious name Ānaka-dundubhi.**

**Комментарий:**

Ānaka-dundubhi is a name of Vasudeva, Kṛṣṇa’s father from Mathurā. Literally, the words *‘ānaka’* and *‘dundubhi’* are the names for large kettledrums. Vasudeva is called by these names, because at the time of his birth the jubilant demigods were beating such drums, knowing that the Lord is going to make His appearance through this child.

### Verse 24-25

*(subalaḥ:)*  
*mahupuraṁ mukkia kahaṁ vaṇa-majjhe tiṇā ārambhido jaṇṇo?*  
*(vṛndā:)*  
*jīvati kaṁsa-hatake kathaṁ mathurāyāṁ tasya yajña-siddhiḥ? atas tenātra bhāgurir nāma gargasya jāmātā sva-pratinidhir nyadyāyi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mahupuram—*the city of Madhu (Mathurā); *mukkia—*leaving; *kahaṁ—*why; *vaṇa—*of the forest; *majjhe—*in the middle; *tiṇā—*by him; *ārambhido—*begun; *jaṇṇo—*the sacrifice; *jīvati—*as long as he is alive; *kaṁsa-hatake—*the wretched Kaṁsa; *katham—*how; *mathurāyām—*in Mathurā; *tasya—*of his (Vasudeva’s); *yajña—*sacrifice; *siddhiḥ—*the accomplishment; *ataḥ—*therefore; *tena—*by him; *atra—*here; *bhāguriḥ nāma—*named Bhāguri; *gargasya—*of GargaMuni; *jāmātā—*the son-in-law; *sva-pratinidhiḥ—*as his personal representative; *nyadhāyi—*has been appointed.

**Перевод:**

**Subala: But why did he leave Mathurā and startthis sacrifice in the middle of the forest?**

**Vṛndā: How can Vasudeva performthe sacrifice in Mathurā as long as wretched King Kaṁsa is alive? Thatis why Vasudeva has appointed a representative, the son-in-law of Garga Muni named Bhāguri, to perform the sacrifice for him here.**

**Комментарий:**

*mantra-nyāsair iha mura-ripos tat-purodhāḥ purastāt*  
*sarvāṅgāni prakaṭa-nigamo bhāgurir yo ’bhirakṣya*  
*āśīrbhiś ca pratidinam aho tac-chiro jighratīdaṁ*  
*vande tāvan muni-sura-pates tasya pādābja-yugmam*

**“How wonderful! Every morning the learned chief priest of Mahārāja Nanda‘s house, Bhāguri Muni, protects all of Kṛṣṇa’s limbs with mantras and** ***nyāsas*****. He then smells Kṛṣṇa’s head, showering Him with benedictions. I bow down to the lotus feet of Bhāguri Muni, the leader of godly sages.”**

### Verse 26-27

*(subalaḥ:)*  
*phuḍaṁ āhiārio eso jaṇṇo.*  
*(vṛndā:)*  
*na hi, na hi! kintu śāntiko ‘yam, yatra sutād apy adhikasya mitra-sūnoḥ kṛṣṇasya sva-putrasya ca rāmasya nikhilāniṣṭa-śāntiḥ phalam.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*phuḍaṁ—*obviously; *āhiārio—*for the purpose of casting an evil spell; *eso—*this; *jaṇṇo—*sacrifice; *na hi—*certainly not; *kintu—*rather; *śāntikaḥ—*for securing peace; *ayam—*this (sacrifice); *yatra—*in which; *sutāt—*then his own son; *api—*even; *adhikasya—*more (dear); *mitra-sūnoḥ—* for his friend’s son; *kṛṣṇasya—*Kṛṣṇa; *sva-putrasya—*for his own son; *ca—*also; *rāmasya—*Balarāma; *nikhila—*of everything; *aniṣṭa—*undesirable; *śāntiḥ—*thecounteraction; *phalam—*the result*.*

**Перевод:**

**Subala: This sacrifice is obviously to cast an evil spell on someone.**

**Vṛndā: Not at all, not at all! This sacrifice is for securing peace. It will counteract everything undesirable for Vasudeva’s son, Balarāma, and for the son of Vasudeva’s friend Nanda. That son of Nanda, Krsna, is more dear to Vasudeva than his own son.**

**Комментарий:**

At the time of Kṛṣṇa’s appearance fire sacrifices by the *brāhmaṇas* had almost stopped. This was so because the demoniac kings such as Kaṁsa didn’t welcome fire sacrifices to the Supreme Lord Viṣṇu. For this reason, Vasudeva, not daring to perform the sacrifice in front of Kaṁsa’s eyes, had to arrange for it in a hidden way in the middle of Vṛndāvana forest.

Subala is still suspicious, for he thinks that the reason why the sacrifice is performed with such secrecy is because it is an *ābhicārika* (*āhiārio*) sacrifice, or a sacrifice intended to harm someone. Vṛndā removes that doubt by explaining in text 27 the actual purpose of Vasudeva.

Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma are the supreme persons, the origins and absolute controllers of this creation. What then could be of danger to Them? Is there a need at all to perform a sacrifice for Their safety? Not from the point of view of *tattva*, or philosophical understanding, but from the consideration of *rasa*, or transcendental loving reciprocation between the Lord and a devotee. Vasudeva is a devotee in *vātsalya-bhāva*, or parental affection, and it is intrinsic to his devotional mood to feel anxious for the safety of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma. The interactions between the Lord and His pure devotees are taking place on the platform of *rasa*, where philosophical considerations often step back to give place for transcendental sentiments.

Vṛndā Devī here clearly says that Kṛṣṇa is actually the son of Vasudeva’s friend Nanda Mahārāja. This is the confidential understanding of Kṛṣṇa’s birth pastime. According to the opinion of the ācāryas, which is based on scriptural evidence, Kṛṣṇa was born simultaneously to Devakī in Mathurā and to Yaśodā in Gokula.

### Verse 28-29

*(subalaḥ:)*  
*(kṣaṇaṁ vibhāvya sa-kautukam)*  
*pia-vaassassa suiraṁ hiaaṭṭhidā sā gariṭṭhā keli-ghaṭṭāhiāridāṇurūvassa rāhiā-pahudīhinto dāṇa-ggaha-vilāsassa lalasā ajja ccea siddhā!*  
*(vṛndā:)*  
*subala, mad-vidhānām api nidhānāyate sā dāna-līlā. tad ehi mānasa-gaṅgā-tīram avatarāva.*  
*(ity ubhau tathā kurutaḥ)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kṣaṇam—*for a moment; *vibhāvya—*thinking; *sa-kautukam—*with eagerness; *pia—*dear; *vaassassa—*of my friend; *suiraṁ—*for a very long time; *hiaa—*in the heart; *ṭṭhidā—*which was been maintain; *sā—*that; *gariṭṭhā—*most intense; *keli—*as a sport; *ghaṭṭa-ahiāridā—*becoming a toll-station authority; *aṇurūvassa—*who acts accordingly; *rāhiā-pahudīhinto—*from (the cowherd girls) beginning with Rādhikā; *dāṇa-ggaha—*of levying a tax; *vilāsassa—*for the pastime; *lālasā—*the longing; *ajja—*today; *ccea—*only; *siddhā—*will be fulfilled; *subala—*o Subala; *mat-vidhānām—*for persons like myself; *api—*also; *nidhānāyate—*is a treasure; *sā—*that; *dāna-līlā—*toll pastime; *tat—*therefore; *ehi—*come; *mānasa-gaṅgā—*of the Mānasa-gaṅgā; *tīram—*to the bank; *avatarāva—*let both of us descend; *iti—*thus; *ubhau—*both of them; *tathā—*in that way; *kurutaḥ—*do.

**Перевод:**

**Subala (Thinking for a moment, says eagerly): To enact the pastime of levying a tax from Rādhikā and other cowherd girls was for a very long time the most cherished heart’s desire of my dear friend. Pretending to be a tax collector, he will fulfill that desire on this very day!**

**Vṛndā: O Subala, the toll pastime is also a treasure for the devotees like me. So come, let us go down to the bank of the Mānasa-gaṅgā.**

**(Both do so.)**

**Комментарий:**

Analyzing the intricacies of *mādhurya-rasa*, Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī has listed the tax pastime as one of special conjugal exchanges that are by themselves considered to be the *anubhāvas* (external actions that indicate one’s inner loving mood) of *mādhurya-rati* (conjugal love).

Mānasa-gaṅgā is a lake situated at Govardhana hill. It is called so because once Lord Kṛṣṇa drew the waters of celestial Gaṅgā into that lake by the power of His mind (*mānasa*). Nowadays it is the largest sacred pond in Vraja area, situated in the middle of Goverdhan town.

### Verse 30-32

*(subalaḥ:)*  
*vṛnde, vaṇ-antarāle akarālāṇaṁ marālāṇaṁ dhaṇi-dhoraṇī dhuṇī-ḍāhiṇe suṇijjau.*  
*(vṛndā:)*  
*nāyaṁ marālāṇāṁ dhvaniḥ, kintu paśu-pāla-bālā-tulākoṭīnām.*  
*(punar nirūpya sānandam)*  
*śoṇe maṇḍita-mūrdhni kuṇḍalatayā kḷpte dukūlottame*  
*nyastāṁ svarṇa-ghaṭīṁ vahanty acaṭulāṁ haiyaṅgavīnojjvalām*  
*dūre paśya tathā-vidhābhir abhitaḥ smerā sakhībhir vṛtā*  
*rādhā mānasa-jāhnavī-taṭa-bhuvaṁ svairaṁ parikrāmati*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vṛnde—*O Vṛnda; *vaṇ—*of the forest; *antarāle—*in the middle; *akarālāṇāṁ—*not ferocious; *marālāṇāṁ—*of swans; *dhaṇi—*of the sound; *dhoraṇī—*a continuous succession; *dhunī—*of the river; *ḍāhiṇe—*on the southern bank; *suṇijjau—*please hear; *na—*is not; *ayam—*this; *marālāṇām—*of swans; *dhvaniḥ—*the sound; *kintu—*but; *paśu-pāla-bālā—*of the cowherd girls; *tulākoṭīnām—*of the ankle-bells; *punaḥ—*again; *nirūpya—*observing; *sa-ānandam—*joyfully; *śoṇe—*red; *maṇḍita—*decorated; *mūrdhni—*on Her head; *kuṇḍalatayā—*in a coil; *kḷpte—*which is arranged; *dukūla-uttame—*covered with the finest cloth; *nyastām—*placed; *svarṇa—*golden; *ghaṭīm—*a pot; *vahantī—*carrying; *acaṭulām—*steady; *haiyaṅgavīna—*with ghee; *ujjvalām—*shining; *dūre—*in the distance; *paśya—*look; *tathā-vidhābhiḥ—*similar; *abhitaḥ—*closely; *smerā—*smiling; *sakhībhiḥ—*by Her girlfriends; *vṛtā—*surrounded; *rādhā—*Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī; *mānasa-jāhnavī—*of the Mānasa-gaṅgā; *taṭa-bhuvam—*along the bank; *svairam—*slowly; *parikrāmati—*walks.

**Перевод:**

**Subala: Vṛnda, listen: Peaceful swans are warbling on the southern bank of the river.**

**Vṛndā: This is not the warbling of swans, but the tinkling of the gopīs’ ankle-bells.**

**(Observing more, she says joyfully:) Just see how, with a smiling face, Rādhārāṇī slowly walks along the bank of the Mānasa-gaṅgā. She has firmly placed a golden pot resplendent with ghee on the finest red cloth which adorns Her head, coiling on the top of it, and Rādhārāṇī’s girlfriends, appearing in the same way, closely encircle Her.**

**Комментарий:**

Viśvanātha Cakravartī comments that even though the piece of cloth which serves as a stand for carrying a pot on the head is usually far away from being considered a decoration, the red coiled cloth under Rādhā’s golden ghee pot served as such. It was as if a red sun disk situated above the dense darkness of Rādhā’s hair, and above that red sun disk was shining a steady round lightning bolt of a golden ghee pot. This is how beautiful Rādhā’s head appeared.

### Verse 33-34

*(subalaḥ:)*  
*ammahe cañcacāhiṁ sahaarīhiṁ puṇo puṇo uddividāe rāhiāe āhaṇḍala-koaṇda-ladāe via jalada-maṇḍalī maṇḍijjaī ṇia-māhurieṇa vundāḍaī!*  
*(vṛndā:) subala, rādhā-mādhuryasya girām apy agra-gāmitvād uddeśe ’pi sāhasikyam evāvadhārayāmi.*  
*(iti mukham ānamayya sāpatrapam)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ammahe—*ah; *cañcacāhiṁ—*restless; *sahaarīhiṁ—*by Her companions; *puṇo puṇo—*again and again; *uddividāe—*who is illumined; *rāhiāe—*by Rādhikā; *āhaṇḍala-koaṇda—*a rainbow; *ladāe—*by the creeper; *via—*as if; *jala-da—*of the clouds; *maṇḍalī—*a group; *maṇḍijjaī—*is graced; *ṇia-māhurieṇa—*by Her own sweetness; *vundāḍaī—*Vṛndāvana (the forest of Vṛndā); *subala—*o Subala; *rādhā-mādhuryasya—*of Rādhārāṇī’s sweetness; *girām—*of the words; *api—*even; *agra-gāmitvāt—*by being ahead; *uddeśe—*when there is a hint; *api—*even; *sāhasikyam—*boldness; *eva—*only; *avadhārayāmi—*I ascertain; *iti—*thus; *mukham—*Her face; *ānamayya—*lowering; *sa-apatrapam—*with bashfulness.

**Перевод:**

**Subala: How beautiful! Just as the slender vine of a rainbow bedecks a cluster of rainclouds, Rādhikā, flashed again and again by Her lightning-like companions, graces the Vṛndāvana forest with the sweetness of Her beauty.**

**Vṛndā: O Subala, the sweet beauty of Rādhā is beyond the power of words. Thus I consider it impudent to even try to describe it.**

**(lowering her face, she shyly says:)**

**Комментарий:**

The word *‘cañcalā’* means “restless.” By extension, it can also mean “lightning”, owing to the lightning’s restless and unsteady nature. Thus, Rādhikā’s restless female friends (*sakhīs*) are like lightnings, which repeatedly cast flashes on rainbow-like Rādhikā. Though rainbow, or literally *‘ākhaṇḍala-daṇḍa’* (“Indra’s bow”), is mainly yellow, it is embedded in the spectrum of other colors—blue, orange, red, green, violet, and purple. In this way, the comparison of Rādhikā to a rainbow suggests that She, of a golden complexion, is decorated by ornaments and garments of various colors. This is Viśvanātha Cakravartī’s explanation.

Surendranath Shastri adds that Vṛndāvana forest is naturally compared to a cluster of rainclouds, which are also dark, because Vṛndāvana is full of dark tamāla trees and surrounded by the dark waters of Yamunā.

### Verse 35

*anālocya vrīḍāṁ yam iha bahu mene bahu-tṛṇaṁ*  
*tyajann īrṣyāpannāṁ madhu-ripur abhīṣṭām api ramām*  
*janaḥ so ’yaṁ yasyāḥ śrayati na hi dāsye ’py avasaraṁ*  
*samarthas tāṁ rādhāṁ bhavati bhuvi kaḥ ślāghitum api*

**Пословный перевод:**

*anālocya—*not considering; *vrīḍām—*shame; *yam—*whom; *iha—*in this world; *bahu—*highly; *mene—*He considered; *bahu-tṛṇam—*as a straw; *tyajan—*giving up; *īrṣyā-āpannām—*afflicted with jealousy; *madhu-ripuḥ—*the enemy of Madhu; *abhīṣṭām—*dearmost; *api—*even; *ramām—*Ramā (Lakṣmī); *janaḥ—*person; *saḥ ayam—*that very; *yasyāḥ—*whose; *śrayati—*attain; *na—*does not; *hi—*indeed; *dāsye—*in the service; *api—*even; *avasaram—*an opportunity; *samarthaḥ—*capable; *tām—*that; *rādhām—*Rādhā; *bhavati—*is; *bhuvi—*in the world; *kaḥ—*who; *ślāghitum—*to glorify; *api—*even.

**Перевод:**

**The enemy of Madhu loved me so much, that without any shame He abandoned as a piece of straw His dearmost wife Lakṣmī, who was afflicted with jealousy. When I myself do not get a chance to even serve Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, who in this world is fit to glorify Her?**

**Комментарий:**

Vṛndā Devī deems Rādhā’s beauty beyond the power of words to describe. It is true indeed, for even most beautiful and awe-inspiring objects of this world that one may use in poetic comparison are by no means sufficient to fully convey Rādhā’s actual splendor, by which She totally captivates the mind of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Rādhā is the essence of Kṛṣṇa’s pleasure potency, and thus He is the One for whose pleasure She eternally manifests Her incomparable beauty. *Dvārakā-māhātmya* of the *Skānda Purāṇa*describes Tulasī, an expansion of Vṛndā Devī, as a wife of Lord Viṣṇu, even more dear to Him than Lakṣmī (*yathā lakṣmīḥ priyā viṣṇos tulasī ca tato ’dhikā*). Despite being so exalted that the Lord neglects even Lakṣmī for her sake, Vṛndā still feels unfit to even serve Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, what to speak of glorifying Her—there could be no person qualified for that. This is Vṛndā Devī’s humble conclusion.

### Verse 36

*bhavatu, tathāpi sva-giraṁ vāsayituṁ tat-saurabhaṁ kiñcid udañcayāmi*  
*(iti subalam ālokya)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhavatu—*let it be; *tathā api—*still; *sva-giram—*my own words; *vāsayitum—*to scent; *tat—*Her; *saurabham—*fragrance; *kiñcit—*slightly; *udañcayāmi—*Iwill evoke; *iti—*thus; *subalam—*at Subala; *ālokya—*looking.

**Перевод:**

**…All right, at least to scent my own words I will still evoke Her fragrance.**

**(looking at Subala, she says the following:)**

**Комментарий:**

Vṛndā implies that it is not Rādhārāṇī’s beauty that will become glorious when described by her words. It is rather Vṛndā’s words that will become glorious by attempting to describe Rādhārāṇī’s beauty.

### Verse 37

*rādhāyā mukha-maṇḍalena balinā candrasya padmasya vā*  
*vyākṣiptā suṣameti keyam abudhaiḥ ślāghā vinirmīyate*  
*yad dūre ’py anubhūya bhūyasi sudhā-śuddhāpi candrāvalī*  
*padmālī ca visṛjya śīryati nijāṁ saundarya-darpa-śriyam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*rādhāyāḥ—*of Rādhā; *mukha-maṇḍalena—*by the oval of Her face; *balinā—*mighty; *candrasya—*of the moon; *padmasya—*of a lotus; *vā—*or; *vyākṣiptā—*overthrown; *su-ṣamā—*beauty; *iti—*thus; *kā—*what; *iyam—*this; *abudhaiḥ—*by the foolish; *ślāghā—*praise; *vinirmīyate—*can be composed; *yat—*because; *dūre—*in the distance; *api—*even; *anubhūya—*perceiving; *bhūyasi—*great; *sudhā—*with nectar; *śuddhā—*pure; *api—*even though; *candra-āvalī—*many moons; *padma-ālī—*many lotuses; *ca—*and; *visṛjya—*rejecting; *śīryati—*shatter; *nijām—*their own; *saundarya—*of beauty; *darpa—*and arrogance; *śriyam—*wealth.

**Перевод:**

**Is this a praise at all: “the mighty oval of Rādhā’s face overthrows the beauty of the moon and a lotus”? Only foolish people would compose such glorification, for the truth of the matter is that many, many moons and lotuses put together, even though resplendent due to their nectar, discard and crush into pieces the wealth of their own beauty and pride, when they behold Rādhā’s face even from very far away.**

**Комментарий:**

The second half of the verse could be understood in another way: “…many, many Candrāvalīs and Lakṣmīs (*padmā-ālī*) put together, even though more pure than nectar, discard and crush into pieces the wealth of their own beauty and pride when they behold Rādhā’s face even from very far away.” Padmā, apart from being a name of Lakṣmī, is also the name of a gopī, a friend of Candrāvalī.

Just as Kṛṣṇa is the original Supreme Person and all other forms of God like Viṣṇu, Rāma and Nṛsiṁha are His partial expansions, Rādhā is Kṛṣṇa’s original female counterpart and all the consorts of other forms of God like Lakṣmī and Sītā, and even other young gopīs of Vraja, are Her partial expansions. The verse from *Brhad-gautamīya-tantra*confirms this:

*devī kṛṣṇa-mayī proktā*  
*rādhikā para-devatā*  
*sarva-lakṣmī-mayī sarva-*  
*kāntiḥ sammohinī parā*

**“The transcendental goddess Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī is the direct counterpart of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. She is the central figure for all the goddesses of fortune. She possesses all the attractiveness to attract the all-attractive Personality of Godhead. She is the primeval internal potency of the Lord.”**

The original forms of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, naturally containing the beauty and potency of Their expansions, are endowed with matchless attributes that none of Their expansions could exhibit:

*viśva-vandya-yauvatābhivanditāpi yā ramā*  
*rūpa-navya-yauvanādi-sampadā na yat-samā*  
*śīla-hārda-līlayā ca sā yato 'sti nādhikā*  
*mahyam ātma-pāda-padma-dāsya-dāstu rādhikā*

**“Although the goddess Ramā (Lakṣmī Devī) is adored by other youthful goddesses, who are themselves glorified throughout the universe, she is still nowhere near Śrī Rādhikā in the matter of beauty, fresh youthfulness, or other divine feminine opulences. The goddess Ramā does not excel Rādhikā in the pastimes of natural affection as well. May Śrī Rādhikā bestow upon me the service of Her lotus feet.”**

Apart from Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, Candrāvalī is another most prominent young *gopī* in Vraja. She is mentioned along with Rādhā in *Puruṣa-bodhinī Upaniṣad*: *‘gokulākhye māthura-maṇḍale…dve parśve candrāvalī rādhikā ca’—*“In Gokula, a part of the Mathurā district… Rādhikā and Candrāvalī are on the both sides of the Lord.” Although Candrāvalī is also Rādhārāṇī’s expansion, she, along with her group of gopīs, exhibits the transcendental spirit of rivalry to Rādhārāṇī and Her group in regard to attracting and serving Kṛṣṇa in the mood of conjugal love. This rivalry is the part of variedness of the spiritual world and serves the purpose of enhancing the conjugal pastimes of Kṛṣṇa. Rūpa Gosvāmī concludes, however, that Rādhā surpasses Candrāvalī in all respects:

*tayor apy ubhayor madhye*  
*rādhikā sarvathādhikā*  
*mahābhāva-svarūpeyaṁ*  
*guṇair ativarīyasī*

**“Of these two gopīs \[Rādhārāṇī and Candrāvalī\], Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī is superior in all respects. She is the embodiment of** ***mahābhāva*****, and She surpasses all in good qualities.”**

The same is confirmed by Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī:

*vaśī-kāraiś candrāvali-mukha-navīna-vraja-satīḥ*  
*kṣipaty ārād yā tāṁ hari-dayita-rādhāṁ bhaja manaḥ*

**“O mind, just worship Lord Hari’s beloved Rādhā. With Her ability to control Him, She leaves far behind all other pious young girls of Vraja headed by Candrāvali.”**

### Verse 38-39

*(subalaḥ:)*  
*eso vi se kettie ukkariso?*  
*(vṛndā:)*  
*subala, govardhana-mūrdhani śyāmala-maṇḍapikāyāḥ pṛṣṭhataḥ śikhaṇḍa-maulis tvayopanīyatām. mayā tu manoharam āsāṁ vihāra-kauśalam avalokayantyā śanair āgantavyam.*  
*(iti subalena saha niṣkrāntā)*  
*(viṣkambhakaḥ)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*eso—*this; *vi—*also; *se—*Her; *kettie—*how great; *ukkariso—*excellence; *subala—*o Subala; *govardhana-mūrdhani—*on the top of the Govardhana Hill; *śyāmala-maṇḍapikāyāḥ—*the dark pavilion; *pṛṣṭhataḥ—*from behind; *śikhaṇḍa-mauliḥ—*Kṛṣṇa, who wears a peacock feather on His head; *tvayā—*by you; *upanīyatām—*should be brought; *mayā—*I; *tu—*however; *manoharam—*charming; *āsām—*of the gopīs; *vihāra—*in the pastimes; *kauśalam—*the expertise; *avalokayantyā—*who is watching; *śanaiḥ—*slowly; *āgantavyam—*should come; *iti—*thus; *subalena saha—*with Subala; *niṣkrāntā—*leaves; *viṣkambhakaḥ—*the interlude.

**Перевод:**

**Subala: Is this how great She is?**

**Vṛndā: Subala, you should bring Kṛṣṇa, whose head is decorated with a peacock feather, from behind the dark pavilion on the top of the Govardhana Hill to this place. In the meantime I will have a look at how, with their fascinating expertise, the gopīs pass their time, and then eventually return.**

**(Saying this, she exits along with Subala.)**

**(Thus ends the interlude.)**

**Комментарий:**

This conversation between Vṛndā Devī and Subala constitutes the interlude (*viṣkambhaka*) of the play. In Sanskrit drama the purpose of the interlude is to connect the past incidents to what is going to happen next. The interlude deals with the first stage (*sandhi*) of the plot, where certain external circumstances invoke the hero’s desire to achieve something. (see appendix…) In *Dāna-keli-kaumudī* this happens when Nāndīmukhī informs Kṛṣṇa that on this day Rādhārāṇī will pass by Govardhana hill on Her way to Govinda-kuṇḍa. As Subala stated, Kṛṣṇa’s long-standing eagerness for enacting the toll pastime will surely arise at His hearing of the wonderful opportunity at hand.

Now, Vṛndā and Subala part from each other, eager to accomplish their individual tasks for the ultimate success of the pastime.

## The Gopīs’ Journey

### Verse 40-41

*(tataḥ praviśati sakhī-catuṣṭayenāsajyamānā rādhā)*  
*(rādhā)*  
*ammahe, vaṇa-lehae loaṇa-lohaṇijjadā!*  
*(iti saṁskṛtam āśritya)*  
*lalite, paśya paśya—*  
*pada-tatibhir alaṅkṛtojjvaleyaṁ*  
*dhvaja-kuliśāṅkuśa-paṅkajāṅkitābhiḥ*  
*nakhara-luṭhita-kuṭḍmalā vanālī*  
*kim api dhinoti dhunoti cāntaraṁ me*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tataḥ—*thereafter; *praviśati—*enters; *sakhī-catuṣṭayena—*by Her four girlfriends; *āsajyamānā—*accompanied; *rādhā—*Rādhā; *ammahe—*O; *vaṇa—*of the forest; *lehae—*of the line; *loaṇa—*for the eyes; *lohaṇijjadā—*the attractiveness; *iti—*thus; *saṁskṛtam—*Sanskrit; *āśritya—*resorting to; *lalite—*o Lalitā; *paśya paśya—*look, look; *pada—*of the footprints; *tatibhiḥ—*by the multitude; *alaṅkṛtā—*decorated; *ujjvalā—*and shining; *iyam—*this; *dhvaja—*by the flag; *kuliśa—*thunderbolt; *aṅkuśa—*elephant goad; *paṅkaja—*and lotus; *aṅkitābhiḥ—*marked; *nakhara—*between the nails; *luṭhita—*pressed; *kuṭḍmalā—*in which the buds; *vana—*of groves; *ālī—*the row; *kim api—*something; *dhinoti—*pleases; *dhunoti—*shakes; *ca—*and; *antaram—*heart; *me—*My.

**Перевод:**

**(Enters Rādhā, accompanied by four sakhīs)**

**Rādhā: Ah, this forest line is so attractive to behold!**

**(speaks in Sanskrit): Lalitā, look, look! These groves are resplendent with many footprints marked with the flag, thunderbolt, elephant goad, and lotus. And the flower buds in those groves are pressed with nails…There is something that makes my heart happy and trembling at the same time.**

**Комментарий:**

The four sakhīs accompanying Rādhā are Lalitā, Viśākhā, Citrā, and Campakalatā. The footprints all over the groves belong to Kṛṣṇa, just as the nailmarks on flower buds that He left while collecting flowers.

*Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi* explainsthat Kṛṣṇa’s footprints and nailmarks, along with His playing flute, bugle, or a string instrument, Kṛṣṇa’s singing, fragrance, the sound of His ornaments, and expertise in various skills, is a type of stimuli called *sambandhy-uddīpana* (“a related stimuli”), or a stimuli which can be perceived even when Kṛṣṇa is not present in front of the eyes. On the other hand, Kṛṣṇa’s footprints and nailmarks, just as garlands worn by Him, are classified as *sannihita* (“proximate”)*-sambandhy-uddīpana*, a stimuli that does not necessitate Kṛṣṇa’s personal presence along with it, unlike His flute playing or singing, which can’t exist without Kṛṣṇa’s being there. According to Viṣṇudāsa, Rādhā feels the happiness of seeing these two stimuli (*uddīpana*)—Kṛṣṇa’s footprints and nailmarks. At the same time, however, Rādhā’s heart is shaking. This is because She experiences pain due to not having Kṛṣṇa’s association.

Viśvanātha Cakravartī analyzes the verse in the following way. Rādhārāṇī uses the word *‘vanālī’* (“the row of groves”) with a double meaning, thus imagining the grove to be a gopī: “This lovely *sakhī* in the form of a forest grove (*vana-ālī*) is resplendent with the footprints marked with the flag, thunderbolt, elephant goad, and lotus. In this way, she appears like a wife whose husband runs around her. Also, there are nailmarks on her bud-like breasts (*nakhara-luṭhita-kuṭmalā*). By these signs She indicates to Me, her affectionate friend, that Kṛṣṇa has enjoyed her. This brings me only joy, and, seeing the signs of conjugal enjoyment on her body, I tremble, full of eagerness for the same.”

Scripturesdescribe how devotees, in their ecstasy, sometimes attribute various transcendental symptoms even to inanimate objects like trees and rivers and dull living entities like birds. Those living entities, that are actually not capable of experiencing transcendental ecstasy, are viewed at that time as animate and full of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Looking at the forest grove, Rādhārāṇī conceives of it as Her sakhī, a female gopī-friend. She is elated up to the point of trembling after noticing that Her “sakhī” have had Kṛṣṇa’s intimate association. How could someone feel and behave in such a way is truly a puzzle to the self-centered materialistic mind, which does not cease its pursuit after self-gratification even in the most sublime worldly relationships. The unique quality of Rādhā’s selfless love is that Her happiness lies in satisfaction of the object of Her love, not Herself—the subject. Rāmānanda Rāya points this out in his conversation with Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu: “Although the gopīs, Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī’s friends, do not desire to enjoy themselves directly with Kṛṣṇa, Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī makes a great endeavor to induce Kṛṣṇa to enjoy Himself with the gopīs. Presenting various pleas for the gopīs, Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī sometimes sends the gopīs to Kṛṣṇa just to enable them to associate with Him directly. At such times, She enjoys happiness ten million times greater than that enjoyed through direct association. The transcendental mellow is nourished by that mutual behavior in transcendental love of Godhead. When Lord Kṛṣṇa sees how the gopīs have developed pure love for Him, He becomes very satisfied.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda writes: “Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī and the gopīs are not interested in their personal happiness derived from association with Kṛṣṇa. Rather, they become happy by seeing one another associate with Kṛṣṇa. In this way their dealings are further nourished by love of Godhead, and seeing this, Kṛṣṇa is very pleased.”

This is the true *kṛṣṇa-prema*, free of any tinge of mundane lust, as expressed by Rādhārāṇī Herself through the mouth of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu: “If a gopī envious of Me satisfies Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa desires her, I shall not hesitate to go to her house and become her maidservant, for then My happiness will be awakened.”

## Verse 42(a-b)

*(lalitā)*  
*(smitvā) visāhe, pekkha pekkha—*  
*(iti saṁskṛtam āśritya)sadā sudhā-bandhura-veṇu-mādhurī*  
*vismāritāśeṣa-śarīra-karmaṇām*  
*ciraṁ tiraścām api yatra kānane*  
*manaḥ samādher na kadāpy udāsyate*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smitvā—*smiling; *visāhe—*o Viśākhā; *pekkha pekkha*—look, look; *iti—*thus; *saṁskṛtam—*Sanskrit; *āśritya*—resorting to; *sadā—*always; *sudhā—*like nectar; *bandhura—*pleasant; *veṇu—*of the flute; *mādhurī—*the sweetness; *vismārita—*are made to be forgotten; *aśeṣa—*all; *śarīra-karmaṇām—*whose bodily activities; *ciram—*for a long time; *tiraścām—*of the animals; *api—*even; *yatra—*in which; *kānane—*forest; *manaḥ—*the minds; *samādheḥ—*to one-pointed absorption; *na—*does not; *kadā api—*at any time; *udāsyate—*are left indifferent*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā smiles: Viśākhā, just see, just see!**

**(speaks in Sanskrit): Hearing the sweet vibration of the flute, even the animals in this forest became oblivious to all their bodily activities for a long time. Their minds are deeply absorbed in trance, for this pleasant like nectar sound never leaves them indifferent.**

**Комментарий:**

Lalitā smiles at how Rādhā lost Her composure just by seeing the forest grove. Seeing Rādhā’s mind so easily moved, Lalitā concludes that it is due to the magic spell of Kṛṣṇa’s flute that continues to influence Rādhā since She heard it the last time. That sweetness is so amazing that by hearing it even the animals, who are normally dull and extremely restless, reach a very deep state of trance. On the contrary, it causes the gopīs, who are usually very steady and knowledgeable, to become restless and lose their ability to discriminate. This is the surprising behavior of Kṛṣṇa’s flute sound—it makes beings and things display characteristics that are opposite to their nature. This is the analysis of the verse by Viśvanātha Cakravartī.

With her words Lalitā implies that it is not surprising that the fully conscious beings become oblivious to the external world at hearing Kṛṣṇa’s flute music, for even the minds of animals have reached the state of *samādhi* by its influence.

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* the gopīs glorify the magical effect of Kṛṣṇa’s flute: “Using their upraised ears as vessels, the cows are drinking the nectar of the flute-song flowing out of Kṛṣṇa's mouth. The calves, their mouths full of milk from their mothers’ moist nipples, stand still as they take Govinda within themselves through their tear-filled eyes and embrace Him within their hearts. O mother, in this forest all the birds have risen onto the beautiful branches of the trees to see Kṛṣṇa. With closed eyes they are simply listening in silence to the sweet vibrations of His flute, and they are not attracted by any other sound. Surely these birds are on the same level as great sages. When the rivers hear the flute-song of Kṛṣṇa, their minds begin to desire Him, and thus the flow of their currents is broken and their waters are agitated, moving around in whirlpools. Then with the arms of their waves the rivers embrace Murāri's lotus feet and, holding on to them, present offerings of lotus flowers…When Lord Kṛṣṇa plays on His flute, the sweet music causes the moving living entities to become stunned and the nonmoving trees to tremble with ecstasy.”

### Verse 43-44

*(rādhā)*  
*(sva-gatam) abi ṇāma atakkidaṁ āadua heaṅgavīṇovahāriṇīṇaṁ amhāṇaṁ maggaṁ ajja rundhissadi vainda-ṇandaṇo.*  
*(prakāśam) halā lalide, eṇhiṁ pasthāṇa-osare īsi vihasia kiṁ bhaṇidaṁ bhaavadīe?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sva-gatam—*to Herself; *abi ṇāma —*hopefully; *atakkidaṁ—*unexpected; *āadua—*coming; *heaṅgavīṇa—*ghee; *uvahāriṇīṇaṁ—*who bring; *amhāṇaṁ—*of us; *maggaṁ—*the way; *ajja—*today; *rundhissadi—*will block; *vainda-ṇandaṇo—*the son of the king of Vraja; *prakāśam—*audibly; *halā—*O; *lalide—*Lalitā; *eṇhiṁ—*now; *pasthāṇa—*of leaving; *osare—*at the time; *īsi—*slightly; *vihasia—*smiling; *kiṁ—*what; *bhaṇidam—*was said; *bhaavadīe—*by the saintly lady.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā to Herself: I hope that the son of the king of Vraja will unexpectedly appear and block our way today as we carry sacrificial ghee.**

**(audibly): Hey Lalitā, when just now we were leaving, what has Bhagavatī said with a slight smile?**

**Комментарий:**

Just as Kṛṣṇa intensely wishes to enjoy the toll pastime, Rādhārāṇī also cherishes the same desire deep in Her heart. She is careful though not to reveal it to others.

Bhagavatī, which in this context could be translated as “the saintly lady,” refers to Paurṇamāsī. Here and further in *Dāna-keli-kaumudī*, the gopīs generally do not mention Her name directly out of utmost respect.

### Verse 45

*(lalitā)*  
*evvaṁ bhaṇidaṁ, ‘ajja tumhāṇaṁ ko bi auruvvā ubasthido dīsai lāho.’*

**Пословный перевод:**

*evvaṁ—*like this; *bhaṇidaṁ—*was said; *ajja—*today; *tumhāṇaṁ—*for us; *ko bi—*some; *auruvvā—*unprecedented; *uvanthido—*available at hand; *dīsai—*is seen; *lāho—*gain.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: She said, ‘I can see that today all of you will obtain something unprecedented.’**

### Verse 46

*(rādhā)*  
*(sa-vismayam) lalide, kadhā-ppasaṅge pucchīadu sā mahā-tābasī savvaṇṇā tantha-hodī poṇṇamāsī.*

**Пословный перевод:**
