*sa-vismayam—*with surprise; *lalide—*o Lalitā; *kadhā—*of the conversation; *ppasaṅge—*at the time; *pucchīadu—*she should be asked; *sā—*that; *mahā-tāvasī—*great ascetic lady; *savvaṇṇā—*omniscient; *tantha-hodī—*the respected one; *poṇṇamāsī—*Paurṇamāsī.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā, with astonishment: Lalitā, let us ask that great ascetic, the omniscient Paurṇamāsī, when we have the chance to speak with her.**

### Verse 47-48

*(lalitā)*  
*kiṁ taṁ pucchidavvaṁ?*  
*(rādhā)*  
*puvva-bhave nandīmuhī-pahudihiṁ kīdisaṁ mahā-vvadaṁ kidaṁ tti.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kiṁ—*what; *taṁ—*that; *pucchidavvaṁ—*should be asked; *rādhā—*Rādhā (speaks); *puvva-bhave—*in their previous birth; *nandīmuhī-pahudihiṁ—*by persons like Nandīmukhī; *kīdisaṁ—*of what kind; *mahā-vvadaṁ—*great vow; *kidaṁ—*performed; *tti—*thus.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: What should we ask her?**

**Rādhā: What kind of severe vow have persons like Nandīmukhī observed in their previous births.**

### Verse 49

*(lalitā)*  
*edāṇaṁ mahā-vvada-kāridā kadhaṁ tue takkidā?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*edāṇaṁ—*their; *mahā-vvada—*of a severe vow; *kāridā—*observance; *kadhaṁ—*how; *tue—*by You; *takkidā—*concluded.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: How have you concluded that they had observed a severe vow?**

### Verse 50

*(rādhā)*  
*haddhī haddhī, ai muddhe! tumaṁ bi evvaṁ pucchasi jaṁ tassa mandandolida-maarakuṇḍala-kiraṇaparāa-kandalī-sundarassa muhāravindassa accariaṁ siviṇe bi sudūrādo tumhādisīṇaṁ dullahagandhalaaṁ mahā-māhurī-maarandaṁ ṇettendindirehiṁ savvadā tāo aṇiāridaṁ pianti! tā bhaṇāmi paramāhiḍṭhāṇubalambha-mummura-jālidāṇaṁ tumhāṇaṁ bi takha mahāvvade dikkhā savvadā ccea juttā jadhā tāṇaṁ ṇandīmuhī-pahudīṇaṁ paavī bhāviṇi bhave bi ṇa dullahā bhave.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*haddhī haddhī—*shame, shame; *ai—*O; *muddhe—*foolish one; *tumaṁ—*you; *vi—*also; *evvaṁ—*like this; *pucchasi—*are asking; *jaṁ—*that; *tassa—*His; *manda-andolida—*slowly swinging; *maara-kuṇḍala—*from the shark-shaped earrings; *kiraṇa—*with the rays; *parāa—*flower pollen; *kandalī—*and *kandalī* flowers; *sundarassa—*beautiful; *muha-aāravindassa—*(His) lotus face; *accariam—*wonderful; *siviṇe—*in dreams; *bi—*even; *sudūrādo—*from very far away; *tumhādisīṇaṁ—*to persons like you; *dullaha—*unobtainable; *gandha—*of the fragrance; *laaṁ—*the whiff; *mahā-māhurī—*of the exquisite sweetness; *maarandaṁ—*the nectar; *ṇetta-indindirehiṁ—*with the bee-like eyes; *savvadā—*always; *tāo—*they (persons like Nāndīmukhī); *aṇiāridaṁ—*without hindrance; *pianti—*are drinking; *tā—*therefore; *bhaṇāmi—*I say; *parama-ahiḍṭha—*the most desired (object); *aṇubalambha—*of not obtaining; *mummura—*in the husk; *jālidāṇaṁ—*who burn; *tumhāṇaṁ—*for you; *vi—*also; *takha—*to that; *mahā-vvade—*severe vow; *dikkhā—*the dedication; *savvadā—*at all times; *ccea—*indeed; *juttā—*is appropriate; *jadhā—*so that; *tāṇaṁ—*of them; *ṇandīmuhī-pahudīṇaṁ—*persons like Nāndīmukhī; *paavī—*the position; *bhāviṇi—*in the next; *bhave—*birth; *vi—*also; *ṇa—*not; *dullahā—*difficult to atttain; *bhave—*will be*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: Shame, shame, O fool! You also asking whether they, without hindrance, constantly drink with the bees of their eyes the exquisitely sweet and extraordinary nectar of His lotus face, beautiful with flower pollen,kandalī flowers, and the rays emanating from His gracefully swinging shark-shaped earrings! The whiff of that nectar girls like you can hardly catch from far away even in your dreams. You girls are smoldering in the chaff of not obtaining your most desired object. Therefore I say that it would be proper for you to give yourselves over to these severe vow at all times, so that the position of the girls like Nāndīmukhī will not be hard for you to achieve in your next birth.**

**Комментарий:**

As Rādhārāṇī says here, the granddaughter of Paurṇamāsī and the sister of Kṛṣṇa’s close friend Madhumaṅgala, Nāndīmukhī, has the privilege to see Kṛṣṇa face to face at any moment. Nāndīmukhī’s relationship with Kṛṣṇa is saturated with the mood of friendly servitude, not conjugal affection. As a messenger in arranging the meetings of Kṛṣṇa and the *gopīs* or settling their quarrels and disputes, she always sides with Kṛṣṇa. All these reasons allow her to be in physical proximity to Kṛṣṇa much more often and longer than the *gopīs*, who are always constricted by social norms pertaining to married ladies. Even though Rādhārāṇī is the dearest of Kṛṣṇa’s beloveds and closer to Him than any other devotee, She still feels incomplete in Her situation and hankers to “attain” Kṛṣṇa.

Viśvanātha Cakravartī says that in order to increase Her own longing She imposes that longing in this speech on Her intimate friends. The same *ācārya* explains elsewhere that *prema* of a devotee increases in proportion to his eagerness to meet the Lord when parted from Him. Therefore, the Lord’s pastime potency (*līlā-śakti*) arranges for separation, which is absolutely indispensable in intensifying love between the Lord and His devotees: “Pure attachment will unite us (Kṛṣṇa and the gopīs) even at the expense of moral and religious duties \[*dharma*\]. Destiny will sometimes bring us together and sometimes separate us.”Expansion of *prema* is an eternally ongoing process, and so, the necessity of separation never expires.

### Verse 51

*(viśākhā)*  
*rāhe, ṇandīmuhī-pahudido goba-kaṇṇā-gaṇādo. kiṁvā aviseseṇa savva-goulavāsi-jaṇādo vi kāvi ekkā mahābhāiṇī kida-mahā-vvada-lakkhā lakkhīadi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*rāhe—*O Rādhā; *ṇandīmuhī-pahudido—*than the persons like Nandimukhī; *gova-kaṇṇā-gaṇādo—*all the cowherd girls; *kiṁvā—*moreover; *aviseseṇa—*without exception; *savva-goulavāsi-jaṇādo—*than all the residents of Gokula; *bi—*even; *kābi—*some; *ekkā—*one; *mahā-bhāiṇī—*greatly fortunate girl; *kida-mahā-vvada-lakkhā—*who performed thousands of severe vows; *lakkhīadi—*is visible.

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā: Rādhā, I see one girl who has observed thousands of severe vows. She is supremely fortunate--more fortunate than all the cowherd girls, persons like Nandīmukhī, and moreover than all the inhabitants of Gokula without exception.**

### Verse 52-55

*(rādhā)*  
*(sotkaṇṭham) visāhe, kā kkhu esā puṇṇavadīṇaṁ sihāmaṇī?*  
*(lalitā)*  
*(sva-gatam) tuatto kā īdisī aṇṇā dudīā?*  
*(rādhā)*  
*sahi, viṇṇādaṁ viṇṇādaṁ! saccaṁ kadhesi.*  
*(iti saṁskṛtena)*  
*ślāghyate kalila-keli-kākalī-*  
*vyākulī-kṛta-samasta-gokulā*  
*śrī-harer adhara-sīdhu-mādhurī-*  
*māditā muralir eva netarā*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-utkaṇṭham—*with eagerness; *visāhe—*O Viśākhā; *kā—*who; *kkhu—*indeed; *esā—*this; *puṇṇavadīṇaṁ—*of pious girls; *sihā-maṇī—*the crest jewel; *sva-gatam—*to herself; *tuatto—*than You; *kā—*who; *īdisī—*such as this; *aṇṇā—*another; *dudīā—*second one; *sahi—*o friend; *viṇṇādaṁ viṇṇādaṁ—*I understand, I understand; *saccaṁ—*the truth; *kadhesi—*you speak; *iti saṁskṛtena—*(speaks) in Sanskrit; *ślāghyate—*is glorified; *kalila—*deep; *keli—*playful; *kākalī—*by the sweet low vibrations; *vyākulī-kṛta—*who agitates; *samasta—*entire; *gokulā—*Gokula; *śrī-hareḥ—*of Lord Hari; *adhara—*of the lips; *sīdhu—*of the honey; *mādhurī—*by the sweetness; *māditā—*intoxicated; *muraliḥ—*the *murali* flute; *eva—*only; *na—*not; *itarā—*another one.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā, with eagerness: O Viśākhā! Who indeed she is, the crest jewel among pious girls?**

**Lalitā, to herself: Is there a second such girl, other than You?**

**Rādhā: O My friend, I understand, I understand! You speak the truth.**

**(says in Sanksrit): The murali flute, intoxicated with the sweetness of the honey from Lord Hari’s lips, agitates the entire Gokula with her sweet and deep playful sound. She only, and not any other girl, is so celebrated.**

**Комментарий:**

Even though Viśākhā so obviously indicated to Rādhā that She is the most fortunate person, Rādhā doesn’t get the point and thinks instead of Kṛṣṇa’s *muralī* flute, a feminine objectwhich is constantly with Kṛṣṇa. The higher is *prema*, the deeper is humility, its inseparable ingredient. Rādhā is not even aware of Her own highest fortune, thinking that it is actually the flute that is so exalted.

On the other hand, the *gopīs* are often jealous of the flute’s fortune. Viśvanātha Cakravartī shows how this verse can be also understood as a covert expression of such transcendental jealousy: “With her sweet and deep playful sound, *muralī* creates only quarrel (*kali-la*)…” Rādhārāṇī suggests that even being a troublemaker, *muralī* has become so close to Kṛṣṇa on the strength of her austerities. Or else, She implies that it is only by the flute’s extreme luck that she can drink the honey of Kṛṣṇa’s lips and become an intoxicant herself. It is definitely not her intrinsic state to be favored so much.

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.21.9 some *gopīs* are in a similar way envious of the fortune of the *veṇu* flute: “My dear *gopīs*, what auspicious activities must the flute have performed to enjoy the nectar of Kṛṣṇa's lips independently and leave only a taste for us *gopīs*, for whom that nectar is actually meant!...”. In the mood of the *gopīs*, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu paraphrased that verse: “The flute is completely unfit for hisposition. We want to know what kind of austerities the flute executed, so that we may also perform the same austerities. Although the flute is unfit, he is drinking the nectar of Kṛṣṇa’s lips. Seeing this, we qualified *gopīs* are dying of unhappiness. Therefore, we must consider the austerities the flute underwent in his past life.”

## Verse 56 (a-b)

*(lalitā)*  
*(sa-smitam) saccaṁ—*  
*muralī kūida-taralī-*  
*kida-dhīra-māṇasā gorī*  
*uttama-vaṁsuppaṇṇā*  
*sārāhīṇā mahā-sarasa-mahurā*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-smitam—*with a smile; *saccaṁ—*true; *muralī—*the *muralī* flute; *kūida—*by its trilling ; *taralī-kida—*which made unsteady; *dhīra—*of the sober persons; *māṇasā—*the minds; *gorī—*tawny; *uttama—*best; *vaṁsa—*from the bamboo; *uppaṇṇā—*produced; *sāra—*of firmness; *ahīṇā—*not deprived; *mahā*—very; *sa-rasa*—full of ecstatic taste; *mahurā—*sweet.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā smiling: That tawny muralī flute has agitated the minds of the sober by her trilling. Carved of the best bamboo, her skin is very firm, and she is very charming, being filled with ecstatic taste.**

**Комментарий:**

On one hand, Lalitā goes along with Rādhā’s ascertainment and praises Kṛṣṇa’s flute. On the other, however, Lalitā is simply reinforcing the opinion of Viśākhā that it is Rādhā who is the most fortunate: “The sober mind (*dhīra-māṇasā*) of that gold-complexioned girl (*gorī*) is agitated by the vibrations of *muralī* flute. That girl’s (*sā*) name (*ṇāma*) is Rādhā (*rāhī*), and She is born (*uppaṇṇā*) in the most exalted (*uttama*) family (*vaṁsa*). She is very charming (*mahurā*), both as a subject and object of joking (*hāsa-rasa*).”

As we see, Rādhārāṇī is now an object of her sakhīs’ friendly ridicule. The *muralī* flute is described as full of sweet, ecstatic taste, because she constantly relishes Kṛṣṇa’s superexcellent musical talent.

### Verse 57

*(rādhā)*  
*halā, kīsa hasasi? manda-bhāiṇā bi imiṇā jaṇeṇa appaṇo paṇa{i}-jaṇāṇaṁ pasādādo taṁ mūha-maṇḍalaṁ do-tiṇṇa-varaṁ bi diṭṭhaṁ akhi. tadhābi paidi-mādaeṇa tiṇā auruvveṇa tassa māhurī-mahuṇā hiaaṁ ummahia tadhā savvaṁ visumārīadi jadhā paṇihāṇeṇa pekkhaṇaṁ bi dullahaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*halā—*O; *kīsa—*why; *hasasi—*you are laughing; *manda-bhāiṇā—*unfortunate; *bi—*even; *imiṇā—*by this; *jaṇeṇa—*person; *appaṇo—*to Myself; *paṇa{i}-jaṇāṇaṁ—*of those who are affectionate; *pasādādo—*by the mercy; *taṁ—*His; *mūha-maṇḍalaṁ—*face; *do—*two; *tiṇṇa-varaṁ—*or three times; *bi—*also; *diṭṭhaṁ akhi—*was seen; *tadhābi—*still; *paidi—*by nature; *mādaeṇa—*intoxicating; *tiṇā—*by that; *auruvveṇa—*unprecedented; *tassa—*His; *māhurī-mahuṇā—*(by) the sweet liquor of sweetness; *hiaaṁ—*My heart; *ummahia—*churning; *tadhā—*in that way; *savvaṁ—*everything; *visumārīadi—*makes Me forget; *jadhā—*as; *paṇihāṇeṇa—*by meditation; *pekkhaṇaṁ—*the sight (of Him); *bi—*even; *dullahaṁ—*hard to achieve*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: Hey, why are you laughing? As the sight of that boy’s face is very hard to obtain even through deep meditation, I have seen it but two or three times, by the mercy of those who are affectionate to such an unfortunate person as Myself. And even then, the naturally intoxicating sweet liquor of its unique charm stirs up My heart and makes Me forget everything else.**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā says “Why are you laughing?” with this in mind: “You are only making fun out of Me by speaking equivocally.” She understood that in the previous verse Lalitā hinted at Her.

### Verse 58

*(ity autsukyam abhinīya saṁskṛtena)*  
*tapasyāmaḥ kṣāmodari varayituṁ veṇuṣu janūr*  
*vareṇyaṁ manyethāḥ sakhi tad akhilānāṁ su-januṣām*  
*tapaḥ-stomenoccair yad iyam urarī-kṛtya muralī*  
*murārāter bimbādhara-madhurimāṇaṁ rasayati*

**Пословный перевод:**

*iti—*thus; *autsukyam—*eagerness; *abhinīya—*expressing; *saṁskṛtena—*(says) in Sanskrit; *tapasyāmaḥ—*we shall perform austerities; *kṣāma-udari—*O slender-waisted one; *varayitum—*in order to ask; *veṇuṣu—*among the bamboo species; *janūḥ—*for birth; *vareṇyam—*the best; *manyethāḥ--*you should consider; *sakhi—*O friend; *tat—*that; *akhilānām—*of all; *su-januṣām—*fortunate births; *tapaḥ-stomena—*by many austerities; *uccaiḥ--*intensely; *yat—*because; *iyam—*this (birth); *urarī-kṛtya—*having accepted; *muralī—*the *muralī* flute; *mura-ārāteḥ--*of the enemy of Mura demon; *bimba—*of a *bimba* fruit color; *adhara—*of the lips; *madhurimāṇam—*the sweet nectar; *rasayati—*relishes.

**Перевод:**

**(Showing eagerness, Rādhā says in Sanskrit): My slender-waisted sakhī, we shall perform austerities to ask for the birth among the bamboo species. You should consider it to be the best of all fortunate births, since the muralī flute, having accepted it as a result of her many austerities, is now relishing the sweet nectar of the red bimba fruit lips of Murāri.**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā directly admits what Lalitā has only hinted at in verse 56—that Rādhā’s heart is made restless by the sound of Kṛṣṇa’s flute.

Rādhā’s *anurāga*, which was glorified in the second opening verse of *Dāna-keli-kaumudī*, manifests in the present verse as *‘aprāṇiny api janma-lālasā-bhara’—*“The excessive longing after taking birth even as an inanimate object.”  *Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi* then gives this verse as an example of such transcendental sentiment. The following is a summary of the *ācāryas’*commentaries on this verse.

Speaking about how *muralī* flute is relishing Kṛṣṇa’s lips, Rādhā shows Her eagerness that She feels due to separation from Kṛṣṇa. In this separation, Rādhārāṇī feels utterly humble and deems Herself unfit for His close association. She expresses Her humility: “My slender-waisted sakhī, what is the use of our human birth with its beauty, if we cannot attain Kṛṣṇa?” Rādhā is not praying for a high birth among the demigods, but for a birth as a bamboo. This is because the *muralī* flute, a bamboo stick, is so close to Kṛṣṇa, and is kissed incessantly by His lips.

Rādhārāṇī is parched with ever increasing thirst for Kṛṣṇa which is never satisfied despite Her repeated association with Him. Her present discontent with Her unfortunate situation of being unable to attain Kṛṣṇa, even though She is in contact with Him, implies that She perceives Him as if for the first time. Such ever-fresh perception is the essence of *anurāga:*

*sadānubhūtam api yaḥ kuryān nava-navaṁ priyam*  
*rāgo bhavan nava-navaḥ so ’nurāga itīryate*

**“*****Anurāga*** **is such love which makes a beloved to appear ever-fresh, even though one perceives a beloved at every moment. In this way,** ***anurāga*** **is love that is experienced as if for the first time.”**

Following Rūpa Gosvāmī’s analysis in the fourteenth chapter of *Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi*, *anurāga* is considered to be one of the fixed moods (*sthāyi-bhāvas*) of conjugal love. One of *anurāga’s* active expressions (*anubhāva*) is *‘aprāṇiny api janma-lālasā-bhara’—*“The excessive longing after taking birth even as an inanimate object.” Eagerness (*autsukya*) and humility (*dainya*), both of which are expressed by Rādhā in the current verse, are *anurāga’s* transitory ecstatic emotions (*vyabhicāri-bhāvas*) that are born of separation from Kṛṣṇa.

### Verse 59

*(vṛndā)*  
*(praviśya) lalite, kathaṁ kathābhiniveśena saṁrabdha-hṛdayāḥ śakra-dhvaja-vedi-padavīm adhirohantam apy ātmānaṁ na jānītha yūyam?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*praviśya—*entering (the stage); *lalite—*O Lalitā; *katham—*how; *kathā-abhiniveśena—*by being absorbed in conversation; *saṁrabdha-hṛdayāḥ—*your minds possessed; *śakra-dhvaja—*of Indra’s flag-pole; *vedi—*of the dais; *padavīm—*the site; *adhirohantam—*climbing; *api—*even; *ātmānam—*of yourself; *na jānītha—*are not aware; *yūyam—*you.

**Перевод:**

**Entering, Vṛndā says: Lalitā, immersed in talking, you girls are overwhelmed. Don’t you notice that you are climbing the platform of Indra’s flagpole \[Śakra-dhvaja\]?**

### Verse 60

*(sarvāḥ)*  
*(parāvṛtya) sahi vṛnde, saccaṁ kadhesi jaṁ goaḍḍhaṇo puṭṭhado saṁvutto. tā ḍāhiṇe goinda-kuṇḍa-vaṭṭaṇīṁ aṇuvaṭṭmha.*  
*(iti tathā kurvanti)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sarvāḥ--*everyone (says); *parāvṛtya—*turning back; *sahi—*O friend; *vṛnde—*Vṛndā; *saccaṁ—*the truth; *kadhesi—*you speak; *jaṁ—*that; *goaḍḍhaṇo—*Govardhana hill; *puṭṭhado—*behind; *saṁvutto—*happens to be; *tā—*so; *ḍāhiṇe—*in the south; *goinda-kuṇḍa—*to Govinda-kuṇḍa; *vaṭṭaṇīṁ—*(the path) leading; *aṇuvaṭṭmha—*we shall follow; *iti—*thus; *tathā—*in this way; *kurvanti—*they act.

**Перевод:**

**Turning back, all the gopīs exclaim: Dear friend Vṛndā, you speak the truth: Govardhana hill is behind our back. So let us turn south and pursue the path to Govinda-kuṇḍa.**

**(They act accordingly.)**

**Комментарий:**

Śakra-dhvaja, or Indra-dhvaja, is a place on the eastern side of Govardhana hill, where Nanda Mahārāja intended to perform a sacrifice to Lord Indra and had raised a flag. The gopīs, are so deeply absorbed in their talking about Kṛṣṇa that they had deviated from the path and instead of walking along Govardhana hill started going eastward away from it in the direction of the elevatedplatform of Śakra-dhvaja. Vṛndā timely reminds them about this. The exact location of Śakra-dhvaja is now lost, but from this description we can understand that it was situated not far from Mānasa-gaṅgā.

## Verse 61(a-b)

*(vṛndā)*  
*(apavārya) campakalate, paśya paśya—*  
*dhruvaṁ nikhila-mādhava-praṇayinī-kadambād alaṁ*  
*vikṛṣya vividhaṁ vidhir madhurimāṇam aty-adbhutam*  
*prabhoḥ parama-tuṣṭaye niramimīta rādhāṁ mudā*  
*yad atra ramate hariḥ parihṛtānya-nārī-spṛhaḥ*

**Пословный перевод:**

*apavārya*—aside; *campakalate—*O Campakalatā; *paśya paśya—*look, look; *dhruvam—*indeed; *nikhila—*of all; *mādhava-praṇayinī—*Lord Mādhava’s (Kṛṣṇa’s) beloveds; *kadambāt—*from the multitude; *alam—*sufficiently; *vikṛṣya—*having extracted; *vividham—*variegated; *vidhiḥ--*Brahmā; *madhurimāṇam—*sweetness; *ati-adbhutam—*extremely wondrous; *prabhoḥ--*of the Lord; *parama-tuṣṭaye—*for the topmost satisfaction; *niramimīta—*has created; *rādhām—*Rādhā; *mudā—*gladly; *yad—*because; *atra—*in Her; *ramate—*delights; *hariḥ--*Lord Hari (Kṛṣṇa); *parihṛta—*giving up; *anya-nārī—*for other women; *spṛhaḥ--*desire.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā (speaking aside): Campakalatā, look, look! Since Hari has abandoned the desire for other women and rejoices in Rādhā alone, Lord Brahmā, to gladly create Her for the highest pleasure of the Lord, must have sufficiently extracted the supremely wondrous sweetness of different kinds from all Mādhava’s beloveds.**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā steals the mind of the Lord by reposing in Herself alone every possible kind of feminine sweetness, charm, and attractiveness. If all kinds of flowers grow in one single garden, is there a need to go various other places for collecting a beautiful bouquet?

In the course of conversation with Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Rāmānanda Rāya delineated the superexcellent position of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī by quoting two verses from *Gītā-govinda* 3.1-2 that describe Kṛṣṇa’s condition after Rādhā left the *rāsa* dance: “Lord Kṛṣṇa, the enemy of Kaṁsa, took Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī within His heart, for He desired to dance with Her. Thus He left the arena of the *rāsa* dance and the company of all the other beautiful damsels of Vraja. Being afflicted by the arrow of Cupid and unhappily regretting His mistreating Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, Mādhava, Lord Kṛṣṇa, began to search for Her along the banks of the Yamunā River. When He failed to find Her, He entered the bushes of Vṛndāvana and began to lament.”

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu elaborates on the above quoted passage: “Simply by considering these two verses one can understand what nectar there is in such dealings. It is exactly like freeing a mine of nectar. Although Kṛṣṇa was in the midst of hundreds of thousands of gopīs during the *rāsa* dance, He still kept Himself in one of His transcendental forms by the side of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. Lord Kṛṣṇa is equal to everyone in His general dealings, but due to the conflicting ecstatic love of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, there were opposing elements. The progress of loving affairs between a young boy and a young girl is like the movement of a snake. On account of this, two types of anger arise between a young boy and girl—anger with cause and anger without cause. When Rādhārāṇī left the *rāsa* dance out of anger and resentment, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa became very anxious because He could not see Her. Lord Kṛṣṇa’s desire in the *rāsa-līlā* circle is perfectly complete, but Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī is the binding link in that desire. The *rāsa* dance does not shine in the heart of Kṛṣṇa without Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. Therefore, He also gave up the circle of the *rāsa* dance and went out to search for Her. When Kṛṣṇa went out to search for Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, He wandered here and there. Not finding Her, He became afflicted by the arrow of Cupid and began to lament. Since Kṛṣṇa’s lusty desires were not satisfied even in the midst of hundreds of thousands of gopīs and He was thus searching after Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, we can easily imagine how transcendentally qualified She is.”

Vṛndā does not define Rādhā’s ontological position by describing Her as Brahmā’s best devotional offering to the Lord. Brahmā does not create Her literally, for Rādhā is not a part of this material creation. Vṛndā speaks in this way simply to express Her astonishment.

### Verse 62

*(rādhā)*  
*(dakṣiṇataḥ prekṣya) ammahe, māṇasa-gaṅgāe upphulla kamala-kalāve rolambāṇāṁ kāalī-kala-alassa komaladā!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dakṣiṇataḥ--*to the right; *prekṣya—*looking; *ammahe—*ah; *māṇasa-gaṅgāe—*of Mānasa-gaṅgā; *upphulla—*of blooming; *kamala—*lotuses; *kalāve—*in the cluster; *rolambāṇāṁ—*of the bees; *kāalī—*of low; *kala-alassa—*humming; *komaladā—*softness.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (looking to the right): Ah, how sweetly the bees in the cluster of lotuses on Mānasa-gaṅgā are humming!**

### Verse 63

*(vṛndā)*  
*(sākūtam)*  
*sarojānāṁ puñje mada-kalam amuṁ paśyata puraḥ*  
*parāgair āpiṅgaiḥ sphurad-adhara-kāyaṁ madhukaram*  
*muhur bhrāmaṁ bhrāmaṁ bhramara-ramaṇīr yaḥ sa-rabhasaṁ*  
*niruddhāno dhvānoddhati-vidhuta-mūrdhā viharati*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-ākūtam—*suggestively; *sarojānām—*of the lotuses; *puñje—*in the bundle; *mada-kalam—*intoxicated; *amum—*that; *paśyata—*look at; *puraḥ--*in front; *parāgaiḥ--*with the pollen; *āpiṅgaiḥ--*yellow; *sphurat—*shining; *adhara-kāyam—*the lower part of whose body; *madhukaram—*the bumblebee; *muhuḥ--*repeatedly; *bhrāmam bhrāmam—*rambling around; *bhramara-ramaṇīḥ--*the beautiful she-bees; *yaḥ--*who; *sa-rabhasam—*brazenly; *niruddhānaḥ--*obstructing; *dhvāna—*of humming; *uddhati—*by the increase; *vidhuta—*shaking; *mūrdhā—*his head; *viharati—*sports.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā (suggestively): Girls, look at that intoxicated bumblebee among the lotuses in front! The lower part of His body glitters with yellow pollen. Hovering around again and again He brazenly obstructs the beautiful she-bees. Thus He sports, buzzing louder and louder so that His head is shaking.**

**Комментарий:**

Vṛndā Devī hints to the gopīs that Kṛṣṇa-bumblebee, dressed in yellow dhoti, is nearby. Soon He will block the gopīs’ path and “buzz” vigorously in the tax quarrel.

This speech of Vṛndā is *upanyāsa*, the first out of seven *sandhy*-*aṅgas* (sub-elements) of *bhāṇikā,* described in the commentary to text 10. *Upanyāsa* is an incidental proclamation of the plot’s final outcome (*kārya*). In the *Dāna-keli-kaumudī* the final outcome is the toll pastime itself, and Vṛndā casually alludes to it by describing how the Kṛṣṇa-bumblebee increases His humming.

### Verse 64-65

*(rādhā)*  
*(sva-gatam) ṇūṇaṁ vundāe kimpi hiae kadua edaṁ vāharīadi.*  
*(prakāśam) halā vunde, dhaṇṇao kusuma-kīḍio jāo kanteṇa samaṁ kīlanti. manda-bhāiṇīṇaṁ uṇa imāṇaṁ sūrobāsiṇīṇāṁ dūrādo kkhaṇaṁ bi tassa pekkhaṇaṁ sudullahaṁ tti.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sva-gatam*—to herself; *ṇūṇaṁ—*indeed; *vundāe—*Vṛndā; *kimpi—*something; *hiae—*in her mind; *kadua—*bearing; *edaṁ—*this; *vāharīadi—*this is uttered; *prakāśam*—audibly; *halā—*O; *vunde—*Vṛndā; *dhaṇṇao—*fortunate; *kusuma—*(among) the flowers; *kīḍio—*the insects; *jāo—*who; *kanteṇa—*with their beloved; *samaṁ—*together; *kīlanti—*are playing; *manda-bhāiṇīṇaṁ—*unfortunate; *uṇa—*again; *imāṇaṁ—*for these; *sūra-ubāsiṇīṇāṁ—*worshipers of the Sun; *dūrādo—*from a distance; *kkhaṇaṁ—*for a moment; *bi—*even; *tassa—*at Him; *pekkhaṇaṁ—*glance; *sudullahaṁ—*extremely rare; *tti—*thus.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (to Herself): Definitely Vṛndā had something in her mind saying this.**

**(audibly): O Vṛndā, the bees that sport with their beloved on those flowers are so fortunate! And we, ill-fated sun god worshipers, find it so very difficult to see our beloved, even from far away and for a moment only!**

**Комментарий:**

Viśvanātha Cakravartī paraphrases Rādhā’s speech: “Despite the fact that in this birth we are dedicated to worshipping god, it is our bad luck that we are born as humans. Look at those insects: they are totally ignorant of any kind of religious duties, but unlike us, they sport together with their beloveds.”

## Verse 66(a-b)

*(iti saṁskṛtena)*  
*lalite, samākarṇaya—*  
*bhavatu mādhava-jalpam aśṛṇvatoḥ*  
*śravaṇayor alam aśravaṇir mama*  
*tam avilokayator avilokaniḥ*  
*sakhi vilocanayoś ca kilānayoḥ*

**Пословный перевод:**

*saṁskṛtena—*(speaking) in Sanskrit; lalite—O Lalitā; *samākarṇya—*listen carefully; *bhavatu—*may there be; *mādhava—*of Mādhava; *jalpam—*to the talking; *aśṛṇvatoḥ--*which do not listen; *śravaṇayoḥ--*(for My) ears; *alam—*appropriately; *aśravaṇiḥ--*deafness; *mama—*for My; *tam—*Him; *avilokayatoḥ--*not beholding; *avilokaniḥ--*blindness; *sakhi—*O friend; *vilocanayoḥ--*for the eyes; *ca—*and; *kila—*indeed; *anayoḥ--*these.

**Перевод:**

**(speaking in Sanskrit): Please listen, O Lalitā! May My ears, which do not listen to Mādhava’s sweet talk, become deaf, and may these eyes of Mine, which do not behold Him, become blind!**

**Комментарий:**

This verse is quoted in *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* 2.4.10 as an example of self-disgust (*nirveda*) born of separation. Such words of despair, called *vinyāsa*, is the second out of the seven elements of *bhāṇikā* (see Appendix…).

### Verse 67

*(vṛndā:)*  
*sakhi rādhe, rātrindivaṁ divya-līlayā dīvyasi, tathāpi kathaṁ nirvidya khidyase?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakhi—*O friend; *rādhe—*Rādhā; *rātrim-divam—*day and night; *divya—*divine; *līlayā—*with Your pastimes; *dīvyasi—*You sport; *tathā api—*still; *katham—*why; *nirvidya—*being despondent; *khidyase—*do You suffer.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: My dear friend Rādhā, though day and night You bask in Your divine pastimes, why now You are in pain, feeling so despondent?**

**Комментарий:**

In the opinion of Viśvanātha Cakravartī, Rādhā won’t answer anything, for She considers Vṛndā’s statement about Her constant sporting in divine pastimes wrong.

### Verse 68-69

*(lalitā:)*  
*halā rāhe, kadhaṁ mantharā via lakkhīasi?*  
*(vṛndā:)*  
*haiyaṅgavīna-mṛdulā tvam ataḥ kathaṁ vā*  
*haiyaṅgavīna-kalasīṁ calitā vahantī*  
*hā mallikārpaṇa-padaṁ vyathate śiras te*  
*mūrdhany amuṁ mama nidhehi kṛpāṁ vidhehi*

**Пословный перевод:**

*halā—*O; *rāhe—*Rādhā; *kadhaṁ—*why; *mantharā—*bent; *via—*as if; *lakkhīasi—*You appear; *haiyaṅgavīna—*like fresh ghee; *mṛdulā—*soft; *tvam—*You; *ataḥ--*therefore; *katham vā—*whether; *haiyaṅgavīna-kalasīm—*a pot of fresh ghee; *calitā—*trembling; *vahantī—*while carrying; *hā—*alas; *mallikā—*jasmine flowers; *arpaṇa—*for putting; *padam—*a place; *vyathate—*aches; *śiraḥ--*the head; *te—*Your; *mūrdhani—*on the head; *amum—*that (pot); *mama—*my; *nidhehi—*please place; *kṛpām—*mercy; *vidhehi—*please show.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: O Rādhā, why do You appear somewhat bent over?**

**Vṛndā: Your body is as soft as fresh ghee, the pot of which You, trembling, carry on Your head—a site for offering jasmine flowers. Oh God, does Your head ache? Please do me a favor: kindly place that pot on my head.**

**Комментарий:**

Ladies in India often decorate their hair with strands of jasmine flowers. Rādhārāṇī’s head is suitable just for the load of tender jasmine flowers, and not of a heavy pot filled with ghee. Vṛndā can’t stand seeing Rādhā suffering from carrying heavy weight: “By seeing You suffering, I suffer even more. Relieve me of that great suffering by giving me Your pot.”

### Verse 70

*(rādhā:)*  
*sahi kalasīe bhāro ṇa maṁ mantharāvedi. pekkha, bhūri-bhūsaṇāṇaṁ ccea jāiṁ ṇiāridāe bi lalidāe pasahaṁ appiddāiṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sahi—*O friend; *kalasīe—*of the pot; *bhāro—*the burden; *ṇa—*does not; *maṁ—*Me; *mantharāvedi—*bend; *pekkha—*look; *bhūri-bhūsaṇāṇaṁ—*(the burden) of heavy ornaments; *ccea—*only; *jāiṁ—*which; *ṇiāridāe—*who were kept back; *bi—*even; *lalidāe—*by Lalitā; *pasahaṁ—*by force; *appiddāiṁ—*were placed*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: Dear friend, it is not the weight of the pot that bends me down, but the burden of these heavy ornaments which Lalitā put on me forcibly, even though I prohibited her.**

**Комментарий:**

Viśvanātha Cakravartī reveals the significance of these words. Rādhā dismisses Vṛndā’s assertion that it is the heavy pot that caused Her to bend. It can’t possibly be so, because Rādhā knows that the pot of ghee will be used by *brāhmaṇas* in the sacrifice. She believes in the sacrifice and so is very eager to carry the pot, not feeling its heavy weight. The confidential reason to Rādhā’s dismissal is that She is burning with the desire that Kṛṣṇa halt Her on the way on the pretext of a tax. That is why She carries Her pot happily, knowing that Kṛṣṇa will see it as an opportunity for enacting the pastime that both of them long for. From Rādhā’s words one can also understand that She doesn’t like abundant jewelry. This is because She is naturally endowed with youthful beauty.

### Verse 71

*(viśākhā)*  
*halā rāhi, kkhaṇaṁ ciṭṭha. suṭṭhu ubbhāremi maṇḍaṇa-bhāraṁ.*  
*(iti yathārham uttārayati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*halā—*O; *rāhi—*Rādhā; *kkhaṇaṁ—*for a moment; *ciṭṭha—*stand; *suṭṭhu—*properly; *ubbhāremi—*I will remove; *maṇḍaṇa—*of the jewelry; *bhāraṁ—*the burden; *iti—*thus; *yathā-arham—*as appropriate; *uttārayati—*takes off.

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā: Hey Rādhā, please stand still for a moment so I can manage to remove the burden of the jewelry.**

**(carefully takes off the ornaments)**

### Verse 72

*(vṛndā)*  
*trapate vilokya padmā*  
*lalite rādhāṁ vināpy alaṅkāram*  
*tad alaṁ maṇimaya-maṇḍana-*  
*maṇḍala-racanā-prayāsena*

**Пословный перевод:**

*trapate—*feels shame; *vilokya—*beholding; *padmā—*Padmā; *lalite—*O Lalitā; *rādhām—*Rādhā; *vinā—*without; *api—*even; *alaṅkāram—*decoration; *tat—*thus; *alam—*enough; *maṇi-maya—*made of gems; *maṇḍana—*of ornaments; *maṇḍala—*the multitude; *racanā—*for arranging; *prayāsena—*of the endeavor.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: O Lalitā, since Padmā is put to shame by beholding Rādhā even without Her decorations, there is no need to take the trouble of arranging for heaps of stone jewelry.**

**Комментарий:**

Why did Lalitā forcibly put jewelry on Rādhā? *Mahaṭī-ṭīkā* explains that with her present statement Vṛndā unveils the reason: it is Lalitā’s strong desire to prove the superexcellence of Rādhārāṇī. Even though it is a self-evident fact that doesn’t need to be supported by extra things like jewelry, still, Lalitā wants to seal an airtight case, leaving no chance for any doubt: “My sakhī is the best.” The best in this case would mean better than the other one—Candrāvalī—Rādhārāṇī’s transcendental rival. “Padmā, a friend of Candrāvalī, will certainly wilt her face in shame, for her sakhī’s beauty doesn’t stand even near to Rādhā’s.”

Lalitā, also known as Anurādhā (“one who follows Rādhā”), and Padmā share a similar status—each of them is the chief assistant of their respective leaders (*yūtheśvarīs*)—Rādhā and Candrāvalī. It is quite clear, therefore, why Lalitā makes it a point to separately mention Padmā and her expected embarrasment.

Surendranath Shastri suggests that this incident with removing Rādhā’s ornaments is an intentional arrangement so that Kṛṣṇa would better appreciate the natural beauty of Her body.

*Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi* 10.25 defines *rūpa*, or beauty:

*aṅgāny abhūṣitāny eva*  
*kenacid bhūṣaṇādinā*  
*yena bhūṣitavad bhāti*  
*tad rūpam iti kathyate*

**“One is said to possess beauty, when one’s body appears as if decorated even without any ornaments.”**

### Verse 73

*(rādhā)*  
*(vihasya)halā vunde, ekha jaṇṇe heaṅgavīṇo-bahārīṇīṇaṁ hariṇī-ṇettāṇaṁ savvaṅgārihassa maṇḍaṇa-ulassa muṇi-jaṇādo ubaladdhī suṇīadi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vihasya—*laughing; *halā—*O; *vunde—*Vṛndā; *ekha—*in this; *jaṇṇe—*sacrifice; *heaṅgavīṇa—*fresh ghee; *ubahārīṇīṇaṁ—*who bring; *hariṇī-ṇettāṇaṁ—*by the doe-eyed (girls); *savva—*for all; *aṅga—*bodily parts; *arihassa—*suitable; *maṇḍaṇa—*of ornaments; *ulassa—*of a multitude; *muṇi-jaṇādo—*from the sages; *ubaladdhī—*the acquirement; *suṇīadi—*I have heard*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (laughing): O Vṛndā, I have heard that in the sacrifice we are going to, the young doe-eyed girls who bring fresh ghee will obtain from the sages all sorts of ornaments for their whole bodies!**

**Комментарий:**

As Rādhārāṇī is being relieved of the heavy weight of the ornaments, why is She so excited about getting heaps of those from the sages? The answer is that Rādhārāṇī believes that the ornaments that will be given by the merciful sages will make Her extremely fortunate. At that time She won’t mind faithfully carrying their extra weight. This is the explanation of Viśvanātha Cakravartī.

### Verse 74

*(vṛndā)*  
*na kevalaṁ maṇḍana-kulasyaivopalabdhiḥ, kintu kriyārambha eva nijābhiṣṭānām api. tad ebhyaḥ prāñjalam añjaliḥ kriyatāṁ kāma-debhyaḥ śailendra-tīrthebhyaḥ.*  
*(iti sarvās tathā kurvanti)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*na—*not; *kevalam—*only; *maṇḍana—*of ornaments; *kulasya—*of a multitude; *eva—*just; *upalabdhiḥ--*the obtainment; *kintu—*but; *kriyā—*of the sacrifice; *ārambhe—*in the beginning; *eva—*very; *nija-abhiṣṭānām—*of their desires; *api—*also; *tat—*therefore; *ebhyaḥ--*to these (holy spots); *prāñjalam—*in an upright position; *añjaliḥ--*the gesture of folding both hands; *kriyatām—*should be made; *kāma-debhyaḥ--*which bestow the desirable; *śaila-indra—*on the king of mountains; *tīrthebhyaḥ--*to the holyplaces; *iti—*thus; *sarvāḥ--*all of them; *tathā—*so; *kurvanti—*do.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: …And not just all sorts of ornaments, but also the fulfillment of their desires at the very commencement of the ritual. Therefore, stand upright and salute with folded hands these wish-fulfilling holy places of Girirāja.**

**(All of them do so.)**

**Комментарий:**

According to Viśvanātha Cakravartī’s commentary, the ritual Vṛndā talks about is the gopīs’ bringing ghee at the sacrifice, and ‘at the very commencement of the ritual’ means ‘during the gopīs’ journey,’ since the offering implies that ghee should be brought by gopīs personally. In this way Vṛndā hints that something very special, which the gopīs long for, will occur on their way. “In order to dispel the hindrances to the obtainment of your desired goal, salute the holy places such as Brahma-kuṇḍa, for the amorous pastimes you covet will happen not far away from here.” Since the gopīs are carrying pots on their heads, Vṛndā instructs them to offer their homage without bowing down.

Brahma-kuṇḍa is a pond situated next to Mānasī Gaṅgā on its western side. After the pastime of holding Govardhana hill, Indra performed the *abhiṣeka* of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Following Indra, other demigods headed by Brahmā also performed Kṛṣṇa’s *abhiṣeka* one by one. Brahma-kuṇḍa is the lake that formed after Brahmā bathed the Lord and it is glorified in *Ādi-varāha Purāṇa* and the *Mathurā-khaṇḍa* section of *Skanda Purāṇa*. Unfortunately, at present Brahma-kuṇḍa is in a neglected state, and the other kuṇḍas, namely Yama, Varuṇa, Kuvera, and Indra, have completely disappeared. Both Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda visited Brahma-kuṇḍa during Their pilgrimages to Vraja.

Following the advice of Vṛndā Devī we can also at this point offer our obeisances to Girirāja Govardhana, who has hosted this unique and hilarious pastime of the toll, with the verses composed by the previous *ācāryas*:

*ghaṭṭa-krīḍā-kutukita-manā nāgarendro navīno*  
*dānī bhūtvā madana-nṛpater gavya-dāna-cchalena*  
*yatra prātaḥ sakhibhir abhito veṣṭitaḥ saṁrurodha*  
*śrī-gāndharvāṁ nija-gaṇa-vṛtāṁ naumi tāṁ kṛṣṇa-vedīm*

**“One morning, Kṛṣṇa, the youthful king of amorous heroes, became eager at heart to enjoy the tax pastime. Proclaiming Himself a toll collector appointed by the king of love and demanding a toll of milk products, He blocked the path of Śrī Gāndharvā (Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī), who was surrounded with Her close friends. I praise the glories of Kṛṣṇa-vedī, the place where the Lord enjoyed this pastime.”**

*rasa-nidhi-nava-yūnoḥ sākṣiṇīṁ dāna-keler*  
*dyuti-parimala-viddhāṁ śyāma-vedīṁ prakāśya*  
*rasika-vara-kulānāṁ modam āsphālayan me*  
*nija-nikaṭa-nivāsaṁ dehi govardhana tvam*

**“O Govardhana, please let me stay by your side. You have manifested Śyāma-vedī that witnesses the tax pastime of the youthful couple, the treasure mine of** ***rasa*****. In this way you are increasing the happiness of the best of devotees who are adept in relishing the moods of devotional service.”**

*yatra svīya-gaṇasya vikrama-bhṛtā vācā muhuḥ phullatoḥ*  
*smera-krūra-dṛg-anta-vibhrama-śaraiḥ śaśvan mitho viddhayoḥ*  
*tad-yūnor nava-dāna-sṛṣṭija-kalir bhaṅgyā hasañ jṛmbhate*  
*kas taṁ tat-pṛthu-keli-sūcana-śilaṁ govardhanaṁ nāśrayet*

**“Govardhana hill is where the ever-fresh quarrel between the two youths over a tax expands by their laughter at each other’s crooked words. They both repeatedly blossom with happiness at the bold statements of their friends and constantly pierce each other with the arrows of playful and stern sidelong glances. Who will not take shelter of Govardhana, whose rocks hint at that great pastime?”**

*yasyādhyakṣaḥ sakala-haṭhinām ādade cakravartī*  
*śuklaṁ nānyad vraja-mṛga-dṛśām arpaṇād vigrahasya*  
*ghaṭṭasyoccair marakata-rucas tasya dhāma-prapañcaiḥ*  
*śyāma-prasthaḥ prathayatu sadā śarma govardhano naḥ*

**“The slopes of Govardhana hill are dark green due to the abundant emerald-color effulgence of his Lord’s toll station. That Lord, the emperor of all oppressors, didn’t accept from the cowherd maidens of Vraja any tax other than their bodies. May Govardhana always increase our joy.”**

*yatraiva kṛṣṇo vṛṣabhānu-putryā*  
*dānaṁ gṛhītuṁ kalahaṁ vitene*  
*śruteḥ spṛhā yatra mahaty ataḥ śrī-*  
*govardhano me diśatām abhīṣṭam*

**“Trying to collect a toll near Govardhana hill, Kṛṣṇa picked up a quarrel with King Vṛṣabhānu’s daughter. The personified Vedas yearn to listen to that quarrel. May Govardhana fulfil my desire.”**

### Verse 75

*(campakalatā)*  
*sahi citte, adi-cittā esā ṇihila-jīa-maṇḍalī-kuṇḍiā-kulālassa puṇḍarīa-joṇiṇo kuṇḍena maṇḍidā girinda-sihara-sthalī ḍāhiṇe rehadi!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sahi—*O friend; *citte—*Citrā; *adi-cittā—*extremely wonderful; *esā—*this; *ṇihila—*of all; *jīa—*living entities; *maṇḍalī-kuṇḍiā—*of the pot-like aggregate; *kulālassa—*of the potter; *puṇḍarīa-joṇiṇo—*Lord Brahmā (who was born from a lotus); *kuṇḍena—*by the pond; *maṇḍidā—*adorned; *giri-inda—*of Govardhan, the king of mountains; *sihara-sthalī—*the peak; *ḍāhiṇe—*in the south; *rehadi—*appears splendid*.*

**Перевод:**

**Campakalatā: O Citrā sakhī, the southern peak of Girirāja appears most wonderfully splendid, adorned by the pond of lotus-born Brahmā, an artisan who fashions all living entities as if they were clay pots!**

**Комментарий:**

Govardhana hill is sometimes called Girīndra, or Girirāja, which means “the king of mountains.” Even though other mountains like the Himālaya may be bigger or more important from geographical point of view, Govardhana earns the position of being the best of all mountains due to His special connection with the best of all persons Kṛṣṇa and His pastimes.

### Verse 76

*(citrā)*  
*sahi, īdha jevva bhaṭṭāṇaṁ vacchalo haridea ṇāmā ṇārāaṇo vasedi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sahi—*O friend; *īdha—*here; *jevva—*indeed; *bhaṭṭāṇaṁ—*of His devotees; *vacchalo—*the affectionate caretaker; *haridea—*Harideva; *ṇāmā—*by the name; *ṇārāaṇo—*Lord Nārāyaṇa; *vasedi—*dwells.

**Перевод:**

**O sakhī, in this very place dwells Lord Nārāyaṇa, known as Harideva. He is the affectionate caretaker of His devotees.**

**Комментарий:**

Harideva is the name of the Deity, the incarnation of Lord Nārāyaṇa, who is situated on Govardhana hill near Mānasī Gaṅgā. This ancient Deity is mentioned in *Ādi-varāha Purāṇa*. While visiting Govardhana hill, Lord Caitanya danced in love of God in front of Harideva. He spent a night at the temple of Harideva, well received by the priests.

## Verse 77(a-b)

*(vṛndā)*  
*paśya paśya—*  
*sakhi bahula-śirastve bhū-bhṛtau ceha sāmyaṁ*  
*dadhad api girir añcaty eṣa śeṣād viśeṣam*  
*agha-ripur ayam aṅke mūrdhni yasyodare ca*  
*praṇayati rati-līlām adbhutāṁ preyasībhiḥ*

**Пословный перевод:**

*paśya paśya—*look, look; *sakhi—*O friend; *bahula-śirastve—*in having many hoods; *bhū-bhṛtau—*in supporting the earth; *ca—*and; *iha—*here; *sāmyam—*the sameness; *dadhat—*maintaining; *api—*though; *giriḥ--*the hill; *añcati—*attains; *eṣaḥ—*this; *śeṣāt—*over Ananta-śeṣa; *viśeṣam—*an excellence; *agha-ripuḥ—*Lord Kṛṣṇa (the enemy of sins); *ayam—*this; *aṅke—*on the lap; *mūrdhni—*on the head; *yasya—*whose; *udare—*on the belly; *ca—*and; *praṇayati—*conducts; *rati—*of conjugal love; *līlām—*the pastimes; *adbhutām—*wondrous; *preyasībhiḥ--*with His dear ones.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: Just see, my friend Rādhā, although Girirāja is equal to Ananta Śeṣa in holding the earth and having many heads, he outstands Him in a particular way: on Girirāja’s lap, head, and in His belly, the enemy of sin Śrī Kṛṣṇa conducts His astonishing amorous pastimes with His beloveds.**

**Комментарий:**

Vṛndā Devī observes two similarities between Ananta-śeṣa and Govardhana hill. The first is that Ananta Śeṣa is a many-headed serpent, and Govardhana hill is a mountain with many peaks, which are like his heads. At present we don’t see the peaks of Govardhana, for since Kṛṣṇa’s leaving Vraja he is constantly shrinking down in separation from the Lord. At the time of Kṛṣṇa’s manifest pastimes in Vraja, however, Govardhan was a formidable mountain, “licking” the sky and touching the clouds with his peaks.

The second similarity is that both Ananta Śeṣa and Govardhana hill hold the earth. Ananta holds the earth, in this context—the whole of *bhū-maṇḍala*, on His head. Govardhana, on the other hand, like any mountain, holds the earth by elevating some part of land above the plain. These two qualities—having many heads and holding the earth—make Ananta Śeṣa and Govardhana equal.

Vṛndā, however, goes on to declare that ultimately Govardhana hill surpasses Ananta Śeṣa. The last serves Lord Nārāyaṇa, who is just a plenary portion of the Nārāyaṇa’s source, Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is served by Govardhana. Lord Kṛṣṇa sports all over the enormous body of Girirāja—on his valleys, peaks, and caves, which are considered to be his lap, heads, and belly respectively. Nārāyaṇa, however, is situated only on Śeṣa’s coils, and is never seen on Śeṣa’s heads or inside his belly. Moreover, Nārāyaṇa is simply lying down on Śeṣa, His feet massaged by single Lakṣmī Devī. On Girirāja, however, Kṛṣṇa is very active in His amorous pastimes, accompanied by not just one, but a multitude of His gopī consorts.

With this verse Vṛndā once againtries to intimate Rādhā that today too such amorous plays will take place on Girirāja.

### Verse 78

*(lalitā)*  
*(rādhām āvekṣya saṁskṛtena)*  
*niviḍa-rucini gaṇḍa-grāva-khaṇḍe gariṣṭhe*  
*surabhiṇi kira dṛṣṭiṁ gauri govardhanasya*  
*sakhi mṛga-mada-paṅkaiḥ suṣṭhu yatropaviṣṭas*  
*tvad-urasi rasikendraḥ patra-vallīm alekhīt*

**Пословный перевод:**

*rādhām—*at Rādhā; *āvekṣya—*looking; *saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *niviḍa—*dense; *rucini—*(at the slab) emitting effulgence; *gaṇḍa—*large; *grāva—*of the rock; *khaṇḍe—*at the slab; *gariṣṭhe—*most heavy; *surabhiṇi—*and fragrant; *kira—*just cast; *dṛṣṭim—*a glance; *gauri—*O golden complexioned one; *govardhanasya—*of Govardhana Hill; *sakhi—*O friend; *mṛga-mada—*with musk; *paṅkaiḥ--*and ointments; *suṣṭhu—*excellently; *yatra—*on which; *upaviṣṭaḥ--*seated; *tvat—*on Your; *urasi—*breast; *rasika-indraḥ--*Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the chief among the enjoyers of *rasa*; *patra-vallīm—*a vine; *alekhīt—*drew.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (Looking at Rādhā, speaks in Sanskrit): My dear friend of golden hue, kindly look at that huge rocky slab on Govardhana Hill. Once, seated on its fragrant and glowing surface, the best of aesthetes skillfully drew with various ointments and musk a vine on Your breasts.**

**Комментарий:**

According to *Mahaṭī-ṭīkā*, Rādhā may have grown desperate at this point, thinking that a meeting with Her beloved will not happen on this day. Lalitā encourages Her by recalling Her amorous meeting with Kṛṣṇa that occurred on Govardhana hill: “Just as Kṛṣṇa drew on Your breasts that day, He will draw today as well. Don’t give up the hope!”

## Verse 79(a-b)

*(campakalatā)*  
*(janāntikaṁ saṁskṛtena:) sakhi, samākarṇyatām—*  
*ayam upari-parisphurad-valākā-*  
*tatir ati-sañcalac-cañcalā-vilāsaḥ*  
*acala-śirasi nīla-maṇḍapasya*  
*dvi-guṇayati dyutim ambudaḥ sva-dhāmnā*

**Пословный перевод:**

*jana-antikam—*aside; *saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *sakhi—*O friend; *samākarṇyatām—*please listen; *ayam—*this; *upari—*on its top; *parisphurat—*quivering; *valākā—*of egrets; *tatiḥ--*with the rows; *ati-sañcalat—*intensely shaking; *cañcalā—*of lightning; *vilāsaḥ--*with the playful flash; *acala—*of the mountain; *śirasi—*on the peak; *nīla—*blue; *maṇḍapasya—*of the pavilion; *dvi-guṇayati—*doubles; *dyutim—*the magnificence; *ambu-daḥ--*the cloud; *sva-dhāmnā—*with its splendor.

**Перевод:**

**Campakalatā (speaks aside in Sanskrit): Listen attentively, my friend: This cloud with its splendor—quivering rows of egrets on the top and wildly dancing lightning flashing forth—makes the blue pavilion on the mountain peak appear twice as magnificent.**

**Комментарий:**

Campakalatā speaks to Lalitā in a way that Rādhā would not hear her words. Yes, Campakalatā sees Kṛṣṇa wearing a swinging necklace and a yellow *dhoti*, but mistakes Him for a cloud with a row of birds flying past and a lightning. Kṛṣṇa is standing near the dark blue pavilion on the top of the hill—the place he was hiding in until now.

### Verse 80

*(lalitā)*  
*(sānandam) campaalade, ṇa kkhu ambudo! pekkha, eso kaṇṭha-vilambi-vipphāra-hāro pīdambaro giri-ṇidambaṁ ālambadi. tā pupphido amhāṇaṁ maṇo-rahasāhi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-ānandam—*happily; *campaalade—*O Campakalatā; *ṇa—*is not; *kkhu—*indeed; *ambudo—*a cloud; *pekkha—*look; *eso—*this; *kaṇṭha—*on His neck; *vilambi—*hanging; *vipphāra—*swinging; *hāro—*the necklace; *pīda-ambaro—*Kṛṣṇa, who wears a yellow *dhotī; giri—*of the hill; *ṇidambaṁ—*on the slope; *ālambadi—*rests; *tā—*therefore; *pupphido—*has blossomed; *amhāṇaṁ—*of our; *maṇo-raha—*desire; *sāhi—*the tree*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (happily): Campakalatā, this isn’t a cloud! Look carefully: this is Kṛṣṇa in yellow dhotī and with a swinging necklace. This means that the tree of our heart’s desire has blossomed!**

**Комментарий:**

Lalitā dispels Campakalatā’s illusion—it is Kṛṣṇa, whom she mistook as a cloud.

This incident constitutes the third element of *bhāṇikā*, called *vibodha*—“dispelling of illusion” (see Appendix…).

### Verse 81

*(rādhā)*  
*(vṛndām avekṣya sa-kampaṁ saṁskṛtena)*  
*urī-kurvan gaurīṁ giri-śikhara-bhāg ambara-ruciṁ*  
*jagad-vaṁśe yuñjan madana-ghana-ghūrṇā-ghuṇa-ghaṭam*  
*dhṛti-dhvāntaṁ bhindann amṛta-nidhir indīvara-dṛśāṁ*  
*dṛśāṁ bandhuḥ ko ’yaṁ vidhur iva purastād udayate*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vṛndām—*at Vṛndā; *avekṣya—*looking; *sa-kampam—*trembling; *saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *urī-kurvan—*diffusing; *gaurīm—*golden; *giri-śikhara—*on the mountain peak; *bhāk—*who is situated; *ambara—*of the sky; *rucim—*luster; *jagat—*of the world; *vaṁśe—*in the bamboo; *yuñjan—*placing; *madana—*amorous; *ghana—*intense; *ghūrṇā—*madness; *ghuṇa—*of termites; *ghaṭam—*as the multitude; *dhṛti—*of sobriety; *dhvāntam—*the darkness; *bhindan—*dispelling; *amṛta—*of nectar; *nidhiḥ--*an ocean; *indīvara—*for the lotuses; *dṛśām—*which are eyes; *dṛśām—*of My eyes; *bandhuḥ--*friend; *kaḥ--*who; *ayam—*this; *vidhuḥ--*the moon; *iva—*like; *purastāt—*in front; *udayate—*is rising.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (trembling, looks at Vṛndā and speaks in Sanskrit): Who is this well-wisher of My eyes, rising like the moon in front of Me? As if conceiving hordes of termites into a bamboo, He injects amorous frenzy into this world. An ocean of nectar for the lotuses of everyone’s eyes, this moonlike person now stands on the mountain peak and spreads His white radiance over the sky, dissipating the darkness of My sobriety.**

**Комментарий:**

It is quite appropriate that Rādhā compares Kṛṣṇa to the moon, since the moon is a powerful stimuli for *mādhurya-rasa.*

The last sentence could also mean “…this moonlike person now stands on the mountain peak and dissipates the darkness of My sobriety by spreading the golden effulgence (*ambara-rucim*) of His *dhoti* (*ambara*).”

There is a traditional opinion that the termites germinate inside bamboo when it is merely touched the moonrays. Rādhā states that in the same way moon-like Kṛṣṇa impregnates the bamboo-like world with the termite-like amorous delirium.

The heart a perfected devotee is constantly stirred up by a type of transcendental emotions, called *sañcāri-*, or *vyabhicāri-bhāvas*. These *bhāvas* are transient by nature and are like the waves in the ocean of *bhakti-rasa*, colliding for a moment with each other and merging back with the ocean. At this moment three of *sañcāri-bhāvas* became prominent in Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī: 1) madness (*unmāda*); 2) restlessness (*cāpalya*); and 3) eagerness (*autsukya*). This is respectively indicated in this verse by the words *‘madana-ghana-ghūrṇā*,*’ ‘dhṛti-dhvānta*,*’* and *‘dṛśām bandhu.’*

## Verse 82(a-b)

*(vṛndā)*  
*sakhi rādhe, samākarṇyatām—*  
*samasta-jagatī-bhuvāṁ mṛga-dṛśām abhīṣṭāśiṣaḥ*  
*samartham abhipūraṇe kim api dolayan dor-yugam*  
*asau kulaja-ballavī-madana-vedanonmādana-*  
*vrata-praṇayinorasā rasika-maulir udbhāsate*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakhi—*O friend; *rādhe—*Rādhā; *samākarṇyatām—*please listen; *samasta—*the whole; *jagatī—*over universe; *bhuvām—*who have been born; *mṛga-dṛśām—*to the doe-eyed girls; *abhīṣṭa—*coveted; *āśiṣaḥ--*blessings; *samartham—*capable; *abhipūraṇe—*of bestowing; *kim api—*some; *dolayan—*swinging; *doḥ--*of His hands; *yugam—*the pair; *asau—*that; *kula-ja—*born in dignified families; *ballavī—*of the cowherd ladies; *madana—*amorous; *vedanā—*the agony; *unmādana—*of madness; *vrata—*the vow; *praṇayinā—*that favors; *urasā—*with His chest; *rasika—*among the connoisseurs of *rasa*; *mauliḥ--*the chief; *udbhāsate—*brilliantly appears.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: My dear friend Rādhā, please listen carefully: That chief of rasikas appears with brilliance: His chest favors the vow of maddening the noble cowherd maidens with amorous agony, and His hands, which He swings to and fro, are capable of bestowing the blessings which the doe-eyed girls from all over the universe covet.**

**Комментарий:**

Lord Kṛṣṇa’s chest and hands are elaborately described by Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī in *Govinda-līlāmṛta* 16.55-66:

“The very broad chest of Agha’s enemy is splendid. The goddess Ramā in the form of a line took shelter of its left side, its right side is handsome with the transcendental luster of Śrīvatsa tuft, and it is illuminated by the sun of Kaustubha gem that rests on the Lord’s neck pit. Graceful with the ever ongoing play of the forest flower garland, the Lord’s chest is the object of the gopīs’ hearts’ desires. That chest , which is like the emerald throne of the king that is Rādhā’s heart, is endowed with the sweetness that steals the minds of the young girls all over the world.”

“I praise the Prayāga of Śrī Kṛṣṇa's chest, a place where the three holy rivers join: Gaṅgā—Kṛṣṇa’s pearl necklace, Yamunā—Kṛṣṇa’s line of hairs, and Sarasvatī—the ruby in Kṛṣṇa’s necklace. In this way, this emperor of all holy places brings auspiciousness for all.”

“The chest of Murāri with His Kaustubha gem is very attractive. It is like a swing on which the amorous couple enjoys its tireless swinging. This swing is fastened to the pair of His pillar-like hands with the ropes of splendor and is raised by handsomeness.”

“I think that Hari’s chest with the coil of Śrīvatsa tuft has turned into a snare of Kāmadeva. The beauty of the post-like mark of Lakṣmī is a net, which is spread there to capture the *khañjana* birds of the gopīs’ eyes.”

“Disguised as Lord Hari's chest, the two sapphire doors, furnished with two very small, exquisite nipple doorknobs, guard the treasury that contains the heart of Śrī Rādhikā, the jewel of youthful maidens.”

“May the splendid hands of Agha’s enemy appear in my heart. They are the two beautiful shoots from the holy wish-yielding tamāla tree that fulfills the desire of the gopīs’ hearts, and they are the tamāla clubs of greedy hunter Kāmadeva, meant to destroy the rabbit-like pride of the gopīs’ chastity. Those hands are the two sapphire clubs that thresh the rice of the gopīs’ hearts, the two well-shaped bolts on the door to the house where the hearts of Rādhā and other girls dwell, and the bars of the cage that imprisons the parrot of Rādhā’s mind. The Lord’s hands—full, long, attractive and well built—possess the charm that the most beautiful ladies in the existence, like Lakṣmī, yearn to behold.”

“Is it that the two elephants named Sweetness and Passion have entered the jungle of Lord Hari’s body that is blooming in the springtime of youth? Holding the excellent lotus flowers—the Lord’s palms—in their graceful trunks, these elephants continuously feed on the creepers of handsomeness—the Lord's knees. Or is it that the creator Brahmā has fashioned these wonderful sapphire pillars, disguised as Kṛṣṇa’s arms and furnished with the swing of His sweetness, for swinging the hearts of Lakṣmī and other women?”

“Poets say that King Kāma placed in Kṛṣṇa's body two sapphire posts, disguised as arms, for the sacrifice to destroy the gopīs' peacefulness. In my opinion, Kṛṣṇa’s hands are the two currents in the nectar ocean of transcendental love.”

### Verse 83

*(rādhā)*  
*(sa-vismayaṁ saṁskṛtena)*  
*prapannaḥ panthānaṁ harir asakṛd asman-nayanayor*  
*apūrvo ’yaṁ pūrvaṁ kvacid api na dṛṣṭo madhurimā*  
*pratīke ’py ekasya sphurati muhur aṅgasya sakhi yā*  
*śriyas tasyāḥ pātuṁ lavam api samarthā na dṛg iyam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-vismayam—*with astonishment; *saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *prapannaḥ--*appeared; *panthānam—*on the path; *hariḥ--*Hari; *asakṛt—*not once; *asmat—*of My; *nayanayoḥ--*eyes; *apūrvaḥ--*unprecedented; *ayam—*this; *pūrvam—*before; *kvacit api—*at any time; *na dṛṣṭaḥ--*not seen; *madhurimā—*sweetness; *pratīke—*in a part; *api—*even; *ekasya—*of one (limb); *sphurati—*appears; *muhuḥ--*again and again; *aṅgasya—*limb; *sakhi—*O friend; *yā—*which (beauty); *śriyaḥ--*of beauty; *tasyāḥ--*that; *pātum—*to drink; *lavam—*a drop; *api—*even; *samarthā—*able; *na—*are not; *dṛk—*eyes; *iyam—*these.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (astonished, speaks in Sanskrit): Even though Hari now and again appears on the path of My eyes, I hadn’t seen this novel sweet beauty ever before. My eyes aren’t able to drink even a drop of that loveliness, which appears in even a part of just one of His limbs.**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā implies: “One drop of Kṛṣṇa’s loveliness is like a tidal wave that inundates My eyes. Unable to drink in that state, they are lost in anxiety.”

Like verse 58, this is another expression of *anurāga.*

### Verse 84

*(vṛndā)*  
*yadā yadā paśyasi mādhavaṁ puras*  
*tadā tadaivāsya vadasy apūrvatām*  
*navaḥ sadā syāt kim ayaṁ tavātha vā*  
*rāgonmade vismarataḥ kim akṣiṇī*

**Пословный перевод:**

*yadā yadā—*whenever; *paśyasi—*You see; *mādhavam—*Mādhava; *puraḥ--*in front; *tadā tadā eva—*at all those times; *asya—*about His; *vadasi—*You speak; *apūrvatām—*novelty; *navaḥ--*new; *sadā—*always; *syāt—*He could be; *kim—*whether; *ayam—*this; *tava—*Your; *atha vā—*or else; *rāga—*of love; *unmade—*in the madness; *vismarataḥ--*they forget; *kim—*whether; *akṣiṇī—*Your eyes.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: Whenever You see Mādhava in front of Yourself, You always say that You have never seen Him before. Could He really appear ever-fresh at all times? Or, mad with love, do Your eyes simply forget Him?**

**Комментарий:**

Each time Rādhā perceives Kṛṣṇa as if She had never seen Him before. Kṛṣṇa is ever fresh, or Rādhā’s love-maddened eyes forget him every time—either of the two reasons suggested by Vṛndā could make Rādhārāṇī see Kṛṣṇa the way She does. Vṛndā hints therefore that both the reasons make it possible for Rādhārāṇī to see Kṛṣṇa as if for the first time.

## The “Toll Gate Show”

*(tataḥ praviśati madhumaṅgala-subalārjunādibhir upāsyamāno nāndīmukhīm abhipṛcchan kṛṣṇaḥ)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tataḥ--*then; *praviśati—*enters (the stage); *madhumaṅgala—*by Madhumaṅgala; *subala—*Subala; *arjuna—*Arjuna; *ādibhiḥ--*and others; *upāsyamānaḥ--*adored; *nāndīmukhīm—*Nāndīmukhī; *abhipṛcchan—*questioning; *kṛṣṇaḥ--*Kṛṣṇa.

**Перевод:**

**(Honored by Madhumaṅgala, Subala, Arjuna, and other cowherd boys, Kṛṣṇa enters, questioning Nāndīmukhī.)**

### Verse 85

*(kṛṣṇaḥ)*  
*(sautsukyam)*  
*govardhanaṁ girim upetya kaṭākṣa-bāṇān*  
*karṇa-sphuran-maṇi-śilopari saṅkṣṇuvānā*  
*kā bhrū-dhanur-dhuvana-sūcita-luñcaneyaṁ*  
*vyāgrī-karoty ahaha mām api sambhrameṇa*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-autsukyam—*with yearning; *govardhanam—*Govardhana; *girim—*Hill; *upetya—*reaching; *kaṭākṣa—*of Her sidelong glances; *bāṇān—*the arrows; *karṇa—*on Her ears; *sphurat—*appearing; *maṇi—*the jewel; *śilā—*rock slab; *upari—*on; *saṅkṣṇuvānā—*sharpening; *kā—*who; *bhrū—*that are Her eyebrows; *dhanuḥ--*of the bows; *dhuvana—*by the shaking; *sūcita—*indicated; *luñcanā—*by whom a robbery; *iyam—*this (girl); *vyāgrī-karoti—*agitates; *ahaha—*oh; *mām—*Me; *api—*even; *sambhrameṇa—*due to restlessness.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (with yearning): Arriving at Govardhana Hill, She is sharpening the arrows of Her sidelong glances on the jewels in Her earrings, and the bows of Her shaking eyebrows suggest that I will be robbed of everything. Oh, who is that lovely girl who scares even Me with Her aggressiveness?**

**Комментарий:**

This verse, quoted in *Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi* 14.3, illustrates mutual conjugal affection between Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī and Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

The summary of Viśvanātha Cakravartī’s commentary to it is as follows. Just as one would sharpen a blade by rubbing it against a rock slab to-and-fro, Rādhārāṇī sharpens the arrows of Her sidelong glances against the rubies that bedeck Her earrings. This shows that Her eyes are unsteady—they are searching for Kṛṣṇa, and in this way reveal Rādhā’s love for Him. “These sharpened arrows of sidelong glances are meant to pierce My heart!” Thinking so, Kṛṣṇa completes the simile by comparing Rādhārāṇī’s dancing eyebrows to bows that naturally shake at shooting arrows. He then continues: “In the middle of the forest, this girl will forcibly loot the wealth of My composure and good sense! Even though I am the bravest hero, famous for My sobriety, gravity, and proper conduct, and even though I am the One who steals the composure of everyone in this universe, I am already scared to death by Her behavior! What will be My fate after She finally shots Me down with those arrows? And who is that girl, after all?”

### Verse 86-87

*(punar nirūpya) hanta, kathaṁ saiva madīya-hṛd-vitaṅkālaṅkāra-pārāvatīyaṁ priyā?(iti nāndīmukhīm abhinandan sānandam)*  
*tāra-śriyā mūrcchita-valgu-rāgā*  
*vistārayantī śruti-pāli-bhūṣām*  
*kalāñcitā hanta mayopalabdhā*  
*svarādhikeyaṁ parivādinīva*

**Пословный перевод:**

*punaḥ--*more; *nirūpya—*observing; *hanta—*ah; *katham—*is; *sā—*She; *eva—*only; *madīya—*My; *hṛt—*which is the heart; *vitaṅka—*of the nest; *alaṅkāra—*the ornament; *pārāvatī—*the dove; *iyam—*this (girl); *priyā—*dear; *iti—*thus; *nāndīmukhīm—*Nāndīmukhī; *abhinandan—*greeting; *sa-ānandam—*joyfully; *tāra—*of pearl necklace (or of high-pitched sounds); *śriyā—*by the beauty; *mūrcchita—*increased (or produced, as of sound); *valgu—*sweet; *rāgā—*My affection for Her (or musical *rāga*); *vistārayantī—*who displays; *śruti-pāli—*on Her earlobes (or of a range of *śrutis*); *bhūṣām—*the decoration; *kalā—*by fine arts (or by soft sounds); *añcitā—*worshiped; *hanta—*ah; *mayā—*by Me; *upalabdhā—*is attained; *svara—*with *svaras*; *adhikā—*abundant; *iyam—*this; *parivādinī—*a seven-stringed *vīṇā*; *iva—*as if.

**Перевод:**

**(observing more): Look, isn’t this lovely girl the dove decorating the nest of My heart?**

**(After saying this, Kṛṣṇa joyfully speaks the following to congratulate Nāndīmukhī): What a good fortune! I have met My vīṇā-like Rādhikā, who now displays Her ear ornaments, just as richly equipped with the seven notes vīṇā produces full range of śrutis. With the splendor of Her pearl necklace She is increasing My tender craving for Her, as if playing a sweet rāga with charming high-pitched sounds. In this way, as seven-stringed vīṇā is worshipped with soft sounds, this vīṇā-like girl is worshipped with fine arts.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa congratulates Nāndīmukhī, because she was the one who delivered Him the news of Rādhārāṇī’s going to the sacrifice at Govinda-kuṇḍa, thereby making it possible for Him to meet Her.

The compound *‘svara-adhikā’* (“richly equipped with the seven notes”) could also be split as *‘sva-rādhikā’—*“My Rādhikā”. In this way Kṛṣṇa mentions the name of His beloved. Also, apart from meaning “the pearl neacklace,” *tāra* can also mean “the eyes.” According to Viśvanātha Cakravartī, the beauty of Rādhārāṇī’s eyes reveals Her *anurāga.*

### Verse 88

*(nāndīmukhī)*  
*goulāṇanda, tumha-pāse ciṭṭhantīṁ maṁ jāva imāo ṇa pekkhanti, kkhaṇaṁ tāva pacchaṇṇā homi.*  
*(iti tathā sthitā)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*goula-āṇanda—*O joy of Gokula; *tumha-pāse—*near You; *ciṭṭhantīṁ—*standing; *maṁ—*me; *jāva—*as long as; *imāo—*these (gopīs); *ṇa pekkhanti—*do not notice; *kkhaṇaṁ tāva—*in a moment; *pacchaṇṇā—*concealed; *homi—*I will be; *iti—*thus; *tathā—*so; *sthitā—*remains.

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: O joy of Gokula, before these gopīs notice me near You, I will hide myself at once.**

**(saying this, she hides herself)**

**Комментарий:**

Nāndīmukhī hides, for she does not want that the gopīs suspect Her in bringing Kṛṣṇa on their path.

### Verse 89

*(kṛṣṇa)*  
*hanta sakhāyaḥ! tūrṇam ādhmāyatāṁ bhavadbhir mahā-ghaṭādhikārābhivyañjakaṁ śṛṅgārādi-vāditram! mayāpi bimbādhare vaṁśī nidheyā.*  
*(iti sarve tathā kurvanti)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*hanta—*attention!; *sakhāyaḥ--*O friends; *tūrṇam—*quickly; *ādhmāyatām—*produce by blowing; *bhavadbhiḥ--*you; *mahā-ghaṭa—*of the great toll gate; *adhikāra—*the dominion; *abhivyañjakam—*suggesting; *śṛṅga—*from horns; *ādi—*and other instruments; *vāditram—*the music; *mayā—*by Me; *api—*also; *bimba-adhare—*to My bimba color lips; *vaṁśī—*the flute; *nidheyā—*should be placed; *iti—*thus; *sarve—*everyone; *tathā—*so; *kurvanti—*does.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Attention, My friends! Blow at once your horns and other instruments to announce the dominion of the toll station! It is also the time for Me to place the flute to My bimba red lips.**

**(everyone does what Kṛṣṇa says)**

### Verse 90-91

*(vṛndā)*  
*(sva-gatam) katham etāḥ keli-muralī-ravākarṇanena vighūrṇita-mūrdhānas tarūn muhur dhārayanti?(iti samantād avalokayantī)*  
*veṇor eṣa kala-svanas taru-latā-vyājṛmbhaṇe doha-daṁ*  
*sandhyā-garja-bharaḥ pika-dvija-kuhu-svādhyāya-pārāyaṇe*  
*ābhīrendu-mukhī-smarānala-śikhotseke salīlānilo*  
*rādhā-dhairya-dharādharendra-damane dambholir unmīlati*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sva-gatam—*to herself; *katham—*how is it; *etāḥ--*these (girls); *keli—*playful; *muralī—*of the *muralī* flute; *rava—*the sound; *ākarṇanena—*due to hearing; *vighūrṇita—*spin; *mūrdhānaḥ--*their heads; *tarūn—*trees; *muhuḥ--*again and again; *dhārayanti—*they hold; *iti—*thus; *samantāt—*around; *avalokayantī—*looking; *veṇoḥ--*of the flute; *eṣaḥ--*this; *kala—*soft; *svanaḥ--*sound vibration; *taru-latā—*of the tree branches; *vyājṛmbhaṇe—*for opening up in blossoms; *dohadam—*a kind of mystical stimuli; *sandhyā—*at dusk; *garja-bharaḥ--*a thunder; *pika—*of cuckoo; *dvija—*birds; *kuhu—*cooing; *svādhyāya—*the recitation; *pāra-āyaṇe—*for bringing to an end; *ābhīra—*from the cowherd community; *indu-mukhī—*of the moon-faced (girls); *smara—*of amorous love; *ānala—*of the fire; *śikhā—*the flames; *utseke—*for increasing; *sa-līla—*frolicksome; *ānilaḥ--*wind; *rādhā—*of Rādhā; *dhairya—*the composure; *dharā-dhara—*of mountains; *indra—*the king; *damane—*for subduing; *dambholiḥ--*a thunderbolt; *unmīlati—*becomes visible*.*

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā (to herself): How is that these girls, lightheaded, are repeatedly clinging to the trees on hearing the playful flute music?**

**(looking all around)**

**This soft flute music unfolds as a mystical stimuli that makes the tree branches burst up in blossoms. It is like a thundering at dusk, that concludes the cooing of the cuckoos, which sounds like the reciting of the Vedas by the brāhmaṇas. This music is a frolicksome wind that increases the flames of amorous fire in the hearts of moon-faced gopīs, and it is like a thunderbolt that subdues the kingly mountain of Rādhā’s composure.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa’s flute music intoxicates the gopīs’ minds to such an extent that they become literally dizzy. In that condition they are now clinging to nearby standing trees just not to fall down onto the ground.

Surendranath Shasrti clarifies that, after speaking verse 91, Vṛndā looks all around, thinking, “If this is what Kṛṣṇa’s flute playing does to the gopīs, what then is happening to other living entities?”

There is a convention among Sanskrit poets that various plants may start blossoming after a particular contact with a beautiful lady, which is a kind of stimuli, called *‘dohada.’* For example, the *aśoka* will bloom if struck by a lady’s foot adorned with a jingling ankle-bells; the *tilaka* and *kubaraka*, if glanced upon or embraced; the *priyaṅgu*, if merely touched; the *bakula*, if sprinkled by liquor from the lady’s mouth; the *mandāra*, if addressed in a jolly fashion; the *campaka*, if softly smiled at; the mango tree, if blown upon; the *nameru*, if a young lady sings to it; and the *karṇikāra*, if danced in front of. Vṛndā decsribes Kṛṣṇa’s flute music as another such mystical stimuli. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī, however, is of the opinion that *‘dohada’* is a kind of medicinal herb. According to him, the music of Kṛṣṇa’s flute acts like this herb that gladdens the trees so much so they begin to bloom.

Cuckoo is known for its sweet and melodious voice. Vṛndā compares Kṛṣṇa’s flute playing to the sound of thunder, a sound, hearing which, cuckoos stop their singing. This implies that cuckoos are so enchanted with the sweetness of Kṛṣṇa’s flute that they do not wish to show off anymore in the presence of such an unsurpassable maestro. Or else, totally mesmerized, they simply loose their ability to sing.

In the verse, cuckoos are described as *‘dvijas,’* or “twice-born,” a term that equally applies to the birds and *brāhmaṇas*. Compared to the *brāhmaṇas*, their singing is likened to *‘svādhyāya,’* or recitation of a Vedic text, a routine brahminical activity. Indeed, recitation of the Vedas, with two or three intonations for the most part, may to some extent remind cooing of a cuckoo. This creates double meaning in connection with the description of Kṛṣṇa’s flute music as a sound of thunder, for there is an injunction stipulating that one should stop one’s study at the rambling of thunder.

Comparing Kṛṣṇa’s flute music to a thunderbolt and Rādhā’s composure to a mountain has to be understood in relation to the fact that in ancient time mountains had wings and were even flying in the sky. Landing wherever they desired, however, they extremely disturbed the human population. Sages then approached lord Indra for help. In response, the king of heaven took up his thunderbolt weapon and cut off the wings from all mountains.

Going through the verse, we see that the sweet sound of Kṛṣṇa’s flute produces the following four states of conscoiusness: joy (of the tree branches), muteness (of the cuckoos), eagerness (of the gopīs), and restlessness (of Rādhā). The inner purport of this, as explained in *Mahaṭī-ṭīkā*, is as follows. Just as in the development of the original material elements beginning with ether, the essential nature of each previous element is present in the following ones, restlessness contains three emotions that were mentioned prior to it: joy, silence, and eagerness. Therefore, by making Rādhā restless, the music of Kṛṣṇa’s flute incites in Her all the four emotions at once.

Kṛṣṇa’s flute playing is an *uddīpana* (stimuli that intensifies one’s love for the Lord) that is classified as a *lagna-sambandhy-uddīpana.* This means that although one may not see Kṛṣṇa directly while He is playing His flute, the flute music necessitates His immediate presence to produce it, unlike, for example, His footprints (see commentary to text 41).

### Verse 92

*(kṛṣṇaḥ)*  
*(rādhāyām apāṅgaṁ nikṣipya sānandam)*  
*vaktrāmbhojam udātta-narma-vacasā sakhyaṁ vinirmitsate*  
*maitrīṁ bhaṅgura-vīkṣitena nayana-dvandvaṁ kramād īpsati*  
*līlā-manda-gatena pāda-yugam apy āripsate saṅgamaṁ*  
*rādhāyāḥ smara-bāndhavena vayasā dehe ’dya sandhitsati*

**Пословный перевод:**

*rādhāyām—*at Rādhā; *apāṅgam—*a sidelong glance; *nikṣipya—*casting; *sa-ānandam—*buoyantly; *vaktra-ambhojam—*Her lotuslike mouth; *udātta—*exalted; *narma—*humorous; *vacasā—*by speech; *sakhyam—*friendship; *vinirmitsate—*desires to establish; *maitrīm—*companionship; *bhaṅgura—*crooked; *vīkṣitena—*by glances; *nayana-dvandvam—*Her two eyes; *kramāt—*gradually; *īpsati—*wish; *līlā—*graceful; *manda—*slow; *gatena—*by movements; *pāda-yugam—*Her two feet; *api—*also; *āripsate—*desire to begin; *saṅgamam—*association; *rādhāyāḥ--*of Rādhā; *smara—*of Cupid; *bāndhavena—*which is a friend; *vayasā—*with Her youth; *dehe—*when Her body; *adya—*today; *sandhitsati—*desires to make a peace treaty.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (casting a sidelong glance at Rādhā, says buoyantly): Now, when Rādhā’s body wishes to make a peace treaty with youth, a friend of Cupid’s, Her lotus mouth, speaking in an exalted, humorous way, wishes to befriend it; Her eyes, glancing sideways, show their desire for companionship; and Her feet, walking slowly and gracefully, long for a meeting.**

**Комментарий:**

This is the general definition of the different stages of growing up: “The toddlerhood (*kaumāra*) lasts until the age of five, childhood (*paugaṇḍa*) up to the age ten and adolescence (*kaiśora*) up to the age of fifteen. From then on, fully developed youth (*yauvana*) begins.”

In the current verse, Kṛṣṇa poetically describes the transition of Rādhārāṇī’s body from childhood to adolescence, which is technically called *vayaḥ-sandhi*. Viśvanātha Cakravartī adds some details to this description. Rādhā’s body feels that childhood is gradually neglecting it. Thus, unable to stay unprotected any longer, Her body decides to hand over the authority over itself to adolescence, a more powerful ally. Rādhā’s lotus mouth, neglected by childish laughing and meaningless chatter, now wishes to befriend adolescence by speaking humorous words, which are full of exalted qualities such as compassion, liberality, and sweetness. Rādhā’s eyes desire adolescence as their companion by glancing crookedly, for childish straight stares have now abandoned them. Finally, Rādhā’s feet, deserted by clumsiness, now walk in a slow and graceful style.

*Vayaḥ-sandhi*, or when an innocent girl just begins to involuntarily show her attractive feminine characteristics, is a very charming age. At such age, Rādhā’s budding youthful beauty acts as a powerful stimuli (*uddīpana*) for Krṣṇa’s amorous sentiments.

### Verse 93

*(rādhā)*  
*(prayatnād ākāra-guptim abhinayantī sāci-kandharam apavārya saṁskṛtena)*  
*kuvalaya-yuvatīnāṁ lehayann akṣi-bhṛṅgaiḥ*  
*kuvalaya-dala-lakṣmī-laṅgimāḥ svāṅga-bhāsaḥ*  
*mada-kala-kalabhendrollaṅghi-līlā-taraṅgaḥ*  
*kavalayati dhṛtiṁ me kṣmādharāraṇya-dhūrtaḥ*

**Пословный перевод:**

*prayatnāt—*with difficulty; *ākāra-guptim—*the concealment of Her feelings; *abhinayantī—*acting out; *sāci—*bent; *kandharam—*Her neck; *apavārya—*aside; *saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *kuvalaya—*of earthly; *yuvatīnām—*young girls; *lehayan—*making lick; *akṣi—*the eyes; *bhṛṅgaiḥ--*like bees; *kuvalaya—*of a lotus flower; *dala—*of the petals; *lakṣmī—*(more than) the beauty; *laṅgimāḥ--*whose attractiveness; *sva—*of His; *aṅga—*body; *bhāsaḥ--*the splendor; *mada-kala—*intoxicated; *kalabha-indra—*(the pastimes) of the king of elephants; *ullaṅghi—*surpass; *līlā—*of His pastimes; *taraṅgaḥ--*the waves; *kavalayati—*devours; *dhṛtim—*composure; *me—*My; *kṣmā-dhara—*near the mountain; *āraṇya—*in the forest; *dhūrtaḥ--*the trickster.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (Hides Her feelings with difficulty and, turning Her head, speaks aside in Sanskrit): With the waves of His amorous plays, the cunning trickster in the forest nearby Govardhana surpasses the intoxicated king of elephants. He devours My composure by enticing the bee-like eyes of the youthful, earthly girls to lick His bodily splendor, which is more charming than the petals of a blue lotus.**

**Комментарий:**

Seeing Kṛṣṇa directly and hearing His flute playing has aroused loving feelings in Rādhārāṇī’s heart. She tries to hide Her ecstatic emotions from Her companions. Turning Her face from them so that they couldn’t hear Her, Rādhā speaks this verse to Herself.

In the scriptures, Kṛṣṇa is often likened to a male elephant. This animal, when intoxicated with lusty desires, is known for its wild and enthusiastic love plays with females. Although Kṛṣṇa’s dallying with the gopīs of Vraja is often compared to the elephant’s love sporting, Kṛṣṇa is more skillful in such activities than the elephant.

With this verse Rādhārāṇī says that it is Kṛṣṇa who made Her lose control over Herself. She is not to be blamed, for She is utterly helpless to resist Him. Rādhārāṇī calls Kṛṣṇa “cunning trickster” in a playful way.

Viśvanātha Cakravartī analyses this verse from a grammatical angle. In Sanskrit there is a syntactic rulewhich ordains the subordinate agent of the causative action that expresses “eating” to become the object of this action by assuming the second case. In this verse, the word *‘lehayan’* (“causing to lick”) expresses the causative activity of eating and its subordinate agent is *‘akṣi-bhṛṅgaiḥ’* (“\[the gopīs’\] bee-like eyes”). The word *‘akṣi-bhṛṅgaiḥ,’* however, is in the third case (a default case for the subordinate agent of the causative action), which may seem to be a deviation from the specific rule mentioned above. This is not a devitation, however, because the word *‘lehayan’* here expresses simply relishing, and not licking followed by swallowing (which would put it in the category of “eating” mentioned in the rule, thereby ordaining the second case for *‘akṣi-bhṛṅgaiḥ,’* which would then appear as *‘akṣi-bhṛṅgān’*). From this it is understood that the gopīs’ bee-like eyes are “licking” the splendor of Kṛṣṇa’s body in the sense that they are tasting its beauty, and not in the sense of consuming it, just as the bees do not consume lotus petals—they simply carry its fragrance in the form of pollen. Therefore, although generally the activity of licking doesn’t exist without the subsequent activity of swallowing, in this case this is an instance of a poetic suggestion which expresses something else than the literal meaning of a word.

This verse is also quoted in *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* 3.5.18 as an example of joy (*harṣa*), which is one of the *vyabhicāri-bhāvas*. There, according to Viśvanātha Cakravartī’s commentary, the word “bee-like eyes” is in the second case (*akṣi-bhṛṅgān*), which does conform to the above mentioned syntactic rule in the sense that the eyes’ activity of licking is followed by swallowing. It suggests, therefore, that Kṛṣṇa’s bodily splendor is like the honey, which the gopīs’ bee-like eyes are enticed by Kṛṣṇa to consume.

### Verse 94

*(kṛṣṇaḥ)*  
*(śṛṅgād avarohaṇaṁ nāṭayan rādhām ālokya sānandam)*  
*sthūle rādhikayā payodhara-yuge hāraḥ prasādī-kṛtaḥ*  
*khyāte bālatayā kace ca kuṭile maulir vitīrṇo ’nayā*  
*vinyastaṁ śruti-sevinor api masī-mālinyam evānayor*  
*ity akṣṇor dvayam īrṣyayā mṛga-dṛśaḥ śaṅke mumocārjavam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*śṛṅgāt—*from the peak; *avarohaṇam—*the descent; *nāṭayan—*enacting; *rādhām—*at Rādhā; *ālokya—*looking; *sa-ānandam—*joyfully; *sthūle—*full; *rādhikayā—*by Rādhikā; *payodhara-yuge—*on Her two breasts; *hāraḥ--*the necklace; *prasādī-kṛtaḥ--*favored; *khyāte—*celebrated; *bālatayā—*for tenderness; *kace—*in Her hair; *ca—*and; *kuṭile—*wavy; *mauliḥ--*an ornament; *vitīrṇaḥ--*bestowed; *anayā—*by Her; *vinyastam—*applied; *śruti—*the ears; *sevinoḥ--*reaching; *api—*even; *masī-mālinyam—*the dirt of mascara; *eva—*only; *anayoḥ--*of these; *iti—*thus; *akṣṇoḥ dvayam--*two eyes; *īrṣyayā—*out of jealousy; *mṛga-dṛśaḥ--*of Her, who has eyes like those of a doe; *śaṅke—*I suspect; *mumoca—*have given up; *ārjavam—*straightness.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (coming down from the peak, gazes at Rādhā and speaks joyfully): Rādhikā has gifted a necklace to Her full breast and has topped Her wavy hair, celebrated for their softness, with an ornament. On the other hand, Her eyes She has only stained with the dirt of mascara, even though they extend up to Her ears.
