For this reason I suspect that this girl’s eyes, which are like those of a doe, have given up their straight glances out of jealousy.**

**Комментарий:**

Speaking ambiguously, Kṛṣṇa puts forward a very interesting reason as to why Rādhā’s from now on look sideways, and not straight. “Because they are jealous of Her breast and hair,” He says. “Even though dull (*sthūle*), Rādhā’s breast has been awarded with a necklace, and even though crooked (*kuṭile*) and widely known for their weakness (*bālatayā*) Her hair has been topped with an ornament!” So, the meaning is that neither Rādhā’s “dull” breast nor Her “crooked” hair are worthy of the gifts they have received. Rādhā’s eyes, however, are superior in their characteristic. They are *‘śruti-sevinoḥ’*—“extend up to Her ears.” Another meaning of *‘śruti-sevinoḥ’* is “studying the Vedas.” In this way, Rādhā’s eyes obviously surpass Her “dull” breast and “crooked” hair. Despite being worthy recepients of some valuable article, however, they are only smeared with “the dirt” (*mālinyam*) of mascara (*masī*). “Mistreated” in this way by Rādhārāṇī, they have a good reason to be jealous of the breast and hair, and naturally “have given up their straight glances.”

### Verse 95

*(lalitā)*  
*(apavārya) halā rāhe, udaradi eso haṭhilladārahaḍi-vaṇijja-mahā-satha-vāha-ṇāho! tā apekkhantīo via sa-vissambhaṁ calamha.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*apavārya—*aside; *halā—*O; *rāhe—*Rādhā; *udaradi—*comes down; *eso—*this; *haṭhilladā—*with force; *ārahaḍi—*and boldness; *vaṇijja—*for trading; *mahā—*large; *satha-vāha—*of the gang; *ṇāho—*(this) master; *tā—*thus; *apekkhantīo—*not seeing; *via—*as if; *sa-vissambhaṁ—*with confidence; *calamha—*let us walk.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: Hey Rādhā, the top head of the violent and daring gang is coming down! Let us walk confidently as if we do not notice Him.**

**Комментарий:**

Lalitā thinks it wise that the gopīs pretend as if they simply do not notice Kṛṣṇa’s presence and thus proceed on. Otherwise, Kṛṣṇa will surely cause them more trouble if He will sense their disregard.

### Verse 96

*(rādhā)*  
*sahi, lahu lahu jāhi jaṁ esā phūḍidovala-maṇḍalī-bandhurā vasundharā-dhara-taḍī.*  
*(iti sadṛṣṭi-kṣepaṁ parikrāmati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sahi—*O friend; *lahu lahu—*slowly, slowly; *jāhi—*go; *jaṁ—*because; *esā—*this; *phūḍida—*broken; *uvala—*of rocks; *maṇḍalī—*with the multitude; *bandhurā—*uneven; *vasundharā-dhara—*of the mountain; *taḍī—*the side; *iti—*thus; *sa-dṛṣṭi-kṣepam—*glancing sideways; *parikrāmati—*walks on.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: Slowly, slowly, sakhī. This side of the hill is uneven with many broken rocks strewn all over.**

**(walks on, glancing sideways)**

**Комментарий:**

Having noticed Her beloved nearby, how could Rādhā allow Herself to escape Him? On the other hand, She is shy, and cannot at this moment disclose Her inner desire to Lalitā, who seemingly wants to avoid Kṛṣṇa by all means. Finding an excuse to slow down the pace is the only thing Rādhā can do to let Kṛṣṇa catch up with the gopīs.

### Verse 97

*(kṛṣṇaḥ)*  
*sakhe subala, katham asmān avamatya calituṁ pravṛttā eva sa-līlam amūr amūka-mañjīrā mañju-bhāṣiṇyaḥ? tatas tūrṇam arjunena sārdham amūṣāṁ ghaṭasva vyāghoṭanāya!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakhe—*O friend; *subala—*Subala; *katham—*why; *asmān—*us; *avamatya—*disregarding; *calitum—*in walking; *pravṛttā—*engaged; *eva—*only; *sa-līlam—*playfully; *amūḥ--*those (girls); *amūka—*with loud; *mañjīrā—*ankle-bells; *mañju--*sweetly; *bhāṣiṇyaḥ--*talking; *tataḥ--*therefore; *tūrṇam—*quickly; *arjunena—*Arjuna; *sārdham—*along with; *amūṣām—*their; *ghaṭasva—*try; *vyāghoṭanāya—*for turning back.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: My friend Subala, those girls have disregarded us! How could they just keep on playfully walking, loudly jingling their ankle bells and sweetly chatting? Go quickly with Arjuna and try to turn them back!**

**Комментарий:**

“They walk in front of Me, the emperor of tax collectors, with the accompaniment of the music of their ankle-bells? Sheer impudence!” This is what Kṛṣṇa thinks, totally absorbed in His role.

### Verse 98

*(subalaḥ)*  
*(sa-tvaraṁ sārjunaḥ parikramya)hanta sa-gavvao gavva-vikkaiṇīo! kahaṁ ghaṭa-cattara-ṇāhaṁ aṇādarantīo sacchandaṁ gacchanti hodīo? tā vahuḍia suṭṭhu ṇo pabohentu!*  
*(sarvāḥ prakāmam aśrutim abhinīya sāvahelaṁ calanti)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-tvaram—*with haste; *sa-arjunaḥ--*with Arjuna; *parikramya—*overtaking them; *hanta—*aha!; *sa-gavvao—*proud; *gavva—*of dairy; *vikkaiṇīo—*sellers; *kahaṁ—*why; *ghaṭa-cattara—*of the toll crossroad; *ṇāhaṁ—*the supervisor; *aṇādarantīo—*disregarding; *sacchandaṁ--*willfully; *gacchanti—*are walking; *hodīo—*you are; *tā—*therefore; *vahuḍia—*turning back; *suṭṭhu—*nicely; *ṇo—*to us; *pabohentu—*explain; *sarvāḥ--*all (the *gopīs*); *prakāmam—*intentionally; *aśrutim—*their non-hearing; *abhinīya—*acting out; *sa-avahelam—*with disdain; *calanti—*walk.

**Перевод:**

**Subala (with Arjuna hastily overtaking the gopīs): Aha! You arrogant dairy sellers, how could you ignore the toll-gate supervisor and willfully proceed on? Turn back and explain all of this to us!**

**(All the gopīs, boldly pretending not to hear, walk on with disdain.)**

### Verse 99—100

*(subalaḥ)*  
*(dhāvann uccaiḥ) haṁho, appaṇo māhappaṁ mā haredha! tuṇṇaṁ parāvaṭṭedhaṁ!*  
*(sarvāḥ:)*  
*(sa-nirvedam iva parāvṛtya) bho puṭṭha-maṁsāda! kadhaṁ tue parāvaṭṭidaṁha?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dhāvan—*running; *uccaiḥ--*loudly; *haṁho—*Hey!; *appaṇo—*own; *māhappaṁ—*glory; *mā—*do not; *haredha—*take away; *tuṇṇaṁ—*quickly; *parāvaṭṭedhaṁ—*return. *sa-nirvedam—*with disgust; *iva—*as if; *parāvṛtya—*turning around; *bho—*O; *puṭṭha-maṁsāda—*back-biter; *kadhaṁ—*why; *tue—*by you; *parāvaṭṭidaṁha—*we are made to turn around.

**Перевод:**

**Subala (shouts while running): Hey, do not spoil your reputation! Return at once!**

**All the gopīs (turning around and speak as if with disgust): You backbiter! Why should we turn back because of you?**

### Verse 101-102

*(subalaḥ)*  
*paḍamaṁ dāva kkhoṇī-vilagga-mathaao vandantu mahā-ghaṭa-dāṇindaṁ hodīo!*  
*(viśākhā)*  
*(sa-smitam) suala, kiṁ vandaṇāriho ṇa hodi vallaindaṇandaṇo? kintu loottarassa jaṇṇassa heaṅgavīṇovaharaṇe āraddhavvadāṇaṁ aṁhāṇaṁ vaṁhaṇedara-vandaṇaṁ bhaavadīe ṇisiddhaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*paḍamaṁ dāva—*firstly; *kkhoṇī—*to the ground; *vilagga—*placing; *mathaao—*your heads; *vandantu—*you should offer respects; *mahā—*great; *ghaṭa-dāṇi—*of toll collectors; *indaṁ—*to the chief; *hodīo—*you. *sa-smitam—*with a smile; *suala—*O Subala; *kiṁ—*why; *vandaṇa—*of being honored; *ariho—*deserving; *ṇa hodi—*is not; *vallainda-ṇandaṇo—*the son of the cowherd king; *kintu—*but; *loottarassa—*to the extraordinary; *jaṇṇassa—*sacrifice; *heaṅgavīṇa—*ghee; *uvaharaṇe—*of bringing; *āraddha—*who have begun; *vadāṇaṁ—*the vow; *aṁhāṇaṁ—*for us; *vaṁhaṇa—*than a *brāhmaṇa; idara—*to anyone other; *vandaṇaṁ—*showing honor; *bhaavadīe—*by the saintly lady (Paurṇamāsī); *ṇisiddhaṁ—*forbidden*.*

**Перевод:**

**Subala: First of all, pay your homage to the top head of toll collectors by touching the ground with your heads!**

**Viśākhā (smiling): Subala, why wouldn’t we honor the son of the cowherd king? But because we have undertaken the vow of bringing ghee to an extraordinary sacrifice, Bhagavatī has forbidden us to show honor to anybody but a brāhmaṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

As Viśvanātha cakravartī says, Viśākhā is lying here. This is the fouth element of *bhāṇikā*, called *‘sādhvasa’*, or “false story” (see Appendix…).

As everywhere in this play, Bhagavatī refers to Paurṇamāsī.

### Verse 103-104

*(arjunaḥ)*  
*(vihāsya) visāhe, eso vundāaṇa-bhū-vundārao aṁha-mahā-dāṇindro bi dāṇiṁ āraddhavvado vaṭṭadi. tā vvadiṇīhiṁ assa vandaṇe dūsaṇaṁ ṇathi.*  
*(lalitā)*  
*kīdisaṁ taṁ vvadaṁ?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*visāhe—*O Viśākhā; *eso—*this; *vundāaṇa—*of Vṛndāvana; *bhū—*of the land; *vundārao—*the master; *aṁha—*our; *mahā—*great; *dāṇi—*of toll collectors; *indro—*the chief; *vi—*also; *dāṇiṁ—*at present; *āraddha—*begun; *vvado—*the vow; *vaṭṭadi—*has; *tā—*therefore; *vvadiṇīhiṁ—*by you, who observe a vow; *assa—*Him; *vandaṇe—*in honoring; *dūsaṇaṁ—*defect; *ṇathi—*there is no. *kīdisaṁ—*what kind of; *taṁ—*that; *vvadaṁ—*vow*.*

**Перевод:**

**Arjuna (smiling): Viśākhā, our top head of toll collectors is the master of Vṛndāvana, and He has also undertaken a vow. Therefore, there is no any fault if you, who are also observing a vow, show Him honor.**

**Lalitā: What type of vow is He performing?**

**Комментарий:**

Arjuna smiles, for he is now going to defeat Viśākhā’s reason as to why the gopīs can’t honor Kṛṣṇa. “He is also like you, dedicated to His vow. And so, offering Him honor will not be wrong.”

### Verse 105

*(kṛṣṇaḥ)*  
*(sa-smitam) nityam abalārbuda-dvija-vasana-dānaṁ nāma vratam.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-smitam—*smiling; *nityam—*daily; *abala—*incapable; *arbuda—*a hundred million; *dvija—*to brāhmaṇas; *vasana—*of clothes; *dānam—*charity; *nāma—*by the name; *vratam—*the vow*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (smiling): My vow is called “Daily charity of clothes to countless poor brāhmaṇas.”**

**Комментарий:**

Not waiting for Arjuna, Kṛṣṇa answers Lalitā’s question Himself.

The words Kṛṣṇa uses hint at His true intention with their double meanings: “Daily crushing (*dāna*) the lips (*dvija-vasana*) of countless young girls (*abalā*).”

### Verse 106

*(lalitā)*  
*tado juttā esā ghaṭāhiāridā jaṁ īdisīe mahāpaavīe samārohaṇaṁ viṇā ṇia-mahā-vadassa rakkhaṇaṁ dukkaraṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tado—*therefore; *juttā—*proper; *esā—*this; *ghaṭa-ahiāridā—*the post of toll gate authority; *jaṁ—*because; *īdisīe—*to such; *mahā-paavīe—*high post; *samārohaṇaṁ—*ascending; *viṇā—*without; *ṇia—*Your; *mahā-vadassa—*of the great vow; *rakkhaṇaṁ—*observance; *dukkaraṁ—*is difficult*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: Then Your post as a toll gate authority is quite suitable. It would be very hard for You to keep Your great vow without assuming such a high rank.**

**Комментарий:**

Lalitā makes a sarcastic remark, picking up on the second meaning of the name of Kṛṣṇa’s vow. According to Viśvanātha Cakravartī, She means the following: “Kṛṣṇa, You will find it very difficult to assault others’ wives in the cities and villages amidst the cultured people. Thus you have chosen to become a toll collector.” The logic of this is that toll stations are generally situated on the roads outside the cities and villages.

## Verse 107(a-b)

*(kṛṣṇaḥ)*  
*(vihasya puraḥ paśyan)sakhe madhumaṅgala, śrūyatām—*  
*abhyukṣya niṣkaṁ patayālunā muhuḥ*  
*svedena niṣkampatayā vyavasthitā*  
*pañcālikā kuñcita-locanā kathaṁ*  
*pañcālikā-dharmam avāpa rādhikā*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vihasya—*laughing; *puraḥ--*in front; *paśyan—*looking; *sakhe—*O friend; *madhumaṅgala—*Madhumaṅgala; *śrūyatām—*listen; *abhyukṣya—*sprinkling; *niṣkam—*Her necklace; *patayālunā—*dropping; *muhuḥ--*again and again; *svedena—*by sweat; *niṣkampatayā—*without trembling; *vyavasthitā—*situated; *pañca-ālikā—*having five friends; *kuñcita—*contracted; *locanā—*Her eyes; *katham—*why; *pañcālikā—*of a doll; *dharmam—*the nature; *avāpa—*has attained; *rādhikā—*Rādhikā.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (laughing, looks ahead): My friend Madhumaṅgala, listen to this: Rādhikā is motionless and is repeatedly sprinkling Her necklace with drops of perspiration. Why has She, even though in the company of Her five friends, squinted and became frozen like a doll?**

**Комментарий:**

Viśvanātha Cakravartī explains the reason why Kṛṣṇa speaks this verse. After announcing the name of His vow, Kṛṣṇa noticed that Rādhā’s body transformed in a specific way. Her symptoms showed that She was deeply affected with amorous feelings at hearing of Kṛṣṇa’s actual intention. Rādhā’s friends can hear what Kṛṣṇa is saying to Madhumaṅgala, and so, Kṛṣṇa in a subtle way hints to them at Rādhā’s real feelings by posing a suggestive question: “How could Rādhā become so scared if She is surronded by Her five friends?” At the same time, Kṛṣṇa supports Rādhā in concealing Her feelings, which is appropriate at this point. He therefore doesn’t say directly that Rādhā’s stupor is actually caused by Her eagerness for Him and instead goes on to falsely describe Rādhā as being scared. Rādhā squints out of bashfulness, but externally it appears as if She did so out of fear, just like Her being stunned. Her profuse sweating is another ecstatic symptom caused by eagerness.

Rādhā is actually accompanied not by five, but by four sakhīs—Lalitā, Viśākhā, Campakalatā, and Citrā. Kṛṣṇa here counts Vṛndā as Rādhā’s sakhī too.

In *Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi* (12.1) this verse illustrates *stambha*, an involuntary ecstatic symptom (*sāttvika-bhāva*) of being stunned out of joy.

Elsewhere, Viśvanātha Cakravartī mentions ten progressive stages of Cupid’s influence on a person who is in love. Stupor is the ninth of them. This suggests the intensity of Rādhā’s lovesickness at this point.

### Verse 108

*(madhumaṅgalaḥ)*  
*(kṛṣṇasya karṇe nibhṛtokti-mudrām abhinayann uccaiḥ) bho pia-vaassa, diṭṭhiā vaḍḍhase. pekkha, gavvida-sahī-sahidā sajjhaseṇa thambhidā rāhī.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kṛṣṇasya—*of Kṛṣṇa; *karṇe—*in the ear; *nibhṛta—*in secret; *ukti—*of speaking; *mudrām—*the pose; *abhinayan—*acting out; *uccaiḥ--*loudly; *bho—*O; *pia-vaassa—*dear friend; *diṭṭhiā—*due to good fortune; *vaḍḍhase—*You prosper; *pekkha—*look; *gavvida—*proud; *sahī—*by girlfriends; *sahidā—*accompanied; *sajjhaseṇa—*out of fear; *thambhidā—*stunned; *rāhī—*Rādhā*.*

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgalaḥ (acting if whispering something secretly in Kṛṣṇa’s ear, speaks audibly): My dear friend, You are blessed with good fortune. Just see how Rādhā, even though accompanied by Her proud girlfriends, is stunned with fear.**

**Комментарий:**

To expand the “toll gate show,” Madhumaṅgala indirectly makes a false accusation against Rādhā, blaming Her to be a smuggler. According to *Mahaṭī-ṭīkā*, Madhumaṅgala makes a gesture of whispering something into Kṛṣṇa’s ear, as if saying the following: “Just see, Kṛṣṇa! She is cheating You, the great tax collector, by smuggling so many goods. Her fear has exposed Her as a smuggler. It is Your good fortune that She Herself made it known by being scared even in the company of Her friends.”

### Verse 109

*(vṛndā)*  
*(sa-smitam)*  
*mṛgādhipati-madhyamaḥ kaṭhina-pīvara-kroḍa-bhāk*  
*bhujaṅgama-bhuja-dvayas tvam asi puṇḍarīkekṣaṇaḥ*  
*ataḥ kathaya sādhvasaṁ na bhavataḥ kathaṁ vindatām*  
*asau puruṣa-kuñjara prakṛti-bhīrur eṇekṣaṇā*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-smitam—*with a smile; *mṛga-adhipati—*like that of a lion; *madhyāmaḥ--*with a waist; *kaṭhina—*stiff; *pīvara—*and stout; *kroḍa—*of thighs; *bhāk—*a possessor; *bhujaṅgama—*snake-like; *bhuja-dvayaḥ--*with two hands; *tvam—*You; *asi—*are; *puṇḍarīka—*like that of a tiger; *īkṣaṇaḥ--*with a glance; *ataḥ--*therefore; *kathaya—*tell; *sādhvasam—*fear; *na—*not; *bhavataḥ--*of You; *katham—*whether; *vindatām—*should attain; *asau—*She; *puruṣa-kuñjara—*O elephant among men; *prakṛti—*by nature; *bhīruḥ--*fearful; *eṇa-īkṣaṇā—*the doe-eyed one.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā (smiling): O best among males, this doe-eyed girl is fearful by nature. Then why shouldn’t she be afraid of You with a waist of a lion, stiff and stout thighs of a boar, snake-like hands, and eyes of a tiger?**

**Комментарий:**

Vṛndā’s smile shows that she can understand what is happening in Rādhā’s heart.

A doe will become fearful in the presence of even one of the animals that are mentioned in the verse. So should it be a surprise that Rādhā, who is like a doe, is fearful of Kṛṣṇa, who embodies the remarkable features of all of them—a lion, a boar, a serpent, and a tiger?

*Mahaṭī-ṭīkā* points out that with this verse Vṛndā implies that all the visible changes in Rādhā’s body took place due to Her tasting the handsomeness of Kṛṣṇa’s waist, thighs, hands, and eyes, which all Vṛndā poetically describes.

Analysing the poetical expressions in this verse, Surendranath Shastri says that Kṛṣṇa’s waist, described as that of a lion, suggests that it is thin and elegant. “Snake-like hands” says that Kṛṣṇa’s hands are soft, smooth, and capable of encircling Rādhā in tight embrace. Finally, “eyes of a tiger” indirectly suggests that Kṛṣṇa’s eyes are like lotuses, because the word *‘puṇḍarīka’* could mean both tiger and lotus. This is a poetic ornament called *‘vyāja-stuti,’* and it means “glorification in disguise (of defamation),” for tiger’s eyes are usually not considered beautiful.

### Verse 110

*(lalitā)*  
*lalidā-pālidāṇaṁ goāliāṇaṁ kahiṁpi bhaa-saddo bi kaṇṇa-kuharaṁ ṇa gado. tahabi imassa goula-rakkhaṇa-vvadassa amha-rāa-kumarassa purado kīdisaṁ bhaaṁ ṇama?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*lalidā—*by Lalitā; *pālidāṇaṁ—*protected; *goāliāṇaṁ—*of the cowherd girls; *kahiṁpi—*at any place; *bhaa-saddo—*the word “fear”; *bi—*even; *kaṇṇa-kuharaṁ—*in the earholes; *ṇa—*did not; *gado—*enter; *taha bi—*still; *imassa—*(of) this (son); *goula—*Gokula; *rakkhaṇa—*in protecting; *vvadassa—*who is fixed; *amha—*our; *rāa—*of king; *kumarassa—*of the son; *purado—*in front; *kīdisaṁ—*what; *bhaaṁ—*fear; *ṇama—*perhaps.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: The cowherd girls who are protected by Lalitā have not heard anywhere even such a word*****—*****“fear.” And anyway, where is the question of fear when we are in front of our prince who is resolved to protect Gokula?**

**Комментарий:**

First Lalitā boasts by implying that she alone is sufficient to remove any cause of fear. Then she adds that Kṛṣṇa’s presence nearby creates even more safety. Lalitā expects that these flatteing words will soften Kṛṣṇa and He will not behave violently towards them gopīs.

### Verse 111

*(kṛṣṇaḥ)*  
*lalāmāṅgi lalite, lalitaṁ bravīṣi. tad imāṁ sakhī-maṇḍalena sārdhaṁ pāṇḍura-śilām adhyāsya samudācāram avadhāraya.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*lalāma—*decorated; *aṅgi—*your body; *lalite—*O Lalitā; *lalitam—*gracefully; *bravīṣi—*you speak; *tat—*therefore; *imām—*(on) this; *sakhī-maṇḍalena—*the assembly of your girlfriends; *sārdham—*together with; *pāṇḍura—*white; *śilām—*on the slab; *adhyāsya—*sitting; *samudācāram—*proper course of action; *avadhāraya—*consider*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: decorated Lalitā, you speak with grace. Please have a seat with your friends on this white slab and think what would be the right thing for you to do.**

**Комментарий:**

Viśvanātha Cakravartī comments that by saying “O decorated Lalitā”, Kṛṣṇa implies the following: “You are proud due to all these ornaments and clothes you wear. Today I shall remove your pride by taking them off with My own hands.” Or else, the word *‘lalāma’* could stand for a banner that signifies one’s supremacy. In this case, the implicit import would be this: “O Lalitā, your body is a victory banner of Rādhā and other girls. I, the great tax collector, shall not tolerate if this banner is raised today, and therefore, shall assault you by levying a tax.”

### Verse 112

*(lalitā)*  
*goula-juarāa, pekkha: arohadi bhaavanto caṇḍa-maūhoo gaaṇa-maṇḍalaṁ. tā āpucchamha tumaṁ turiaṁ jaṇṇa-maṇḍavoralambhaṇassa.*  
*(kṛṣṇo bhruvor āghūrṇena madhumaṅgalaṁ vyāpārayati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*goula—*of Gokula; *jua-rāa—*O king of youths; *pekkha—*look; *ārohadi—*is rising; *bhaavanto—*the (sun-)god; *caṇḍa—*scorching; *maūho—*his rays; *gaaṇa-maṇḍalaṁ—*on the sky; *tā—*therefore; *āpucchamha—*we should take leave; *tumaṁ—*from You; *turiaṁ—*quickly; *jaṇṇa—*of the sacrifice; *maṇḍava—*the arena; *uvalambhaṇassa—*to get to; *kṛṣṇaḥ--*Kṛṣṇa; *bhruvoḥ--*of His eyebrows; *āghūrṇena—*by the movement; *madhumaṅgalam—*Madhumaṅgala; *vyāpārayati—*induces.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: O king of youths of Gokula, just see, the sun with its fierce rays is rising in the sky. So we should take leave from You to reach the sacrificial arena soon.**

**(Moving His eyebrows, Kṛṣṇa urges Madhumaṅgala)**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa urges Madhumaṅgala to say something in response.

### Verse 113

*(madhumaṅgalaḥ)*  
*lalide, ajja tumhāhinto sulukkaṁ geṇhiduṁ ṇa kkhu juttaṁ jaṁ saṅgave heaṅgava-bhāra-bhaṅgura-majjhamāo appa-siṇiddhāo hodio ghaṭṭe āadua vaṭṭanti. tā rittattaṇa-dūsaṇa-ṇivāraṇakhaṁ kimpi thoaṁ aṇumaṇṇia jaha-sokkhaṁ jāntu.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*lalide—*O Lalitā; *ajja—*now; *tumhāhinto—*from you; *sulukkaṁ—*the fee; *geṇhiduṁ—*to accept; *ṇa—*not; *kkhu—*indeed; *juttaṁ—*proper; *jaṁ—*because; *saṅgave—*in the late morning; *heaṅgava—*of butter; *bhāra—*under the weight; *bhaṅgura—*bent; *majjhamāo—*your waists; *appa-siṇiddhāo*kind by nature; *hodio—*you; *ghaṭṭe—*to the toll station; *āadua—*having come; *vaṭṭanti—*are; *tā—*therefore; *rittattaṇa—*of being empty-handed; *dūsaṇa—*the fault; *ṇivāraṇakhaṁ—*in order to remove; *kimpi—*something; *thoaṁ—*little; *aṇumaṇṇia—*being permitted; *jaha-sokkhaṁ—*happily; *jāntu—*please go*.*

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala: O Lalitā, indeed, it would not be proper to accept any fee from you at this time, for all of you kind-hearted gopīs have come to the toll station with your waists bent under the weight of butter. But just to avoid the offence of leaving us empty-handed, give something small, and then proceed happily with our permission.**

### Verse 114-115

***viśākhā:*** *ammahe, adiṭṭha-puvvaṁ hi kkhu goaḍḍhaṇe ghaṭṭa-dāṇaṁ!*  
***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *viśākhe, satyaṁ vyāharasi. bhavad-vidhāḥ khalu kathaṁ drakṣyanti yad adyāpi paśyantyo’pi na paśyanti?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ammahe*—Ha!; *adiṭṭha*—not seen; *puvvam*—previously; *hi kkhu*—indeed; *goaḍḍhaṇe*—at Govardhana; *ghaṭṭa-dāṇaṁ*—toll tax. *viśākhe—*O Viśākhā; *satyam—*the truth; *vyāharasi—*you speak; *bhavad-vidhāḥ—*\[person\] like you; *khalu—*indeed; *katham—*how; *drakṣyanti—*will see; *yat—*if; *adya—*today; *api—*even; *paśyantyaḥ--*seeing; *api—*though; *na paśyanti—*do not see.

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā: What a surprise! We have not seen anybody paying a tax at Govardhana hill.**

**Kṛṣṇa: O Viśākhā, you speak the truth. How will somebody like you see it happening, if even today, although seeing, you don’t notice it at all?**

### Verse 116-117

***lalitā:*** *(janāntikam) hanta sahīo, paḍhamaṁ sāma-ghaṭṭanā jebba juttā.*  
***sarvāḥ:***  *suṭṭhu bhaṇāsi!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*jana-antikam—*(speaks) aside; *hanta—*O; *sahīo—*friends; *paḍhamaṁ—*firstly; *sāma—*for pacification; *ghaṭṭanā—*the endeavor; *jebba—*only; *juttā—*appropriate. *sarvāḥ--*all \[the gopīs\]; *suṭṭhu—*well; *bhaṇāsi—*you speak.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (speaks to other gopīs): O sakhīs, in the beginning peaceful negotiation is the most appropriate means.**

**All the gopīs: Well spoken!**

### Verse 118

***lalitā:*** *(sa-praśrayam abhupetya) goulāṇanda, ekka-ggāma-vāsisu visuddha-paidisu mādisa-jaṇesu ṇa juttaṁ kira su-saloassa sal-loa-mauliṇo tumhādisassa pāḍiullāaraṇaṁ. tā aṇujāṇīhi turiaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-praśrayam—*with humility; *abhupetya—*approaching; *goula-āṇanda—*O bliss of Gokula; *ekka-ggāma—*of the same village; *vāsisu—*toward the residents; *visuddha—*very pure; *paidisu—*having nature; *mādisa—*like us; *jaṇesu—*people; *ṇa—*not; *juttaṁ—*proper; *kira—*indeed; *su-saloassa—*for famous; *sal-loa—*of saintly; *mauliṇo—*the best; *tumhādisassa—*(for) a person like You; *pāḍiulla—*inimical; *āaraṇaṁ—*behavior; *tā—*thus; *aṇujāṇīhi—*let us go; *turiaṁ—*fast.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (approaching Kṛṣṇa with humility): O bliss of Gokula, You are reputed as the most well-behaved person. Inimical behavior toward pure-hearted people like us, who live in the same village, is not proper for one like You. So please let us go fast.**

**Комментарий:**

The implied message is that for Gokula, Kṛṣṇa is the source of bliss, not fear.

### Verse 119

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(sa-kāruṇyam iva) hanta sukumāri, suṣṭhu nirbadhnatā. duranta-śāsanena tenāṭavī-cakravartinātra ghore ghaṭṭa-karmaṇi niyukto ’smi. kim asvairī kariṣye?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-kāruṇyam—*with compassion; *iva—*as if; *hanta—*O; *sukumāri—*girl of fresh youth; *suṣṭhu—*very strong; *nirbadhnatā—*opposition; *duranta—*has a bad end; *śāsanena—*whose punishment; *tena—*by that; *aṭavī—*of the forest; *cakravartinā—*supreme ruler; *atra—*in that; *ghore—*terrible; *ghaṭṭa-karmaṇi—*work of levying the toll tax; *niyuktaḥ--*employed; *asmi—*I; *kim—*what; *asvairī—*not independent; *kariṣye—*will do*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (feigning pity): Alas, o youthful maiden, I am shackled by the cruel grove emperor in this terrible duty of levying the toll tax. What can I do? I am not independent.**

**Комментарий:**

By addressing Lalitā as *‘sukumāri*,*’* Kṛṣṇa implies that her words are as innocent as she. Kṛṣṇa can’t help, however, but faithfully proceed to fulfill His duty despite this heart-softening circumstance.

### Verse 120-121

***viśākhā:*** *kiṁ kkhu kaṁseṇa?*  
***kṛṣṇaḥ****: nahi nahi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kim—*whether; *kkhu—*indeed; *kaṁseṇa—*by Kaṁsa. *na hi—*not at all; *nahi—*not at all*.*

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā: By Kaṁsa?**

**Kṛṣṇa: Not at all, not at all.**

### Verse 122-123

***viśākhā:*** *tado keṇa?*  
***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *krīḍā-kaṭākṣa-cchaṭā-nirdhūta-kaṁsādinā mahā-manmathābhidhena.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tado—*then; *keṇa—*by whom. *krīḍā—*playful; *kaṭā-akṣa—*sidelong glances; *chaṭā—*by many; *nirdhūta—*by one who shakes; *kaṁsa-ādinā—*persons like Kaṁsa; *mahā-manmatha—*Manmatha the Great; *abhidhena—*by one whose name*.*

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā: Then by whom?**

**Kṛṣṇa: His playful sidelong glances shake Kaṁsa and others, and His name is Manmatha the Great.**

**Комментарий:**

The word *‘manmatha’* literally means “the churner of minds,” and conventionally refers to Cupid.

### Verse 124

***lalitā:*** *ammo, kahimpi ṇa suṇido esa mahā-mammaha ṇāmā cakkabaṭṭī.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ammo—*Wow!; *kahimpi ṇa—*nowhere; *suṇido—*has been heard of; *esa—*this; *mahā-mammaha—*Manmatha the Great; *ṇāmā—*by the name; *cakkabaṭṭī—*emperor.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: Wow, nowhere has anybody heard about the emperor by the name Manmatha the Great.**

### Verse 125

***madhumaṅgalaḥ:*** *(sāṭṭa-hāsam) hī hī! accariaṁ accariaṁ! mahā-mammaho bi imāhiṁ ṇa suṇido? mahā-kaḍae pamada-mañjarī ṇāma jassa rāadhāṇī. mahumuha-mahābala-vijaa-ppamuhā jassa amacca-varā. uttamā-rāmāvalī jassa vihāra-paaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa—*with; *aṭṭa—*loud; *hāsam—*a laughter; *hī hī—*Ha-ha!; *accariaṁ accariaṁ—*surprise, surprise; *mahā-mammaho—*Mahā-manmatha; *bi—*also; *imāhiṁ--*by them; *ṇa—*not; *suṇido—*heard of; *mahā—*great; *kaḍa—*in the military camp; *pada-mañjarī—*Pramada-mañjarī; *ṇāma—*by the name; *jassa—*his; *rāadhāṇī—*royal residence; *mahu-muha--*by Madhumukha; *mahā-bala—*Mahābala; *vijaa—*and Vijaya; *ppamuhā—*headed by; *jassa—*whose; *amacca—*ministers; *varā—*best; *uttamā—*best; *ārāma—*of groves; *āvalī—*the multitude; *jassa—*whose; *vihāra—*of sports; *paaṁ—*place.

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala (laughing loudly): Ha ha! What a surprise, what a surprise! They haven’t heard of Manmatha the Great also? Pramada-mañjarī is his royal residence, situated in His vast military camp. His best ministers are headed by Madhumukha, Mahābala, and Vijaya, and many beautiful groves is the place of his enjoyment.**

**Комментарий:**

Madhumaṅgala ridicules Lalitā and other gopīs as well for not knowing not only about the taxation at Govardhana hill, but also about Manmatha the Great, who is actually Kṛṣṇa Himself. Speaking outwardly about the emperor and his royal attributes and entourage, Madhumaṅgala, using synonyms, actually describes Kṛṣṇa performing His amorous pastimes on Govardhana hill with the help of His confidential friends. The inner meaning of Madhumaṅgala’s speech is as follows: “‘The Blossom of Amorous Pleasure’ (*pramada-mañjarī*) is the name of His royal residence on the vast slope (*kaṭaka*) of Govardhana hill. His best ministers are headed by Madhumaṅgala (*madhumukha*), Subala (*mahābala*), and Vijaya, and many lovely (*uttamā*) young women (*rāmā*) is the place of His enjoyment.”

The name Madhumukha refers to Madhumaṅgala, for it literally means “One, whose name starts with the word ‘*madhu.*’” Mahābala denotes Subala because the prefixes *‘mahā’* and *‘su’* have the same meaning—‘great’—when connected to the word*‘bala’* (“strength”). Vijaya denotes …

### Verse 126

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *kiṁ bahunā? santatam amī kuraṅga-bhṛṅga-kokilādayo’pi yasya nideśa-cāriṇaś cāratām aṅgī-kurvate.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kim—*what; *bahunā—*of many; *santatam—*at all times; *amī—*these; *kuraṅga—*deer; *bhṛṅga—*bumblebees; *kokila—*cuckoos; *ādayaḥ--*and others; *api—*also; *yasya—*whose; *nideśa-cāriṇaḥ--*of the order carriers; *cāratām—*the activity; *aṅgī-kurvate--*accept*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: What need is there to speak more about Him? These deer, bumblebees, cuckoos, and other forest creatures are His order carriers.**

**Комментарий:**

Creatures that Kṛṣṇa mentiones are known for inspiring amorous sentiments. They are often mentioned in the description of conjugal pastimes of the Lord.

### Verse 127

***campakalatā:*** *(sācchuritam) lalide, ado dāṇaṁ apariharanto bi vainda-ṇandaṇo tue ṇāsuidabbo jaṁ parido cora-cakkavaṭṭiṇo carā caranti.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-ācchuritam—*with a horselaugh; *lalide—*O Lalitā; *ado—*therefore; *dāṇaṁ—*the tax; *apariharanto—*not taking away; *bi—*even; *vainda-ṇandaṇo—*the son of the king of Vraja; *tue—*by you; *ṇāsuidabbo—*shouldn’t be envied; *jaṁ—*because; *parido—*all around; *cora—*of the thieves; *cakkabaṭṭiṇo—*of the emperor; *carā—*the spies; *caranti—*are walking*.*

**Перевод:**

**Campakalatā (laughing uproariously): Lalitā, the spies of the emperor of thieves wander all around. So you shouldn’t be upset with the son of Vraja’s king even when He doesn’t exact the tax.**

**Комментарий:**

Campakalatā asks Lalitā to not put the gopīs in danger by speaking anything bad about Kṛṣṇa, for the spies of His master are all around.

### Verse 128

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *lalite, nīti-jñāsi. tad atra kumbhān anavaropya nirūpayantu bhavatyaḥ śulka-kṛtyam.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*lalite—*O Lalitā; *nīti—*of the justice; *jñā—*the knower; *asi—*you are; *tat—*therefore; *atra—*here; *kumbhān—*the pots; *anavaropya—*not putting down; *nirūpayantu—*please decide; *bhavatyaḥ--*your; *śulka-kṛtyam—*what should be submitted as the tax.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O Lalitā, you know what is right and wrong, and therefore, without putting the pots down, please decide right here what will you gopīs give me as the tax.**

**Комментарий:**

“Campakalatā and others don’t really know what they are talking, for they don’t know what is right and wrong. They can’t reply to Me properly. You, however, know what exactly to do in each situation.”

### Verse 129

***viśākhā:*** *mohaṇa, ghaṭṭa-pāṅgaṇe kulaṅgaṇāṇaṁ tilaṁ bi bilambaṇaṁ biḍambaṇaṁ ccea!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mohaṇa—*O bewilderer; *ghaṭṭa-pāṅgaṇe—*on the toll station; *kulaṅgaṇāṇaṁ—*the respectable ladies; *tilaṁ—*slightly; *bi—*even; *bilambaṇaṁ—*holding back; *biḍambaṇaṁ—*abuse; *ccea—*indeed.

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā: Tricky boy, holding back respectable ladies at the toll station even for a moment is indeed an abuse!**

### Verse 130

***citrā:*** *(sa-dākṣiṇyam) goulāṇanda, tattaṁ suṇāhi: dijjantahmika aḍḍiā-mette bi ghaṭṭa-dāṇe jaṇṇia-ghiaṁ asuddhaṁ hodi tti suṇijjai. ṇa uṇa ahmāṇaṁ pañca-tāmmiā-dāṇe kādaradā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-dākṣiṇyam—*with kindness; *goulāṇanda—*O bliss of Gokula; *tattaṁ—*the truth; *suṇāhi—*hear; *dijjantahmi—*when given; *kaaḍḍiā—*a cowrie shell; *mette—*only; *bi—*even; *ghaṭṭa-dāṇe—*at the toll station; *jaṇṇia—*sacrificial; *ghiaṁ—*ghee; *asuddhaṁ—*impure; *hodi—*becomes; *tti—*thus; *suṇijjai—*heard; *ṇa—*not; *uṇa—*again; *ahmāṇaṁ—*our; *pañca-tāmmiā—*five *tāmrikās*; *dāṇe—*in giving; *kādaradā--*stinginess*.*

**Перевод:**

**Citrā (with kindness): O bliss of Gokula, please hear the truth: It is not that we are reluctant to give You even five tāmrikās, but we have heard that even if only one cowrie is paid at a toll station, one’s sacrificial ghee becomes impure.**

**Комментарий:**

Here and further in the text we are introduced to some extent to the monetary and weight values of ancient Vedic times. Cowrie is a small, polished and brightly colored shell used as money. Twenty cowries equal one *tāmrikā*, a copper coin of a particular weight. Five *tāmrikās* thus equal one hundred cowries.

“We could pay You much more, but we are bound by the regulation of this ritual.” This is what Citrā means to say.

### Verse 131

***lalitā:*** *halā rāhe, imiṇā mahā-bhāreṇa kiliṭṭāsi. tā ettha ghaṭṭiaṁ udārehi ghaṭṭiaṁ. (iti sarvā ghaṭṭikāvatāraṇaṁ nāṭayanti)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*halā—*O my friend; *rāhe—*Rādhā; *imiṇā—*due to this; *mahā-bhāreṇa—*very heavy weight; *kiliṭṭa—*troubled; *asi—*You are; *tā—*because of that; *ettha—*here; *ghaṭṭiaṁ—*for half an hour; *udārehi—*put down; *ghaṭṭiaṁ—*the pot; *iti—*thus; *sarvā—*all \[gopīs\]; *ghaṭṭika—*the pots; *avatāraṇam—*putting down; *nāṭayanti—*act out.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: He Rādhā, You suffer due to the heavy weight You carry. Please put down Your pot here for just half an hour.**

**(All the gopīs put down their pots.)**

**Комментарий:**

*Ghaṭikā* is a measure of time that equals twenty four minutes. As Viśvanātha Cakravartī explains, Lalitā thinks that it would be better if on the pretext of being tired Rādhā keeps Her pot down Herself, rather than forced to do so by Kṛṣṇa and His gang.

### Verse 132

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *sakhe subala, ghaṭṭasyādya prathamātithir asau sa-suhṛj-janā lalitā. tad asyāḥ kāmam apūrva-mādhuryeṇa phaṇivallī-parṇa-vīṭikā-pañcakena mānanam aupayikam.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakhe—*O friend; *subala—*Subala; *ghaṭṭasya—*of the toll station; *adya—*today; *prathama—*first; *atithiḥ--*guest; *asau—*that; *sa-suhṛd-janā—*with Her friends; *lalitā—*Lalitā; *tat—*therefore; *asyāḥ--*her; *kāmam—*desire; *apūrva—*unprecedented; *mādhuryeṇa—*with sweetness; *phaṇivallī—*of betel; *parṇa—*leaves; *vīṭikā—*mixture of areca nuts and spices; *pañcakena—*with five; *mānanam—*honoring; *aupayikam—*appropriate*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: My friend Subala, today the first guest at our toll station is Lalitā with her close friends. It would be appropriate if we honor her in an exceptionally sweet manner with five betel leaves filled with spices.**

**Комментарий:**

By asking Rādhā and other gopīs to put down their pots for at least half an hour, Lalitā actually turns the “toll gate show” in Kṛṣṇa’s favor—He has more chances to fulfill His purpose when the gopīs are at ease. Kṛṣṇa is very happy with Lalitā’s action and wants to reward her and other four gopīs with pān.

In India and other South-east asian countries, leaves of the betel plant (*Piper betle*) and nuts of areca palm (*Areca catechu*) are widely in use up to this day. Plain or mixed with lime paste and some spices, areca nuts are wrapped in betel leaves, and the combination of both is called pān. It is a custom to offer pān to respectable guests as a symbol of welcome and honor. Pān is a breath freshener, soft stimulant, and a digestive as well.

### Verse 133-134

***subalaḥ:*** *(ratna-sampuṭakam udghāṭya) lalide, geṇaha pañca-viḍiāo. (ity agre vinyasyati)*  
***viśākhā:*** *suala, alaṁ tambolena jaṁ kadhidaṁ ccea vvadiṇīo ahmeti.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ratna—*jeweled; *sampuṭakam—*case; *udghāṭya—*opening; *lalide—*O Lalitā; *geṇaha—*accept; *pañca—*five; *biḍiāo—*betel leaves; *iti—*thus; *agre—*in front; *vinyasyati—*places. *suala—*O Subala; *alaṁ—*enough; *tambolena—*of betel; *jaṁ—*because; *kadhidaṁ—*it has been said; *ccea—*indeed; *vvadiṇīo—*ladies who have undertaken a vow; *ahm—*we; *eti—*thus*.*

**Перевод:**

**Subala (opening a jeweled case): Lalitā, please accept these five betel leaves with spices.**

**(places the case in front of Lalitā)**

**Viśākhā: Subala, no need of pān, for we told you that we are observing a vow.**

### Verse 135

***lalitā:*** *suala, kiṁ me muhaṁ pekkhasi? avisāsiṇī visāhā maha kuppantī edaṁ bhaṇādi, ‘ghaṭṭ-ālehiṁ ṭagga-vaḍiā paujjīadi tti pasiddhī suṇīadi.’ tā ala imāṇaṁ bhuaṅgaladā-pallaveṇa.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*suala—*O Subala; *kim—*whether; *me—*my; *muhaṁ—*face; *pekkhasi—*you see; *avisāsiṇī—*faithless; *visāhā—*Viśākhā; *maha—*at me; *kuppantī—*angry; *edam—*this; *bhaṇādi—*speaks; *ghaṭṭa-ālehiṁ—*by the toll gate officers; *ṭagga-vaḍiā—*a kind of inebriating herbal pills; *paujjīadi—*used; *tti—*thus; *pasiddhī—*rumors; *suṇīadi—*heard; *tā—*therefore; *ala—*enough; *imāṇaṁ—*for them; *bhuaṅga-ladā—*of betel; *pallaveṇa—*leaves.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: Subala, why do you stare in my face? Suspicious Viśākhā has just told me angrily that there are rumors about toll gate officers passing out intoxicating pills. So forget about the pān for us.**

**Комментарий:**

Lalitā feels that Subala expects her to give permission to the gopīs to accept the pān. That is why he is looking her in the face. Viśākhā is supposed to be angry at Lalitā for getting her and other gopīs into trouble by bringing them to this “toll station.”

*Ṭhaga-vaṭikā* is a kind of pill made of a certain herb that bewilders one’s mind and incapacitates one’s body. Viśākhā supposedly told Lalitā that according to rumors, some cunning toll gate officers feed travelers such pills in order to rob them of all their belongings.

### Verse 136

***madhumaṅgalaḥ:*** *lalide, bimboṣṭhīṇaṁ alaṁ vo tamboleṇa. tā kallāṇī hohi jaṁ uvvaridaṁ vīṭiā-pañcaaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*lalide—*O Lalitā; *bimba-uṣṭhīṇaṁ—*with bimba colored lips; *alaṁ—*enough; *vo—*for you; *tamboleṇa—*of pān; *tā—*therefore; *kallāṇī—*fortunate; *hohi—*be; *jaṁ—*because; *uvvaridaṁ—*are in excess; *bīṭiā—*betel leaves with spices; *pañcaaṁ—*five.

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala: O Lalitā, there is no need of pān for you girls, whose lips are the color of bimba. Be blessed—you have saved us five betel leaves.**

**Комментарий:**

Madhumaṅgala is happy that Lalitā refused to accept pān—it will not get wasted, for he is here to consume it. According to Viśvanātha Cakravartī, this is the reasoning of funny Madhumaṅgala: “After all, the gopīs’ lips are bright red like *bimba* fruit, which means that they chew pān quite often.”

### Verse 137

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(tāmbūlam upabhujya carvitaṁ ditsan sabhrū-bhramam) rādhe, dvija-saṁskṛtam idaṁ tāmbūlāmṛtam āsvādya nirātaṅkā nikāmam anāsvādita-carīr gṛhāṇa sampuṭatas tāmbūla-vīṭikāḥ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tāmbūlam—*pān; *upabhujya—*after enjoying; *carvitam—*the chewed mass; *ditsan—*wishing to give; *sa-bhrū-bhramam—*with a movement of eyebrows; *rādhe—*O Rādhā; *dvija—*by My teeth; *saṁskṛtam—*enhanced; *idam—*this; *tāmbūla—*pān; *amṛtam—*nectarean; *āsvādya—*tasting; *nirātaṅkā—*fearless; *nikāmam—*as desired; *anāsvādita-carīḥ--*previously not tasted; *gṛhāṇa—*please accept; *sampuṭataḥ--*from the case; *tāmbūla—*pān; *vīṭikāḥ--*with a spice mixture.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (After chewing pān, He wishes to give His remnants to Rādhā and speaks, moving His eyebrows): O Rādhā, don’t be afraid. First relish this nectarean pān enhanced by My teeth. After that, without any worry You can take the pān from this case as much as You like. You have never tasted a pān like this before.**

**Комментарий:**

To pacify fearful gopīs, Kṛṣṇa chewes in front of them the pān that He offered them. *‘Dvija-saṁskṛta’* could alternatively mean ‘sanctified by twice-born *brāhmaṇas*.’ Kṛṣṇa implies that His teeth are as pure as *brāhmaṇas.*

### Verse 138

***lalitā:*** *imassa kāmuī-lakkhobabhoeṇa suṭṭhu pābaṇassa muha-bimbassa jaī uggāraṁ ṇa geṇahissadi me sahī tado kadhaṁ appāṇaṁ puṇissadi?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*imassa—*His; *kāmuī—*lovers; *lakkha—*a hundred thousand; *ubabhoeṇa—*due to enjoying; *suṭṭhu—*well; *pābaṇassa—*purifying; *muha-bimbassa—*of bimba-colored mouth; *jaī—*if; *uggāraṁ—*discharge; *ṇa geṇahissadi—*do not accept; *me—*my; *sahī—*friend; *tado—*then; *kadhaṁ—*how; *appāṇaṁ—*Her own self; *puṇissadi—*will purify.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: If my sakhī will not accept the remnants from His bimba-colored mouth, which is thoroughly purifying for it has enjoyed countless lusty girls, how will she purify Her own self?**

**Комментарий:**

This is Lalitā’s sarcastic remark in response to Kṛṣṇa’s calling His teeth *brāhmaṇas.*

### Verse 139

***subalaḥ:*** *lalide, vibarīda-āriṇo amhe diṭṭhiā tumhehiṁ ccea ubadiṭṭhāo. tā dāṇiṁ dāṇaṁ ccea aṇumāṇṇijja{u}.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*lalide—*O Lalitā; *vibarīda—*contrary; *āriṇo—*who act; *amhe—*we; *diṭṭhiā—*fortunately; *tumhehiṁ—*by you; *ccea—*only; *ubadiṭṭhāo—*are instructed; *tā—*therefore; *dāṇiṁ—*now; *dāṇam—*the payment; *ccea—*only; *aṇumāṇṇijja{u}—*let be considered.

**Перевод:**

**Subala: O Lalitā, we acted wrongly, but luckily you are instructing us. So now just let us settle the tax.**

**Комментарий:**

As Viśvanātha Cakravartī says, Subala admits that it was wrong from the side of the toll station party to offer the gopīs pān. He means to say that the gopīs did not deserve such honor from officials. Tax officers should only execute their duty and not waste time in unnecessary pleasantries.

### Verse 140-141

***campakalatā:*** *kiṁ tumhe bamhaṇāo jaṁ vo amhehiṁ dāṇaṁ kira uṇumantavvam?*  
***madhumaṅgalaḥ:*** *bhodi campaalade, eso kulīṇo aṇūāṇo bamhaṇo mhi. tā uara-puraṁ dijja{u} sa-macchaṇḍiaṁ heaṅgavīṇaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kiṁ—*are*; tumhe—*you; *bamhaṇāo—brāhmaṇas*; *jaṁ—*that; *vo—*to you; *amhehiṁ—*by us; *dāṇaṁ—*payment; *kira—*indeed; *uṇumantabbaṁ—*should be considered*. bhodi—*O respected; *campaalade—*Campakalatā; *eso—*this; *kulīṇo—*of a respected lineage; *aṇūāṇo—*well-versed in the Vedic literature; *bamhaṇo—*a *brāhmaṇa; mhi—*I am*; tā—*therefore; *uara-puraṁ—*so that the stomach would be full; *dijja{u}—*should be given; *sa-macchaṇḍiaṁ—*with sugar-candies; *heaṅgavīṇaṁ—*ghee*.*

**Перевод:**

**Campakalatā: Are you brāhmaṇas, that we have to consider giving you something?**

**Madhumaṅala: O respected Campakalatā, I am a brāhmaṇa from a reputed family, and am well-versed in the Vedic knowledge. Therefore, you should give me ghee mixed with with sugar candies to fill my belly!**

### Verse 142

***viśākhā:*** *sahi campaalade, ghaṭṭī-cchaleṇa ede uarambhariṇo bhikkhanti. tā dijja{u} ekkā kāgiṇīṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sahi—*O friend; *campaalade—*Campakalatā; *ghaṭṭī—*of a toll gate; *chaleṇa—*on the pretext; *ede—*these; *uaram-bhariṇo—*belly-fillers; *bhikkhanti—*begging; *tā—*therefore; *dijja{u}—*let be given; *ekkā—*one; *kāgiṇīṁ—kākinī* (a monetary unit)

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā: My friend Campakalatā, these gluttons are begging by faking a toll station. Let us give them one kākinī.**

**Комментарий:**

One *kākinī* equals to twenty cowrie shells, which in ancient times, a was used as one of the smallest coins.

*Mahaṭī-ṭīkā* suggests that Viśākhā patronizes the boys with her “compassionate” speech, as if saying the following: “Let these fellows buy and chew some chick peas as they go on herding their cows.”

### Verse 143

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *sakhe paramādyūna, mahā-dāninām asmākaṁ mahā-pātram asi, tad adya haiyaṅgavīnena kukṣi-pūrtāv api tava daridratā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakhe—*O friend; *parama—*topmost; *ādyūna—*glutton; *mahā-dāninām—*great tax collectors; *asmākam—*of us; *mahā-pātram—*the great recepient; *asi—*you are; *tat—*therefore; *adya—*today; *haiyaṅgavīnena—*with ghee; *kukṣi—*your belly; *pūrtau—*when filled; *api—*even; *tava—*your; *daridratā—*poverty.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O My extremely voracious friend, we are munuficient donors, and you are our prime recipient. Still you are so poor that today you can’t even fill your belly with ghee.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa reproaches Madhumaṅgala for his petty desire to get just some ghee with sugar. Madhumaṅgala is unsuccessful in his independent endeavor to get even that little. On the other hand, as Kṛṣṇa implies, if Madhumaṅgala sticks to the “toll gate” squad, he will certainly be rewarded in excess. The phrase “we are munuficient donors, and you are our prime recipient” alternatively means “we are great tax collectors (*mahā-dānīnām*), and you are our prime minister (*mahā-pātram*).”

### Verse 144

***rādhā:***  *halā lalide, ede mahā-dāṇiṇo tti attāṇaṁ salāhanti. tā phuḍaṁ paramuttama-vaṇṇāṇaṁ vo savvuttamaṁ paatthaṁ dāīssanti.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*halā—*O friend; *lalide—*Lalitā; *ede—*these \[persons\] *mahā-dāṇiṇo—*great donors *tti—*thus; *attāṇaṁ—*themselves; *salāhanti—*praise; *tā—*therefore; *phuḍaṁ—*clearly; *param—*most; *uttama—*exalted; *vaṇṇāṇaṁ—*social division; *vo—*to you; *savva-uttamaṁ—*the topmost; *paatthaṁ—*thing; *dāīssanti—*will give.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: Lalitā, if they praise themselves by saying “we are munificent donors”, they will definitely give the topmost thing to you gopīs, who are of the highest caste.**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā is playing on the word *‘varṇa,’* which can also mean “body.” In this way, *‘paramuttama-vaṇṇāṇam’* also means “to you gopīs, who are most beautiful.”

### Verse 145

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(smitvā) vara-varṇini, satyaṁ bravīṣi tad etan nija-mahā-vaibhavaṁ dāsyāmi. (ity āliṅgana-mudrām abhinīya subalam āliṅgati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smitvā—*smiling; *vara—*the best; *varṇini—*whose body; *satyam—*the truth; *bravīṣi—*you speak; *tat—*that; *etat—*this; *nija—*own; *mahā—*great; *vaibhavam—*opulence; *dāsyāmi—*I will give; *iti—*thus; *āliṅgana—*of embracing; *mudrām—*the gesture; *abhinīya—*performing; *subalam—*Subala; *āliṅgate—*He embraces*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (smiling): O most lovely one, you speak the truth that I will give My greatest opulence.**

**(embraces Subala by enacting the suitable movement)**

**Комментарий:**

Viśvanātha Cakravartī says that Kṛṣṇa smiles because He could understand Rādhārāṇī’s message, which reveales Her excessive longing for Him. His addressing Rādhārāṇī as *‘vara-varṇini’* is ironic, for it also means “the foremost *brahmacāriṇī*.”

By embracing Subala, Kṛṣṇa implies that His embrace is His greatest wealth.

### Verse 146-147

***rādhā:*** *(sa-romāñcam ātma-gataṁ saṁskṛtena)*  
*api guru-puras tvām utsaṅge nidhāya visaṅkaṭe*  
*vipula-pulakollāsaṁ svairī pariṣvajate hariḥ*  
*praṇayati tava skandhe cāsau bhujaṁ bhujagopamaṁ*  
*kva subala purā siddha-kṣetre cakartha kiyat tapaḥ*  
*(ity amarṣam ivābhinayantī bhruvau vibhujya prakāśam) lalide, diṭṭhā paivvadāsu mādisīsu vidūsaadā tuha ṇiuñja-rāassa!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-roma-añcam—*with Her hairs standing on end; *ātma-gatam—*to Herself; *saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *api—*is it (a question mark); *guru—*of elders; *puraḥ--*in front; *tvām—*you; *utsaṅge—*on His lap; *nidhāya—*holding; *visaṅkaṭe—*broad; *vipula—*abundant; *pulaka—*with your hairs on end; *ullāsam—*appearing; *svairī—*independent; *pariṣvajate—*embraces; *hariḥ--*Hari; *praṇayati—*is ushering; *tava—*your; *skandhe—*on shoulder; *ca—*also; *asau—*He; *bhujam—*His hand; *bhujaga—*a snake; *upamam—*resembling; *kva—*in which; *subala—*O Subala; *purā—*in the past; *siddha—*holy; *kṣetre—*land; *cakartha—*you performed; *kiyat—*how much; *tapaḥ--*austerity; *iti—*thus; *amarṣam—*impatience; *iva—*as if; *abhinayantī—*acting out; *bhruvau—*eyebrows; *vibhujya—*curving; *prakāśam—*audibly; *lalide—*O Lalitā; *diṭṭhā—*is seen; *pai-vvadāsu—*of the chaste ladies; *mādisīsu—*like us; *vidūsaadā—*mocking behavior; *tuha—*your; *ṇiuñja-rāassa—*grove king.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (Her hairs standing on end, speaks to Herself in Sanskrit): Is it that even in front of superiors, Hari, sitting you on His broad lap, freely embraces you and ushers His snakelike hand on your shoulder? At that time your hairs stand on end out of joy. O Subala, how many austerities you have executed and in which holy place you have performed them?**

**(After saying this, slightly impatient Rādhā, curves Her eyebrows and speaks audibly): Lalitā, just see how your king of forest groves mocks chaste ladies like us!**

**Комментарий:**

In the opinion of Viśvanātha Cakavartī, Rādhā says the Sanskrit verse with this in mind: “Since there is simply no one as fortunate as you, anybody would find it difficult to ascertain the type of penance you performed, the extant of that penance, and the holy place where you performed it. On the other hand, there are many like Me, whose piety is meager.” Seeing Kṛṣṇa so freely embracing Subala, Rādhā feels desperate about receiving Kṛṣṇa’s embrace and can’t help but think of how He does so even in front of superiors like Balarāma and Nanda Mahārāja.

Next, Rādhā reproaches Lalitā, for She feels that it is Lalitā who put the gopīs into trouble by forcibly bringing them to this place. Since Rādhā will reproach Lalitā for the same reason two more times (texts 163 & 448), it seems that it was Lalitā who had inspired the gopīs to participate in the sacrifice at Govinda-kuṇḍa.

This verse is quoted in *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* (3.3.99) in relation to *romañca*, or hairs standing on end, which is one of the *sāttvika-bhāvas* (involuntary ecstatic symptoms).

### Verse 148

***lalitā:*** *(sāpadeśaṁ saṁskṛtena)*  
*nava-mukulitāṁ dṛṣṭvābhyarṇe rasāla-latām itaḥ*  
*katham iva mudhā dhṛṣṭaḥ kūṭaṁ bhajann abhidhāvasi*  
*parimalavatī snigdhā cāsau dvirepha-patiṁ śritā*  
*parihara kuhūkaṇṭhotkaṇṭhām iyaṁ sulabhā na te*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-apadeśam—*with pretense; *saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *nava-mukulitām—*with new buds; *dṛṣṭvā—*seeing; *abhyarṇe—*in proximity; *rasāla-latām—*a mango creeper; *itaḥ--*from here; *katham—*how; *iva—*as if; *mudhā—*in vain; *dhṛṣṭaḥ--*audacious; *kūṭam—*to deceit; *bhajan—*resorting; *abhidhāvasi—*you are intent (upon a mango creeper); *parimalavatī—*fragrant; *snigdhā—*tender; *ca—*and; *asau—*she; *dvirepha-patim—*of the bumblebee husband; *śritā—*took shelter; *parihara—*give up; *kuhūkaṇṭha—*O cuckoo; *utkaṇṭhām—*eagerness; *iyam—*this; *sulabhā—*easily obtainable; *na—*not; *te—*for you.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (says figuratively in Sanskrit): Having seen nearby a mango twig full of new buds, how dare You, o audacious One, to be after it, vainly resorting to pretense? This fragrant and tender twig has taken shelter of its husband the bumblebee. O sweet-voiced male cuckoo, give up your eagerness for this twig! For You it is not easily obtainable.**

**Комментарий:**

Lalitā accepts Rādhā’s reproach and now tries to correct her “mistake” by challenging Kṛṣṇa in a figurative way: “O male cuckoo Kṛṣṇa, by staging this toll gate show, how dare you desire to enjoy this mango twig Rādhā?” The comparison drawn in the verse is natural, for cuckoos are well known as the enjoyers of new mango buds in spring season.

*Mahaṭī-ṭīkā* provides the following insight into the meaning of this verse. *‘Dvirepha,’* apart from denoting a bumblebee, can also mean “barbarian.” In that case, Lalitā’s mentioning a husband barbarian is a complaint about Rādhārāṇī: “Although She is endowed with all virtues, Her husband is a barbarian! She is even devoted to him!” Finally, Lalitā gives a warning to Kṛṣṇa: “For You She is not easily obtainable, for You can even lose your head by trying to get Her.”

This is an instance of Lalitā’s characterisctic disposition – she is daring and fearless in front of Kṛṣṇa, outwardly creating a hassle to “protect” Rādhā from His hands. In this way, Lalitā is an epitome of *vāma-sakhī*, an unsubmissive and imperious gopī girl. Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī describes her nature further and prays to her as follows:

*sāndra-prema-rasaiḥ plutā priyatayā prāgalbhyam āptā tayoḥ*  
*prāṇa-preṣṭha-vayasyayor anudinaṁ līlābhisāraṁ kramaiḥ*  
*vaidagdhyena tathā sakhīṁ prati sadā mānasya śikṣāṁ rasair*  
*yeyaṁ kārayatīha hanta lalitā gṛhṇātu sā māṁ gaṇaiḥ*

**“Flooded with the moods of intense** ***prema*****, Lalitā Devī has developed an affectionately arrogant disposition. Step by step she arranges for the daily rendezvous of her two friends, who are dearer to her than her own life. She expertly instructs her sakhī (Rādhā) at all times in the art of jealous anger. May that Lalitā Devī, with Her associates, accept me.”**

On the other hand, the *ācārya* also points out that by saying that the mango twig Rādhā is protected by Her husband, the bumblebee, Lalitā means just the opposite: “It is Her trick (*kūṭaṁ bhajan*) that She has taken shelter of that dull barbarian (*dvirepha*). The mango twig Rādhā is meant to be enjoyed by You, and not some wretched bumblebee. If She is totally subdued just by hearing Your sweet voice even from afar, what then is happening to Her when She sees You directly? .”

Surendranath Shastri confirms the same idea by showing how some words in the verse indicate that Lalitā is not only challenging Kṛṣṇa, but indirectly encouraging Him as well: “This mango twig (slim-bodied Rādhā) is full of new buds (*nava-mukulitām*), i.e. Her face lits up with joy, only when You are nearby. Why then You, so arrogant, are persecuting Her by making up this toll gate show? She is fragrant (*parimalavatī*), i.e. perfect for Your enjoyment, and tender (*snigdhā*), i.e. in love for You. Unfortunately, She has been given over to Her bumblebee husband. On the other hand, You are a cuckoo, and are capable of bringing Her under Your dominion by Your sweet voice. Is She not, therefore, easily obtainable for You? (*‘Iyaṁ sulabhā na te’* is a rhetorical question that natually demands the answer “Yes, She is.”) Now, when You know the means, You should give up wondering how to obtain Her.”

Kṛṣṇa’s loving relationship with the gopīs of Vraja exists on the ground of *parakīya-bhava*, or the mood of conjugal affairs beyond marital ties. For that to take place, the gopīs, who are in fact Kṛṣṇa’s eternal consorts, have to be convinced that they are married to somebody else. Rādhārāṇī’s so-called husband is Abhimanyu. Abhimanyu, however, is different from the husbands of the gopīs other than Rādhā in that he is envious and jealous of Kṛṣṇa, whereas they are not. For this reason it is concluded that Abhimanyu is a creation of *yogamāyā.* It is also explained that so-called husbands of the gopīs in Vraja Gokula (the manifestation of Goloka Vṛndāvana on the earth) are incarnations of their *parakīya-abhimāna*, or the intrinsic self-conception that they belong as wives to somebody else.

For further discussion on this topic please refer to the commentary on text 350.

### Verse 149

***viśākhā:*** *(saṁskṛtena)*  
*ṛju-vṛttiḥ kila kuṭile*  
*vaiguṇyāyaiva kalpate tvaritam*  
*iti darśayan dhanuḥ-stho*  
*guṇa-cyutiṁ sphuṭam iṣur yāti*

**Пословный перевод:**

*saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *ṛju—*honest; *vṛttiḥ--*dealings; *kila—*indeed; *kuṭile—*in \[the association of\] the crooked; *vaiguṇyāya—*into faultiness; *eva—*only; *kalpate—*transform; *tvaritam—*quickly; *iti—*thus; *darśayan—*showing; *dhanuḥ--*on the bow; *sthaḥ--*situated; *guṇa—*from the string; *cyutim—*to the abandonment; *sphuṭam—*clearly; *iṣuḥ--*the arrow; *yāti—*proceeds.

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā (in Sanskrit): An arrow raised on a bow immidiately leaves the string. This shows that an honest person spoils quickly by mixing with the crooked.**

**Комментарий:**

In the previous verse, Lalitā has stated that Kṛṣṇa has turned to deception (*kūṭam bhajan*) by pretending to be a tax collector. Supporting Lalitā’s point, Viśākhā hints in the present verse that the gopīs must leave Kṛṣṇa’s association immidiately, following the example of an arrow. Arrow’s shape is straight, but the shape of both bow and string is curved. Similarly, the gopīs are straightforward and honest, but Kṛṣṇa and his friends are crooked and deceitful. The compound *‘guṇa-cyuti’* (“getting off the string”) could also mean “falldown (*cyuti*) from virtue (*guṇa*).” Viśākhā warns her companions that if they stay any longer with Kṛṣṇa and His friends, they will lose their virtues. This is the explanation of Viśvanātha Cakravartī.

### Verse 150

***citrā:***  *hanta ghaṭṭajjhakkhā, ghaṭṭāhiāro ja{i} tumhāṇaṁ ahiṭṭho tado bahu-jaṇa-saṁghaṭṭe jauṁṇ-āghaṭṭe ccea cattaraṁ juttaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*hanta—*alas; *ghaṭṭa—*of the toll gate; *ajjhakkhā—*O superintendent; *ghaṭṭa—*over the toll gate; *ahiāro—*the charge; *ja{i}—*if; *tumhāṇaṁ—*by you; *ahiṭṭho—*desired; *tado—*then; *bahu—*of many; *jaṇa—*people; *saṁghaṭṭe—*where there is hustle; *jauṁṇā—*on Yamunā river; *ghaṭṭe—*at the bathing place; *ccea—*indeed; *cattaraṁ—*the crossing; *juttaṁ—*appropriate.

**Перевод:**

**Citrā: Alas, o respected superintendent of the toll station. If you wish to be in charge of a toll station, then the crossing at the crowded bathing place on the Yamunā would be the better spot.**

**Комментарий:**

In other words, “This solitary forest path is not a very good location for your toll gate.”

### Verse 151

***campakalatā:*** *a{i} visuddha-citte sahi citte, ede sulukka-lakkheṇa luṇṭhiduṁ jjebba ettha dugga-vaṇe ciṭṭhanti. tā viramehi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*a{i}—*O; *visuddha—*pure; *citte—*whose heart; *sahi—*friend; *citte—*Citrā; *ede—*these \[people\]; *sulukka—*of a payment; *lakkheṇa—*on the pretext; *luṇṭhiduṁ—*to plunder; *jjebba—*only; *ettha—*in this; *dugga—*impassable; *vaṇe—*forest; *ciṭṭhanti—*stay; *tā—*therefore; *viramehi—*give up.

**Перевод:**

**Campakalatā: O my pure-hearted friend Citrā, these fellows stay in this dense forest just to plunder on the pretext of a tax. Stop talking to them.**

### Verse 152

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *sakhe subala, mitrāyitaṁ citrayā. tad adya goṣṭhasya gopure ghaṭṭa-catvaram upaṣkriyatām, yad atra vana-madhye palāyante paritaś capalāyata-locanāḥ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakhe—*O friend; *subala—*Subala; *mitrāyitam—*we should act as friends; *citrayā—*with Citrā; *tat—*therefore; *adya—*today; *goṣṭhasya—*of the cowherd village; *gopure—*at the gate; *ghaṭṭa—*with a toll gate; *catvaram—*an intersection; *upaṣkriyatām—*must be arranged; *yat—*because; *atra—*here; *vana—*of the forest; *madhye—*in the middle; *palāyante—*are escaping; *paritaḥ--*all around; *capalāyata-locanāḥ--*\[girls\] with unsteady eyes.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: My friend Subala, Citrā has been friendly to us. So today let us arrange for a toll-station intersection at the gate of the village, since here, in the middle of the forest, these girls with unsteady eyes are escaping in all directions.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa appreciates Citrā’s advice and thereby takes her as His well-wisher.

### Verse 153

***subalaḥ:*** *pia-vaassa, saccaṁ bhaṇāsi. pekkha sahīṇaṁ sahassaṁ rāhiaṁ aṇusappadi. eṇhiṁ kkhu cauṭṭhaaṁ jjebba.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*pia—*dear; *vaassa—*friend; *saccaṁ—*the truth; *bhaṇāsi—*you speak; *pekkha—*look; *sahīṇaṁ—*of girlfriends; *sahassaṁ—*one thousand; *rāhiaṁ—*Rādhikā; *aṇusappadi—*follow; *eṇhiṁ—*now; *kkhu—*indeed; *cauṭṭhaaṁ—*four; *jjebba—*only.

**Перевод:**

**Subala: Dear friend, you speak the truth. Look, usually a thousand sakhīs follow Rādhikā, but now there are only four.**

**Комментарий:**

Subala backs up Kṛṣṇa’s statement about the escaping girls with a logical proof: “How is it possible that Rādhā is accompanied just by four girls? Where is another thousand?”

### Verse 154

***rādhā:***  *(sva-gatam) pahāde ccea kundaladāe saddhaṁ jaṇṇe pesidaṁ me sahī-ulaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sva-gatam—*to Herself; *pahāde—*early in the morning; *ccea—*only; *kundaladāe—*Kundalatā; *saddhaṁ—*with; *jaṇṇe—*to the sacrifice; *pesidaṁ—*was sent; *me—*by Me; *sahī—*of girlfriends; *ulaṁ—*the group*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (to Herself): Just at dawn I have sent a group of sakhīs with Kundalatā to the sacrifice.**

**Комментарий:**

According to Viśvanātha Cakravartī’s insight, Rādhā hopes that the sakhīs She sent have by this time delivered some ghee to the sacrificial arena, and the sacrifice has already started. In this way She could spend some more time with Kṛṣṇa here without any worry.

### Verse 155

***madhumaṅgalaḥ:*** *suala, ṇiccidaṁ āadāo viddhi sahio. ṇahi vasanta-lacchīe odāre saṁvutte kala-kaṇṭhiṇaṁ aṇubalambho sambhāvijja{i}.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*suala—*O Subala; *ṇiccidaṁ—*definitely; *āadāo—*are coming; *viddhi—*do know; *sahio—*the girlfriends; *ṇa—*not; *hi—*certainly; *basanta—*of the spring; *lacchīe—*by the beauty; *odāre—*when the descent; *saṁvutte—*comes about; *kala-kaṇṭhiṇaṁ--*of the cuckoos; *aṇubalambho—*the non-perception; *sambhāvijja{i}—*is possible.

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala: Subala, know it for sure, the sakhīs are on the way. When the beauty of spring descends, the sweet-voiced cuckoos can’t be out of sight.**

**Комментарий:**

“The beauty of spring” is Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, and “the sweet-voiced cuckoos” are Her female companions. Cuckoos are very active in spring, and so, the two are inseparable. Madhumaṅgala tells Subala that Rādhā’s companions cannot remain away from Her for too long. They must be coming soon.

### Verse 156

***viśākhā:*** *(sa-smitam) tado tāṇaṁ sahīṇaṁ calaṇa-lacchīhiṁ tumhāṇaṁ ghaṭṭo asoattaṇaṁ laddhūṇa upphullissadi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-smitam—*with a smile; *tado—*then; *tāṇaṁ—*of those; *sahīṇaṁ—*girls; *calaṇa—*on the feet; *lacchīhiṁ—*due to the marks; *tumhāṇaṁ—*your; *ghaṭṭo—*toll station; *asoattaṇaṁ—*the state of an aśoka tree; *laddhūṇa—*attaining; *upphullissadi—*will blossom*.*

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā (smiling): When the sakhīs will have reached, your toll station will turn into blooming aśoka tree because of the auspicious signs on their feet.**

**Комментарий:**

As it was mentioned in the commentary to text 92, it is the opinion of the poets that *aśoka* tree starts blossoming when stroke by the foot of a young beautiful girl. *Mahaṭī-ṭīkā* says that by quoting this conventional belief, Viśākhā makes a sarcastic joke disguised as a good wish: “This “toll station,” your very means of sustenance, will be free from all sorrow (*aśokatvam labdhvā*) when the sakhīs will kick it with their feet. It will then become supremely fortunate. You shall realize that this will be its perfection.” In other words, it’s a threat and challenge from the side of the proud gopīs to Kṛṣṇa and his party.

### Verse 157

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(savyato vilokya sānandam) kathaṁ kāñcana-marīci-rociṣāṁ sahacarīṇāṁ sañcayena brahma-kuṇḍasya puro bhūmir alaṅkriyate?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*savyataḥ--*to the left; *vilokya—*glancing; *sa-ānandam—*with joy; *katham—*how; *kāñcana—*golden; *marīci—*of the rays; *rociṣām—*whose effulgence; *sahacarīṇām—*of female attendants; *sañcayena—*with a multitude; *brahma-kuṇḍasya—*of Brahma-kuṇḍa; *puraḥ--*in front; *bhūmiḥ--*the ground; *alaṅkriyate—*is decorated*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (glancing to the left, says with joy): Is it that the shore of Brahma-kuṇḍa is adorned with Rādhā’s companions, all shining with golden effulgence?**

**Комментарий:**

Hearing that so many other gopīs are supposed to be nearby, Kṛṣṇa wished to have fun by halting them at His toll station as well. He became obsessed with that idea so much that He even imagined all these gopīs coming at a distance. This is the insight of Viśvanātha Cakravartī.

### Verse 158

***lalitā:***  *(smitvā) ‘jagad dhana-mayaṁ lubdhāḥ kāmukā kāminī-mayaṁ’ tti porāṇa-vaaṇassa attho paccakkhī-kīdo.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smitvā—*smiling; *jagat—*the world; *dhana-mayam—*full of wealth; *lubdhāḥ--*greedy; *kāmukāḥ--*lusty persons; *kāminī-mayam—*full of lovers; *tti—*thus; *porāṇa—*Purāṇic; *vaaṇassa—*of the statement; *attho—*the meaning; *paccakkhī—*evident; *kīdo—*is made*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (smiling): The truth of the Purāṇic statement that “The greedy see everywhere wealth and the lusty–women” is now evident.**

**Комментарий:**

The other half of the quoted verse is as follows: *‘nārāyaṇa-mayaṁ dhīrāḥ paśyanti paramārthinaḥ’* (“…\[But\] the sober spiritualists see everywhere Nārāyaṇa.”)

### Verse 159

***madhumaṅgalaḥ:***  *bho pia-vaassa, asaccaṁ ṇa hasijja{i} lalidāe jaṁ kamala-kiñjakka-reṇu-puñja-piñjaridāo haṁsīo tue sahīo kijjanti.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bho—*O; *pia—*dear; *baassa—*friend; *asaccaṁ—*without good reason; *ṇa—*not; *hasijja{i}—*laughing has been done; *lalidāe—*by Lalitā; *jaṁ—*the fact that; *kamala—*of lotuses; *kiñjakka—*on the stamens; *reṇu—*of the pollen; *puñja—*due to the heaps; *piñjaridāo—*golden; *haṁsīo—*female swans; *tue—*by You; *sahīo—*into young girls; *kijjanti—*are made*.*

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala: O dear friend, Lalitā is laughing for a good reason—golden due to the abundant pollen from the lotuses’ filaments, the female swans appear to You as young girls.**

### Verse 160

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(sa-smitam) kim asmākam anāgata-cintayā? samprati ghaṭṭa-śulkasya puṇyāhaḥ pravartatām.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-smitam—*with a smile; *kim—*what is the use; *asmākam—*to us; *anāgata—*unarrived; *cintayā—*of anxiety; *samprati—*at the present; *ghaṭṭa—*at the toll station; *śulkasya—*of the payment of tax; *puṇya—*of the righteous work; *ahaḥ—*the day; *pravartatām—*let it go on*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (smiling): Why should we worry about something which is not there? For now, let the righteous day’s work of the toll tax proceed.**

**Комментарий:**

Viśvanātha Cakravartī says that Kṛṣṇa smiles at His overly active imagination, because He understood that the “girls” He saw at Brahma-kuṇḍa were not really there. At the same time, Kṛṣṇa does not want others to notice His excessive enthusiasm about young girls. Therefore, to dispel any possible suspicion, He speaks the words “what is the use” with contempt, as if collection of taxes is the only thing He is concerned about: “I am interested in tax collection only, and that is why I am so enthusiastic to see those girls, frinds of Rādhā. How could you even think of any other reason?”

### Verse 161

***rādhā:***  *(bhruvaṁ vikṣipya) tilloke ko kkhu so mahā-sāhasiāṇaṁ sihā-maṇī ciṭṭhadi jo kkhu goula-bāliāṇaṁ dāṇaṁ geṇhiduṁ vāāmetteṇa bi maṇāuṁ vāharissadi tatthabi sūrobāsiāṇaṁ imāṇaṁ?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhruvam—*Her eyebrows; *vikṣipya—*curving; *ti-lloke—*in the three worlds; *ko—*who; *kkhu—*indeed; *so—*that; *mahā-sāhasiāṇaṁ—*of great robbers; *sihā-maṇī—*the crest-jewel; *ciṭṭhadi—*is situated; *jo—*who; *kkhu—*indeed; *goula—*of Gokula; *bāliāṇaṁ—*from the girls; *dāṇaṁ—*a payment; *geṇhiduṁ—*to accept; *vāā—*with words; *metteṇa—*only; *bi—*even; *maṇāuṁ—*slightly; *vāharissadi—*will speak; *tattha—*among them (the girls of Gokula); *abi—*also; *sūra—*of the sun god; *ubāsiāṇaṁ—*among the worshipers; *imāṇaṁ—*these.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (raising Her eyebrows): In the three worlds, where indeed is that crest-jewel of daring freebooters, who will even talk about exacting a tax from the girls of Gokula? And moreover, about exacting a tax from us, who worship the sun god?**

**Комментарий:**

In other words, even the most daring freebooter wouldn’t even talk about taxing the girls of Gokula, what ot speak of doing it himself. And also, among the Gokula girls, Rādhā and Her present companions are worshipers of the sun god. So there is no question at all of exacting any tax from them. Speaking in this way, Rādhā is trying to discourage Kṛṣṇa from returning to His miscevious idea.

### Verse 162

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(smitaṁ kṛtvā) lakṣmī-mukhi dākṣiṇyataḥ śikṣayāmi: niravadyam udyātamāne tasminn udyāna-cakravartini sāmpratam asāmpratam īdṛśaṁ mṛga-dṛśāṁ girām aurjityam!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smitam kṛtvā—*smiling; *lakṣmī-mukhi—*O you with a charming mouth; *dākṣiṇyataḥ--*as a favor; *śikṣayāmi—*I shall instruct; *niravadyam—*without reproach; *udyātamāne—*ready to punish; *tasmin—*this; *udyāna—*of the groves; *cakravartini—*about the emperor; *sāmpratam—*now; *asāmpratam—*improper; *īdṛśam—*such; *mṛga-dṛśām—*of the doe-eyed \[girls\]; *girām—*of the words; *aurjityam—*the strength*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (putting on a smile): O sweet-spoken One, I shall now do you a favor by teaching you a lesson: His Majesty the emperor of the forest groves is brisk in meting out punishment. At this point it is improper for you doe-eyed girls to talk about Him in such an impudent manner!**

**Комментарий:**

According to *“Mahaṭī-ṭīkā,”* Kṛṣṇa’s smile shows that actually Rādhā’s sharp words appear very sweet to Him. He addresses Her as *‘lakṣmī-mukhi,’* or “One whose mouth is filled with the charm of sweet words,” ironically implying that Her words are in fact bitter. After all, Rādhā has indirectly called the emperor “the crest-jewel of daring freebooters.” Next, by saying *‘dākṣiṇyataḥ’* (“as a favor”), Kṛṣṇa means that even though He is capable of reciprocating in exactly the same harsh way, He has tolerated the insult to the emperor out of His kindness and will instead teach the gopīs a rule of proper conduct: they are doe-eyed girls, who cannot behave in such an arrogant way with the emperor, who has the power to mete out punishment on the spot.

### Verse 163

***rādhā:***  *sahi lalide, dhiṭṭha-ghaṭṭī-āla-ghaṭṭaṇe paḍidāsi. tā ṇa juttaṁ ettha kuṇṭhattaṇaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sahi—*O friend; *lalide—*Lalitā; *dhiṭṭha—*arrogant; *ghaṭṭī-āla—*of the toll gate peon; *ghaṭṭaṇe—*into the sieve; *paḍida—*have fallen; *asi—*you; *tā—*therefore; *ṇa—*not; *juttaṁ—*proper; *ettha—*here; *kuṇṭhattaṇaṁ—*weakness*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: My friend Lalitā, since you have already fallen into the sieve of this arrogant toll gate peon, it won’t be proper to remain inert at this point.**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā again reproaches Lalitā, but urges her to action at the same time: “If you have brought all of us here—you have to get us out of here too.”

It doesn’t seem like Kṛṣṇa is going to let the gopīs go very easily. For this reason, Rādhā compares His “toll-gate” to a sieve—He will not let the gopīs pass through without squeezing or straining whatever He wants out of them.

### Verse 164-165

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *sakhe subala, śrutam asyāḥ kaṭhorārabhaṭī-gariṣṭhaṁ girāṁ visphūrjitam!*  
*(ity ardha-niḥsṛtāṁ rasanāṁ sandaśya) kaṣṭaṁ, bho kaṣṭam! dīvyad-arvācīna-yauvana-garva-sarvasvayā bāḍham etayā mahā-ghaṭṭasāmrājya-paṭṭa-labdhābhiṣeko ’haṁ catvarikāṇāṁ cakravartī ghaṭṭī-pālaḥ kṛto ’smi!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakhe—*O friend; *subala—*Subala; *śrutam—*heard; *asyāḥ--*Her; *kaṭhora—*rough; *ārabhaṭī—*due to arrogance; *gariṣṭham—*weighty; *girām—*of Her words; *visphūrjitam—*bursting out; *iti—*thus; *ardha—*half; *niḥsṛtām—*put out; *rasanām—*tongue; *sandaśya—*biting; *kaṣṭam—*how terrible; *bho—*O; *kaṣṭam—*how terrible; *dīvyat—*frolicsome; *arvācīna—*and new; *yauvana—*of youth; *garva—*the pride; *sarva-svayā—*for whom everything; *bāḍham—*indeed; *etayā—*by Her; *mahā—*great; *ghaṭṭa—*of toll stations; *sāmrājya—*of the sovereignity; *paṭṭa—*with a turban; *labdha—*have obtained; *abhiṣekaḥ--*a coronation; *aham—*I; *catvarikāṇām—*of the cross-roads; *cakravartī—*the emperor; *ghaṭṭī-pālaḥ--*into a tollgate man; *kṛtaḥ--*made; *asmi—*I*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: My friend Subala, you have heard how heavily She exploded with these rough and arrogant words! (biting His half-protruded tongue) How terrible, how painful! Her be-all and end-all is frolicsome and fresh youthful pride. She called Me, the monarch of the cross-roads with absolute dominion over the toll stations…yes—a mere tollgate peon!**

**Комментарий:**

By saying that frolicsome and fresh youthful pride is Rādhā’s be-all and end-all, Kṛṣṇa implies that it is this very pride that deserves to be crushed in the first place.

### Verse 166

***lalitā:***  *(saṁskṛtena)*  
*sa-cchidrā laghu-vaṁśajā ca muralī yaṣṭiḥ kaṭhorā bhṛśaṁ*  
*stabdhātmā ca viṣāṇikātimalinā vakra-svarūpāpi ca*  
*ābhiḥ santatam uttamābhir abhito yasyāṅgam āliṅgyate*  
*ghaṭṭī-pālatayā bhayānaka-vane vṛttir na tasyādbhutā*

**Пословный перевод:**

*saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *sa-cchidrā—*with holes; *laghu—*thin; *vaṁśa-jā—*born from a bamboo; *ca—*and; *muralī—*the *muralī* flute; *yaṣṭiḥ--*the stick; *kaṭhorā—*hard; *bhṛśam—*very; *stabdha—*stiff; *ātmā—*whose nature; *ca—*and; *viṣāṇikā—*the bugle; *ati-malinā—*extremely contaminated; *vakra—*crooked; *svarūpā—*whose form; *api—*also; *ca—*and; *ābhiḥ--*by them; *santatam—*constantly; *uttamābhiḥ--*most exalted; *abhitaḥ--*on all sides; *yasya—*whose; *aṅgam—*body; *āliṅgyate—*embraced; *ghaṭṭī-pālatayā—*as a tollgate officer; *bhayānaka—*scary; *vane—*in the forest; *vṛttiḥ--*occupation; *na—*not; *tasya—*His; *adbhutā—*astonishing.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (in Sanskrit): These ‘most excellent’ companions—the holed muralī flute carved of thin bamboo, the very hard stick, and the stiff, extremely impure and crooked bugle—constantly embrace this person’s body on all sides. So it is not surprising that He is engaged as a tax collector in this fearsome forest.**

**Комментарий:**

Viśvanātha Cakravartī paraphrases Lalitā’s message: “If You call Yourself ‘the monarch of the crossroads,’ then these are Your “exalted” queens—the flute, the stick, and the bugle. They perfectly match Your character with their individual qualities. The first queen, the flute, is full of faults (*‘sa-cchidrā’*) and born in a low family (*‘laghu-vaṁśajā’*). The second queen, the stick, is stubborn and hard-hearted (*‘kaṭhora’*). The third queen is the bugle, and she has a very dirty (*‘ati-mālinā’*) body and is of a devious nature (*‘vakra-svarūpā’*). It is *their* youthful pride, and not Rādhā’s, which deserves to be crushed in the first place, for they always shamelessly embrace Your body in front of everyone. And also, possessive of You, they are very watchful of Your actions. So You better forget Your desire for others’ wives. And moreover, unlike Your low-class queens, we are noble, simple-hearted, and chaste ladies. Therefore, the way You deal with us is most despicable.” Lalitā concludes: “It is not surprising that such an ‘emperor,’ thirsty to trap beautiful young girls as You like, have become a tax collector in this fearsome forest. It is obvious that You would never succeed in Your endeavors in a populated country ruled by a righteous king. ”

This sarcastic remark of Lalitā is an introduction to the verbal attack that Rādhā is just about to utter (168-169).

### Verse 167-169

***subalaḥ:*** *hanta dummada-muharāo, ṇa kkhu tumhehiṁ heliduṁ jutto eso mahā-dāṇassa ahīso.*  
***rādhā:*** *hodu ahīso. tado bi kiṁ? (iti saṁskṛtena)*  
*dharṣaṇe nakula-strīṇāṁ bhujaṅgeśaḥ kṣamaḥ katham*  
*yad etā daśanair eṣa daśan nāpnoti maṅgalam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*hanta—*hey; *dummada—*infatuated; *muharāo—*talkative \[girls\]; *ṇa—*not; *kkhu—*indeed; *tumhehiṁ—*by you; *heliduṁ—*to be neglected; *jutto—*fit; *eso—*this; *mahā-dāṇassa—*where very high taxes are paid; *ahīso—*supervisor*. hodu—*let it be; *ahi—*of snakes; *īso—*the master; *tado—*from that; *bi—*also; *kiṁ—*what; *iti—*thus; *saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *dharṣaṇe—*to assault; *nakula—*mongoose; *strīṇām—*the females; *bhujaṅga—*of snakes; *īśaḥ--*the master; *kṣamaḥ--*capable; *katham—*how; *yad—*because; *etāḥ--*them; *daśanaiḥ--*with His teeth; *eṣaḥ--*this; *daśan—*by biting; *na—*not; *āpnoti—*attains; *maṅgalam—*auspiciousness.

**Перевод:**

**Subala: Listen here, infatuated talkative girls: it is not proper for you to ignore the supervisor of a toll gate where very high taxes are exacted.**

**Rādhā: All right, He is the snake king. So what?**

**(in Sanskrit): Since by biting the female mongooses with His teeth the snake king will not meet any good, how could He assault them?**

**Комментарий:**

The Prākṛt word *‘ahīso’* is ambiguous, for in Sanskrit it could denote both “supervisor” (*adhi-īśa*) and “snake king” (*ahi-īśa*). Playing on this word, Rādhā takes its last meaning, not conceived of by Subala, and calls Kṛṣṇa “the snake king.” She expresses the gopīs neglect: “Even if He is the the snake king, what difference it makes for us?”

Rādhā then switches to Sanskrit and throws at Kṛṣṇa a seemingly contemptuous challenge. Mongoosesare known to be the natural enemies of the snakes, the last being their food. By using the word *bhujaṅgeśa* (“the snake king”), which also means “the best of lovers,” Rādhā warns Kṛṣṇa that He shouldn’t even dare to assault the gopīs (*kula-strīṇām*), for if he would, His destiny will not be a happy one.

In his two commentaries to this verseViśvanātha Cakravartī extracts three subtle meanings given below.

First meaning: “Since by biting these noble ladies (*kula-strīṇām*) with his teeth the best of lovers (*bhujaṅga-īśa*) will face inauspiciousness (lit. “will not meet any good”), could He be capable (*kṣamaḥ*) to assault (*dharṣaṇena*) them?” In other words, He is incapable. Just as the snake would suffer immidiate death from the bites of the mongoose, even being the best of lovers would have to suffer contempt from general public, punishment from the husband and the king for His “immoral” act, and hellish existence brought about by His “sin.” Biting the noble ladies means biting their lips during kissing.

Second meaning: “Since by biting these noble ladies with his teeth the best of lovers will not meet any good, is He not enduring enough (*na kṣamaḥ*) in assaulting (*dharṣaṇe*) them?” Yes, but it is only *the king* of snakes, and not just any snake, who is enduring enough to assault the female mongooses, and it is only *the best* of lovers, and not just any debauchee, who is indeed enduring enough to withstand the consequences.

Third meaning: “Since by biting these noble ladies with his teeth the best of lovers *will* attain (*‘daśan āpnoti’*) auspiciousness, could He be patient (*‘kṣamaḥ’*) enough *not* to assault (*‘dharṣaṇe na’*) them?” Of course, He couldn’t, and He would assault them immidiately. Auspiciousness here means amorous pleasure. It is also suggested that it is only by enjoying *these* noble ladies, the present group of gopīs, that Kṛṣna will attain such pleasure. He will not be as blissful by trying to enjoy other ladies, for their love for Him is inferior. Moreover, it is only *the king* of snakes and only *the best* of lovers, who would not be patient enough to attain *maṅgalam*, which then would refer to the happiness from considering oneself a valorous hero. The best of lovers, then, would think “When I have dared to seduce the noble ladies, all others will automatically fall into my arms.”

### Verse 170-171

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *bhaṅgurāpāṅgi, hṛdayaṅ-gamam āttha. tataḥ samākarṇaya (iti sa-harṣam)*  
*aprauḍha-dvija-rāja-rājad-alikā labdhā vibhūtiṁ rucāṁ*  
*navyām ātmani kṛṣṇa-vartma-vilasad-dṛṣṭir viśākhāñcitā*  
*kandarpasya vidagdhatāṁ vidadhatī netrāñcalasya tviṣā*  
*tvaṁ rādhe śiva-mūrtir ity urasi māṁ bhogīndram aṅgī-kuru*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhaṅgura—*curved; *apāṅgi—*O You, the corners of whose eyes; *hṛdayam-gamam—*charmingly; *āttha—*You speak; *tataḥ--*therefore; *samākarṇaya—*listen carefully; *iti—*thus; *sa-harṣam—*with joy; *aprauḍha—*only partly grown; *dvija-rāja—*as the moon; *rājat—*splendid; *alikā—*whose forehead; *labdhā—*who has obtained; *vibhūtim—*the ashes; *rucām—*of beauty; *navyām—*fresh; *ātmani—*on Your body; *kṛṣṇa-vartma—*with fire; *vilasat—*beaming; *dṛṣṭiḥ--*whose eye; *viśākha—*by Kārttikeya; *añcitā—*who are worshipped; *kandarpasya—*of Cupid; *vidagdhatām—*the state of being incinerated; *vidadhatī—*accomplishing; *netra—*of Your eye; *añcalasya—*from the corner; *tviṣā—*with the glare; *tvam—*You; *rādhe—*O Rādhā; *śiva—*of lord Śiva; *mūrtiḥ--*the embodiment; *iti—*therefore; *urasi—*on Your chest; *mām—*Me; *bhogi—*of snakes; *indram—*the king; *aṅgī-kuru—*accept.

**Перевод:**
