**Kṛṣṇa: O You, the corners of whose eyes are beautifully curved, since You are saying something that touches My heart, please listen attentively:**

**(joyfully): Your forehead is resplendent with the crescent-moon, and the fresh ashes of beauty cover Your body. Your third eye dazzles with fire, and once with the glare coming from its corner You incinerated Cupid. O Rādhā, You are the embodiment of Lord Śiva, adored by Kārttikeya! Please accept Me, the snake king, on Your chest.**

**Комментарий:**

As stated by Viśvanātha Cakravartī, Kṛṣṇa is actually pleased with Rādhā’s outwardly challenging statement. He has understood its hidden meanings and is now indirectly expressing to Her His amorous desire.

Kṛṣṇa accepts the new epthet—‘the snake king’—that Rādha has just now given Him and purposefully compares Her beautiful form to that of Lord Śiva. Because the snake king Vāsuki coils on Lord Śiva’s neck, Kṛṣṇa petitions Rādhā to accept Him, “the snake king,” on Her chest. The suggested idea is that then only, after acquiring this missing detail of Śiva’s attire—a serpent that hangs as a necklace, Rādhā will become the complete embodiment of Lord Śiva. This ultimately means that Rādhā is not complete without Kṛṣṇa’s loving embrace.

Lord Śiva usually smeares his body with ashes from crematorium—the remanants of burnt bodies that were once upon a time beautiful. That is why in the verse, the ashes on his body are described as “the fresh ashes of beauty.”

Lord Śiva has three eyes, and his middle eye can generate fire. Once upon a time a demon named Tāraka was terrorizing the universe. According to the boon he had received from Brahmā, he could be killed only by Śiva’s son. At that time Śiva had no any offspring, and was engaged in deep meditation. Cupid was sent by the demigods to arouse Lord Śiva’s passion, which would engage him in procreation. When Cupid was trying to disrupt Śiva’s meditation, the last noticed the cause of disturbance and angrily incinerated Cupid with the fire from his third eye.

Since the words of the verse have double meaning, it can also be understood as a direct glorification of Śrī Rādhā, as pointed out by Viśvanātha Cakravartī: “Your forehead is resplendent like the half-moon and Your body has attained the glorious (*navyām*) wealth (*vibhūtim*) of beauty (*rucām*). Fringed with black eyelashes (*kṛṣṇa-vartma*), Your eyes are playful (*vilasat*), and with the gleam of their corners You exert (*vidadhatī*) Your amorous wits (*vidagdhatām*). O Rādhā, accompanied by Visākhā (*viśākhā-añcita*), Your form is all-auspicious. Hence, please accept Me, the supreme enjoyer (*bhogi-indram*), on Your chest.” The adjective *‘kṛṣṇa-vartma-vilasad-dṛṣṭiḥ’* could also mean Rādhā, “whose eyes are playful (*vilasat*) as She looks wherever Kṛṣṇa goes (*kṛṣṇa-vartma*, literally “at Kṛṣṇa’s path”).”

It is noteworthy that the moon is described in the verse as *‘aprauḍha,’* literally meaning “not full.” This allows the description to be equally applicable to both Lord Śiva and Rādhā. In the case of Lord Śiva “not full” moon means the crescent-moon that is situated on his forehead, and in the case of Rādhā it means Her forehead, which is shaped like the half-moon.

This and the previous versespoken by Rādhā is an instance of *jalpa* (“conversation”), which is one of the special conjugal exchanges (see commentary to text 29).

### Verse 172

***lalitā:*** *kaṇha, imāe kūḍa-bāurāe duggahā lalidā dhutta-hariṇī tti tumha-sahaarā bi suṭṭhu jāṇenti. tā muñca vihala dhiṭṭha-gaṇṭhilattaṇaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kaṇha—*O Kṛṣṇa; *imāe—*with this; *kūḍa—*deceitful; *bāurāe—*the trap; *duggahā—*difficult to catch; *lalidā—*Lalitā; *dhutta—*cunning; *hariṇī—*doe; *tti—*thus; *tumha—*Your; *sahaarā—*companions; *bi—*also; *suṭṭhu—*well; *jāṇenti—*know; *tā—*therefore; *muñca—*give up; *vihala—*fruitless; *dhiṭṭha—*arrogant; *gaṇṭhilattaṇaṁ—*knottiness*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: O Kaṇha, since Your companions know very well how difficult it is to capture the cunning doe Lalitā in the trap of tricky speech, You should give up this vain and proud stubbornness.**

**Комментарий:**

According to *Mahaṭī-ṭīkā*, Lalitā, presenting herself as an obstacle to Kṛṣṇa’s obtaining Rādhā, wants to say that Rādhā is under her safe shelter. Lalitā also wants to instill fear in Kṛṣṇa’s friends by reminding them of some incidents from the past, when they had a chance to directy experience her boldness.

Kaṇha is the way people of Vraja call Kṛṣṇa even today.

*(kṛṣṇaḥ subalam avalokate)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*subalam—*at Subala; *avalokate—*looks.

**Перевод:**

**(Kṛṣṇa looks at Subala.)**

**Комментарий:**

*Mahaṭī-ṭīkā* states that Kṛṣṇa, with His glance, sends the following message to Subala: “Defeat Lalitā by eloquently speaking something that will put her to shame.”

Rūpa Gosvāmī describes, Kṛṣṇa and Subala can communicate with each other in such a way that no one is able to understand the meaning of their conversation.

*vayasya-goṣṭhyām akhileṅgiteṣu*  
*viśāradāyām api mādhavasya*  
*anyair durūhā subalena sārdhaṁ*  
*saṁjñā-mayī kāpi babhūva vārtā*

**“In the assembly of friends, who were experts in all types of subtle indications, Mādhava had some discussion with Subala. That discussion was full of gestures that even others in that assembly couldn’t comprehend.”**

### Verse 173

***subalaḥ:*** *lalide, ede kahaṁ kira ṇa jāṇissanti jehiṁ tahiṁ gandha-halī-haraṇe ṇikkuḍa-sāmiṇā luṇṭhida-maṇi-maṇḍalāṇaṁ sūrobāsiāṇaṁ kāṇaṁ bi mahā-pahābāṇaṁ danta-sihara-pantisu tiṇa-guccha-maragaehiṁ āraddhā kā bi accariā-lacchī pacchakkhī-kidā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*lalide—*O Lalitā; *ede—*they; *kahaṁ—*how; *kira—*indeed; *ṇa—*not; *jāṇissanti—*will know; *jehiṁ—*by whom; *tahiṁ—*at that time; *gandha-halī—campaka* flowers; *haraṇe—*at the time of stealing; *ṇikkuḍa—*of the grove; *sāmiṇā—*by the owner; *luṇṭhida—*were taken away; *maṇi—*of jewels; *maṇḍalāṇaṁ—*whose heaps; *sūra—*of sun-god; *ubāsiāṇaṁ—*of the worshippers; *kāṇaṁ bi—*some; *mahā-pahābāṇaṁ—*who possess great strength; *danta—*of teeth; *sihara—*ruby-like; *pantisu—*between the rows; *tiṇa—*of straw; *guccha—*bundles; *maragaehiṁ—*by the emeralds; *āraddhā—*composed; *kā bi—*some; *accariā—*wondrous; *lacchī—*opulence; *pacchakkhī-kidā—*made visible*.*

**Перевод:**

**Subala: O Lalitā, how is it possible that my friends aren’t aware of your glory? One day they have exposed an astounding wealth of some “formidable” sun god worshippers who were collecting campaka flowers. That wealth was the straw bundle emeralds between the rows of ruby teeth. These sun god worshippers had the heaps of their jewelry taken away by the owner of that garden.**

**Комментарий:**

Subala reminisces the pastime of the gopīs’ plucking flowers for the worship of the sun god. This pastime takes place regularly in Vraja. The actual purpose of the gopīs, however, is meeting with Kṛṣṇa, and they are able to accomplish it on the pretext of this noble religious cause which allows them to get free from their elders’ vigilant watch. Kṛṣṇa usually disguises Himself as the owner the garden and angrily hampers the gopīs from their business.

It seems that in the particular pastime reminisced by Subala gopīs had to beg pardon for their “unlawful” act. The phrase ‘the emeralds of straw bundles between the rows of ruby teeth’ signifies the gopīs’ holding straw in their mouths as a sign of submission. To make it even more humiliating, Subala sarcastically describes the gopīs at that time as *‘mahā-prabhavānām’*, or “formidable.” Moreover, according to Subala’s statement, the garden owner, who is Kṛṣṇa, had at that time removed the abundant jewelry from the gopīs’ bodies. In this way, Subala’s account is supposed to embarrass Lalitā by defeating Her self-confidence of being Rādhā’s safe protector.

### Verse 174-175

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *sakhe, vismṛtaṁ tat kutūhalam punar atra kāṇḍe suṣṭhu bhavatā smāritam! (iti smitaṁ kṛtvā)*  
*asminn adrau kati na hi mayā hanta hārādi-vittaṁ*  
*hāraṁ hāraṁ hariṇa-nayanā grāhitā jaina-dīkṣām*  
*yāḥ kākūkti-sthagita-vadanāḥ patra-dānena dīnās*  
*tūrṇaṁ dūrād anujagṛhire prauḍha-vallī-sakhībhiḥ*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakhe*—O friend; *vismṛtam*—forgotten; *tat*—that; *kutūhalam*—fun; *punaḥ—*again; *atra—*on this; *kāṇḍe—*occasion; *suṣṭhu—*well; *bhavatā—*by you; *smāritam—*reminded of; *iti—*thus; *smitam kṛtvā—*putting on a smile; *asmin—*on this; *adrau—*hill; *kati—*how many; *na—*not; *hi—*indeed; *mayā—*by Me; *hanta—*ah; *hāra-ādi—*such as necklaces; *vittam—*the wealth; *hāram hāram—*after repeatedly seizing; *hariṇa—*doe-like; *nayanāḥ—*\[girls,\] whose eyes; *grāhitāḥ—*were made to accept; *jaina-dīkṣām—*the jain initiation; *yāḥ—*who; *kāku—*piteous; *ukti—*with speeches; *sthagita—*choked; *vadanāḥ—*whose throats; *patra—*of their (vines’) leaves; *dānena—*by giving; *dīnāḥ—*poor; *tūrṇam—*rapidly; *dūrāt—*from a distance; *anujagṛhire—*were shown compassion; *prauḍha—*large; *vallī—*vines; *sakhībhiḥ—*by their friends.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O friend, excellent! At this moment you have reminded us once again of the fun we all forgot!**

**(putting on a smile): Ah, on this hill, how many doe-eyed girls weren’t forced by Me to accept the Jain initiation after I have repeatedly robbed them of their wealth in the form of necklaces and so on? The voices of these poor girls choked with piteous requests, as friendly thick vines in a distance quickly showed mercy on the girls by offering their foliage.**

**Комментарий:**

By saying that He forced the gopīs to accept the Jain initiation, Kṛṣṇa falsely boasts that He stripped the gopīs naked, since Jain monks of the Digambara order renounce all their worldly possessions, including their clothing. As Kṛṣṇa describes, the gopīs hid their naked bodies behind the foliage of thick vines.

This verse, quoted in *Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi* 15.229, illustrates *vitathokti*, or false speech, which is a type of special conjugal exchange(see commentary to text 29).

### Verse 176-177

***viśākhā:*** *alam imiṇā alīa-dappa-ḍiṇḍiyamāḍambareṇa!*  
***lalitā:*** *sahi visāhe, hanta hanta! kalle paccutāṇaggha-maṇi-kañcana-sañcaa-kiraṇodañcidāe rāhie kañculiāe ulluñcaṇa-vuttantaṁ sahī-jaṇe ajjāe viṇṇabeduṁ pa{u}tte muha-majjha-ṇikkhitta-tajjaṇī-siharassa surinda-gandhavva-ṇāmaṁ adhīraṁ puṇo puṇo vāharantassa kassa bi dappa-soṇḍassa mahā-paaṇḍassa sā kā bi cāḍu-gaṇḍidā kaṇṭha-kāalī kaṁ vā jaṇaṁ ṇa kkhu kāruṇṇeṇa oṇṇidabadī?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*alam—*enough; *imiṇā—*of this; *alīa—*of false; *dappa—*pride; *ḍiṇḍiyama—*of the *ḍiṇḍima* drum; *āḍambareṇa—*tumult*. sahi—*O friend; *bisāhe—*Viśākhā; *hanta hanta—*alas, alas; *kalle—*yesterday; *paccuta—*fallen; *aṇaggha—*priceless; *maṇi—*of jewels; *kañcana—*and gold; *sañcaa—*of lots; *kiraṇa—*by the rays; *udañcidāe—*made exalted; *rāhie—*of Rādhā; *kañculiāe—*the corset; *ulluñcaṇa—*of yanking; *vuttantaṁ—*of the incident; *sahī****-****jaṇe—*when a sakhī; *ajjāe—*the respected lady (refers to Paurṇamāsī); *viṇṇabeduṁ—*in informing; *pa{u}tte—*was engaged; *muha—*of the mouth; *majjha—*at the middle; *ṇikkhitta—*placed; *tajjaṇī—*of whose index finger; *siharassa—*the tip; *surinda—*of the heavenly king; *gandhavva—*of the musician; *ṇāmaṁ—*the name; *adhīraṁ—*impatiently; *puṇo puṇo—*again and again; *vāharantassa—*who was uttering; *kassa bi—*of someone; *dappa—*with pride; *soṇḍassa—*intoxicated; *mahā-paaṇḍassa—*very large-bodied; *sā—*that; *kā bi—*some; *cāḍu—*with pleasing words; *gaṇḍidā—*strung; *kaṇṭha-kāalī—*soft voice; *kaṁ vā—*which; *jaṇaṁ—*person; *ṇa—*not; *kkhu—*indeed; *kāruṇṇeṇa—*with mercy; *oṇṇidabadī—*bathed.

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā: Enough of this din from the drum of Your false pride!**

**Lalitā: Viśākhā sakhī, hear this: Yesterday, as one sakhī was informing the noble mistress of how somebody yanked Rādhā’s choli, one “extremely daring and proud fellow,” placing the forefinger into his mouth, was repeatedly and without restraint uttering the name of Indra’s musician. Whom at all did that soft voice beset with plaintive appeals not inspire with compassion? The sakhī then continued describing how the rays from the heaps of seized priceless stones and gold increased the beauty of Rādhā.**

**Комментарий:**

Proud Lalitā can’t let herself and the gopīs stay unrevenged after Kṛṣṇa and His friends have so deeply humiliated them. She now humiliates Kṛṣṇa in return in the same sarcastic manner by relating the recent incident when He had to beg for the gopīs’ mercy.

Viśvanātha Cakravartī unveils the meaning of Lalitā’s speech. Lalitā says here is that on the previous day Kṛṣṇa tried to open Rādhā’s choli. Later, when He and the gopīs were in the presence of “the noble mistress” Jaṭilā, who is the mother-in-law of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, one gopī complained to Jaṭilā about Kṛṣṇa’s mischief. Kṛṣṇa was so scared of Jaṭilā’s chastisement that out of fear He placed the forefinger into his mouth and was continuously whimpering. Lalitā expresses this indirectly by saying that Kṛṣṇa was “uttering the name of Indra’s musician.” Scriptures mention that there is a *gandharva*, or celestial musician, by the name Hāhā. So, in Sanskrit, “uttering the name of Indra’s musician” is a figurative way to say that so and so is laughing or crying. In this way, Lalitā intentionally imitates Subala’s indirect and figurative style of speaking in text 173. And just as Subala has sarcastically called the gopīs “formidable,” Lalitā is ridiculing Kṛṣṇa by calling Him “extremely daring and proud.”

Exposed in this way in front of Jaṭilā, Kṛṣṇa desperately appealed to the gopīs, begging for their compassion. The gopīs melted, and the spokesman sakhī went on describing how “the rays from the heaps of seized priceless stones and gold increased the beauty of Rādhā.” Implying with this that Kṛṣṇa’s motive behind yanking Rādhā’s choli was nothing else but to exact His naugty so-called tax, the gopīs calmed Jaṭilā down. “If not for our intervention,”—suggests Lalitā—“no one knows what would have happened to You.”

Another point is that Lalitā has exposed Kṛṣṇa’s lies in His last statement: He didn’t strip naked the gopīs as He exaggerated—He only yanked Rādhā’s choli.

The invective that is now taking place between Lalitā and Kṛṣṇa’s party is called *samarpaṇa* (angry denunciation), another element of a *bhāṇikā* play (see Appendix…).

### Verse 178

***subalaḥ:*** *dullahā ettha sā ajjā kaṇṭaāḍaī. tā lukkaṇe bi kiṁpi oṭṭambhaṇaṁ ṇa pekkhāmi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dullahā—*difficult to find; *ettha—*here; *sā—*that; *ajjā—*respected lady; *kaṇṭa—*thorny; *aḍaī—*forest; *tā—*therefore; *lukkaṇe—*in robbing; *bi—*also; *kiṁpi—*any; *oṭṭambhaṇaṁ—*impediment; *ṇa pekkhāmi—*I do not see.

**Перевод:**

**Subala: It is hard to find this noble mistress, the “thorny forest,” in this place. So I don’t see any reason why not to rob you all as well.**

**Комментарий:**

Subala calls Jaṭilā “the thorny forest,” which suggests that she has been always creating impediments for His friend Kṛṣṇa. And since she is not even nearby, there will be no any obstacle in forcing the gopīs to pay the tax.

### Verse 179

***campakalatā:*** *vijaadu so lalidāṇuhāva-bhakkaro jo kkhu takkara-vikkamaṁ kuṇṭhedi!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vijaadu—*may be glorious; *so—*that; *lalidā—*of Lalitā; *aṇuhāva—*of the power; *bhakkaro—*sun; *jo—*that; *kkhu—*indeed; *takkara—*of the thieves; *vikkamaṁ—*the courage; *kuṇṭhedi—*makes weak*.*

**Перевод:**

**Campakalatā: Glory to the sun of Lalitā’s vigor that thwarts the courage of thieves for sure!**

**Комментарий:**

According to *Mahaṭī-ṭīkā*, Campakalatā implies: “Jaṭilā is not here, but Lalitā is with us!” Lalitā’s vigor is rooted in her hot temper. As the sun sets an impediment for thieves with its bright light, the sun of Lalitā’s vigor won’t allow the thief Kṛṣṇa and His gang to deprive the gopīs of their wealth.

Another meaning of *‘lalitānubhāva-bhāskaraḥ’* is “the sun, whose power is graceful.”

### Verse 180

***vṛndā:***  *(kṛṣṇam avalokya)*  
*kapardam api kāṇaṁ tavātra duravāpam*  
*yad ugratara-karmā kumāra lalitāsau*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kṛṣṇam*—at Kṛṣṇa; *avalokya*—looking; *kapardam—*a cowrie; *api—*even; *kāṇam—*pierced; *tava—*Your; *atra—*in this matter; *duravāpam—*unobtainable; *yat—*because; *ugratara—*more fierce; *karmā—*whose activities; *kumāra—*O youth; *lalitā—*Lalitā; *asau—*that.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā (looking at Kṛṣṇa): O prince, You will not get even a holed cowrie out of this dispute, for Lalitā can be even more fierce than You.**

**Комментарий:**

The two last words of this verse—*‘kumāra-lalitā’* (“the child’s play”)—is the name of the syllabic meter in which this verse is arranged. This meter consists of four quarters with seven syllables in each.

### Verse 181

***kṛṣṇa:***  *(serṣyam iva) vṛnde, vipakṣatām āsādayantī sthāne gopikā-pakṣāñcala-sañcāriṇī saṁvṛttāsi. bhavatu, paśyādya me cañcala-ballavī-rasāla-vallībhyaḥ śulka-kuṭmala-grahaṇe puṁs-kokilatā-keli-vaidagdhīm.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-īrṣyam—*with jealousy; *iva—*as if; *vṛnde—*O Vṛndā; *vipakṣatām—*the side of the opposing party; *āsādayantī—*assuming; *sthāne—*rightfully; *gopikā—*of the *gopīs; pakṣa—*of the party; *añcala—*to the side; *sañcāriṇī—*moving; *saṁvṛttā—*happen to be; *asi—*you; *bhavatu—*let it be; *paśya—*just see; *adya—*today; *me—*My; *cañcala—*from the unsteady; *ballavī—*cowherd girls; *rasāla-vallībhyaḥ—*who are like mango creepers; *śulka—*as a tax; *kuṭmala—*buds; *grahaṇe—*in levying; *puṁs-kokilatā—*as a male cuckoo; *keli—*in the pastime; *vaidagdhīm—*expertise*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (with slight jealousy): Vṛndā, you have rightfully taken the side of the gopīs by supporting them. Let it be so. But just see how expertly I shall exact the tax from these restless cowherd maidens like a male cuckoo taking the buds from mango twigs.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa speaks the word ‘rightfully’ with sarcasm. His message to Vṛndā is as follows: “You are one of them, Vṛndā. So it is quite appropriate that you openly take their side. But just see how I shall accomplish My cuckoo business even without your help!”

### Verse 182

***rādhā:*** *sahi vunde, ṇivārīadu para-ssa-ggaha-āhiṇivesī appaṇo vaṇa-ppio. dāṇiṁ dummuha-silīmuha-pālidāhiṁ rasāla-vallīhiṁ pallaa-hattheṇa pallattho bhavia ṇaa-māliā-mallīo tumha-sahīo māhāsedu eso.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sahi—*O friend; *vunde—*Vṛndā; *ṇivārīadu—*should be held back; *para—*of others; *ssa—*the property; *ggha—*with seizing; *āhiṇibesī—*obsessed; *appaṇo—*own; *vaṇa-ppio—*the cuckoo; *dāṇiṁ—*now; *dummuha—*by sharp; *silīmuha—*stings; *pālidāhiṁ—*protected; *rasāla-vallīhiṁ—*by the mango twigs; *pallaa—*leaf-like; *hattheṇa—*with its hand; *pallattho—*surrounded; *bhavia—*becoming; *ṇaa—*of fresh; *māliā—*jasmine; *mallīo—*having garlands; *tumha—*your; *sahīo—*friends; *mā hāsedu—*should not make laugh; *eso—*this (cuckoo).

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: My friend Vṛndā, your cuckoo, who is mad after taking others’ property, must be restrained. But for now, may He create a festival for your friends the mango twigs, who are garlanded with fresh jasmine flowers. Protected by the sharp-stinged bees, they surround this cuckoo with the arms of their new foliage.**

**Комментарий:**

In text 148 Lalitā compared Rādhā to a new mango twig, Her husband to a bumblebee, and Kṛṣṇa to a cuckoo, who wishes to enjoy the “mango twig” Rādhā. Continuing here the same simile, Rādhā takes shelter of Vṛndā, who at this moment has taken the gopīs’ side. Rādhā asks Vṛndā to stop the Kṛṣṇa cuckoo, who, under the pretext of the toll gate, is actually obsessed in seizing the gopīs, the so-called property of their formal husbands. These husbands are like the sharp-stinged bees. That is to say, they are very harsh-mouthed. On the other hand, the word *‘silīmuha’* can also mean “arrow.” A sharp arrow is certainly dangerous to a tender mango twig. Thus it is implied that the gopīs’ husbands are actually their enemies, and therefore, with the contradictory phrase “protected by the sharp arrows,” the gopīs actually complain at their bad luck.

By saying *‘appaṇo vaṇa-ppio’* (“your cuckoo”) Rādhā indirectly denotes Kṛṣṇa, for the words literally mean “the lover of your \[Vṛndā’s\] forest.” The cuckoo Kṛṣṇa is in the “dangerous” situation, just about to be surrounded by the new foliage of the mango twigs. This is ironic, for tender leaves of the mango twigs are rather pleasing to the cuckoos, who are known to be very fond of mango trees. In Rādhā’s statement the new foliage of the mango twigs represents the outstretched hands of the gopīs, who, out of their contrary feminine nature, may try to physically reject Kṛṣṇa’s amorous advances. Such contrary mood in the gopīs is just another expression of their pure love, and therefore, Kṛṣṇa will feel only joy at seeing the gopīs’ self-protective hand motions. In such situation, the proud gopīs will also become elated only, and will never refuse themselves to Kṛṣṇa: the “cuckoo” will indeed create a festival for them. In this way, by comparing their hands to the new mango leaves, Rādhā expresses the gopīs zeal for Kṛṣṇa. These are Viśvanātha Cakravartī’s deep insigts into this verse.

### Verse 183-184

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(sa-khela-smitam)*  
*ghaṭṭa-śulka-pradānāya*  
*guhātithya-grahāya vā*  
*spṛhāṁ te hema-gaurāṅgi*  
*giras tāṁ gocarī-kuru*  
*(rādhā serṣyam avajñāṁ nāṭayantī tūṣṇīṁ tiṣṭhati)*  
*aravinda-dṛśām apaścimā*  
*tvam apūrvā bahu-rūpa-līlayā*  
*kapaṭodghāṭanād adakṣiṇā*  
*na kathaṁ bhavitāsy anuttarā*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa—*with; *khela—*a playful; *smitam—*smile; *ghaṭṭa—*the toll-gate; *śulka—*tax; *pradānāya—*for offering; *guhā—*in a cave; *ātithya—*the role of a guest; *grahāya—*for accepting; *vā—*or; *spṛhām—*wish; *te—*Your; *hema—*golden; *gaura—*yellow; *aṅgi—*O one whose body; *giraḥ—*to the speech; *tām—*that; *gocarī-kuru—*make reachable; *rādhā—*Rādhā; *sa—*with; *īrṣyam—*jealousy; *avajñām—*neglect; *nāṭayantī—*expressing; *tūṣṇīm—*silently; *tiṣṭhati—*stands; *aravinda—*like lotuses; *dṛśām—*of those whose eyes; *apaścimā—*not inferior; *tvam—*You; *apūrvā—*unprecedented; *bahu-rūpa—*with various; *līlayā—*pastimes; *kapaṭa—*Your duplicity; *udghāṭanāt—*due to revealing completely; *adakṣiṇā—*not straightforward; *na—*not; *katham—*how; *bhavitā—*will become; *asi—*You; *anuttarā—*not superior.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (smiling playfully): O golden-hued, please tell me outright what You wish to do: Do You want to pay the toll tax or become a guest in My cave?**

**(Rādhā stands silently, resentfully expressing Her indifference)**

**You are the most beautiful of those whose eyes look like lotuses, and You are unprecedented with Your varied pastimes. At the same time You are devious, for You have very openly revealed Your duplicity. This being so, will You not become degraded?**

**Комментарий:**

Viśvanātha Cakravartī comments on the both verses in the following way. Kṛṣṇa has understood the hidden meaning of Rādhā’s words and says the verse beginning *‘ghaṭṭa-śulka.’* “Enough of indirect suggestions, say it all directly,” He urges Her. Rādhā feels resentful, because with His straightforward question Kṛṣṇa spotlighted Her extreme eagerness for Him, eagerness that had dispelled Her shyness and made Her bold when She was speaking to Vṛndā. Rādhā is trying to fake indifference by acting as if She does not care about what Kṛṣṇa has just asked. Her silence, however, is the sign that She is embarrased.

Thinking that He is now victorious by making Rādhā speechless, Kṛṣṇa joyfully speaks the second verse starting *‘aravinda-dṛśām.’* He calles Rādhā “unprecedented,” because She creates great wonder with Her varied pastimes. Having mentioned Rādhā’s unparalleled excellences in the first half of the verse, Kṛṣṇa proceeds to mention Her shortcoming that made possible His victory over Her: “You have very openly revealed Your duplicity. This being so, will You not become degraded by falling down from the exalted position out of deviousness?”

The adjectives describing Rādhārāṇī in the second verse—*‘apaścimā,’ ‘apūrvā,’ ‘adakṣiṇa,’* and *‘anuttarā’*—could also mean “not western”, “not eastern,” “not southern,” and “not northern” respectively. By this, Kṛṣṇa means to say that Rādhā, whom He “exposed” in His first verse, has no direction left to escape. That is why She is now standing in one place, unable to find shelter in anywhere. *‘Anuttarā’* can be also understood as “answerless.” In this way, with the question “Will You not become answerless?” Kṛṣṇa deems it right that embarrased Rādhā is now standing silently, having nothing to say in response. And finally, if the word *‘anuttarā’* is taken to mean “one who has no one superior to Her,” the last question turns into glorification: “…This being so, will You not become unexcelled?”

## Tax Calculation

### Verse 185

***nāndīmukhī:***  *(mandaṁ maṇḍapam āśritya) ṇāarinda, bhaavadī sandisati.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mandam—*slowly; *maṇḍapam—*the bower; *āśritya—*entering; *ṇāar-inda—*O best of the suave; *bhaavadī—*the saintly lady; *sandisati—*sends a message*.*

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī (slowly entering the bower): O best of the smart, Bhagavatī has sends You a message.**

**Комментарий:**

Earlier, Nāndīmukhī hid herself before Kṛṣṇa and His friends had halted the gopīs (text 89). Now, after hearing Kṛṣṇa’s words beginning *‘ghaṭṭa-śulka’* (verse 183), Nāndīmukhī comes out of her hiding at the proper moment. She is going to help settling the paying of the tax.

Bhagavatī, as everywhere else in the text, refers to Paurṇamāsī.

### Verse 186

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *nāndīmukhi, satvaram āvedaya kim ājñāpayati tatra-bhavatī.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nāndīmukhi—*O Nāndīmukhī; *sa-tvaram—*with haste; *āvedaya—*inform; *kim—*what; *ājñāpayati—*orders; *tatra-bhavatī—*Her honor*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Nandīmukhī, tell quickly what does her honor order.**

### Verse 187

***nāndīmukhī:***  *esā bhaṇādi,‘rāhī-pamuhāo amha-bāliāo ajja heaṅgavīṇaṁ ghettūna jaṇṇe gamissanti. ta imāṇaṁ ghaṭṭa-dāṇe aṇuuleṇa hodavvaṇ suhaṁjuṇā.’*

**Пословный перевод:**

*esā—*she; *bhaṇādi—*says; *rāhī-pamuhāo—*headed by Rādhā; *amha—*our; *bāliāo—*girls; *ajja—*today; *heaṅgavīṇaṁ—*fresh ghee; *ghettūna—*taking; *jaṇṇe—*to the sacrifice; *gamissanti—*will go; *ta—*therefore; *imāṇaṁ—*their; *ghaṭṭa-dāṇe—*in the matter of the toll payment; *aṇuuleṇa—*favorably; *hodavvaṇ—*should behave; *suhaṁjuṇā—*the auspicious One*.*

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: She says that since today her gopī girls headed by Rādhā will carry fresh ghee to the sacrifice, the auspicious One should be lenient regarding their toll tax.**

### Verse 188-189

***kṛṣṇa:*** *(pramodam ivābhinīya) mūrdhani gṛhīto ’yaṁ mahā-prasādaḥ.*  
*(pārśvato vilokya) sakhe madhumaṅgala, suṣṭhu madhuraṁ khalu mihiropāsikānāṁ kiśorīṇāṁ haiyaṅgavīnam iti gokule garīyasī prasiddhiḥ. tataḥ samucite ’pi prati-ṭaṅkaṁ hema-ṭaṅka-traye sphuṭam eka-ṭaṅka-niṣṭaṅkanena gaṇaya śulka-vittāni, yad amuṣu bhagavatyāḥ pakṣa-pātitā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*pramodam—*happiness; *iva—*slight; *abhinīya—*expressing; *mūrdhani—*on My head; *gṛhītaḥ—*accepted; *ayam—*this; *mahā-prasādaḥ—*great mercy; *pārśvataḥ—*on side; *vilokya—*glancing; *sakhe—*O friend; *madhumaṅgala—*Madhumaṅgala; *suṣṭhu—*very; *madhuram—*sweet; *khalu—*indeed; *mihira—*of the sun-god; *upāsikānām—*of the worshipers; *kiśorīṇām—*young girls; *haiyaṅgavīnam—*the ghee; *iti—*thus; *gokule—*in Gokula; *garīyasī—*highly respected; *prasiddhiḥ—*well-known opinion; *tataḥ—*thus; *samucite—*are proper; *api—*though; *prati-ṭaṅkam—*for each *ṭaṅka*; *hema—*of gold; *ṭaṅka-traye—*when three *ṭaṅkas*; *sphuṭam—*clearly; *eka-ṭaṅka—*one *ṭaṅka*; *niṣṭaṅkanena—*by determining; *gaṇaya—*count; *śulka—*payable; *vittāni—*wealth; *yat—*since; *amuṣu—*in them; *bhagavatyāḥ—*of the saintly lady \[Paurṇamāsī\]; *pakṣa-pātitā—*the partiality*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (feigning joy): I accept this great mercy on My head.**

**(glancing on the side): My friend Madhumaṅgala, in Gokula there is a highly respected opinion that the ghee of young girls who worship the sun god is indeed extremely tasty. So, even though three ṭaṅkas of gold are obviously a suitable tax for each ṭaṅka of goods, you count the amount by fixing one ṭaṅka as the tax. This is because the saintly Paurṇamāsī favors these girls.**

### Verse 190-191

***madhumaṅgalaḥ:*** *pia-vaassa,*  
*ṭaṅkaiś caturbhiḥ karṣaḥ syāt*  
*taiś caturbhir bhavet palam*  
*bhavet tulā pala-śataṁ*  
*bhāraḥ syād viṁśatis tulāḥ*  
*tti gaṇaṇā-veiṇo bhaṇanti.*  
*eso uṇo rāhi-ādiṇo pacceaṁ mahābhāro jaṁ lalidāe appaṇo muheṇa bhaṇidaṁ, ‘halā imiṇā mahā-bhāreṇa kiliṭṭhāsi’ tti.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*pia—*O dear; *vaassa—*friend; *ṭaṅkaiḥ—ṭaṅkas*; *caturbhiḥ—*four; *karṣaḥ—*a *karṣa*; *syāt—*become; *taiḥ—*those (karṣas); *caturbhiḥ—*four; *bhavet—*become; *palam—*a *pala*; *bhavet—*is; *tulā—*a *tulā*; *pala-śatam—*one hundred *palas*; *bhāraḥ—*a *bhāra*; *syāt—*is; *viṁśatiḥ—*twenty; *tulāḥ—tulās*; *tti—*thus; *gaṇaṇā—*of calculation; *veiṇo—*the knowers; *bhaṇanti—*say; *eso—*this; *uṇo—*again; *rāhi-ādiṇo—*of the girls headed by Rādhā; *pacceaṁ—*each; *mahā-bhāro—mahābhāra*; *jaṁ—*because; *lalidāe—*by Lalitā; *appaṇo—*with her own; *muheṇa—*mouth; *bhaṇidaṁ—*said; *halā—*O friend; *imiṇā—*due to this; *mahā-bhāreṇa—mahābhara*; *kiliṭṭhā—*tormented; *asi—*you; *tti—*thus*.*

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala: My dear friend, the experts of counting say that karṣa consists of four ṭaṅkas, and four karṣas make one pala; tulā consists of one hundred palas, and twenty tulās is one bhāra.**

**Moreover, Rādhā and each other gopī have one mahābhāra of ghee. Lalitā herself said so: ‘My friend, You suffer due to the mahābhāra You carry.’**

**Комментарий:**

Madhumaṅgala defines the weight units starting from *ṭaṅka*. *Ṭaṅka* itself, however, consists of smaller weight units. The table below shows the comparative value of ancient weight units mentioned by Madhumaṅgala.

kṛṣṇala the weight of one raktikā(guñjaberry\[91\]) n/a   māṣa 5 kṛṣṇalas n/a   ṭaṅka 4 māṣas n/a   karṣa n/a 4 ṭaṅkas   pala 4 karṣas 16 ṭaṅkas   tulā 100 palas 1,600 ṭaṅkas   bhāra 20 tulās 32,000 ṭaṅkas   mahābhāra 50 bhāras 1,60,000 ṭaṅkas

In text 131 Lalitā told Rādhā *‘imiṇā mahā-bhāreṇa kiliṭṭāsi’—*“You suffer due to the great weight (*mahā-bhāra*) You carry”. Madhumaṅgala takes the word *‘mahābhāra’* there as a name of a particular weight unit, thereby “proving” that each of the gopīs is carrying that much of ghee.

*Guñja* is a climbing plant, whose red with black spot berries are used in medicine and as decoration. As a *kṛṣṇala* weight unit it measures approximately 0,1gram.

### Verse 192

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(smitvā) tatas tataḥ?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smitvā—*smiling; *tataḥ tataḥ—*then, then*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (smiling): Then, then?**

**Комментарий:**

With His smile Kṛṣṇa praises Madhumaṅgala’s erudition.

### Verse 193

***madhumaṅgalaḥ:*** *bhārāṇaṁ pañcāseṇa mahābhāro bhaṇijjaī. ado jevva pañcāṇaṁ goiāṇaṁ heaṅgavaṇehiṁ ṭaṅkāṇaṁ asīdi-lakkhāiṁ honti. paraṁ bi ghaṭṭa-āla-vaṭṭaṇa-ṇivvāhaṇassa ṭaṅka-lakkha-ca{u}kkaṁ mae vaḍḍhidaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhārāṇaṁ—bhāras*; *pañcāseṇa—*fifty; *mahābhāro—*a *mahābhāra*; *bhaṇijjaī—*said; *ado—*therefore; *jevva—*only; *pañcāṇaṁ—*of five; *goiāṇaṁ—*gopīs; *heaṅgavaṇehiṁ—*by the ghee; *ṭaṅkāṇaṁ—ṭaṅkas*; *asīdi—*eighty; *lakkhāiṁ—*lakhs; *honti—*are; *paraṁ—*another; *bi—*also; *ghaṭṭa-āla—*of the tollgate workers; *vaṭṭaṇa—*the livelihood; *ṇivāhaṇassa—*for providing; *ṭaṅka—ṭaṅkas*; *lakkha—*lakhs; *ca{u}kkaṁ—*four; *mae—*by me; *vaḍḍhidaṁ—*increased*.*

**Перевод:**

**(Madhumaṅgala:) It is said that one mahābhāra consists of fifty bhāras. Thus it is concluded that for their ghee the five gopīs owe eighty lakhs ṭaṅkas. Apart from this I have increased the tax on four lakh ṭaṅkas to provide the livelihood for the tollgate staff.**

**Комментарий:**

One *mahābhāra* constitutes fifty *bhāras*, thus amounting in 1,60,000 *ṭaṅkas* (32,000 X 50). Five *mahābhāras* equal to 80,000,000 *ṭaṅkas* (1,60,000 X 5), the amount the five gopīs owe for their ghee. Increased by four lakhs for the toll gate staff, the tax amounts to 84,000,000 *ṭaṅkas* of gold, which is 168,000 kilos.

### Verse 194-195

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *sakhe rasa-lubdha, vardhitam iti mṛṣoktam. nūnam utkoca-rocanayā gaṇane saṅkṣiptir evācaritā yad atra bhavad-gaṇanayā hema-ṭaṅkānāṁ caturaśīti-lakṣa-mātraṁ siddham.*  
*(madhumaṅgalaḥ kṛṣṇasya karṇe mukhaṁ vinyasya kathana-mudrāṁ cābhinīya kim apy akathayann iva viśliṣyati)*  
*(sa-smitam) āṁ vijñātaṁ vijñātam. samyag ācaritam. tad atra gaṇita-vittāni yathā jhaṭity amūr ghaṭṭa-catvare kūṭayanti tathodyamaḥ kriyatām.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakhe—*O friend; *rasa—*after nectar; *lubdha—*greedy; *vardhitam—*increased; *iti—*thus; *mṛṣā—*falsely; *uktam—*said; *nūnam—*indeed; *utkoca—*with the bribe; *rocanayā—*out of being pleased; *gaṇane—*in counting; *saṅkṣiptiḥ—*abridgement; *eva—*only; *ācaritā—*made; *yat—*because; *atra—*in this tax; *bhavat—*by your; *gaṇanayā—*counting; *hema—*of gold; *ṭaṅkānām—ṭaṅkas*; *catur-aśīti—*eighty-four; *lakṣa—*lakhs; *mātram—*only; *siddham—*fixed; *madhumaṅgalaḥ—*Madhumaṅgala; *kṛṣṇasya—*to Kṛṣṇa’s; *karṇe—*ear; *mukham—*mouth; *vinyasya—*placing; *kathana—*of speaking; *mudrām—*the gesture; *ca—*and; *abhinīya—*performing; *kim api—*anything; *akathayan—*not saying; *iva—*as if; *viśliṣyati—*separates; *sa-smitam—*with a smile; *ām—*yes; *vijñātam vijñātam—*understood, understood; *samyak—*perfectly; *ācaritam—*done; *tat—*therefore; *atra—*here; *gaṇita—*counted; *vittāni—*wealth; *yathā—*as; *jhaṭiti—*quickly; *amūḥ—*they; *ghaṭṭa-catvare—*on the toll crossing; *kūṭayanti—*declined from giving; *tathā—*likewise; *udyamaḥ—*operation; *kriyatām—*let be done*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O friend greedy for nectar, you are lying that you have ‘increased’ the tax. Actually, you have cut it down, being pleased with a bribe, since according to your calculation the tax is as little as eighty-four lakhs ṭaṅkas of gold only.**

**Madhumaṅgala places his mouth to Kṛṣṇa’s ear, moves his lips as if speaking, and turns his mouth away, as if he didn’t say anything.**

**Kṛṣṇa continues (smiling): Ah, I understand, I understand. Perfectly done. So let us do our job just as immidiately as they have refused to submit the counted wealth to the toll station.**

**Комментарий:**

Viśvanātha Cakravartī explains why Kṛṣṇa has accused Madhumaṅgala of bribery. According to the law, one fourth of a tax sum has to be added to the tax for the maintenance of the toll gate staff. Thus Madhumaṅgala should have added twenty lakhs to the eighty of the actual tax, which should have resulted in one crore (hundred lakhs) *ṭaṅkas*. Instead, he added only four lakhs, quoting the total amount as eighty four lakhs. This made Kṛṣṇa suspicious: “You must have cut the other sixteen lakhs with a desire to get from the gopīs some of their ghee!” That is what Kṛṣṇa hints at by accusing Madhumaṅgala of bribery.

The gopīs are left only to guess what secret message Madhumaṅgala whispered to Kṛṣṇa in His ear. *Mahaṭī-ṭīkā* adds, however, that Madhumaṅgala wants them to think that he communicated to Kṛṣṇa the following: “The quantity of *ṭaṅkas* meant for the king You will certainly get, but how You, an extravagant towner, will get in this wild jungle the rest, for Your sustenance? For this end, however, You could seize one of these gopīs, according to Your choice. Therefore, the four lakhs that I added are not for You but for Subala and others.” In other words, Madhumaṅgala offers Kṛṣṇa to take one of the gopīs, when Subala and others will be content with four lakhs *ṭaṅkas.*

Lalitā and the other gopīs can only understand that Kṛṣṇa’s smile shows that He is pleased with Madhumaṅgala’s confidential advice.

### Verse 196-197

***citrā:***  *dāṇīnda, ja{i} pañca-gaggariāṇaṁ sulukkaṁ ccea ca{u}r-asīdi lakkha-ppamāṇaṁ saṁvuttaṁ tado ṇa jāṇe mollaṁ vā kettiaṁ.*  
***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *citre, maivaṁ bravīḥ. katham anyathā dīrgha-darśino yājakās te nirbharam anarghāṇi viśrāṇayanti maṇi-maṇḍanāni.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dāṇī-inda—*O chief tax collector; *ja{i}—*if; *pañca—*for five; *gaggariāṇaṁ—*pots; *sulukkaṁ—*the tax; *ccea—*only; *ca{u}r-asīdi—*by eighty-four; *lakkha—*lakhs; *ppamāṇaṁ—*measured; *saṁvuttaṁ—*happens; *tado—*then; *ṇa—*not; *jāṇe—*I know; *mollaṁ—*the price; *vā—*indeed; *kettiaṁ—*how big; *citre—*O Citrā; *mā—*do not; *evam—*like this; *bravīḥ—*talk; *katham—*how; *anyathā—*otherwise; *dīrgha-darśinaḥ—*far-seeing; *yājakāḥ—*performers of the sacrifice; *te—*to you; *nirbharam—*excessively; *anarghāṇi—*priceless; *viśrāṇayanti—*will give; *maṇi—*of jewels; *maṇḍanāni—*the ornaments.

**Перевод:**

**Citra: O chief tax collector, if eighty-four lakhs happens to be just the tax for five pots of ghee, then I don’t know what would be the actual price of the pots.**

**Kṛṣṇa: Citra, don’t talk in this way. How would then wise priests abundantly offer you in return priceless jeweled ornaments?**

**Комментарий:**

“If your ghee was not priceless, why would wise priests offer you so much in return?”

### Verse 198

***nāndīmukhī:***  *pukkharikkhaṇa, ṇa dukkharaṁ kkhu imāṇaṁ ettha ca{u}r-asīdi-lakkhāṇaṁ dāṇam. tā aṇukampia kiṁpi samāhāṇaṁ cintehi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*pukkhara-ikkhaṇa—*O lotus-eyed; *ṇa—*not; *dukkharaṁ—*difficult; *kkhu—*indeed; *imāṇaṁ—*for them; *ettha—*here; *ca{u}r-asīdi—*eighty; *lakkhāṇaṁ—*lakhs; *dāṇam—*tax; *tā—*thus; *aṇukampia—*bestowing compassion; *kiṁpi—*of some; *samāhāṇaṁ—*settlement; *cintehi—*think*.*

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: O lotus-eyed one, since it is not at all difficult for them to pay eighty-four lakhs as a tax, please be kind and think of some solution.**

**Комментарий:**

In some manuscripts of *Dāna-keli-kaumudī* the word *‘ṇa’* (“not”) is not there, which makes Nandīmukhī's statement self-explanatory: “…since it is very difficult for them to pay eighty-four lakhs as a tax, please be kind and think of some solution.”

It is obvious, however, that Viśvanātha Cakravartī had the manuscript with the word *‘ṇa.’* In *Mahatī-ṭīkā*, He explains the resulting meaning of Nāndīmukhī’s speech. “By saying that paying the tax is not difficult for the gopīs, Nāndīmukhī openly glorifies them as being supremely rich and suggests that she has now taken their side in the dispute. Her statement implies that the fuss about paying the tax is an external show with the real purpose hidden behind it.”

### Verse 199

***madhumaṅgalaḥ:***  *pia-vaassa, ṇāndīmuhīe suttidaṁ, ‘ekkekkaso ca{u}r-asīdi-lakkha-jīa-jāda-rūbehinto bhūiṭṭhaṁ variṭṭha-rūbāo honti imāo…(ity ardhokte smitvā mukhaṁ vyāvartayati )*

**Пословный перевод:**

*pia-vaassa—*O dear friend; *ṇāndī-muhīe—*by Nāndīmukhī; *suttidam—*concisely said; *ekkekkaso—*each one; *caur-asīdi—*eighty-four; *lakkha—*lakhs; *jīa—*shining; *jāda-rūbehinto—*than gold; *bhūiṭṭhaṁ—*exceedingly; *variṭṭha-rūbāo—*more beautiful; *honti—*are; *imāo—*they; *iti—*thus; *ardha—*half-way; *ukte—*spoken; *smitvā—*smiling; *mukham—*mouth; *vyāvartayati—*opens wide.

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala: My dear friend, Nāndīmukhī said in brief that each gopī is unlimitedly more valuable than eighty-four lakhs ṭaṅkas of shining gold…**

**(Half-way through the sentence, He smiles and opens his mouth wide.)**

**Комментарий:**

As an adjective for the gold, the word *‘jīva’* means “supremely effulgent.” It also means “living entity.” In this case, *‘jīva-jāta-rūpebhyaḥ’* means “the bodies of life species.” With this meaning of the compound, the whole sentence then is understood in the following way: “My dear friend, Nāndīmukhī said in brief that each gopī is unlimitedly more beautiful than any of the eighty-four lakhs of living species.”

*Mahaṭī-ṭīkā* concludes that by opening his mouth wide, Madhumaṅgala conveys to Kṛṣṇa the following: “I have merely unpacked the Nāndīmukhī’s sūtra. It is You, however, who have to purport it openly. I feel shy to do this in front of these girls.”

### Verse 200-201

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *sakhe, samyag ākalitam. tenātra kāpy ekatarā gṛhyatām iti nāndīmukhyāḥ śikṣā-cāturī.*  
***lalitā:*** *(sotprāsa-smitam) edaṁ kkhu maṇoraha-metteṇa dakkhā-bhakkhaṇaṁ adakkhassa loluba-kīra-juāṇassa!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakhe—*O friend; *samyak—*well; *ākalitam—*thought; *tena—*by this; *atra—*here; *kā api ekatarā—*any one; *gṛhyatām—*should be taken; *iti—*thus; *nāndīmukhyāḥ—*of Nāndīmukhī; *śikṣā—*of precept; *cāturī—*the wisdom; *sa-utprāsa-smitam—*with a horselaugh; *edaṁ—*this; *kkhu—*indeed; *maṇo-raha—*by whim; *metteṇa—*only; *dakkhā—*grapes; *bhakkhaṇaṁ—*eating; *adakkhassa—*unskillful; *loluba—*greedy; *kīra—*parrot; *juāṇassa—*of a young*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Well guessed, my friend. So, according to you, Nāndīmkhī wisely advices that we should just take any one of these gopīs.**

**Lalitā (laughing wildly): This indeed seems as if a young clumsy, but greedy parrot is dreaming of eating grapes!**

### Verse 202-203

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *śriyā nātīva tāratamyavatīṣv apy etāsu lalitā-jīvātur eṣā rājīva-locaneyaṁ mahyam abhirocate.*  
***vṛndā:*** *nikuñja-yuvarāja, nihnuta-maṇi-maṇḍaleyaṁ rādhā. tad eṣā bhūri-bhūṣaṇa-bhūṣitā lalitaiva śulka-kāryāya paryāpnoti.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*śriyā—*by beauty; *na—*not; *atīva—*very; *tāratamyavatīṣu—*different in degree; *api—*even; *etāsu—*among them; *lalitā—*Lalitā; *jīvātuḥ—*whose life; *eṣā—*she; *rājīva-locanā—*doe-eyed; *iyam—*this; *mahyam—*to me; *abhirocate—*appeals; *nikuñja—*of groves; *yuva-rāja—*O prince; *nihnuta—*from whom was taken; *maṇi—*of jewels; *maṇḍalā—*the multitude; *iyam—*this; *rādhā—*Rādhā; *tat—*thus; *eṣā—*this; *bhūri—*abundant; *bhūṣaṇa—*with ornaments; *bhūṣitā—*decorated; *lalitā—*Lalitā; *eva—*certainly; *śulka—*the payment; *kāryāya—*for making; *paryāpnoti—*will suffice*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Even though these girls are not so different from each other in terms of beauty, this doe-eyed one, the life and soul of Lalitā, appeals to Me very much.**

**Vṛndā: O prince of forest groves, since Rādhā is deprived of Her vast jewelry, Lalitā, who is decorated to her neck, will certainly suffice for paying the fee.**

**Комментарий:**

Viśākhā had previously removed all the ornaments from Rādhā’s body to relieve Her of the fatique caused by the ornaments’ heavy weight (text 72). By saying that Rādhā has no jewelry on Her, Vṛndā again shows that she is on the gopīs' side (see text 181). Kṛṣṇa has defined the “emperor’s” share in gold, and not in any other form of the tax. This means that bound by His own definition of the tax, He has no business with Rādhā, for Lalitā with her ornaments would suit much more as a commodity the tax liquidation. On the other hand, Kṛṣṇa has arranged for the whole pastime with a sole objective to obtain Rādhā. Therefore, He must by all means find the way out of this predicament set by Vṛndā.

### Verse 204

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *seyaṁ mugdhe śikhara-daśanā padma-rāgādharauṣṭhī*  
*rājan-muktā-smita-madhurimā candrakāntāsya-bimbā*  
*uddīptendropala-kaca-ruciḥ paśya hī rādhiketi*  
*tyaktuṁ yuktā na kila taruṇī-ratna-mālā-mahiṣṭhā*  
*(iti rādhām anusarpati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sā iyam—*this very; *mugdhe—*O bewildered; *śikhara—*ruby-like gems; *daśanā—*of whose teeth; *padma-rāga—*ruby; *adhara-oṣṭhī—*of whose lips; *rājat–*shining; *muktā—*pearl-like; *smita—*of a smile; *madhurimā—*who has the sweetness; *candra-kānta—*as *candrakānta* gem; *āsya—*of whose mouth; *bimbā—*the *bimba* fruit; *uddīpta—*like glaring; *indra-upala—*sapphire; *kaca—*of Her hair; *ruciḥ--*the luster; *paśya—*look; *hī—*ah!; *rādhikā—*Rādhikā; *iti—*therefore; *tyaktum—*to abandon; *yuktā—*proper; *na—*not; *kila—*indeed; *taruṇī—*new; *ratna-mālā—*of many jewels; *mahiṣṭhā—*the greatest; *iti—*thus; *rādhām—*Rādhā; *anusarpati—*approaches.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O bewildered one, just look, this is that very Rādhikā: Surrounded by ruby-red lips, Her teeth are like śikhara gems that appear as the seeds of a ripe pomegranate. Her bimba-colored mouth is a candrakānta jewel laden with the sweetness of Her smile, which shines like a radiant pearl, and Her hair is radiant with the effulgence of sapphire. Ah, Rādhikā! It would not be proper to reject Her, the most splendrous among immaculately radiant gems. (draws near Rādhā)**

**Комментарий:**

In this verse Kṛṣṇa counters Vṛndā’s suggestion that Rādhā, not wearing any ornaments, is of no interest to Him. Kṛṣṇa says here that even the face of Rādhārāṇī and Her hair are in themselves “priceless jewels,” like rubies, pearls, *candrakānta*, and sapphire, which makes Rādhā “the most splendrous among immaculately radiant gems” (*‘taruṇī-ratna-mālā-mahiṣṭhā’*). Or else, this phrase can be understood as “the most splendrous *on account of* many immaculately radiant gems.” The same compound, however, has yet another meaning: “the greatest among the multitude (*mālā*) of the foremost (*ratna*) youthful maidens (*taruṇī*).” Kṛṣṇa is amazed at the value of Rādhārāṇī and expresses His wonder by saying *‘hī rādhikā’* (“Ah, Rādhikā”). Otherwise, if read as *‘hīra-adhikā,’* it means “Rādhā, who has many (*adhikā*) diamonds (*hīra*).”

### Verse 205

***rādhā:*** *(līlayā sādhvasātirekam abhinayantī) sahi visāhe, parittāhi parittāhi! (iti sa-bhrū-bhaṅgam apasarpati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*līlayā—*playfully; *sādhvasa—*of fear; *atirekam—*the excess; *abhinayantī—*expressing; *sahi—*O friend; *visāhe—*Viśākhā; *parittāhi parittāhi—*save, save; *iti—*thus; *sa-bhrū-bhaṅgam—*knitting Her eyebrows; *apasarpati—*retreats.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (feighning panic): O Viśākhā, My friend, save Me, save Me! (Frowning, She backs off.)**

### Verse 206

***viśākhā:***  *bho duvvāra-vāraṇa, imāe dullalidāe lalidāe mahā-vārīe adikkame saṁvutte ccea campaaladādi-veḍḍhidāe amia-sarasīe vigāhaṇaṁ de sulahaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bho—*O; *duvvāra—*unstoppable; *bāraṇa—*elephant; *imāe—*of this; *dullalidāe—*rough; *lalidāe—*Lalitā; *mahā—*large; *vārīe—*trap or rope for capturing elephants; *adikkame—*when the attack; *saṁvutte—*will have occurred; *ccea—*only; *campaaladā-ādi—*by plants like *campaka* creeper; *veḍḍidāe—*surrounded; *amia—*of nectar; *sarasīe—*in the lake; *vigāhaṇam—*plunging; *de—*by You; *sulahaṁ—*easy.

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā: O mad elephant, only when this rude elephant trap named Lalitā will finish Her onslaught, You could easily dive into the pond of nectar, which is encircled by the campaka creeper and other plants.**

**Комментарий:**

The “pond of nectar” is Rādhā, and “the *campaka* creeper and other plants” are Campakalatā (literally, the *campaka* creeper) and the other three gopīs.

### Verse 207

***lalitā:*** *haṁho kumbha-sambhaa-ppia-girinda-sindhura, esā ṇa kkhu juttā adibhūmī!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*haṁho—*hey; *kumbha-sambhaa—*of Agastya Muni; *ppia—*favorite; *giri-inda—*the king of mountains, Vindhya; *sindhura—*the elephant; *esā—*this; *ṇa—*not; *kkhu—*indeed; *juttā—*proper; *adibhūmi—*transgression.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: Hey, Agastya’s darling “mad elephant” Vindhya, it is improper—You are certainly going beyond Your limits!**

**Комментарий:**

As *Mahābhārata* narrates , once upon a time, the Vindhya range became envious of the mount Meru. Vindhya demanded that just as the Sun circumambulates Meru while traversing its orbit, it should circumambulate him as well. The Sun couldn’t do that, and enraged Vindhya, wishing to attain the position of Meru, started to grow higher and higher, eventually blocking the path not only of the Sun, but of the Moon, and the stars and other planets as well. The demigods were unable to dissuade Vindhya from this wrathful action and approached the sage Agastya for help. Agastya Muni then came to Vindhya and asked if he could lower himself, allowing the sage to pass through the range on his way to the south. The sage promised that upon his return Vindhya could again rise up as much as his wished to. Vindhya relented, but the sage never returned. In this way the range had to remain in a low position, unable to disturb the universal order anymore.

Just as the Vindhya range broke all the principles of etiquette and stood on the path of the Sun, Kṛṣṇa is now impetuously daring to stand on the path of Rādhā. This is the reason why Lalitā calls Kṛṣṇa the “mad elephant” Vindhya. She boldly declares Kṛṣṇa’s behavior as transgression of moral principles. On the other hand, as hinted by Viśvanātha Cakravartī, she indirectly instigates Vindhya-like Kṛṣṇa for more action, for “going beyond Your limits” could be also understood in relation to Vindhya’s lowering itself. In other words, Kṛṣṇa is not enough vehement and persistent.

### Verse 208

***vṛndā:*** *(apavārya) sakhi lalite, cāṭubhir abhyarthyamānāsi. manāg adya tuṣṇīṁ bhava. paśyāmi bhāvodbhāsitām anayor vyāvahāsīm.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*apavārya—*to the side; *sakhi—*O friend; *lalite—*Lalitā; *cāṭubhiḥ--*with sweet words; *abhyarthyamānā—*respectfully requested; *asi—*you; *manāk—*a little bit; *adya—*now; *tuṣṇīm—*silent; *bhava—*become; *paśyāmi—*I shall see; *bhāva—*from emotions; *udbhāsitām—*resplendently arising; *anayoḥ—*between them two; *vyāvahāsīm—*joking conversation.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā (to the side): O friend Lalitā, I beg you with sweet words, please stay a bit silent for now. I wish to see how both of them exchange jokes bursting with their ecstatic emotions.**

**Комментарий:**

The following exchange of joking words between Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa is surcharged with Their *bhāvas*, or transcendental ecstatic emotions, such as resentment, pride, and joy, that churn Their mutual affection. Vṛndā restrains Lalitā’s fury to allow the amazing verbal contest of the divine couple take place.

## Verbal Contest Between Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa

### Verse 209

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *sadma-nardini, kiṁ palāyase śulkam apradāya? durlabhā te padād api padāntara-gatiḥ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sadma—*at home; *nardini—*O shouter; *kim—*whether; *palāyase—*you are fleeing; *śulkam—*the payment; *apradāya—*without giving; *durlabhā—*difficult; *te—*your; *padāt—*from one foot; *api—*even; *pada—*to foot; *antara—*another (foot); *gatiḥ—*the moving.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O One who can shout only at home, are you trying to escape without paying the tax? It will be difficult for You to make even one step.**

**Комментарий:**

*Mahaṭī-ṭīkā* explains that by addressing Rādhā “One who can shout only at home” Kṛṣṇa implies that although timid by nature, She has guts to speak in an arrogant way only when supported by Lalitā and the other gopīs.

### Verse 210

***rādhā:*** *kiṁ amhe vaṇijja-jīviāo jaṁ ghaṭṭa-ālādo tuatto bhaeṇa palāissamha?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kim—*are; *amhe—*we; *vaṇijja—*by merchandising; *jīviāo—*who subsist; *jaṁ—*that; *ghaṭṭa-ālādo—*the tollgate peon; *tuatto—*of you; *bhaeṇa—*out of fear; *palāissamha—*we will flee.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: Are we merchants to flee in fear of You, the toll gate peon?**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa implied that Rādhā is timid, but Rādhā here wants to prove the opposite by speaking in a very bold way. The logic of Her statement is that it is merchants who are commonly the “victims” of tax collectors. The gopīs aren’t merchants, and so, why should they fear Kṛṣṇa?

### Verse 211

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *sādhu sādhu. kṣaṇaṁ sthirī-bhava yāvad eṣa te payodharopari vilakṣitāṁ nakṣatra-mālām apahartuṁ kalyatām āsādayāmi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sādhu sādhu—*very good, very good; *kṣaṇam—*for a moment; *sthirī-bhava—*become still; *yāvat—*until; *eṣa—*this Me; *te—*Your; *payo-dhara—*the clouds; *upari—*on; *vilakṣitām—*appearing; *nakṣatra—*of stars; *mālām—*the line; *apahartum—*to take away; *kalyatām—*the state of being the dawn; *āsādayāmi—*I shall attain*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Very good, very good. Please stand still for a moment until I become the dawn to dispel the line of stars resting on Your clouds.**

**Комментарий:**

With this statement Kṛṣṇa is trying to break Rādhā’s boldness by indirecltly “threatening” Her. This is the hidden meaning of Kṛṣṇa’s statement: “Please stand still for a moment until I become ready (*kalyatām*) to yank (*apahartum*) the pearl necklace (*nakṣatra-mālām*) resting on Your breasts (*payodhara*).” Also, according to *Amara-kośa* dictionary, *nakṣatra-mālā* is a necklace consisting of twenty seven pearls.

### Verse 212

***rādhā:*** *esā sudīhatamā tāmasī sāmā. tā kudo kallassa abbhuggamā-saṅkābi?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*esā—*this; *sudīhatamā—*very long; *tāmasī—*dark; *sāmā—*night; *tā—*therefore; *kudo—*wherefrom; *kallassa—*of dawn; *abbhuggamā—*of the appearance; *saṅkā—*the fear; *abi—*even.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: Since I, the dark night of waning fortnight, am extremely long, why should I worry at all about breaking of dawn?**

**Комментарий:**

Alternative meaning: “Since I, this pleasant young girl (*sāmā—‘śyāmā’*), am extremely tall (*sudīhatamā—‘sudīrghatamā’*) and furious (*tāmasī*), there is not even a slight worry that a person fit (*kallassa—‘kālyasya’*) for doing that \[removing the pearl necklace\] will appear.”

In other words, Kṛṣṇa is not fit to snatch the pearl necklace from Rādhā, for He won’t be able to reach to it. “Extremely tall,” however, is not to be taken literally. Rather than describing the physical measurement of Her body, Rādhā hints that She will be unyielding to Kṛṣṇa’s attempts to physically reach Her.

According to some Sanskrit poets, *‘śyāmā’* (*sāmā* in Prākṛt) is a gold-complexioned woman who is pleasantly warm in winter and pleasantly cool in summer, and whose mouth emanates the fragrance of lotus flower.

### Verse 213

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(smitaṁ kṛtvā) prollasac-caṇḍakare praphulla-puṇḍarīkekṣaṇe visphurati hārita-tāroru-hārā skhalita-tamisra-vasanā tāmasī śyāmā svayam eva sadā palāyate.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smitam kṛtvā—*smiling; *prollasat—*rising; *caṇḍakare—*the sun; *praphulla—*causing blooming; *puṇḍarīka—*of the lotuses; *īkṣaṇe—*to the eyes; *visphurati—*when appears; *hārita—*stolen; *tārā—*of stars; *uru—*grand; *hārā—*whose necklace; *skhalita—*falling; *tamisra—*of darkness; *vasanā—*with the dress; *tāmasī—*dark; *śyāmā—*the night; *svayam—*herself; *eva—*only; *sadā—*always; *palāyate—*flees.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (smiling): When the sun, which opens the lotuses’ eyes, is up in the sky, the dark night, robbed of her grand necklace of stars, always flees on her own accord, dropping the garment of darkness.**

**Комментарий:**

“The lotuses’ eyes” are the flower portions of lotus plants, which open daytime. *‘Praphulla-puṇḍarīkekṣaṇe’* can be also understood as referring to the sun: “When the sun, which opens its eyes in the form of lotuses, is up in the sky…”

Using the word *‘caṇḍakara’* to denote the sun, Kṛṣṇa aptly hints at Himself, for *‘caṇḍa-kara’* literally means “one with fierce hands.” With this, the whole sentence is understood in a different way: “When I appear on the scene with My passionate and violent arms (*prollasac-caṇḍa-kare*) and eyes like blooming lotuses (*praphulla-puṇḍarīkekṣaṇe*), that furious girl (*tāmasī śyāmā*), robbed of Her grand pearl necklace (*tāroru-hārā*), always flees on Her own accord, dropping Her dark garment (*tamisra-vasanā*).”

### Verse 214

***rādhā:*** *hanta sūra, rāhutthāṇe ṇa kkhu caṇḍa-arassa caṇḍimā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*hanta—*O; *sūra—*Sun; *rāhu—*of Rāhu; *utthāṇe—*when there is the rising; *ṇa—*not; *kkhu—*indeed; *caṇḍa-arassa—*of the sun; *caṇḍimā—*fierceness.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: O Sun, You definitely never glare when Rāhu rises.**

**Комментарий:**

Rāhu is a demon, who by trick drank some nectar of immortality after the demons and the demigods had churned the milk ocean. Although Rahu’s head was severed from the rest of the body by Sudarśana-cakra, it remained alive due to the effect of the nectar. Rāhu then became a subtle dark planet that eclipses the sun and causes the moon to wane. The double meaning of the sentence is slightly sarcastic: “O ‘hero’ (*sūra—śūra*), when Rādhā appears on the scene (*rāha-uttāṇe*), Your ‘fierce’ hands (*caṇḍa-arassa*) definitely lose all their fierceness (*ṇa caṇḍimā*).”

### Verse 215

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *paśya durviṣahatā-yuto ’yaṁ cakra-lakṣmā. kathaṁ tad-utthānaṁ sambhāvyate?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*paśya—*look; *durviṣahatā—*with the quality of being intolerable; *yutaḥ—*endowed; *ayam—*this; *cakra—*of the *cakra*; *lakṣmā—*the mark; *katham—*how; *tad—*his; *utthānam—*rising; *sambhāvyate—*possible.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Look, this is the mark of the intolerable cakra. Now, how could Rāhu possibly rise?**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa shows the mark of *cakra* on His right palm. With the help of Sudarśana-cakra, the Lord in the form of Mohinī has severed Rāhu’s head from his body. Kṛṣṇa’s logic is that in fear of Sudarśana-cakra, Rahu will surely not come in its presence.

### Verse 216

***rādhā:*** *(vihasya) hanta, phukkāra-duvviseṇa hadājuda-cakka-lakkhaṇa-ṇāara-ṇāa! moha-dāiṇaṁ visāṇaṁ mahāsāraṁ kiṁ tti ullāsesi? tumaṁ gabbharaṁ gadua muraliā-ṇāiṇīṁ cumbehi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vihasya—*laughing; *hanta—*alas; *phuk-kāra—*of hissing; *duvviseṇa—*by the poison; *hada—*which killed; *ajuda—*ten thousand; *cakka—*of a *cakra*; *lakkhaṇa—*having the mark; *ṇāara—*among suave men; *ṇāa—*the snake; *moha—*of bewilderment; *dāiṇaṁ—*the giver; *visāṇaṁ—*of the poison; *mahā—*abundant; *āsāraṁ—*the shower; *kiṁ tti—*why; *ullāsesi—*you cause to appear; *tumaṁ—*You; *gabbharam—*in a cave; *gadua—*going; *muraliā—muralī*; *ṇāīṇīṁ—*the she-snake; *cumbehi—*kiss.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (laughing): Alas, o snake among suave men, marked with a cakra! You have slayed thousands with Your virulent hissing! Why are You creating a shower of poison? You had better go to some cave and kiss the snake Muralī.**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā calls Kṛṣṇa “a snake marked with a *cakra,*” which is a visual description of Indian cobra, which on its hood has two ring marks connected by a curved line.

Viśvanātha Cakravartī explains that with Her laughter, Rādhā acknowledges the help of Sarasvatī Devī in making it possible to twist Kṛṣṇa’s words. *‘Durviṣahatā-yutaḥ’* from the previous text Rādhā breaks as *‘duvvisa-hada-ajuta* (*durviṣa-hata-ayuta*)*,’* which means “\[the snake among suave men,…\] who has killed thousands (*hata-ayuta*) with Your poison (*durviṣa*)\[of hissing\].”

In the sentence “Why are You creating a shower of poison,” the Prākṛt word *‘visāṇam’* (*viṣāṇām*—of poison) could also stand for Sanksrit *‘viṣāṇam,’* or “fang.” In this case, Rādhārāṇī’s question is still directed to the snake Kṛṣṇa: “Why are You flashing (*ullāsesi*) Your fang (*visāṇam*), which gushes out a shower \[of poison\]?” Here, *‘mahāsāram’* could be also split as *‘mahā-sāram,’* which means “very hard,” also referring to the fang. In the end, Rādhā ridicules Kṛṣṇa by telling Him to go to a cave and “kiss” another snake—His *muralī* flute.

### Verse 217

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *śulka-nāgari, tathyam eva nāgara-nāgaḥ supratīko ’yaṁ yat padminīnāṁ vaḥ karahāṭakam ākraṣṭu-kāmo mahā-sāraṁ viṣāṇam ullāsayati.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*śulka—*\[purchased for \] a fee; *nāgari—*O smart girl; *tathyam—*actual truth; *eva—*indeed; *nāgara—*of the suave boys; *nāgaḥ—*the foremost; *supratīkaḥ—*a name of an elephant presiding over one of the eight directions; *ayam—*this; *yat—*because; *padminīnām—*the lotuses; *vaḥ—*of you; *karahāṭakam—*to the root; *ākraṣṭu—*to chastise; *kāmaḥ—*desiring; *mahā-sāram—*very hard; *viṣāṇam—*tusk; *ullāsayati—*flashing*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O Rādhā, You are a smart girl obtained for a fee. It is certainly true that one whom You call “the snake of suave men” (nāgara-nāga) is the elephant Supratīka, because He is flashing His rigid tusk, wanting to “chastise” the roots of you lotuses.**

**Комментарий:**

Here Kṛṣṇa calls Himself the elephant Supratīka, one of the eight great elephants standing in the eight directions of the universe, because *‘ṇāara-ṇāa’* (*nāgara-nāga*), or “the snake among the suave men,” from Rādhā’s last statement can also mean “the elephant (*nāga*) among suave men (*nāgara*).” Also, Kṛṣṇa aptly takes advantage of Rādhā’s words*‘visāṇaṁ mahāsāram,’* which alternatively mean “rigid tusk.” By this He is threatening the gopīs, whom He calls lotuses (*padminīnām*), because elephants naturally like sporting in ponds, uprooting in their zeal whatever flora may be around.

This is the hidden meaning of Kṛṣṇa’s present speech: “…it is certainly true that this best of suave boys (*nāgara-nāgaḥ*) is very handsome (*su-pratīkaḥ*), since He is flashing His rigid buffalo horn (*viṣāṇam*), wanting to “chastise” the golden bangles (*kara-hāṭakam*) of you beautiful girls (*padminīnām*).”

According to *kāma-śāstra*, women are classified according to four categories, of which *padminī* is the first. It is defined as follows:

*bhavati kamala-netrā nāsikā-kṣudra-randhrā*  
*avirala-kuca-yugmā cāru-keśī kṛśāṅgī*  
*mṛdu-vacana-suśīlā gīta-vādyānuraktā*  
*sakala-tanu-suveśā padminī padma-gandhā*

**“A woman is called** ***padminī*** **if she is fragrant like lotus (*****padma*****), has lotus-like eyes, small nostrils, closely situated breasts, beautiful hair and slim figure.** ***Padminī*** **speaks sweetly, has a very pleasant character, always likes to sing and play musical instruments, and covers her whole body with nice garments.” (*****Rati Mañjarī*** **4)**

### Verse 218

***rādhā:*** *paumiṇīe varāḍaassa bi appadāṇaṁ jāṇīhi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*paumiṇīe—*of a lotus; *varāḍaassa—*the whorl; *bi—*even; *appadāṇaṁ—*not giving; *jāṇīhi—*know.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: Know it that this lotus won’t give You even its whorl.**

**Комментарий:**

Understanding that Kṛṣṇa has compared the gopīs to lotuses, Rādhārāṇī implies the following: “\[You may flash Your tusk as much as You like, but\] know that this lotuslike girl (*paumiṇīe*) won’t give even one cowrie (*varāḍaassa*).” In other words, if Rādhā is not ready to give even one cowrie, how Kṛṣṇa can think of a golden bangle, which he is thinking of pulling off from the gopīs’ hands with His buffalo horn?

### Verse 219

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(smitvā) kāmini, varāṭakayāpi kim ātma-dānaṁ kartum udyatāsi yad ayam artha-grahila-cakravartī nāṅganābhis tuṣyati?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smitvā—*smiling; *kāmini—*O lovely lady; *varāṭakayā—*with a cowrie; *api—*also; *kim—*whether; *ātma—*of the self; *dānam—*the giving; *kartum—*to do; *udyatā—*intent; *asi—*You; *yat—*because; *ayam—*this; *artha—*of wealth; *grahila—*of seizers; *cakravartī—*the emperor; *na—*not; *aṅganābhiḥ—*with beautiful women; *tuṣyati—*is satisfied.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (smiling): I am the emperor of those who seize others’ wealth, and I am not satisfied just with beautiful ladies. For this reason, o lusty One, are you ready to offer also a cowrie along with Yourself?**

**Комментарий:**

In Rādhā’s last sentence Kṛṣṇa took the Prākṛt word *‘appadāṇam’ (‘apradānam’*—“not giving”) as *‘ātma-dānam,’* which means “giving of oneself.” Rādhā’s sentence is then understood as “Know it that the beautiful girl offers Herself along with a cowrie.” He calls Rādhā *‘kāminī’* (“a lusty woman”), since She has expressed Her desire to voluntarily give Herself along with a cowrie.

The *‘artha-grahila-cakravartī’* (“the emperor of those who seize others’ wealth”) Kṛṣṇa, however, pretends to be interested not in Her, but in a cowrie only. On the other hand, *‘artha-grahila-cakravartī’* could be understood as “the emperor of those who grasp (*grahila*) the actual meanings (*artha*).” Taken in this way, it implies that Kṛṣṇa understands Rādhā’s confidential proposal.

### Verse 220

***rādhā:*** *(sotprāsaṁ vihasya) hanta kūḍa-ghaṭṭa-maṇḍalāhaṇḍala, pasīda pasīda! sulukka-kide saaṁ ccea kāruṇṇeṇa geṇha imaṁ jaṇaṁ!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-utprāsam—*with bursting; *vihasya—*laughing; *hanta—*alas; *kūḍa—*the foremost; *ghaṭṭa—*of toll stations; *maṇḍala—*of the multitude; *āhaṇḍala—*the king; *pasīda pasīda—*be pleased, be pleased; *sulukka-kide—*for the payment; *saaṁ—*Yourself; *ccea—*only; *kāruṇṇeṇa—*with compassion; *geṇha—*accept; *imaṁ—*this; *jaṇaṁ—*person.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (laughing from the top of Her lungs): Alas, O king of the best toll stations, have mercy, have mercy! Out of compassion, please accept this person as the tax payment!**

**Комментарий:**

Accepting for fun Kṛṣṇa’s interpretation of Her words, Rādhā now makes a show of humbly pleading Kṛṣṇa to accept Her instead of money.

“O king of the best toll stations” can also be undestood as “O king of fake (*kūṭa*) toll stations.”

### Verse 221-222

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(sphuṭaṁ vihasya) caṇḍi, svārtha-paṇḍitāsi yad upahāsa-mudrayaiva kṛtā kākur bhaṅgi-bhareṇa bhavatyā vāstave paryavasāyyate. tatas tathyam ākarṇaya:*  
*gavya-bhāra-bhara-bhugna-kandharāṁ*  
*tvad-vidhāṁ vidhura-gātri mad-vidhaḥ*  
*spraṣṭum apy ahaha lajjate padā*  
*dainyam ācara na hāsa-dambhataḥ*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sphuṭam—*openly; *vihasya—*laughing; *caṇḍi—*O ferocious One; *sva-artha—*in one’s own interest; *paṇḍitā—*the expert; *asi—*You; *yat—*because; *upahāsa—*of ridicule; *mudrayā—*with an indirect expression; *eva—*certainly; *kṛtā—*made; *kākuḥ—*the pitiful intonation; *bhaṅgi—*of a hint; *bhareṇa—*with the weight; *bhavatyā—*by You; *vāstave—*to be the fact; *paryavasāyyate—*is concluded; *tataḥ—*therefore; *tathyam—*the truth; *ākarṇaya—*listen; gavya—of a cow product; bhāra—the heavy weight; bhara—due to carrying; bhugna—bent; kandharām—whose neck; tvad-vidhām—a person like You; vidhura—distressed; gātri *—*O One, whose body; *mad-vidhaḥ—*a person like Me; *spraṣṭum—*to touch; *api—*even; *ahaha—*ah; *lajjate—*feels embarrased; *padā—*with a foot; *dainyam—*humility; *ācara—*enact; *na—*not; *hāsa—*of ridicule; *dambhataḥ—*on the pretext.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (laughing openly): O ferocious girl, You are expert in the art of achieving Your own ends. Pregnant with a hint, the ambiguous words You have spoken in a pitiful tone to ridicule Me ended up being a fact. So hear now the actual truth: Your neck bends due to the heavy weight of ghee You are carrying, O exhausted One. Ah, a person like Me feels embarrased to touch a girl like You even with his feet. So don’t display humility on the pretext of ridiculing Me.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa is laughing by thinking of the meaning of the verse beginning *‘gavya-bhāra’* that He is just about to utter. But before that He calls Rādhā ‘expert in achieving Her own ends’—giving Herself to Kṛṣṇa. Addressing Rādhā as a “ferocious girl” He ridicules Her: “Before You spoke with so much anger, and now You are requesting Me to accept You?” As seen in text 219, Kṛṣṇa is now playing a part of a true tax collector, who is not interested in anything but money. Indignant, He speaks how Rādhā’s ambiguous words (text 218—‘*appadāṇam*’) have exposed Her true “low” intention behind what was supposed to be His ridicule (text 220).

In the verse beginning *‘gavya-bhāra’* Kṛṣṇa speaks the “truth,” which is His “disgust” at Rādhā’s “immoral” plea to accept Her instead of the tax. That request, as He deems, wasn’t just another statement that implied an opposite meaning. By saying that He doesn’t want to touch Rādhā even with His feet, Kṛṣṇa implies that there is no way for Him to touch Her with His hands. In this way Kṛṣṇa vindicates Himself from being considered a lusty tax collector thirsty after young ladies.

### Verse 223-224

***rādhā:*** *(smitvā) imaṁ ccea mahā-viḍambaṇe bi kijjantimmi suṭṭhu-sakkāra-buddhie dappuddhuradā ṇāma bhaṇīadi —*  
*siṅgāroida-dāmā*  
*viakkhaṇo hosi savvado bhadda*  
*kalide jambula-uḍe*  
*pasāda-maṇaṇeṇa jaṁ phullo*  
*(sarvāḥ sa-śabdaṁ hasanti)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smitvā—*smiling; *imaṁ—*this; *ccea—*only; *mahā-viḍambaṇe—*great mockery; *bi—*even; *kijjantimmi—*when performed; *suṭṭhu—*grand; *sak-kāra—*as honor; *buddhie—*by thinking; *dappa—*of pride; *uddhuradā—*unrestrainedness; *ṇāma—*indeed; *bhaṇīadi—*is said; *siṅga—*on the horns; *aroida—*placed; *dāmā—*with a rope; *viakkhaṇo—*different; *hosi—*You are; *savvado—*in all respects; *bhadda—*O bull; kalide—when given; jambula—putrid; guḍe—molasses; pasāda—as kindness; maṇaṇeṇa—with thinking; jaṁ—because; phullo—smiling; *sarvāḥ—*all; *sa-śabdam—*loudly; *hasanti—*laugh.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (smiling): When one is being utterly ridiculed but thinks that he is being honored—this is exactly what is called “the unrestrained pride.”**

**“O bull, You look different from everybody else with a rope on Your horns, since when given putrid molasses, You look cheerful, taking it as kindness.”**

**(All the gopīs loudly laugh.)**

**Комментарий:**

In text 220, by pleading Kṛṣṇa to accept Her instead of the tax, Rādhā treated Kṛṣṇa as a real tax collector. She also called Him ‘the king of the best toll stations.’ And although with the same words She ridiculed Him (see commentary to text 220), Kṛṣṇa seems like have not noticed the pun and, inspired by the epithet, acted as a truly incorruptible tax collector (221-222). Therefore, in the present speech Rādhā makes fun of Kṛṣṇa. Blinded by excessive pride, He has accepted Her plea to accept Her instead of the tax, and the otherwise pun-intended words ‘king of the best toll stations,’ as meant for honoring Him. To give a vivid example of such excessive and blinding pride She speaks the verse about the bull (224).

Verse 224 could also refer to Kṛṣṇa directly: “O all-auspicious One (*savvato-bhadda—‘sarvato-bhadra’*), You look very special (*viakkhaṇo—‘vilakṣaṇaḥ’*) with the garland on Your head (*siṅga—‘śṛṅga’*). Still, only when You are given a stick of *jambu* tree(*jambu-laguḍe*) You look cheerful, taking it as kindness.” Rādhā means to say that although giving Kṛṣṇa a *jambu* tree stick for grazing cows is certainly a disgrace (since cowherding is not considered a high social occupation), He nevertheless considers it as honor and becomes cheerful.

### Verse 225

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *samprati vāṇī viśrāmyatu, pāṇī hīraka-hāraṁ haratām.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*samprati—*now; *vāṇī—*words; *viśrāmyatu—*let rest; *pāṇī—*both hands; *hīraka—*pearl; *hāram—*necklace; *haratām—*let remove*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Now let words rest and may My hands remove Your pearl necklace.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa is tired of this verbal contest with Rādhā and wants to stop it here. Rādhā, however, doesn’t accept this proposal and continues twisting Kṛṣṇa’s words.

### Verse 226

***rādhā:*** *pāṇi-pallaassa kudo vajjāṇaṁ pphaṁsaṇe sāhasaṁ? tā alaṁ muhāḍobeṇa. esā tumhāṇaṁ pekkhantāṇaṁ calida mhi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*pāṇi-pallaassa—*of One with lotus-like hands; *kudo—*wherefrom; *vajjāṇaṁ—*the lightnings; *pphaṁsaṇe—*in touching; *sāhasaṁ—*boldness; *tā—*thus; *alaṁ—*enough; *muha—*the mouths; *āḍobeṇa—*of opening widely; *esā—*this; *tumhāṇaṁ—*you; *pekkhantāṇaṁ—*while looking; *calida—*gone; *mhi—*I.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: How can You dare to touch thunderbolts with Your lotuslike hands? So don’t open Your mouth anymore. I am leaving in front of your eyes.**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā takes Kṛṣṇa’s words *‘hīraka-hāra’* (“pearl necklace”) to mean “the row (*hāra*) of thunderbolts (*hīraka*).” Kṛṣṇa’s hands are lotuslike in the sense they are as tender as lotuses. Such hands cannot touch Indra’s thunderbolt, which is made from Dadhīci Muni’s bones, and is one of the hardest things in the world.

### Verse 227-228

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *sudīrgha-kuntala-pakṣāsi. tataḥ sphuṭam uḍḍīya gamiṣyasi.*  
***rādhā:*** *savvadā hi sāriā-sahassa-seā-rada, ṇāhaṁ sārī jaṁ uḍḍīassaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sudīrgha—*very long; *kuntala—*of hairlocks; *pakṣā—*have the wings; *asi—*You; *tataḥ—* therefore; *sphuṭam—*clearly; *uḍḍīya—*flying; *gamiṣyasi—*You will go. *savvadā—*at all times; *hi—*indeed; *sāriā—*of Mynas; *sahassa—*to thousands; *seā—*the service; *rada—*fond of; *ṇa—*not; *ahaṁ—*I; *śārī—*Myna; *jaṁ—*that; *uḍḍīyassaṁ—*I will fly.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: The locks of Your very long hair are Your wings. So, You will literally fly away.**

**Rādhā: O You, who are ever fond of serving thousands of Mynas, I am not one that I would fly.**

**Комментарий:**

If the first part of the sentence is split as *‘savvadā ahisāriā-seā-rada’* (*‘sarvadā abhisārikā-sevā-rata’*)*,* it would mean “O One ever fond of service from thousands of rendezvous girls (*abhisārikā*).” In this way, Rādhā indirectly calls Kṛṣṇa a libertine, who enjoys women in the forest.

### Verse 229

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  
*lolākṣa-dāya-bhajanād*  
*añṭāpadam abhi dhṛtāsi puraḥ*  
*iti sārī bhavasi tvaṁ*  
*śṛṅkhalayiñyāmy ato bhavatīm*  
*(iti pāṇim ādhātum icchati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*lola—*tossed; *akṣa—*of the two dice; *dāya—*in the game; *bhajanāt—*due to taking part; *aṣṭā-padam—*at the dice-board; *abhi—*aiming; *dhṛtā—*focused; *asi—*You; *puraḥ—*in front; *iti—*therefore; *sārī—*a dice player; *bhavasi—*are; *tvam—*You; *śṛṅkhalayiṣyāmi—*I will bind; *ataḥ—*therefore; *bhavatīm—*You; *iti—*thus; *pāṇim—*His hand; *ādhātum—*to place; *icchati—*desires.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: By taking part in a dice game, You are focused on the gameboard in front of You. Thus, You are a dice player, and therefore I shall corner You.**

**(wishes to put His hand on Rādhā)**

**Комментарий:**

Although Rādhā has just said that She is not a *śārī* (parrot), Kṛṣṇa shows that She is. However, He uses the word *śārī* with its another meaning—“dice player.” Kṛṣṇa says that He will corner Rādhā. This could refer to some position in a dice game that Kṛṣṇa is thinking of, where the defeated player has no room to move on the board, like checkmate in chess.

The actual message of Kṛṣṇa, which He hides behind the word jugglery, is this: “Since You are holding (*bhajanāt*) My share (*dāya*), I have halted (*dhṛta*) You in front of Me (*puraḥ*). My eyes are restless (*lola-akṣa*) and I am after My gold(*aṣṭāpadam abhi*). Thus You *are* an escaper (*sārī*), and therefore I shall arrest (*śṛṅkhalayiṣyāmi*) You \[in My arms\].”

### Verse 230

***rādhā:*** *haddhī haddhī! ṇūṇaṁ eso mahā-mammahassa seāe pahāvo jaṁ paivvadā-pphaṁse pāpādo de bhaaṁ ṇatthi!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*haddhī haddhī—*shame, shame; *ṇūṇaṁ—*indeed; *eso—*this; *mahā-manmahassa—*to Mahā-manmatha; *seāe—*of the service; *pahāvo—*the influence; *jaṁ—*that; *paivvadā—*the chaste wives; *pphaṁse—*in touching; *pāpādo—*of the sin; *de—*for You; *bhaaṁ—*fear; *ṇatthi—*there isn’t.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: Shame, shame! This is indeed the result of the serving His Majesty Mahā-manmatha: You are not afraid of the sin of touching a chaste lady!**

**Комментарий:**

The imaginary emperor Mahā-manmatha is Cupid, the great churner (*matha*) of the mind (*man*). When one’s discretion is taken away by Cupid, one doesn’t fear to approach with amorous intention even a married woman.

### Verse 231

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(smitvā) bhāvini, satyaṁ tvam utkopa-patau baddha-vratāsi. atas tavoru-sevāyām abhilāṣuko ’smi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smitvā—*smiling; *bhāvini—*O noble lady; *satyam—*truth; *tvam—*You; *utkopa—*furious; *patau—*to Your husband; *baddha—*bound; *vratā—*by the vow; *asi—*are; *ataḥ—*therefore; *tava—*Your; *uru—*excellent; *sevāyām—*of service; *abhilāṣukaḥ—*the coveter; *asmi—*I.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (smiling): O noble One, it is true that You are bound by a vow to Your furious husband. That is why I strongly desire to honor You in the best way.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa has noticed that Rādhā was unable to say something that would have a double meaning—the fear that He would touch Her has paralysed Her intelligence. This is the reason behind Kṛṣṇa’s smile, as revealed in *Mahaṭī-ṭīkā.*

Speaking ambiguously, Kṛṣṇa indirectly expresses His amorous request: “O noble One, it is true that You are bound by a vow to Me, Your passionate (*utka*) paramour (*upapatau*). That is why I strongly desire to honor Your (*tava*) thighs (*ūru*).” Or else, the first part could be understood as: “…it is true that You are eager (*utkā*) for Your paramour…” If the statement is understood in any of these ways, Kṛṣṇa’s addressing Rādhā as a “noble One” is ironic, for a noble lady will never have a paramour.

### Verse 232-233

***rādhā:*** *(sa-praṇaya-roṣam) vaṅka-vidaṇḍā-paṇḍida, viramehi! kulaṅgaṇā-pphaṁsaṇaṁ kkhu accāhida-ppadaṁ hodi.*  
***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *kulīnam-manye, kim aham akulīno, yad adya bhavatyās tanu-sparśe ’pi me ’naucitī?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa—*with; *praṇaya—*of love; *roṣam—*anger; *vaṅka—*crooked; *vidaṇḍā—*arguments; *paṇḍida—*O expert; *viramehi—*stop; *kula-aṅgaṇā—*of the noble ladies; *pphaṁsaṇaṁ—*the touching; *kkhu—*indeed; *accāhida—*of a great calamity; *padaṁ—*the giver; *hodi—*is. *kulīnam-manye***—**O One who deems Herself a noblewoman; *kim—*am; *aham—*I; *akulīno—*not noble; *yat—*that; *adya—*today; *bhavatyāḥ—*Your; *tanu—*body; *sparśe—*in touching; *api—*even; *me—*My; *anaucitī—*inaproppriateness.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: O expert in crooked captious arguments, please stop! Polluting noble ladies brings great calamity.**

**Kṛṣṇa: O girl who deems Herself a noblewoman, have I become low-class, that today it has become improper for Me to even touch Your body?**

### Verse 234-235

***rādhā:*** *kulīṇa-jaṇāṇaṁ kira evvaṁ caridaṁ jaṁ ṇijjaṇa-vaṇe para-vaṇidāṇaṁ ṇirundhaṇeṇa edaṁ viḍambaṇaṁ.*  
***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *kāmini, parā tvaṁ vanitāsi iti vane ’tra manyase. tena nitarāṁ vitarādya ghaṭṭa-dānam.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kulīṇa-jaṇāṇaṁ—*of noble people; *kira—*indeed; *evvaṁ—*in this way; *caridaṁ—*behavior; *jaṁ—*that; *ṇijjaṇa—*in the solitary; *vaṇe—*forest; *para—*of others; *vaṇidāṇaṁ—*the wives; *ṇirundhaṇeṇa—*by stopping; *edaṁ—*this; *viḍambaṇaṁ—*disgrace. *kāmini—*O passionate One; *parā—*superior; *tvam—*You; *vanitā—*wife; *asi—*are; *iti—*thus; *vane—*in the forest; *atra—*this; *manyase—*You are considered; *tena—*because of that; *nitarām—*by all means; *vitara—*give; *adya—*now; *ghaṭṭa-dānam—*the toll tax.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: The ways of the noble are such indeed, that, in comparison to them, this behavior of Yours—stopping others’ wives in a solitary forest—is a disgrace.**

**Kṛṣṇa: O passionate One, so You think You are the high-class lady in this forest. Then by all means You should pay the entire tax.**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā considers Herself to be another’s wife (*para-vaṇidāṇam—‘para-vanitānām’*). Kṛṣṇa, however, cleverly takes *para* (“another”) with another meaning—“high.” Rādhā, therefore, is bound to pay the tax, since high-class people never avoid paying taxes.

### Verse 236

***rādhā:*** *mohaṇa, jadhā tumhādiseṇa takkīyadi tadhā eso jaṇo ṇa hodi. tā ettha bhammanta-bhū-bhuaṅga-juala-ṇaccaṇeṇa āhituṇḍiadā-līlāḍambarehiṁ alaṁ. dullahā de ettha sulukka-bhikkhā!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mohaṇa—*O trickster; *jadhā—*how; *tumhā-diseṇa—*by one like You; *takkīyadi—*considered; *tadhā—*like that; *eso—*this; *jaṇo—*person; *ṇa hodi—*is not; *tā—*therefore; *ettha—*here; *bhammanta—*moving; *bhū-bhuaṅga—*the eyebrow serpents; *juala—*of the two; *ṇaccaṇeṇa—*by dancing; *āhi-tuṇḍiadā—*of being a serpent-catcher; *līlā—*of the pastime; *āḍambarehiṁ—*of the tumult; *alaṁ—*enough; *dullahā—*difficult to get; *de—*for You; *ettha—*here; *sulukka—*for the payment; *bhikkhā—*the begging.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: O trickster, this person is not what someone like You might think Her to be. Enough of Your big pretense to be a serpent catcher with the dancing of Your quivering snakelike eyebrows. You will not get Your tax!**

**Комментарий:**
