Calling Kṛṣṇa a “trickster,” Rādhā expresses Her joy, pride and pretense, all of which have come about due to Her present refractory mood. By saying the She is not like what Kṛṣṇa might think Her to be, Rādhā implies that She is not a girl who is going to submit herself to Him so easily. Rādhā reviles Kṛṣṇa’s vain attempts to capture Her with the movements of His eyebrows, as if He were a serpent catcher.

### Verse 237

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  
*ayi sukale varam adhunā*  
*śulkaṁ tvāṁ dātum udyatāṁ prekñya*  
*paramotsava-caṭuleyaṁ*  
*kurute bhrū-nartakī nṛtyam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ayi—*O; *sukale—*generous One; *varam—*best; *adhunā—*now; *śulkam—*the fee; *tvām—*at You; *dātum—*to give; *udyatām—*intent; *prekṣya—*looking; *parama—*topmost; *utsava—*due to the festival; *caṭulā—*unsteady; *iyam—*this; *kurute—*performs; *bhrū-nartakī—*the eyebrow dancer; *nṛtyam—*the dance.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O generous One, by seeing You about to pay the best of taxes, this eyebrow-dancer, excited due to this greatest festival, is dancing.**

**Комментарий:**

Here Kṛṣṇa explains why His eyebrows are dancing. The first half of the verse can be understood in another way: “By seeing You about to pay the best of taxes to One with a beautiful hood (*su-kale*)…” *‘Su-kale,’* as “One with a beautiful hood” can also refer to Rādhā, describing Her as a serpent. This makes sense here, because in Her last statement Rādhārāṇī addressed Kṛṣṇa as the serpent-catcher. Or else, *‘sukale’* and *‘varam’* could be accepted as one word: “Ah, by seeing You about to offer Your beautiful body (*su-kalevaram*) as the tax, this eyebrow-dancer, excited due to this greatest festival, is dancing.”

### Verse 238

***Rādhā:*** *(saṁskṛtena)*  
*lohamayy alaghu-kṛñṇa-vartmanaḥ*  
*stambhinī pratikṛtiḥ sphuraty asau*  
*yatra yānti kuhakasya bandhyatāṁ*  
*bhūri-bhoga-bharitasya cāśiñaḥ*

**Пословный перевод:**

*saṁskṛtena—*(speaks) in Sanskrit; *lohamayī—*made of metal; *alaghu—*not light; *kṛṣṇa-vartmanaḥ—*to fire; *stambhinī—*resisting; *pratikṛtiḥ—*a statue; *sphurati—*appears; *asau—*that (girl); *yatra—*where; *yānti—*attain; *kuhakasya—*of the Kuhaka serpent; *bandhyatām—*the condition of being stuck; *bhūri—*many; *bhoga—*hoods; *bharitasya—*having; *ca—*also; *āśiṣaḥ—*fangs.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (in Sanskrit): This girl is like an iron statue resistant to strong fire. The fangs of the many-hooded Kuhaka serpent are useless against Her.**

**Комментарий:**

By the word *‘stambhinī,’* which here can mean “stunned,” Rādhā describes the present condition of Her body, which was brought about due to Her transcendental joy. Also, even if Kṛṣṇa is a serpent with beautiful hoods (as He has described Himself in the previous verse), Rādhā is not going to surrender to Him easily, just as a golden statue won’t easily give in to the heat of strong fire.

Apart from this, the first half of the verse can be alternatively understood in the following ways: “This girl is like a golden (*lohamayī*) statue that resists (*stambhinī*) Kṛṣṇa’s tenacious (*alaghu*) ways (*vartmanaḥ*),” or “…that resists the assured (*alaghu*) way (*vartmanaḥ*) of giving Herself to Kṛṣṇa.” The last two lines then would carry the following meaning: “The desires (*āśiṣaḥ*) of this trickster (*kuhakasya*), who is full (*bharitasya*) of eagerness for abundant (*bhūri*) amorous enjoyment (*bhoga*), are frustrated (*bandhyatām yānti*) in Her.”

### Verse 239

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  
*pratimāsy adbhutā rādhe*  
*bahu-lohamayī dhruvam*  
*tataḥ svayaṅ-grahāśleñaṁ*  
*cumbake mayy urī-kuru*  
*(iti mandaṁ mandam upasarpati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

pratimā—a statue; asi—You are; adbhutā—wonderful; rādhe—O Rādhā; bahu—a lot; lohamayī—made of gold; dhruvam—certainly; tataḥ—therefore; svayam-grahā—\[You,\] who decides Yourself what to accept; āśleñam—clinging; cumbake—the magnet; mayi—to Me; urī-kuru—accept. *iti—*thus; *mandam mandam—*very slowly; *upasarpati—*approaching.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O Rādhā, since You are truly a wonderful deity made of so much iron, and decide Yourself what to accept, please accept clinging onto Me, who am the magnet.**

**(saying this, Kṛṣṇa very slowly gets near Rādhā)**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa makes up a logic why Rādhā should accept His embrace—She is a metal deity and He is the magnet. Kṛṣṇa tactfully implores Rādhā to accept His embrace, for, as a deity, she is free to accept items of worship of Her own choice. Nobody can oblige a deity to accept everything that one has to offer.

Another meaning: “O Rādhā, every month (*‘prati-māsi’*) of the year You are truly wonderful. And since you are also very thoughtful (*‘bahula-ūhamayī’*) and decide Yourself what to accept, please accept the embrace (*āśleṣam*) of Me, the kisser (*‘cumbake’*).” With this in mind, Kṛṣṇa slowly approaches Rādhā to fulfill His desire.

The words “every month of the year You \[Rādhā\] are truly wonderful” imply that Rādhā’s youth is ever-fresh.

## Verse 240(a-b)

***rādhā:***  *(manāk parāvṛtya) avehi avehi! (ārta-svaram) mā dharehi!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*manāk—*slightly; *parāvṛtya—*turning away; *avehi avehi—*go away, go away; *ārta-svaram—*with a voice expressing distress; *mā—*don’t; *dharehi—*hold*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (slightly turning away): Go away, go away! (with a voice expressing distress) Don’t touch me!**

**Комментарий:**

*‘Mā dharehi’* can be also understood as “embrace Me (*mā*).” Though outwardly scornful, Rādhā accepts Kṛṣṇa’s proposal.

### Verse 241

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  
*lakñaiś catur-aśītyā hi*  
*śulkair vinimayaṁ gatām*  
*na yauvata-śikhā-ratnaṁ*  
*kutas tvāṁ dhārayāmy aham*  
*(iti didhīrṣuḥ prasarpati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

lakṣai*ḥ—*for lakhs; *catur-aśītyā—*eighty-four; *hi—*indeed; *śulkaiḥ—*payable; *vinimayam—*the exchange; *gatām—*became; na*—*not; *yauvata—*among the young girls; *śikhā-ratnam—*the crest jewel; *kutaḥ—*how; *tvām—*of You; *dhārayāmi—*will take hold; *aham—*I; *iti—*saying this; *didhīrṣuḥ--*desirous to take hold; *prasarpati—*advances forward.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: How will I not take hold of You, the crest jewel of young girls, who is an equivalent to the tax of eighty-four lakh golden ṭaṅkas?**

**(advances forward, wanting to catch hold of Rādhā)**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa has taken *‘mā dharehi’* as “embrace Me,” and replies with a rhetorical question “How will I not embrace You, who is so valuable?”

## Revision Of The Tax

### Verse 242

***rādhā:***  *(sa-sambhramam abhinīya sāci vicalantī) lalide, tumaṁ kiṁ kkhu kudūhalaṁ pekkhasi?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-sambhramam—*with bewilderment; *abhinīya—*acting; *sāci—*on the left; *vicalantī—*moving away; *lalide—*O Lalitā; *tumaṁ—*you; *kiṁ—*what; *kkhu—*indeed; *kudūhalaṁ—*fun; *pekkhasi—*are watching.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (bewildered, moving away to the left): Lalitā, are you just watching the “fun,” or what?**

**Комментарий:**

By reproaching Lalitā for her inaction in the present situation, Rādhārāṇī urges her to come for help.

### Verse 243

***nāndīmukhī:*** *sahi rāhe, alaṁ imiṇā suṭṭhu kuṭṭamideṇa. kiṁ tti palāyissasi?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sahi—*O friend; *rāhi—*Rādhā; *alaṁ—*enough; *imiṇā—*of this; *suṭṭhu—*well; *kuṭṭamideṇa—*loving repulse; *kiṁ tti—*why; *palāyissasi—*You will escape*.*

**Перевод:**

**Nandīmukhī: My friend Rādhā, stop repelling so vehemently. Why would You run away?**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā is exhibiting *‘kuṭṭamita’—*one of the feminine attitudes in *mādhurya-rasa.* Along with other ecstatic attitudes, *kuṭṭamita* is like an “ornament” of Rādhā’s body, by which She stirs the ocean of Kṛṣṇa’s happiness.

*stanādharādi-grahaṇe hṛt-prītāv api sambhramāt*  
*bahiḥ krodho vyathitavat proktaṁ kuṭṭamitaṁ budhaiḥ*

**“When the lover touches the girl’s breast, lips, and so on, the girl, although happy at heart, out of confusion puts on an outward show of anger, as if hurt. Learned scholars call this attitude** ***kuṭṭamita*****.”**

*Kuṭṭamita* is also described in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 14.200):

*pāṇi-rodham avirodhita-vāñchaṁ*  
*bhartsanāś ca madhura-smita-garbhāḥ*  
*mādhavasya kurute karabhorur*  
*hāri śuṣka-ruditaṁ ca mukhe ’pi*

**“Actually She has no desire to stop Kṛṣṇa’s endeavor to touch Her body with His hands, yet Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, whose thighs are like the trunk of a baby elephant, protests His advances and, sweetly smiling, admonishes Him. At such times She cries without tears on Her charming face.’**

Nāndīmukhī gives friendly advice to Rādhā not to reject Kṛṣṇa, but to fulfill both His and Her desires.

### Verse 244

***lalitā:*** *(puraḥ parikramya) ja{i}bi dullalida-sīlāṇaṁ luṇṭhaāṇaṁ tumhāṇaṁ dāṇa-gaṇaṇā-palābaṁ ṇa kkhu amhe kaṇṇa-perante bi appamha tahabi kiṁ pi bhaṇidu-kāmamhi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*puraḥ—*in front; *parikramya—*coming; *ja{i}bi—*even if; *dullalida—*rough; *sīlāṇaṁ—*whose nature; *luṇṭhaāṇaṁ—*robbers; *tumhāṇaṁ—*of you; *dāṇa—*the tax; *gaṇaṇā—*counting; *palābaṁ—*talk; *ṇa—*not; *kkhu—*indeed; *amhe—*we; *kaṇṇa—*of the ear; *perante—*the edge; *bi—*even; *appamha—*gave; *tahabi—*still; *kiṁ pi—*something; *bhaṇidu-kāma—*wish to say; *mhi—*I.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (coming in front of Kṛṣṇa): Even though we didn’t bent an ear even slightly to your tax calculation, you rude freebooters, still I wish to say something.**

**Комментарий:**

For quite some time Lalitā was submerged in the nectarean ocean of Rādhā’s and Kṛṣṇa’s eloquence. But now, asked to help her desperate friend, she again boldly speaks out.

### Verse 245-246

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *kaṭhine, kāmaṁ bhaṇyatām.*  
***lalitā:*** *(saṁskṛtena)*  
*amūr vraja-mṛgekñaṇāś catur-aśīti-lakñādhikāḥ*  
*pratisvam iti kīrtitaṁ savayasā tavaivāmunā*  
*ihāpi bhuvi viśrutā priya-sakhī mahārghety asau*  
*kathaṁ tad api sāhasī śaṭha jighṛkñur enām asi*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kaṭhine—*O harsh one; *kāmam—*as desired; *bhaṇyatām—*please say; *saṁskṛtena*—in Sanskrit; *amūḥ—*these; *vraja—*of Vraja; *mṛga—*doe-like; *īkṣaṇāḥ—*with eyes; *catur-aśīti—*than eighty-four; *lakṣa—*lakhs; *adhikāḥ—*more; *pratisvam—*each one; *iti—*thus; *kīrtitam—*praised; *savayasā—*by the friend; *tava—*Your; *eva—*only; *amunā—*this; *iha—*here; *api—*also; *bhuvi—*in the world; *viśrutā—*famous; *priya—*dear; *sakhī—*friend; *mahā-arghā—*very precious; *iti—*thus; *asau—*this; *katham—*how; *tat api—*still; *sāhasī—*aggressive; *śaṭha—*O cheater; *jighṛkṣuḥ—*desiring to seize; *enām—*Her; *asi—*You are.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O harsh girl, please say whatever you wish.**

**Lalitā (in Sanskrit): Your friend has declared, that each one of these doe-eyed Vraja girls is more valuable than eighty-four lakhs of golden ṭaṅkas. Also, it is well known that my dear sakhī is the most precious of even all of us. How is that You, an aggressive cheater, want to seize particularly Her?**

**Комментарий:**

Lalitā mentions Madhumaṅgala’s statement in text 199, where he purported Nāndīmukhī’s words by saying that each gopī is more valuable than eighty-four lakhs of golden *ṭaṅkas*. Lalitā wants to expose Kṛṣṇa’s actual motives with her sharp question as to why He wants to seize Rādhā, who is much more valuable than eighty-four lakhs of golden ṭaṅkas, which is the amount of the tax.

Lalitā is a typical example of a *vāma* (left-wing, or refractory) girl:

*māna-grahe sadodyuktā*  
*tac-chaithilye ca kopanā*  
*abhedyā nāyake prāyaḥ*  
*krūrā vāmeti kīrtyate*

**“A girl who is always ready to get angry at her lover, who is infuriated by his mere attempt to cool her down, and who is rough with him for the most time, is called** ***vāma*****.”**

The current verse of *Dāna-keli-kaumudī,* quoted in *Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi* 8.36, illustrates how such *vāma* girl is rough with the boy (*nāyake krūrā*). Viśvanātha Cakravartī says that Lalitā calls Kṛṣṇa *‘sāhasī’* (“aggressor”) with this in mind: “Are you not afraid of even the reproach from the king for taking Rādhā, who is more valuable than the calculated tax amout?” She also calls him *‘śaṭha’* (“cheater”): “You are a cheater, and therefore, it is better to call the King’s horsemen to settle the dispute.”

Although Lalitā is as qualified as to be the leader of her own group of gopīs (*yūtheśvarī*), she is so devoted and obedient to Rādhā, that she refuses the status of being a *yūtheśvarī* independent of Rādhā and recognizes Rādhā to be the supreme *yūtheśvarī*. Declaring in this verse that Rādhā is the most precious of all the gopīs, Lalitā thinks: “My *yūtheśvarī* cannot be compared to any other gopī.”

### Verse 247-248

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(sva-gatam) bāḍhaṁ nirvacanī-kṛto ’smi.*  
*(iti sa-vyājam utkarṇo bhavan) subala, keyaṁ gabhīra-dharmāpi dhvani-dhoraṇī vidūratvād asphuṭeva prasarantī mām uccālayati. (iti subalasya karṇe lagati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sva-gatam—*to Himself; *bāḍham—*definitely; *nirvacanī—*speechless; *kṛtaḥ—*made; *asmi—*I; *iti—*thus; *sa-vyājam—*with pretense; *utkarṇaḥ—*attentive listener; *bhavan—*becoming; *subala—*O Subala; *kā—*what; *iyam—*this; *gabhīra-dharmā—*heavy by nature; *api—*though; *dhvani—*of sounds; *dhoraṇī—*succession; *vidūratvāt—*due to being far away; *asphuṭā—*indistinct; *iva—*as if; *prasarantī—*spreading; *mām—*Me; *uccālayati—*agitate; *iti—*thus; *subalasya—*to Subala’s; *karṇe—*ear; *lagati—*clings.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (to Himself): All right, You made Me speechless.**

**(pretentiously listening with rapt attention): Subala, what are those sounds that agitate Me? Even though heavy, they are somewhat indistinct because of coming from far away.**

**(clings to Subala’s ear)**

**Комментарий:**

According to *Mahaṭī-ṭīkā*, Kṛṣṇa is unable to answer Lalitā on the spot. To cover up His confusion He pretends as if He does not care for Lalitā’s question and diverts everybody’s attention somewhere else. Kṛṣṇa whispers something into Subala’s ear, as if to make the gopīs speculate about what He could have whispered. They could have guessed that Kṛṣṇa spoke this: “Subala, if this is the sound of the king’s mighty army forces, then go, inform the king about everything, and bring Me the punishment act.”

### Verse 249

***subalaḥ****: eso kolāhalassa ppahavaṁ viṇṇāduṁ calido mhi. (iti niṣkrāntaḥ)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*eso—*this; *kolāhalassa—*of the noise; *ppahavaṁ—*the origin; *viṇṇāduṁ—*to know; *calido—*going; *mhi—*I; *iti—*thus; *niṣkrāntaḥ—*went off (the stage).

**Перевод:**

**Subala: I’m going to see where this noise is coming from. (going off the stage)**

### Verse 250

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *kaṭhora-bhāṣiṇi lalite, bhavatu bhavatyāḥ sakhī catur-aśīti-lakṣādhikā tathāpi koṭiṁ nātikramiṣyaty eva. tataḥ parair api kalā-lakṣais tvām avaśyaṁ nāgara-candro ’yaṁ yojayiṣyati.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kaṭhora—*rough; *bhāṣiṇi—*whose speech; *lalite—*O Lalitā; *bhavatu—*let it be; *bhavatyāḥ—*your; *sakhī—*friend; *catur-aśīti-lakṣa—*than eighty-four lakhs; *adhikā—*more; *tathā api—*still; *koṭim—*one crore; *na—*not; *atikramiṣyati—*will surpass; *eva—*indeed; *tataḥ—*therefore; *paraiḥ—*with other; *api—*also; *kalā—*sixteen; *lakṣaiḥ—*lakhs; *tvām—*you; *avaśyam—*necessarily; *nāgara—*among the elegant boys; *candraḥ—*moon; *ayam—*this; *yojayiṣyati—*will add.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O sharp-tongued Lalitā, even though your friend costs more than eighty-four lakhs, still, Her value will never surpass one crore. So, this moon among the suave boys will have to add you as the other sixteen lakhs ṭaṅkas of gold.**

**Комментарий:**

By now Kṛṣṇa has gathered His wits to answer Lalitā’s question befittingly. This is the logic behind His answer: The flat tax amount is eighty lakhs *ṭaṅkas*, and, according to the law, the quarter of this amount (twenty lakhs) should be added for the maintenance of the toll gate staff, making the final amount one crore (hundred lakhs). From text 194, however, we understand that Madhumaṅgala has decreased the twenty lakh addition to only four lakhs, expecting some butter from the gopīs as a reward for his favor. In this way, he calculated the final amount of the tax to be eighty-four lakhs, which is sixteen lakhs less than the due sum—one crore. Kṛṣṇa, by saying that He will have to add Lalitā in place of the other sixteen lakhs (that were discounted by Madhumaṅgala), shows that He still plans to justly exact one fourth of the flat tax amount for the toll gate staff. To achieve that, He intends to add sixteen lakhs worth Lalitā to Rādhā, who is worth at least eighty four lakhs (text 199).

As pointed out by Viśvanātha Cakravartī, the second sentence can be also understood in another way: “So, this smart moon (*‘nāgara-candro’*) will have to add You as its ‘other’ sixteen phases (*‘kalā-lakṣais’*).” Because the moon has only sixteen phases anyway, the statement suggests that Kṛṣṇa will accept Lalitā as His own self.

### Verse 251

***rādhā:*** *ṇandīmuhi, bhaavadīe ṇidesassa paḍibālaṇaṁ sāhu saṁvuttaṁ jaṁ koḍi-guṇī-bhūdaṁ ccea ghaṭṭa-dāṇaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ṇandīmuhi—*O Nandīmukhī; *bhaavadīe—*of Bhagavatī (Paurṇamāsī); *ṇidesassa—*of the order; *paḍibālaṇaṁ—*the execution; *sāhu—*well; *saṁvuttaṁ—*happened; *jaṁ—*that; *koḍi-guṇī—*one crore times; *bhūdaṁ—*became (increased); *ccea—*certainly; *ghaṭṭa-dāṇaṁ—*the tax.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: Nandīmukhī, the order of Bhagavatī is ‘well executed’: the tax is increased by one crore times.**

**Комментарий:**

In the message which Nāndīmukhī delivered to Kṛṣṇa, Paurṇamāsī requested that He should be favorable to the gopīs in regard to collecting the tax (see text 187). Rādhārāṇī sarcastically remarks that the order is “well executed.” In other words, She doesn’t notice any leniency on the part of Kṛṣṇa.

### Verse 252

***nāndīmukhī:***  *sahi rāhe, kahaṁ paḍivālaṇaṁ ṇa saṁvuttaṁ jaṁ sulukkassa ti-hāo geṇhīadi?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sahi—*O friend; *rāhe—*Rādhā; *kahaṁ—*how; *paḍivālaṇaṁ—*execution; *ṇa—*not; *saṁvuttaṁ—*happened; *jaṁ—*because; *sulukkassa—*of the fee; *ti-hāo—*one third; *geṇhīadi—*is taken*.*

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: My friend Rādhā, how is that it is not executed? Only one third of the fee is exacted.**

**Комментарий:**

Nāndīmukhī tries to convince Rādhā by referring to Kṛṣṇa’s statement that instead of exacting three *ṭaṅkas* of gold per one *ṭaṅka* of goods, He takes only one *ṭaṅka* (text 189).

### Verse 253

***subalaḥ:*** *(praviśya savayasyaḥ) pia-vaassa, ṇia-vāhiṇī-ṇigghosa-vahirī-kida-disā-maṇḍalā. vijenti ujjāṇa-cakkavaṭṭi-sīhāo!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*praviśya—*entering; *sa-vayasyaḥ—*with a friend; *pia-vaassa—*O dear friend; *ṇia—*own; *vāhiṇī—*of the army; *ṇigghosa—*by the sound; *vahirī-kida—*made deaf; *disā-maṇḍalā—*all surroundings; *vijenti—*may be victorious; *ujjāṇa—*of groves; *cakkavaḍḍi—*among the emperors; *sīhāo—*the lion.

**Перевод:**

**Subalaḥ (entering with his friend): Dear friend, all glories to the lion among the forest grove emperors, whose army makes the surroundings deaf with its tumult!**

**Комментарий:**

Subala brings the news that the imaginary noise (text 248) was created by the army of His Majesty Mahā-manmatha.

### Verse 254-255

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *priya-vayasya ujjvala, nūnaṁ lekha-hāro ’si cakravartinām.*  
***ujjvalaḥ:*** *adha{i}ṁ! mahā-bhaṭṭāraassa mahā-ghaṭṭā-hiāre eso leho. (iti kṛṣṇa-kare ketakī-koraka-patram arpayati. kṛṣṇaḥ savyājam aśabdam eva lekha-vācana-mudrām abhinayati.)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*priya-vayasya—*O dear friend; *ujjvala—*Ujjvala; *nūnam—*indeed; *lekha-hāraḥ—*the letter-carrier; *asi—*you are; *cakravartinām—*of the emperor; *adha{i}ṁ—*how otherwise; *mahā-bhaṭṭāraassa—*of the great king; *mahā-ghaṭṭā—*over the great toll station; *hiāre—*in the matter of the authority; *eso—*this; *leho—*letter; *iti—*thus; *kṛṣṇa-kare—*in Kṛṣṇa’s hand; *ketakī-koraka—*on the bud of *ketakī* flower; *patram—*a letter; *arpayati—*places; *kṛṣṇaḥ—*Kṛṣṇa; *sa-vyājam—*with pretense; *aśabdam—*inaudibly; *eva—*only; *lekha—*the letter; *vācana—*of reading; *mudrām—*the gesture; *abhinayati—*acts out.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Dear friend Ujjvala, you are indeed the postman of the emperor.**

**Ujjvala: Of course! This is His Majesty’s letter in the matter of Your supervision over the main toll station. (Places the letter written on the ketakī bud in Kṛṣṇa’s hand. Kṛṣṇa pretends to inaudibly read the letter to Himself.)**

### Verse 256

***vṛndā:*** *nāgarendra, vayam apy ākarṇayitum icchāmo varṇa-dūtam. tan mukha-bandham utsṛjya kāryam eva samuccāryatām.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nāgara-indra—*O best of the smart; *vayam—*we; *api—*also; *ākarṇayitum—*to hear; *icchāmaḥ—*wish; *varṇa—*letter; *dūtam—*the messenger; *tat—*therefore; *mukha-bandham—*the preface; *utsṛjya—*leaving aside; *kāryam—*the matter; *eva—*only; *samuccāryatām—*speak aloud.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: O best of the suave boys, we also wish to hear the letter. So please, skip the introduction and read out the main matter.**

### Verse 257

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(spaṣṭaṁ vācayati)*  
*pāṇḍityaṁ caṇḍa-dhāmnaḥ paricaraṇa-vidhau prāpya gūḍhoru-garvāḥ*  
*kurvāṇā ghaṭṭa-ghātaṁ ghaṭita-nikṛtayaḥ subhruvo vibhramanti*  
*kartavyas tāsu yatnaḥ paṭima-paricayād apramattair bhavadbhir*  
*drāghiṣṭhaṁ chadma dṛṣṭvā kim api śata-guṇas tatra śulko vidheyaḥ*

**Пословный перевод:**

*spaṣṭam—*crearly; *vācayati—*reads out; *pāṇḍityam—*expertise; *caṇḍa-dhāmnaḥ—*of the sun; *paricaraṇa—*of the service; *vidhau—*in the manner; *prāpya—*obtaining; *gūḍha—*concealed; *uru—*big; *garvāḥ—*pride; *kurvāṇāḥ—*showing; *ghaṭṭa-ghātam—*the defiance of toll gates; *ghaṭita—*who perform; *nikṛtayaḥ—*cheating; *subhruvaḥ—*girls with beautifully shaped eyebrows; *vibhramanti—*wander around; *kartavyaḥ—*should be made; *tāsu—*in their case; *yatnaḥ—*an endeavor; *paṭima—*of wit; *paricayāt—*by accumulation; *apramattaiḥ—*alert; *bhavadbhiḥ—*by You; *drāghiṣṭham—*very long; *chadma—*deceit; *dṛṣṭvā—*seeing; *kim api—*some; *śata-guṇaḥ—*hundred times; *tatra—*from them; *śulkaḥ—*the fee; *vidheyaḥ—*should be exacted.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (reads out audibly): Certain knavish girls with beatifully shaped eyebrows harbor great pride by becoming expert worshippers of the sun god. They are wandering around, defying the toll gates. Gather Your wits and try to tax them. But be alert. When you see them lying in their teeth, exact a hundred times fee from them.**

### Verse 258

***nāndīmukhī:***  *dāṇinda, paidi-visuddhāṇaṁ imāṇaṁ kudo kūḍa-lesa-sikkhāhilāso bi?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dāṇinda—*O king of tax collectors; *paidi—*by nature; *visuddhāṇaṁ—*pure; *imāṇaṁ—*these; *kudo—*wherefrom; *kūḍa—*cheating; *lesa—*a little bit; *sikkha—*for learning; *ahilāso—*the desire; *bi—*even.

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: O king of tax collectors, how could these pure-hearted girls even wish to learn how to cheat even slightly?**

### Verse 259-261

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *tathāpy avaśyam anuṣṭheyam iti kāntārādhirājasya tasya mahā-śāsanam.*  
*(iti kiñcid upasṛtya) hanta, citram idaṁ yad etat samyag-anivṛtta-śaiśavānām apy amūṣāṁ nirbharam ucchūnam uraḥ samīkṣyate!*  
*(punar nirbhālya) kathaṁ vā varāmbara-saṁvṛtād api vakṣasaḥ kāñcanamayyo mayūkha-vīcayaḥ sañcaranti? (rādhā sābhyasūyaṁ tiro dṛgantaṁ pātayati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tathā api—*still; *avaśyam—*necessary; *anuṣṭheyam—*should be executed; *iti—*thus; *kāntāra—*of the forest; *adhirājasya—*of the emperor; *tasya—*that; *mahā-śāsanam—*the prime order; *iti—*thus; *kiñcit—*slightly; *upasṛtya—*coming near; *hanta—*ah; *citram—*surprising; *idam—*this; *yat—*that; *etat—*these; *samyak—*completely; *anivṛtta—*not ceased; *śaiśavānām—*whose childhood; *api—*even; *amūṣām—*of these (girls); *nirbharam—*excessively; *ucchūnam—*swollen; *uraḥ—*their breasts; *samīkṣyate—*are seen; *punaḥ—*again; *nirbhālya—*looking; *katham—*how; *vā—*indeed; *vara-ambara—*with best garments; *saṁvṛtāt—*covered; *api—*even; *vakṣasaḥ—*from their breasts; *kāñcanamayyaḥ—*golden; *mayūkha—*of rays; *vīcayaḥ—*the waves; *sañcaranti—*come forth; *rādhā—*Rādhā; *sa-abhyasūyam—*with jealousy; *tiraḥ—*secretly; *dṛk-antam—*sidelong glance; *pātayati—*throws.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Nevertheless, it is the prime order of the forest emperor that the tax should be exacted.**

**(slightly moving forward): Ah, how amazing! Even though these girls still have milk on their lips, I can see their extremely swollen breasts.**

**(looking more): And how is it that the waves of golden rays beam forth from their breasts, even though they are completely covered with choisest garments? (With resentment, Rādhā secretly throws a sidelong glance.)**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa suspects that the gopīs hide lots of gold behind their garments. Otherwise, how their breasts could appear so effulgent?

### Verse 262

*(sa-kautukam ātma-gatam)*  
*paṭonnamana-līlayā pulaka-vṛndam ārundhatī*  
*smitaṁ tv adhara-cāturī-paricayena gāndharvikā*  
*mṛṣā-bhrukuṭi-bandhurī-kṛta-mukhī mad-ukti-śravān*  
*nirasyati dṛg-añcala-bhramibhir atra ruṣṭeva mām*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-kautukam—*with eagerness; *ātma-gatam—*to Himself; *paṭa—*Her clothes; *unnamana—*of pulling up; *līlayā—*with the pretense; *pulaka—*of hairs standing on end; *vṛndam—*the multitude; *ārundhatī—*while suppressing; *smitam—*Her smile; *tu—*but; *adhara—*of lips; *cāturī—*of the expertise; *paricayena—*by the accumulation; *gāndharvikā—*Gāndharvikā (Rādhā); *mṛṣā—*falsely; *bhrukuṭi—*with a frown; *bandhurī-kṛta—*made beautiful; *mukhī—*Her face; *mat-ukti—*My statement; *śravāt—*due to hearing; *nirasyati—*is throwing; *dṛk-añcala—*of Her sidelong glances; *bhramibhiḥ—*with the whirlwinds; *atra—*here; *ruṣṭā—*angry; *iva—*as if; *mām—*Me;

**Перевод:**

**(eagerly to Himself): Gāndharvikā, as if angry upon hearing My statement, has adorned Her face with a false frown and is trying to suppress horripilation on the pretext of covering Her limbs with clothes. Gathering all the expertise of Her lips, She is sending to Me Her smile and at the same time wards Me off with the whirlwind of Her sidelong glance.**

**Комментарий:**

The hairs standing on end on Rādhā’s body indicate the arousal of Her intrinsic amorous feelings. This has occurred due to Kṛṣṇa’s intense looking at Her breast. Rādhā smiles, for She actually feels happy about Kṛṣṇa’s behavior, but She pretends to be angry and frowns. This mixture of emotions indicates a *kuṭṭamita* attitude, described in the commentary to text 243.

Rādhārāṇī is known as Gāndharvikā, because She is expert in singing, dancing, and playing musical instruments—all the arts practiced by the *gandharvas,* a class of semi-demigods.

### Verse 263-264

*(prakāśaṁ) sādhu mahodyāna-cakravartin, sādhu sādhu. satyeyam ‘upary upari-buddhīnāṁ carantīśvara-buddhayaḥ’ iti prasiddhiḥ.*  
*(savyato dṛśaṁ kṣipan) nāndīmukhi, paśya paśya: pañcabhir amūbhir viṁśater ardhāñ chāta-kumbha-kumbhān vakṣasi kauśalena nihnuvānābhiḥ kṛtino ’pi ghaṭṭādhikāriṇaḥ pratāryante!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*prakāśam—*audibly; *sādhu—*well done; *mahā—*great; *udyāna-cakravartin—*O emperor of the forest groves; *sādhu sādhu—*well done; *satyā—*true; *iyam—*this; *upari—*above; *upari-buddhīnām—*the wise; *caranti—*move; *īśvara-buddhayaḥ—*the godly; *iti—*thus; *prasiddhiḥ—*famous saying; *savyataḥ—*on the left; *dṛśam—*His glance; *kṣipan—*throwing; *nāndīmukhi—*O Nāndīmukhī; *paśya paśya—*look, look; *pañcabhiḥ—*five; *amūbhiḥ—*these; *viṁśateḥ—*of twenty; *ardhān—*halves; *śāta-kumbha—*golden; *kumbhān—*pots; *vakṣasi—*on the chest; *kauśalena—*with dexterity; *nihnuvānābhiḥ—*hiding; *kṛtinaḥ—*successful; *api—*even; *ghaṭṭa-adhikāriṇaḥ—*toll station supervisors; *pratāryante—*are deceived*.*

**Перевод:**

**(audibly): Well done, Your Majesty the emperor of the forest groves, well done. The old saying is true: “The godly soar above the wise.”**

**(glancing to the left): Nāndīmukhī, look, look! Even the experienced tax gate officials are deceived by these five girls who expertly hide on their chests ten golden pots.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa praises His Majesty’s for sending Him the informative and instructive letter. Inspired by it, He has spotted the gopīs smuggling. Each gopī carries two more “golden pots,” which are actually her breasts. Thus, apart from the five golden pots on their heads, the gopīs smuggle ten more on their chests. Quoting an old saying, Kṛṣṇa presents Himself to be a godly person who has outsmarted the wise gopīs.

### Verse 265

***sarvāḥ:*** *(saṁrambheṇa bhrū-kārmukāṇi kuṭilī-kṛtya sākrośam) rada-hiṇḍaa, hiṇḍehi ṇia-maṇḍabaṁ!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sarvāḥ—*all the gopīs; *saṁrambheṇa—*with anger; *bhrū-kārmukāṇi—*eyebrow-bows; *kuṭilī-kṛtya—*curving; *sa-ākrośam—*with abuse; *rada-hiṇḍaa—*O seducer; *hiṇḍehi—*go; *ṇia—*to Your; *maṇḍabaṁ—*bower*.*

**Перевод:**

**All the gopīs (Curving the bows of their eyebrows, say abusively in anger): You woman abducter, go to Your bower!**

### Verse 266-267

***kṛṣṇa:*** *(apavārya) vṛnde, vilokaya kuñcita-bhruvaṁ pañca-mukhīm. (iti sa-gadgadam)*  
*kāmaḥ kāñcid avāpya pañca-mukhatas tīvrāṁ vyathām ugrataḥ*  
*saumyāṁ pañca-mukhīṁ bhajan dhruvam imāṁ labdhoru-vidyaḥ kṛtī*  
*bhrū-cāpeṣu samaṁ kaṭākṣa-viśikhān pañcārpayan pañcasu*  
*kruddhaḥ pañcamukhogra-vikramam asau māṁ hantum udyacchate*  
*(ity udghūrṇāṁ nāṭayati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*apavārya—*to the side; *vṛnde—*O Vṛndā; *vilokaya—*see; *kuñcita-bhruvam—*with curved eyebrows; *pañca-mukhīm—*the five faces; *iti—*having said so; *sa-gadgadam—*in a faltering voice; *kāmaḥ—*Kāma; *kāñcit—*some; *avāpya—*obtaining; *pañca-mukhataḥ—*from the five-faced (Lord Śiva); *tīvrām—*intense; *vyathām—*pain; *ugrataḥ—*fierce; *saumyām—*beautiful; *pañca-mukhīm—*the five faces; *bhajan—*by worshiping; *dhruvam—*for sure; *imām—*this; *labdha—*has obtained; *uru-vidyaḥ—*a great skill; *kṛtī—*successful; *bhrū-cāpeṣu—*in the eyebrow*-*bows; *samam—*together; *kaṭākṣa—*of sidelong glances; *viśikhān—*the arrows; *pañca—*five; *arpayan—*placing; *pañcasu—*in the five; *kruddhaḥ—*angry; *pañca-mukha—*like that of Lord Śiva; *ugra-vikramam—*with My fierce power; *asau—*this; *mām—*Me; *hantum—*to kill; *udyacchate—*is getting ready. *iti—*thus; *udghūrṇām—*dizziness; *nāṭayati—*acting out.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (to the side): Vṛndā, look at those five faces with curved eyebrows.**

**(in a faltering voice): After being severely hurt by the five-faced furious Lord Śiva, Kāmadeva, by worshipping this face quintet, has surely become successful in obtaining vast knowledge of the military art. Now angry, he is getting ready to kill Me, who am as fiercely powerful as Lord Śiva, by placing the arrows of sidelong glances on the eyebrow-bows on those five faces. (shows dizziness)**

**Комментарий:**

Faltering voice is one of the ecstatic symptoms. It signifies that a person exhibiting it is experiencing ecstatic bliss. As *Mahaṭī-ṭīkā* points out, by speaking in a faltering voice, Kṛṣṇa wants to make Vṛndā happy by drawing her attention to His blissful amorous state, and also give her the following indirect message: “*You* have to make another attempt to make Me happy.”

Kṛṣṇa tells Vṛndā that Kāmadeva, after being burnt by Lord Śiva, has been worshiping the faces of the five gopīs—the gopīs standing in front of Him, Kṛṣṇa. In that way Kāmadeva has obtained the knowledge of the military art. Still harboring anger at Lord Śiva, he now wants to challenge him by killing Kṛṣṇa, who is at least *‘pañcamukhogra-vikrama’—*“as powerful as Lord Śiva (*pañca-mukha*).” Kāmadeva plans to do that by shooting the arrows of sidelong glances from the eyebrow bows of the five gopīs. This figurative description is quite appropriate, because Kāmadeva is known to possess five flowery arrows that strike person’s five senses—eyes, nose, ears, skin, and tongue. *‘Pañcamukhogra-vikrama’* can alternatively mean “as fierce as the lion (*pañca-mukha*),” or “more fierce than the lion.”

After speaking, Kṛṣṇa shows dizziness. This suggests that He is really under the powerful sway of Kāmadeva.

One of Lord Śiva’s forms has five faces, which are associated with His five potencies—creative, sustaining, destructive, revealing, and concealing.

### Verse 268

***madhumaṅgala:*** *(apavārya) hanta, kīsa vimhalantaṁ bi attāṇaaṁ ṇa rundhasi jaṁ kaḍakkhijjamha jimha-diṭṭhīhiṁ kisoriāhiṁ?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*apavārya—*to the side; *hanta—*alas; *kīsa—*why; *vimhalantaṁ—*agitated; *bi—*even; *attāṇaaṁ—*Yourself; *ṇa rundhasi—*do not conceal; *jam—*that; *kaḍakkhijjamha—*we are being looked at sideways; *jimha—*with sly; *diṭṭhīhiṁ—*glances; *kisoriāhiṁ—*by these girls*.*

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgalaḥ (to the side): Alas, why You do not conceal Your agitated heart? The girls are leering at us.**

**Комментарий:**

Madhumaṅgala tries to compose his friend. After all, the gopīs are watching them.

### Verse 269

***Kṛṣṇa:*** *(sāvahitthaṁ) sakhe madhumaṅgala, kuṭila-bhruvāṁ kauṭilya-vaicitrībhir vismito ’smi. bhavatu, kiṁ nas tena? kaitava-nihnutānāṁ hiraṇmaya-paṅkti-kumbhānāṁ śulko dvi-guṇī-kṛtya punaḥ pañca-daśānām eva śata-guṇī-kriyatām.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-avahittham—*with dissimulation; *sakhe—*O friend; *madhumaṅgala—*Madhumaṅgala; *kuṭila-bhruvām—*of these girls with curved eyebrows; *kauṭilya—*of the curvedness; *vaicitrībhiḥ—*by the astonishing diversity; *vismitaḥ—*surprised; *asmi—*I am; *bhavatu—*let it be; *kim—*what; *naḥ—*to Me; *tena—*by that; *kaitava—*by deceit; *nihnutānām—*concealed; *hiraṇmaya—*made of gold; *paṅkti—*ten; *kumbhānām—*for the pots; *śulkaḥ—*the fee; *dvi-guṇī—*double; *kṛtya—*making; *punaḥ—*again; *pañca-daśānām—*of the five and ten; *eva—*only; *śata-guṇī—*a hundred times more; *kriyatām—*please make*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (hiding His feelings): O friend Madhumaṅgala, I am amazed how astonishingly crooked these girls with curved eyebrows are. Let it be. What do we care about it? Now you please double the fee for the ten golden pots that are being smuggled, and then increase hundred times the fee for both those ten pots and the five that the girls carry openly.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa conceals His emotional stir and, having composed Himself, turns back to the tax issue. He doubles the tax for the ten pots that are being smuggled, as if feeling that the gopīs’ express their offensive neglect with their sidelong glances.

At this point, as Kṛṣṇa has increased the fee, the calculation of the complete figure is as follows: three hundred twenty lakhs for the ten pots that are being smuggled (3.2 crores—16 lakhs per one pot doubled and multiplied by ten) are added to eighty lakhs for the five pots carried openly (16 lakhs per pot, multiplied by five), which amounts in four crores (fourty million). Multiplied by one hundred, as per the order of the emperor, it will result in four hundred crores (four billion) *ṭaṅkas* of gold, which approximately measures 485,000 kilos.

### Verse 270

***madhumaṅgala:*** *suṇāhi: rāa-ulassa vinda-ca{u}ṭṭhāaṁ, ahiāriṇo savva-vijjā-guruṇo de kalā-koḍī, saṁkkhāhiṇṇassa kāatthassa me tatta-koḍī, suala-pahudīṇaṁ daṇḍiāṇaṁ pasu-bālāṇaṁ pasuba{i}-koḍī tti.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*suṇāhi—*listen; *rāa-ulassa—*of the royal entourage; *binda-ca{u}ṭṭhāaṁ—*four hundred crores; *ahiāriṇo—*superintendent; *savva-vijjā—*of all arts; *guruṇo—*the guru; *de—*for You; *kalā-koḍī—*sixty four crores; *saṁkkhā—*of counting; *ahiṇṇassa—*the scholar; *kāatthassa—*the clerk; *me—*for me; *tatta-koḍī—*twenty five crores; *suala-pahudīṇa—*starting with Subala; *daṇḍiāṇa—*with sticks; *pasu-bālāṇaṁ—*for the cowherds; *pasuba{i}-koḍī—*eleven crores; *tti—*thus*.*

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala: Listen here: the royal entourage has four hundred crores. And out of one hundred crores (one billion) that is added for the maintenance of the toll station, sixty four crores are for You, the superintendent and the teacher of all arts; twenty five crores are for me, the clerk; and for the cowherds like Subala, who carry sticks, eleven crores.**

**Комментарий:**

Four hundred crores is the flat sum that belongs to the king. The fourth part of it—one hundred crores—is added for the maintenance of the toll station as per the law, and is shared by its staff—the superintendent, the clerk, and the squad. Now, Madhumaṅgala fairly divides the income according to each other’s position. The superintendent should justly receive sixty four crores, for he is the teacher of all fine arts, which number sixty four in total. The list of these arts is given by Vātsyāyana Ṛṣi in *Kāma-sūtra.* Next, Madhumaṅgala allots to himself twenty five crores. This is because he is clerk, knowing the science of counting, and because according to the philosophy of *sāṅkhya* (the metaphysical counting of the universal elements) there are twenty five elements that constitute the universe. Finally, Subala and others—the squad armed with sticks—get eleven crores. They are cowherds, *paśu-pālas* (“the protectors of animals”) in Sanskrit, which is similar in meaning to *paśu-pati* (“the lord of animals”), the name of Lord Śiva. Since in the end of creation Lord Śiva expands into eleven forms, called Rudras, Subala and other boys are allotted eleven crores.

### Verse 271

***kṛṣṇa:***  *sambhūya vṛnda-pañcakaṁ siddham.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sambhūya—*taking together; *vṛnda-pañcakam—*five billions; *siddham—*gained*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Five hundred crores of golden ṭaṅkas all together.**

**Комментарий:**

Four hundred crores for the king plus one hundred crores for the toll station staff equal five hundred crores (five billions). Five hundred crores of *ṭaṅkas* amounts to 1,000,000,000 kilos.

### Verse 272

***rādhā:*** *(smitaṁ kṛtvā) tumhāṇaṁ bhāaṇāiṁ ṇa dīsanti kahiṁ māissanti ettiāiṁ vittāiṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smitam kṛtvā —*putting on a smile; *tumhāṇaṁ—*your; *bhāaṇāiṁ—*vessels; *ṇa dīsanti—*not seen; *kahiṁ—*where; *māissanti—*will measure; *ettiāiṁ—*this much; *vittāiṁ—*wealth.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (smiling): I don’t see your containers where will you measure this much wealth.**

**Комментарий:**

According to Viśvanātha Cakravartī, Rādhā implies that Kṛṣṇa should employ Madhumaṅgala, His clerk, to bring from the village a large bull, buffalo, and camel carts, for carrying such an enormous tax.

### Verse 273

***kṛṣṇa:***  
*narmaṇā kṛtam etena*  
*karmaṇā svayam arpaya*  
*harṣād udita-varṣmātra*  
*vittaṁ hariṇa-locane*

**Пословный перевод:**

*narmaṇā—*joking; *kṛtam—*done; *etena—*of this; *karmaṇā—*by activity; *svayam—*Yourself; *arpaya—*do offer; *harṣāt—*out of joy; *udita—*mentioned; *varṣma—*whose measure; *atra—*here; *vittam—*wealth; *hariṇa-locane—*O doe**-**eyed One.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Enough of this joking, o doe-eyed girl. Do something practical. Cheerfully offer at this toll gate the counted wealth.**

**Комментарий:**

The hidden meaning of the second sentence: “Cheerfully offer at this toll gate Your blooming body (*udita-varṣma*).”

### Verse 274

***nāndīmukhī:***  *(kṛṣṇāntikam āsādya sāsūyam iva) mohaṇa, amha-bhaavadīe siṇeha-bhāaṇāṇaṁ ujjuāṇaṁ edāṇaṁ bālāṇaṁ kīsa aliaṁ ccea panti-kumbhāṇaṁ dāṇaṁ tue vaḍḍhīadi?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kṛṣṇa-antikam—*the proximity to Kṛṣṇa; *āsādya—*reaching; *sa-asūyam—*with resentment; *iva—*as if; *mohaṇa—*O bewilderer; *amha—*of our; *bhaavadīe—*saintly lady (Paurṇamāsī); *siṇeha—*of affection; *bhāaṇāṇaṁ—*the objects; *ujjuāṇaṁ—*honest; *edāṇa—*these; *bālāṇaṁ—*of girls; *kīsa—*why; *aliaṁ—*falsely; *ccea—*indeed; *panti-kumbhāṇaṁ—*for the ten pots; *dāṇaṁ—*the fee; *tue—*by You; *vaḍḍhīadi—*increased*.*

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī (coming close to Kṛṣṇa and says with slight resentment): O cunning boy, why do You falsely increase the tax by adding ten more pots? These honest gopīs are the objects of the Bhagavatī’s affection.**

**Комментарий:**

Although Nāndīmukhī reminds Kṛṣṇa of Paurṇamāsī’s message (text 187) and pretends to favor the gopīs, she actually acts for Kṛṣṇa, which will become obvious very soon.

### Verse 275

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *nāndīmukhi, na kadāpy alīkam idam. satyam eva pañcemāḥ pañca-daśa-kalasī-vilāsa-bhājaḥ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nāndīmukhi—*O Nāndīmukhī; *na kadā api—*never; *alīkam—*falsely; *idam—*this; *satyam—*truly; *eva—*only; *pañca—*five; *imāḥ—*these (gopīs); *pañca-daśa—*of fifteen; *kalasī—*pots; *vilāsa—*the effulgence; *bhājaḥ—*possessing.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Nāndīmukhī, the tax was never falsely increased. It is the naked truth that these five gopīs emanate the effulgence of fifteen golden pots.**

### Verse 276-277

***nāndīmukhī:***  *ṇāarinda, mahā-vvadiṇīe pavva{i}āe pariaṇo mādiso jaṇo ṇiddhāridaṁ ajāṇia ṇa kkhu viṇṇavedi. tahabi ja{i} maha vaaṇe sandihāṇo si tado saaṁ ccea āadua paccakkhaṁ pekkha.*  
*(iti kṛṣṇena saha rādhām abhyupetya) halā rāhi, eso dullalido goulinda-ṇandaṇo sa-divvaṁ bi maha vaaṇaṁ ṇa pattiāedi. tā pasīda, īsi ambaram ukkhibia ṇia-vakkha-perantaṁ pekkhāventī moāvehi haḍhilla-seharassa hatthādo saha-parivāraṁ appāṇaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ṇāarinda—*O best of the suave boys; *mahā-vvadiṇīe—*given to severe vows; *pavva{i}āe—*of the lady ascetic; *pariaṇo—*an attendant; *mādiso—*like me; *jaṇo—*a person; *ṇiddhāridaṁ—*the ascertained fact; *ajāṇia—*not knowing; *ṇa—*does not; *kkhu—*indeed; *viṇṇavedi—*declare; *tahabi—*still; *ja{i}—*if; *maha—*my; *vaaṇe—*about statement; *sandihāṇo—*doubtful; *si—*You are; *tado—*then; *saaṁ—*personally; *ccea—*only; *āadua—*coming; *paccakkhaṁ—*directly; *pekkha—*see; *iti—*thus; *kṛṣṇena saha—*with Kṛṣṇa; *rādhām—*Rādhā; *abhyupetya—*approaching; *halā—*O friend; *rāhi—*Rādhā; *eso—*this; *dullalido—*rough; *goulinda-ṇandaṇo—*son of Gokula’s king; *sa-divvaṁ—*solemn; *bi—*even; *maha—*my; *vaaṇaṁ—*words; *ṇa pattiāedi—*does not believe; *tā—*therefore; *pasīda—*be kind; *īsi—*slightly; *ambaram—*Your garment; *ukkhibia—*lifting; *ṇia—*of Your; *vakkha—*breast; *perantaṁ—*the edge; *pekkhāventī—*showing; *moāvehi—*release; *haḍhilla—*of the violent; *seharassa—*of the chief; *hatthādo—*from the hand; *saha-parivāraṁ—*along with Your retinue; *appāṇaṁ—*Yourself.

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: O most elegant one, an attendant of the rigorous ascetic \[Paurṇamāsī\] like myself surely does not declare anything without knowing the actual fact. But if You still doubt my words, come and see with Your own eyes.**

**(taking Kṛṣṇa along with her, Nāndīmukhī comes near Rādhā) My friend Rādhā, this ill-mannered son of the king of Gokula doesn’t believe even my solemn words. So be kind, lift up Your garment a little and show the edge of Your breast. By doing so, You shall release Yourself along with Your retinue from the hands of this despot.**

### Verse 278

***sarvāḥ:*** *(sābhyasūyam) abehi dubbuddhie, abehi!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sarvāḥ—*all (the gopīs); *sa-abhyasūyam—*with indignation; *abehi—*go away; *dubbudhie—*O crooked one; *abehi—*go away*.*

**Перевод:**

**All the gopīs (indignanly): Get lost, fraud! Get lost!**

**Комментарий:**

“You are a troublemaker! Luckily we know what you mean—‘Rādhā, go off to some secluded spot and show Kṛṣṇa the edge of Your breast.’”

### Verse 279

***nāndīmukhī:*** *(smitvā) kadhaṁ hida-kadhaṇe bi kuppadha?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smitvā—*smiling; *kadhaṁ—*why; *hida-kadhaṇe—*at beneficial words; *bi—*even; *kuppadha—*you are angry.

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī (smiling): Why are you getting angry even at good advice?**

**Комментарий:**

Nāndīmukhī pretends to be the gopīs’ well-wisher. Viśvanātha Cakravartī paraphrases her statement to the gopīs: “This unrestrainable ruffian will soon surely see your breasts by tearing open Your cholis with His own hand. I thought this would be too much. So I have suggested to all of you that it is better that Rādhā voluntarily shows just a part of Her breast to Him. What’s wrong with this advice?”

### Verse 280

***kṛṣṇa:***  *kiṁ naś chinnam yad atra kāñcana-raktikam api na parihariṣyati hariḥ?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kim—*what; *naḥ—*for us; *chinnam—*disturbed; *yat—*because; *atra—*in this affair; *kāñcana—*of gold; *raktikam—*a *raktika* (the smallest weight unit, see commentary to texts 190-191); *api—*even; *na parihariṣyati—*will not spare; *hariḥ—*Hari*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: What will we lose if Hari won’t spare them even one golden raktikā in taxing these girls?**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa consoles Nāndīmukhī by saying that she should not worry, for whether the gopīs are angry or not, the toll gate staff will not be at loss—He will make sure the tax is paid to the last penny. Kṛṣṇa makes a pun by calling Himself Hari, because this name is derived from the verbal root *‘hṛ’* (“to take away”). And taking away is exactly what He is up to.

## “Sacrificing” Vṛndā

### Verse 281-282

***viśākhā:***  *(sva-gatam) paḍhamaṁ kaṇhassa dudiaṁ vundaṁ ccea sulukke ubajjujjantī dība-sihāe aggiṇo pūaṇaṁ ārimbhissaṁ.*  
*(prakāśam) ṇāarinda, kahiṁ pañca ghia-ghaḍiāo, kahiṁ ettiaṁ ghaḍidaṁ ghaṭṭa-sulukkaṁ? hodu, tadhabi ubaāriṇaṁ rāa-kumaraṁ tumaṁ abekkhia ettha ṇikkae amhehiṁ ṇia-pia-sahī vundā tuha appidā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sva-gatam—*to herself; *paḍhamaṁ—*firstly; *kaṇhassa—*of Kṛṣṇa; *dudiaṁ—*a messenger; *vundam—*Vṛndā; *ccea—*only; *sulukke—*as the fee; *ubajjujjantī—*using; *dība-sihāe—*with a flame of a lamp; *aggiṇo—*of Agni; *pūaṇaṁ—*the worship; *ārimbhissaṁ—*I shall begin; *prakāśam—*audibly; *ṇāarinda—*O best of the suave boys; *kahiṁ—*where; *paṁca—*five; *ghīa-ghaḍiāo—*ghee pots; *kahiṁ—*where; *ettiam—*this far; *ghaḍidaṁ—*procured; *ghaṭṭa-sulukkaṁ—*the toll fee; *hodu—*let it be; *tadhabi—*still; *ubaāriṇaṁ—*favorable; *rāa-kumaraṁ—*the prince; *tumaṁ—*You; *abekkhia—*considering; *ettha—*in this; *ṇikkae—*barter; *amhehiṁ—*by us; *ṇia-pia-sahī—*Your beloved sakhī; *vundā—*Vṛndā; *tuha—*to You; *appidā—*is offered.

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā (to herself): Let me first of all worship fire with its own flame by offering Kaṇha’s own messenger Vṛndā as the fee.**

**(audibly): O best of the suave, how is that You demand so large tax for just five pots of ghee? Let it be so. But still, thinking of You as our kind prince, we offer to You in this exchange Your beloved friend Vṛndā.**

**Комментарий:**

Just as a flame tongue is itself a part of the whole fire, Vṛndā, even though outwardly supportive of the gopīs for some time, is ultimately on the side of Kṛṣṇa. Thinking in this way, Viśākhā attempts to settle the matter in a way to avoid losing any of the gopīs as the tax fee. She offers to Kṛṣṇa Vṛndā, as if Vṛndā was belonging to the gopīs’ party.

### Verse 283

***subalaḥ:***  *(saṁskṛtena)*  
*vṛnda-pañcataye yuktam*  
*eka-vṛndārpaṇaṁ katham*  
*saṅkhyā-vidāṁ na naḥ śakyaṁ*  
*go-saṅkhyānāṁ pratāraṇam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *vṛnda-pañcataye—*when five hundred crores; *yuktam—*proper; *eka-vṛndā—*of one Vṛndā; *arpaṇam—*giving; *katham—*how; *saṅkhyā-vidām—*of the experts in counting; *na—*not; *naḥ—*us; *śakyam—*is possible; *go-saṅkhyānām—*the cowherds; *pratāraṇam—*the cheating.

**Перевод:**

**Subalaḥ (in Sanskrit): Is it fair to offer just one Vṛndā, when the tax is five hundred crores? It is not possible to cheat us, the cowherds, who are experts in counting cows.**

**Комментарий:**

Another meaning of the verse is as follows: “Is it fair to offer just one hundred crores (*vṛnda-arpaṇam*), when the tax is five hundred crores? It is not possible to cheat us, the clerks (*saṅkhyā-vidām*), famous all over the world (*go-saṅkhyānām*).”

### Verse 284

***lalitā:*** *(roṣam ivābhinīya) visāhe, suṭṭhu muddhāsi jaṁ lahue imassiṁ atthe guruīe appaṇo sahīe vundāe appaṇaṁ kāduṁ icchasi!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*roṣam—*anger; *iva—*as if; *abhinīya—*expressing; *visāhe—*O Viśākhā; *suṭṭhu—*completely; *muddhā—*foolish; *asi—*you are; *jaṁ—*that; *lahue—*petty; *imassiṁ—*in this; *atthe—*matter; *guruīe—*of our guru; *appaṇo—*and Your own; *sahīe—*friend; *vundāe—*of Vṛndā; *appaṇaṁ—*offering; *kāduṁ—*to do; *icchasi—*You wish.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (feigning anger): Viśākhā, You are so foolish to give away our guru and You own friend Vṛndā in this petty matter!**

**Комментарий:**

Lalitā’s anger is false. Lalitā wants to impress upon Kṛṣṇa how it is close to impossible for the gopīs to give away priceless Vṛndā, who “belongs” to them. She hopes that Kṛṣṇa, convinced by her show, would then adamantly grab hold onto Vṛndā. In fact, however, Lalitā and the gopīs have no possessivness toward Vṛndā, and thus, they couldn’t possibly become angry with Viśākhā’s proposal. They would rather welcome such an easy way out of the whole predicament.

### Verse 285

***madhumaṅgalaḥ:***  *lalide, ciṭṭhadu edaṁ alia-māhappaṁ!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*lalide—*O Lalitā; *ciṭṭhadu—*let stop; *edaṁ—*this; *alia—*false; *māhappaṁ—*glory.

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgalaḥ: Lalitā, stop this false praise!**

**Комментарий:**

Madhumaṅgala understands Lalitā’s strategy.

### Verse 286

***lalitā:***  *baḍua, suṇāhi: ‘tettisa-koḍi-surāṇaṁ saāsādo surindo variṭṭho jaṁ eso saa-koḍi-hattho. tado bi bhaavanto hiraṇṇagabbho jaṁ eso di-paraddha-vehavo. tado bi deī lacchī jaṁ esā savva-saṁpattīṇaṁ īsarī. tado bi vundā jā kira lacchīṁ bi tucchī-kadua kāe bi a{u}rubba-sirie luddheṇa viṇṇuṇā kāmida’ tti bhaavadīe muhādo suṇīadi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*baḍua—*O *brāhmaṇa* boy; *suṇāhi—*listen; *tettisa-koḍi-surāṇam—*the thirty-three crores of demogids; *saāsādo—*than; *surindo—*Indra; *variṭṭho—*the best; *jaṁ—*because; *eso—*he; *saa-koḍi—*has one hundred crores; *hattho—*in his hands; *tado bi—*than even (him); *bhaavanto—*the possessor of all riches; *hiraṇṇagabbho—*Hiraṇyagarbha (Lord Brahmā); *jaṁ—*because; *eso—*his; *di-paraddha—*two *parārdhas*; *vehavo—*opulence; *tado bi—*than even (him); *deī lacchī—*goddess Lakṣmī; *jaṁ—*because; *esā—*she; *savva-saṁpattīṇaṁ—*of all riches; *īsarī—*the mistress; *tado bi—*than even (her); *vundā—*Vṛndā; *jā—*who; *kira—*indeed; *lacchīm—*Lakṣmī; *bi—*even; *tucchī-kadua—*making insignificant; *kāe bi—*some; *a{u}rubba-sirie—*due to unprecedented beauty; *luddheṇa—*by bewildered; *viṇṇuṇā—*Viṣṇu; *kāmida—*was coveted; *tti—*thus; *bhaavadīe—*of Bhagavatī (Paurṇamāsī); *muhādo—*from the mouth; *suṇīadi—*is heard*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: O brahmaṇa boy, please listen to what I have heard from Bhagavatī: Indra is the best of the thirty-three crores of demigods, for He has one hundred crores in his hands; Brahmā, however, is more rich, possessing the grandeur of two parārdhas; and again, the goddess Lakṣmī is superior to even Brahmā, for she is the mistress of all opulences; but Vṛndā is still better, for with some unique charm she leaves far behind even Lakṣmī, and Lord Viṣṇu, bewildered, wishes to enjoy with Vṛndā.**

**Комментарий:**

*Mahaṭī-ṭīkā* gives two possible reasons why Lalitā patronizingly addresses Madhumaṅgala “a *brāhmaṇa* boy*.*” The first is that she feels Madhumaṅgala was unable to give a response suitable to the present issue of giving away or holding back Vṛndā. And the second reason is that being still a lad, he is certainly ignorant of the topic Lalitā is just about to speak on.

Playing with words, Lalitā proves Vṛndā to be the most valuable personality in the existence. *‘Śata-koṭi-hasta’* denotes Indra, who has a thunderbolt (*śata-koṭi*) in his hand (*hasta*). Lalitā, however, takes *‘śata-koṭi’* literally—one hundred crores. In the same way, *parārdha* is a number of one with seventeen zeros. This many years multiplied by two measure the duration of Lord Brahmā’s life. Lalitā again takes it literally, thereby establishing Brahmā to be richer than Indra. Finally, Lalitā concludes that Vṛndā must be even more precious than Lakṣmī, the mistress and the source of all riches, for even Lord Viṣṇu desires Vrindā’s association more than that of Lakṣmī. This being so, is it fair to offer such precious Vṛndā in place of only five *vṛndas*, or five hundred crores?

### Verse 287-288

***viśākhā:*** *(pade nipatya kākum ātanvatī) sahi lalide, mahuraṁ mantesi. tadhābi takkāliaṁ dussaha-dukkhaṁ parihariduṁ evvaṁ ajuttaṁ bi kādu-kāmamhi. tā pasīda, aṇumaṇṇehi vundā-samappaṇaṁ. puroḍāsa-ogghāṇeṇa tuṇṇaṁ puṇamha attāṇaaṁ.*  
*(lalitā smereva śiro vinamayya tūṣṇīṁ tiṣṭhati) lalide, viṇṇādaṁ de āūdaṁ jaṁ ekkaṁ diahaṁ ccea aṇumaṇṇesi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*pade—*at the feet; *nipatya—*falling; *kākum—*a plaintive intonation; *ātanvatī—*making; *sahi—*O friend; *lalide—*Lalitā; *mahuraṁ—*sweetly; *mantesi—*you are talking; *tadhābi—*still; *takkāliaṁ—*present; *dussaha—*intolerable; *dukkhaṁ—*suffering; *parihariduṁ—*to take away; *evvaṁ—*like this; *ajuttaṁ—*improper thing; *bi—*even; *kādu- kāmamhi—*I wish to do; *tā—*therefore; *pasīda—*be pleased; *aṇumaṇṇehi—*allow; *vundā-samappaṇaṁ—*offering of Vṛndā; *puroḍāsa—*sacrificial rice cakes; *ugghāṇeṇa—*by smelling; *tuṇṇaṁ—*quickly; *puṇamha—*we will purify; *attāṇaaṁ—*ourselves*. lalitā—*lalitā; *smerā—*smiling; *iva—*as if; *śiraḥ—*her head; *vinamayya—*lowering; *tūṣṇīm—*silently; *tiṣṭhati—*stands. *lalide—*O Lalitā; *viṇṇādaṁ—*is understood; *de—*your; *āūdaṁ—*intention; *jaṁ—*because; *ekkaṁ—*for one; *diahaṁ—*day; *ccea—*only; *aṇumaṇṇesi—*you allow.

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā (falling at Lalitā’s feet, says in a plaintive tone): O Lalitā-sakhī, your talk is very sweet. But still, in order to dispel our present plight I wish to do even such an improper thing: please be kind and allow us to offer Vṛndā as the fee. We shall quickly purify ourselves by smelling the sacrificial cakes.**

**(Lalitā stands silently, bowing down Her head and slightly smiling.) Lalitā, I understand what you mean: you approve of this only for today.**

**Комментарий:**

Falling at Lalitā’s feet, Viśākhā falsely demonstrates to Kṛṣṇa that she is on His side. She does it in order to make Kṛṣṇa happy.

Sacrificial cakes, or *puroḍāśas*, is a type of oblation in a Vedic sacrifice. These cakes are generally made out of barley flour and are smeared with ghee at the time of offering. Traditionally, they are honored not by eating, but by smelling.

### Verse 289

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *hanta nāndīmukhi, dṛṣṭam aty-adbhutaṁ bhavatyā. tataḥ pṛcchyatām iti katham etābhir mat-karṇa-yugārabdha-tāṇḍavayor makara-kuṇḍalayor dvandvaṁ na śulkitam.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*hanta—*alas; *nāndīmukhi—*O Nāndīmukhī; *dṛṣṭam—*is seen; *aty-adbhutam—*great wonder; *bhavatyā—*by you; *tataḥ—*therefore; *pṛcchyatām—*may be asked; *iti—*thus; *katham—*how; *etābhiḥ—*by these (girls); *mat-karṇa—*on My ears; *yuga—*both; *ārabdha—*that has begun; *tāṇḍavayoḥ—*its frantic dance; *makara—*shark-shaped; *kuṇḍalayoḥ—*of earrings; *dvandvam—*the pair; *na śulkitam—*not made a fee.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Alas, Nandīmukhī, did you see this “wonder?” After this, it may be asked how is that these gopīs didn’t offer as the fee My shark-shaped earrings that dance wildly on My ears.**

**Комментарий:**

Smart Kṛṣṇa cannot allow Himself to be fooled by Viśākhā’s trick. To offer Him Vridā, who is on His side anyway, is as ridiculous as to offer Him His own earrings.

### Verse 290

***nāndīmukhī:***  *kittidā-kitti-dāiṇi rāhie, ajjuttaṁ kkhu edaṁ jaṁ vaṇamāliṇo ccea vundāe imassa sulukkappaṇaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kittidā—*to Kīrtidā; *kitti-dāiṇi—*the giver of fame; *rāhie—*O Rādhikā; *ajjuttaṁ—*improper; *kkhu—*indeed; *edaṁ—*this; *jaṁ—*that; *vaṇamāliṇo—*of Vanamālī; *ccea—*only; *vundāe—*to Vṛndā; *imassa—*this; *sulukka-appaṇaṁ—*offering of the fee.

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: O Rādhikā, You give glory to Kīrtidā! It’s not at all right to offer Vṛndā as the fee. She belongs to Vanamālī anyway.**

**Комментарий:**

Kīrtidā is the name of Rādhārāṇī’s mother. And although it means “the giver of glory,” it is Rādhikā who gives glory to Her fortunate mother, as Nāndīmukhī declares. Vanamālī is Kṛṣṇa’s name that means “One, who wears a garland of forest flowers.”

### Verse 291

***rādhā:*** *sahi vunde, kiṁ tti tuṇhīṁ ciṭṭhasi? tuṇṇaṁ appaṇo pakkhaṁ ullāsehi!*  
*(kṛṣṇaḥ vṛndā-vaktram avalokayan vilocana-koṇaṁ kūṇayati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sahi—*O friend; *vunde—*Vṛndā; *kiṁtti—*why; *tuṇhīṁ—*silently; *ciṭṭhasi—*you stand; *tuṇṇaṁ—*immidiately; *appaṇo—*your own; *pakkhaṁ—*affiliation; *ullāsehi—*demonstrate; *kṛṣṇaḥ—*Kṛṣṇa; *vṛndā-vaktram—*at Vṛndā’s face; *avalokayan—*looking; *vilocana—*of His eyes; *koṇam—*the corners; *kūṇayati—*narrows.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: Sakhī Vṛndā, why are you silent? Show immidiately who are you siding with!**

**(Looking at Vṛndā’s face, Kṛṣṇa squints.)**

### Verse 292-293

***vṛndā:***  *nāgarendra, kṛtaṁ nirarthakaṁ dṛśas tāṇḍavena yad iyaṁ vṛndā vṛndāvaneśvarīm anuvartate.*  
***sarvāḥ:*** *(sotprāsaṁ vihasya) bhaavadi lajje, kahiṁ gadāsi? pasīda pasīda!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nāgara-indra—*O best of the suave boys; *kṛtam—*is done; *nirarthakam—*meaningless thing; *dṛśaḥ—*of Your eye; *tāṇḍavena—*by the dance; *yat—*because; *iyam—*this; *vṛndā—*Vṛndā; *vṛndāvana-īśvarīm—*the mistress of Vṛndāvana, Rādhā; *anuvartate—*follows. *sa-utprāsam—*with outburst; *vihasya—*laughing; *bhaavadi—*O holy; *lajje—*shyness; *kahiṁ—*where; *gadāsi—*you have gone; *pasīda pasīda—*be kind, be kind.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: O smartest One, the dancing of Your eyes is meaningless. This very Vṛndā is the follower of Vṛndāvaneśvarī.**

**All the gopīs (wildly laughing): O goddess of shyness, where have you gone? Please have mercy, have mercy!**

**Комментарий:**

The gopīs are startled at how openly could Vṛndā declare her pretentious affiliation with Rādhārāṇī. They say that Vṛndā must have lost all shyness to say so.

### Verse 294-295

***vṛndā:***  *sakhi vṛndāvaneśvari, mamātra kācid vijñaptir avāptāvasarā vartate.*  
***rādhā:*** *sahi vunde, kīdisī esā bhaṇīadu.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakhi—*O friend; *vṛndāvana-īśvari—*the mistress of Vṛndāvana; *mama—*my; *atra—*in this regard; *kācit—*one; *vijñaptiḥ—*statement; *avāpta-avasarā—*that has got its occasion; *vartate—*there is; *sahi vunde—*O sakhī Vṛndā; *kīdisī—*of what type; *esā—*this; *bhaṇīadu—*tell*.*

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: My friend Rādhā, the mistress of Vṛndāvana, I have something to say in this regard.**

**Rādhā: Tell Me what, sakhī Vṛndā.**

### Verse 296

***vṛndā:***  *dyūtakāra-saṁsado ’pi bhūyiṣṭhaṁ ghaṭṭa-pāla-goṣṭhī sādhubhir abhitaḥ ślāghyate. tad atra na samañjasaḥ prāñjalo ’yaṁ janaḥ. vikrayopakramaś cet kvacid anyatra vikrīyatām.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dyūta-kāra—*of gamblers; *saṁsadaḥ—*than the assembly; *api—*even; *bhūyiṣṭham—*greatly; *ghaṭṭa-pāla—*of tollgate men; *goṣṭhī—*the assembly; *sādhubhiḥ—*by saints; *abhitaḥ—*everywhere; *ślāghyate—*is glorified; *tat—*therefore; *atra—*in this matter; *na samañjasaḥ—*does not suit; *prāñjalaḥ—*honest; *ayam—*this; *janaḥ—*person; *vikraya—*of business dealing; *upakramaḥ—*the beginning; *cet—*if; *kvacit anyatra—*somewhere else; *vikrīyatām—*the business dealing should be done.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: Saintly people proclaim everywhere that toll gate men are even worse than gamblers. This honest person does not belong to them. If these people want to bargain over taxes, let them do it elsewhere.**

**Комментарий:**

Vṛndā wants to say that an innocent person such as herself could not possibly be a member of Kṛṣṇa’s team.

### Verse 297

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(sa-smitam) satyam amūr asambhuktā evāpratima-pūrṇa-lakṣmī-bharāḥ subhruvo rāja-kula-kāryam arhanti. vṛndā tu viṣṇunā suciraṁ sambhujya nirbharam apāreṇa vaibhavena riktī-kṛtā. tad alam etayā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-smitam—*with a smile; *satyam—*it is true; *amūḥ—*these; *asambhuktāḥ—*not enjoyed by any man; *eva—*indeed; *apratima—*incomparable; *pūrṇa-lakṣmī—*of complete beauty; *bharāḥ—*carrying the weight; *subhruvaḥ—*\[the gopīs\] with beautiful eyebrows; *rāja-kula—*in the royal court; *kāryam—*a lawsuit; *arhanti—*deserve; *vṛndā—*Vṛndā; *tu—*but; *viṣṇunā—*by Viṣṇu; *suciram—*long ago; *sambhujya—*having been enjoyed; *nirbharam—*excessively; *apāreṇa—*by boundless; *vaibhavena—*power; *riktī-kṛtā—*is emptied; *tat—*therefore; *alam—*enough; *etayā—*of Her*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (smiling): These virgins with beautiful eyebrows, carrying the weight of unparalleled beauty, truly deserve a lawsuit in the royal court. Vṛndā, however, having been enjoyed by Viṣṇu long ago, is abandoned by Her boundless opulence for good. So let’s forget about Her.**

**Комментарий:**

As if convinced to accept Vṛndā, Kṛṣṇa now rejects her for the reason that she is now bereft of her glorious opulence that Lalitā has recently described (text 286). In this regard, Kṛṣṇa refers to the pastime of Viṣṇu enjoying with Tulasī, Vṛndā Devī’s expansion, in disguise of her husband, the demon Śaṅkhācūḍa. Viṣṇu did so for the purpose of making the demon vulnerable, since the last was blessed to be invincible as long as his wife’s chastity wasn’t violated.

### Verse 298

***rādhā:*** *(vihasya) edaṁ kkhu ‘alābhād aṅganā-tyāgas turaṅga-brahmacaryakam’ tti dhīrehiṁ bhaṇīadi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vihasya—*laughing; *edaṁ—*this; *kkhu—*indeed; *alābhāt—*due to not obtaining; *aṅganā-tyāgaḥ—*renunciation from women; *turaṅga—*of a horse; *brahmacaryakam—*celibacy; *tti—*thus; *dhīrehiṁ—*by the self-controlled; *bhaṇīadi—*is said*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (laughing): The wise say: “One who renounces a woman because of his inability to obtain her is called ‘a celibate horse.’”**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā wants to impress upon everybody that despite Kṛṣṇa’s proud remarks, He is actually unable Vṛndā in place of the fee, because the latter is so precious. Rādhā sarcastically labels Kṛṣṇa as a “celibate horse.” A stallion can be called a celibate as long as it is physically kept apart from the mares. In the same way, if someone renounces a woman because he cannot obtain her, his celibacy is of no value.

## Poor Madhumaṅgala

### Verse 299

***madhumaṅgalaḥ:***  *(janāntikam) visāhe, ṇiccidaṁ tumhāṇaṁ ghaṭṭa-dāṇe aṇuūlo huvissaṁ jaṁ kāattha-vijjā-pāraṅgado mhi. tā dehi me kiṁpi pāritosiaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*jana-antikam—*to the person standing next; *visāhe—*O Viśākhā; *ṇiccidaṁ—*definitely; *tumhāṇaṁ—*your; *ghaṭṭa-dāṇe—*in the matter of paying the toll tax; *aṇuūlo—*favorable; *huvissaṁ—*I will become; *jaṁ—*because; *kāattha-vijjā—*in clerkship; *pāraṅgado—*thoroughly learned; *mhi—*I am; *tā—*therefore; *dehi—*give; *me—*me; *kiṁpi—*some; *pāritosiaṁ—*gift*.*

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala (whispering): Viśākhā, for sure I will help you paying the tax, for I am expert in clerkship. Just bribe me.**

### Verse 300-302

***viśākhā:*** *ajja, ṇavvaṁ sakkaraṁ dāissaṁ.*  
***madhumaṅgalaḥ:*** *visāhe, ṇūṇaṁ parihasasi.*  
***viśākhā:***  *bhaavantassa sūrassa sabāmi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ajja*—O respected sir; *ṇavvam—*fresh; *sakkaraṁ—*candy; *dāissaṁ—*I will give*. visāhe—*O Viśākhā; *ṇūṇaṁ—*indeed; *parihasasi—*you are joking*. bhaavantassa—*of god; *sūrassa—*Sūrya; *sabāmi—*I swear.

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā: O ārya, I will give you a fresh candy.**

**Madhumaṅgala: Viśākhā, you are surely joking.**

**Viśākhā: In the name of the sun god I swear.**

### Verse 303-304

***madhumaṅgalaḥ:***  *(sa-harṣaṁ kṛṣṇam abhyupetya) pia-vaassa, māṇa-samae pamadā-saa-koḍi-rosa-bhañjaṇe viakkhaṇo mhi. dīvāalī-kodue surahī-saa-koḍi-pūāvaṇe ācārio mhi. tā dijja{u} ajja mahā-mahūsave majjhaṁ aṇabbhatthida-puvvā saa-koḍi-dakkhiṇā.*  
*(kṛṣṇaḥ smitvā tūṣṇīṁ tiṣṭhati)*  
*(apavārya) visāhe,‘maunaṁ sammati-lakṣaṇam’ tti jāṇāsi ccea. tā dehi parissudaṁ. (ity añjaliṁ prasārayati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-harṣam—*with joy; *kṛṣṇam—*to Kṛṣṇa; *abhyupetya—*coming close; *pia-vaassa—*O dear friend; *māṇa-samae—*at the time of anger; *pamadā—*women; *saa-koḍi—*of one billion; *rosa—*the anger; *bhañjaṇe—*in breaking; *viakkhaṇo—*the expert; *mhi—*I am; *dīvāalī—*of Dīpāvalī; *kodue—*during the festival; *surahī—*cows; *saa-koḍi—*of one billion; *pūāvaṇe—*in worshiping; *ācārio—*the preceptor; *mhi—*I am; *tā—*therefore; *dijja{u}—*should be given; *ajja—*today; *mahā-mahūsave—*in the greatest occasion; *majjhaṁ—*to me; *aṇabbhatthida—*not asked for; *puvvā—*previously; *saa-koḍi—*by a billion; *dakkhiṇā—*the remuneration*. kṛṣṇaḥ—*Kṛṣṇa; *smitvā—*smiling; *tūṣṇīm—*silently; *tiṣṭhati—*stands; *apavārya—*aside; *visāhe—*Viśākhā; *maunam—*silence; *sammati—*of consent; *lakṣaṇam—*the sign; *tti—*thus; *jāṇāsi—*you know; *ccea—*certainly; *tā—*therefore; *dehi—*give; *parissudaṁ—*what was promised; *iti—*thus; *añjalim—*his palms; *prasārayati—*stretching.

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala (joyfully coming near Kṛṣṇa): My dear friend, I am the expert in breaking the fury of one billion sulky women and I am the ācārya in worshiping one billion cows during the Dīpāvalī festival. For this reasons, in today’s grand occasion I should be given the remuneration of one billion golden coins, the remuneration I never asked before.**

**(Kṛṣṇa smiles and stands silently. Madhumaṅgala speaks to the side.) Viśākhā, you certainly know that silence is a sign of approval. So give what you have promised. (stretching his palms)**

**Комментарий:**

Madhumaṅgala wants to get something more than just a piece of sugar candy. He therefore appraoches Kṛṣṇa and gets His silent approval to accept from Viśakhā a really big *dakṣiṇā.*

### Verse 305

***viśākhā:*** *(smitvā) geṇhiadu esā sakkarā. (iti karparāṁ samarpayati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smitvā—*smiling; *geṇhiadu—*may be accepted; *esā—*this; *sakkarā—*candy*. iti—*thus; *karparām—*a piece of broken pot; *samarpayati—*offers.

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā (smiling): Please take this “candy.” (offers a piece of a broken pot)**

**Комментарий:**

Viśākhā mocks Madhumaṅgala by taking the word *‘sakkarā’* (*‘śarkarā’*) with another meaning. “I am giving you exactly what I promised,” she implies.

### Verse 306-307

***madhumaṅgalaḥ:*** *(uccair vihasya) dhutte, ciṭṭha ciṭṭha! ṇikkidaṁ vo karissaṁ!*  
*(iti kṛṣṇāntikam āsādya) pia-vaassa, lahuammi kajje alaṁ vilaṁbeṇa. geṇha sulukkaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*uccaiḥ—*loudly; *vihasya—*laughing; *dhutte—*O cheater; *ciṭṭha ciṭṭha—*stand, stand; *ṇikkidaṁ—*the revenge; *vaḥ—*to you; *karissaṁ—*I will do*. iti—*thus; *kṛṣṇa-antikam—*the proximity to Kṛṣṇa; *āsādya—*reaching; *pia-vaassa—*O dear friend; *lahuammi—*in a petty; *kajje—*affair; *alaṁ—*enough; *vilaṁbeṇa—*of procrastination; *geṇha—*take; *sulukkaṁ—*the payment*.*

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala (loudly laughing): You fraud, wait! I shall avenge myself!**

**(coming close to Kṛṣṇa) Dear friend, enough of putting off in this petty matter. Just take the tax.**

**Комментарий:**

Here is what Madhumaṅgala has in mind by calling Viśākhā fraud: “You cheater, I am an innocent *brāhmaṇa*. And because I just wanted a sugar candy so badly, I wasn’t sharp enough to remember the word’s another meaning.”

## Vehement Kṛṣṇa

### Verse 308

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *sakhe madhumaṅgala, madhusūdano ’smi. tad eṣā rādhikākhyāṁ gatā bhramarī śulkārtham ādeyā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakhe madhumaṅgala—*O friend Madhumaṅgala; *madhusūdanaḥ—*Madhusūdana; *asmi—*I am; *tat—*therefore; *eṣā—*this; *rādhikā-ākhyām gatā—*has assumed the name Rādhikā; *bhramarī—*lusty woman; *śulka-artham—*for the sake of the tax; *ādeyā—*must be taken*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: My friend Madhumaṅgala, I am Madhusūdana, and so, I must seize this lusty girl named Rādhā as the tax.**

**Комментарий:**

The name Madhusūdana, apart from “the killer (*sūdana*) of the demon Madhu,” could be interpreted as “one who destroys flower nectar (*madhu*),” referring to the bumblebee. In this way, Kṛṣṇa calls Himself a male bumblebee, which justifies His seizing of Rādhā. This is so because the rest of words could be undrestood in an alternative way, implying that Rādhā is the bee: “…and so, I must seize this bee (*bhramarī*) who has more ‘r’ sounds in Her name(*ra-adhika-ākhyā*) as the tax payment.”

### Verse 309

***vṛndā:***  *phulleyaṁ campakalataiva sa-tṛṣo madhusūdanasyocitā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*phullā—*blossoming; *iyam—*this; *campaka-latā—*campaka creeper; *eva—*only; *sa-tṛṣaḥ—*for thirsty; *madhusūdanasya—*bumblebee; *ucitā—*is suitable*.*

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: Only this blossoming campaka creeper is suitable for a thirsty bumblebee.**

**Комментарий:**

Vṛndā indirectly says that of all the gopīs, only blooming in her youth Campakalatā is suitable for the bumblebee Madhusūdana, since her name literally means “*campaka* creeper.”

### Verse 310

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *vṛnde, tattvānabhijñāsi. rādhā khalv abhirūpā yā viparītāpi dhārā mādhvīkamayī sampadyate.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vṛnde—*O Vṛndā; *tattva-anabhijñā—*ignorant of the truth; *asi—*you are; *rādhā—*Rādhā; *khalu—*indeed; *abhirūpā—*beautiful; *yā—*who; *viparītā—*back to front; *api—*even; *dhārā—*the stream; *mādhvīkamayī—*full of intoxicating nectar; *sampadyate—*will turn out well*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Vṛndā, you don’t know the truth. This lovely girl Rādhā will do fine for Me, for even when Her name is read back to front, She is the stream of sweet intoxicating beverage.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa takes up the word play further, offering another reason why it is right that Rādhā be given to Him. *‘Rādhā’* becomes *‘dhārā’*, or “stream,” when read backwards. *‘Viparītā’* also means “contrary,” and so, the second sentence can be alternatively understood as follows: “This lovely girl Rādhā will do fine for Me, for even when She behaves in a refractory way (*viparītā*), She is the stream of sweet intoxicating beverage.”

### Verse 311

***citrā:*** *goula-vīra-vareṇṇa, ‘appaḍima-puṇṇa-lacchī-bharāo imāo’ tti saaṁ ccea samatthidaṁ. tā vinda-pañcaeṇa kadhaṁ amhāṇaṁ ekkadamā geṇhīduṁ juttā?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*goula-vīra-vareṇṇa—*O best of Gokula’s heroes; *appaḍima—*incomparable; *puṇṇa-lacchī—*of complete beauty; *bharāo—*carrying the weight; *imāo—*these \[girls\]; *tti—*thus; *saaṁ—*personally; *ccea—*certainly; *samatthidaṁ—*determined; *tā—*therefore; *vinda-pañcaeṇa—*with five hundred crores; *kadhaṁ—*how; *amhāṇaṁ—*from us; *ekkadamā—*one; *geṇhīduṁ—*to be accepted; *juttā—*is appropriate*.*

**Перевод:**

**Citrā: O foremost hero of Gokula, you yourself have determined that we “carry the weight of unparalleled beauty.” So how could anyone among us be fairly taken in place of just five hundred crores?**

**Комментарий:**

Citrā reverses Kṛṣṇa’s own recent statement (text 297) against Him. She means that each gopī is much more valuable than just five hundred crores.

### Verse 312

***vṛndā:***  *sakhi citre, ślāghyāsi yad eṣa viśṛṅkhalaḥ komala-vāg-vallī-pallavena bhavatyā stambhito gambhīra-vedī stamberamaḥ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakhi citre—*O friend Citrā; *ślāghyā—*praiseworthy; *asi—*you are; *yat—*because; *eṣa—*He; *viśṛṅkhalaḥ—*unrestrained; *komala-vāg—*of your sweet words; *vallī—*of the vine; *pallavena—*by the foliage; *bhavatyā—*by you; *stambhitaḥ—*blocked; *gambhīra—*deeply; *vedī—*feeling; *stamberamaḥ—*the elephant*.*

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: Citrā-sakhī, you are great, for you have trapped this restive elephant with the tender vine foliage of your sweet words!**

**Комментарий:**

Vṛndā compares Kṛṣṇa to a restive elephant. Such elephant is defined as follows:

*tvag-bhedāc choṇita-śrāvān*  
*māṁsasya vyadhanād api*  
*saṁjñāṁ na labhate yas tu*  
*gajo gambhīra-vedy asau*

**“An elephant is called** ***‘gambhīra-vedī,’*** **or “thick-skinned,” when it does not come to its senses even when its skin is cut, blood flows out, or its flesh is pierced.” (from** ***Śāli-hotra*****, a work on the veterinary science)**

So far Kṛṣṇa was invincible to the gopīs arguments. Now, however, smart Citrā has made Him speechless.

### Verse 313

***madhumaṅgalaḥ:***  *pia-vaassa, ‘saa-guṇo-sulukko’ tti ettha asaṅkha-vāiṇo saa-saddassa panti-dahammi pajjavasāṇaṁ kuṇantehiṁ amhehiṁ tassa ujjāṇa-cakkavaṭṭiṇo suṭṭhu abaraddhaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*pia-vaassa—*dear friend; *saa-guṇo—*hundredfold; *sulukko—*fee; *tti—*thus; *ettha—*here; *asaṅkha—*countless; *vāiṇo—*in the phrase that expresses; *saa-saddassa—*of the word ‘*śata*’; *panti-dahammi—*in the meaning of ‘one hundred’; *pajjavasāṇaṁ—*the conclusion; *kuṇantehiṁ—*who are making; *amhehiṁ—*by us; *tassa—*to him; *ujjāṇa—*of groves; *cakkavaṭṭiṇo—*the emperor; *suṭṭhu—*heavily; *abaraddhaṁ—*insult.

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala: My dear friend, we greatly insult the forest grove emperor by concluding that the word ‘hundred’ in his statement ‘hundred times’ means literally one hundred, when it is supposed to mean “countless.”**

**Комментарий:**

Madhumaṅgala quotes from the emperor’s letter (verse 257), where “His Majesty” ordered not to spare the gopīs and increase the tax in case of them caught lying. The word *‘tassa’* (*‘tasmai’*) also implies that the “emperor” will be enraged by such a literal interpretation of his orders.

### Verse 314-315

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *sakhe, sādhu sādhu. priyārtha-rasa-mādhurīm upabhojayan kāyasthikā-rasavatī-paurogavo ’si. satyam, asaṅkhyāny eva vittāni cakravarti-varāṇām abhiprāyeṇa kroḍī-kṛtāni. tathā ca smaryate:*  
*drāghiṣṭhe chadmani jñāte*  
*prakṛtyā garva-śālinām*  
*atyunnata-śriyogrāṇāṁ*  
*yatheṣṭaṁ daṇḍa iṣyate*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakhe—*O friend; *sādhu sādhu—*well said, well said; *priya—*desired; *artha—*that has an objective; *rasa—*of the taste; *mādhurīm—*the sweetness; *upabhojayan—*causing to enjoy; *kāyasthikā—*clerkly; *rasavatī—*in the kitchen; *paurogavaḥ—*the chef; *asi—*you are; *satyam—*truly; *asaṅkhyāni—*countless; *eva—*only; *vittāni—*wealth; *cakravarti-varāṇām—*of the foremost emperors; *abhiprāyeṇa—*by the intention; *kroḍī-kṛtāni—*is embraced; *tathā—*likewise; *ca—*also; *smaryate—*is remembered; *drāghiṣṭhe—*most lasting; *chadmani—*when the deceit; *jñāte—*is known; *prakṛtyā—*by nature; *garva-śālinām—*to the arrogant; *atyunnata—*extremely vast; *śriyā—*due to wealth; *ugrāṇām—*fierce; *yathā-iṣṭam—*to the heart’s content; *daṇḍaḥ—*the punishment; *iṣyate—*is desired.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: My friend, well said, well said. You are the chef of the burocratic “kitchen,” feeding us the sweet taste of our desired goal. Yes, what you say is true: the intended sense of those words is that His Majesty embraces only countless wealth.**

**In this connection I remember a statement: “One can prescribe any punishment to those arrogant frauds, who, aggressive due to their extremely exalted beauty, cheat others for a very long time.”**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa’s glorification of Madhumaṅgala has a hidden meaning: “You are the chef of the burocratic “kitchen,” causing Me to enjoy (*upabhojayan*) the sweet loving mood (*rasa*) of My beloved (*priyā*), who is My ultimate objective (*artha*).”

### Verse 316

***lalitā:***  *daṇḍeṇa viṇā kkhaṇaṁ bi gobāṇaṁ ṇatthi olaṁbo. tā juttaṁ jevva daṇḍa-ggahaṇaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*daṇḍeṇa—*punishment; *biṇā—*without; *kkhaṇaṁ—*a moment; *bi—*even; *gobāṇam—*of the cowherds; *ṇatthi—*there is no; *olaṁbo—*support; *tā—*therefore; *juttaṁ—*proper; *jebba—*certainly; *daṇḍa-ggahaṇaṁ—*accepting a punishment.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: The cowherds are not maintained without punishment even for a moment. So it is fair indeed for them to be punished.**

**Комментарий:**

Lalitā aptly points out that it is Kṛṣṇa and His gang, who perfectly deserve an arbitrary punishment ordained by the rule that He Himself has just now quoted (verse 315). Lalitā plays on the word *‘daṇḍa’* from that rule, which also means “a stick.” The double meaning of Lalitā’s words is as follows: “Without their sticks (*daṇḍeṇa viṇā—‘daṇḍena vinā’*), the cowherds do not find support even for a moment. So it is fair that they accept sticks (*daṇḍa-ggahaṇam—‘daṇḍa-grahaṇam’*).” It is a common behavior of cowherds to lean on their sticks while grazing the cattle.

### Verse 317

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *yadyapi pañcabhir api na śulka-paryāptis tathāpi dvitīyaiva mama rakṣaṇīyā yā khalu candralekhonmīlana-kṣamā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*yadyapi—*even if; *pañcabhiḥ—*with five; *api—*even; *na—*not; *śulka-paryāptiḥ—*completion of the payment; *tathāpi—*still; *dvitīyā—*the second; *eva—*only; *mama—*by Me; *rakṣaṇīyā—*to be kept; *yā—*who; *khalu—*indeed; *candra-lekha—*the moon crescent; *unmīlana—*of opening; *kṣamā—*capable*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Even if all the five girls won’t suffice for the tax, I should still keep this second one, who can surely display the moon crescent.**

**Комментарий:**

According to Kṛṣṇa, the “second” girl is Rādhā (Lalitā is the “first.”) *‘Dvitīyā’* (“second”) here is also taken as the second day (*tithi*) of the waxing fortnight. On the night of *dvitīya-tithi* one can see the thin moon crescent, known in Sanskrit as *‘candra-lekha.’* The word *‘candra-lekha’* also means “a nail mark,” for the shape of a nail mark reminds of the moon crescent. This is an erotic suggestion, for nail marks is the sign that a lover leaves on the body of his beloved during their passionate loving exchanges. Kṛṣṇa in this way says that He will take Rādhā, who has His nail marks on Her body.

### Verse 318

***rādhā:*** *(sotprāsaṁ vihasya) hanta, hanta! devadobāsaṇassa kusumaṁ ociṇṇantīṇaṁ khañjaṇacchīṇaṁ khemaṁ kkhu vundāaṇaṁ ummadeṇa imiṇā ekkeṇa mahā-kalahindeṇa savvaṁ ākkamia bhaaṅkaraṁ kidaṁ. kealaṁ selindassa ubasalla-mettaṁ olambaṇaṁ āsi. haddhī, haddhī! ettha bi dāṇindārambha-dambheṇa vaṭṭa-pāḍidā āraddhā. kassa viṇṇavissamha?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-utprāsam—*with outburst; *vihasya—*laughing; *hanta hanta—*alas, alas; *devada-ubāsaṇassa—*for worship of a deity; *kusumaṁ—*for flowers; *ociṇṇantīṇaṁ—*for those who are searching; *khañjaṇa-acchīṇaṁ—*with eyes like *khañjana* birds; *khemaṁ—*safe; *kkhu—*indeed; *vundāaṇaṁ—*Vṛndāvana; *ummadeṇa—*mad; *imiṇā—*by this; *ekkeṇa—*one; *mahā-kalahindeṇa—*great lord of elephants; *savvaṁ—*the whole; *ākkamia—*having invaded; *bhaaṅkaraṁ—*fearsonme; *kidaṁ—*made; *kealaṁ—*only; *selindassa—*of the lord of mountains; *ubasalla—*the precincts; *mettaṁ—*just; *olambaṇaṁ—*support; *āsi—*was; *haddhī haddhī—*fie, fie; *ettha—*there; *bi—*even; *dāṇi-inda—*of the king of tax collectors; *ārambha—*of the enterprise; *dambheṇa—*by the deceit; *vaṭṭa-pāḍidā—*waylaying; *āraddhā—*begun; *kassa—*whom; *viṇṇavissamha—*we shall tell.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (laughing wildly): Alas, alas! The safe forest of Vṛndāvana, where the girls with eyes like khañjana birds search for flowers to worship their deity, was made fearsome by one mad chief of elephants, who has invaded the whole of it. The close surroundings of Govardhana hill were the only shelter left to those girls. And what a misfortune! Even there the king of tax collectors has established a fake toll station to waylay us. Whom shall we complain about our predicament?**

**Комментарий:**

Like a poor citizen harrassed by the unjust government, Rādhā asks in desperation: “Who could possibly restrain a prince?”

### Verse 319

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *nāndīmukhi, tava karṇam adhirūḍhāsau gurvī girām anargalatā. yad atra san-mārga-rakṣā-prakhyāta-viśuddhau mayy api vartma-pātitā-parivāda-kālimādhyāsa-sāhasitā. tad enāṁ dor-daṇḍa-yugalena gāḍhaṁ pīḍayāmi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nāndīmukhi—*O Nāndīmukhī; *tava—*your; *karṇam—*ear; *adhirūḍhā—*has reached; *asau—*this; *gurvī—*heavy; *girām—*of the words; *anargalatā—*the being without obstacle; *yat—*that; *atra—*here; *sat-mārga—*of the righteous path; *rakṣā—*of the protection; *prakhyāta—*renowned; *viśuddhau—*having the purity; *mayi—*on Me; *api—*even; *vartma-pātitā—*for waylaying; *parivāda—*of the blame; *kālimā—*the blackness; *adhyāsa—*of imposing; *sāhasitā—*the arrogance; *tat—*therefore; *enām—*Her; *doḥ-daṇḍa—*of My stick-like hands; *yugalena—*by the pair; *gāḍham—*strongly; *pīḍayāmi—*I will press.

**Перевод:**
