**Kṛṣṇa: O Nāndīmukhī, this violent flow of speech has reached your ears. The glory of My virtue of protecting the path of righteousness is spread everywhere, but this gopī is impudent enough to stain it by accusing Me of waylaying. Therefore, I shall squish Her with My boltlike arms.**

### Verse 320

***nāndīmukhī:*** *(puro ’vasthāya vārayantī) suvīra, mahā-tābasī-parivārassa me samakkham akkhamaṁ kkhuṁ edaṁ kula-bālā-pīḍaṇaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*puraḥ--*in front; *avasthāya—*standing; *vārayantī—*while blocking; *suvīra—*O noble hero; *mahā-tābasī—*of great ascetics; *parivārassa—*who comes from the family; *me—*for me; *samakkham—*in front of my eyes; *akkhamam—*intolerable; *kkhuṁ—*indeed; *edam—*this; *kula-bālā—*of noble girls; *pīḍaṇaṁ—*squishing.

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī (standing in front of Kṛṣṇa, blocking His way): O noble hero, I am coming from the family of great ascetics and cannot tolerate when somebody squishes noble girls in front of my eyes.**

**Комментарий:**

By addressing Kṛṣṇa “noble hero,” Nāndīmukhī hopes that Kṛṣṇa will refrain from the audacious act He intends to perform.

### Verse 321

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  
*goṣṭha-mahendra-kumāraś*  
*cūḍāmaṇir asmi muhur ahaṁyūnām*  
*katham unmada-yuvatīnām*  
*upekṣitāhe ’dya darpakoṣmāṇam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*goṣṭha—*of a cowherd village; *mahā-indra—*of the great king; *kumāraḥ—*the son; *cūḍāmaṇiḥ—*the crest jewel; *asmi—*am; *muhuḥ—*again; *ahaṁyūnām—*of the proud; *katham—*how; *unmada—*insane; *yuvatīnām—*of young girls; *upekṣitāhe—*I can overlook; *adya—*today; *darpaka—*of pride; *uṣmāṇam—*the causticity.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: I am the son of the lord of the cowherd village and the crest jewel of the proud as well. How then I can overlook the vitriolic arrogance of these insane girls?**

**Комментарий:**

*‘Ahaṁyūnām’* can also be read as two separate words *‘aham’* and *‘yūnām,’* which would result in meaning “I (*aham*) am the crest jewel of the young boys (*yūnām*).” Kṛṣṇa’s question has a double meaning: “How then I can overlook the amorous (*darpaka*) exuberance (*uṣmāṇam*) of these insane girls?”

### Verse 322

***lalitā:***  *kaṇha, sakkhaṁ kadhesi. ettha ṇāvarajjhasi tumaṁ. ghaṭṭī-deīe ccea eso apuvvo ko bi pasādo jeṇa sak-kula-kumaro bi dhutta-dhurīṇāṇaṁ bi suṭṭhu vimhābaṇīṁ kañcana-vijjaṁ jhatti ajjhāvido si.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kaṇha—*O Kṛṣṇa; *sakkhaṁ—*sweetly; *kadhesi—*You speak; *ettha—*here; *ṇa—*are not; *avarajjhasi—*the offender; *tumaṁ—*You; *ghaṭṭī-deīe—*of the toll gate goddess; *ccea—*only; *eso—*this; *apuvvo—*unprecedented; *ko bi—*some; *pasādo—*mercy; *jeṇa—*due to which; *sak-kula-kumaro—*the son of a noble family; *bi—*even; *dhutta—*of the cunning; *dhurīṇāṇaṁ—*of the chiefs; *bi—*even; *suṭṭhu—*well; *vimhābaṇīṁ—*astounding; *kañcana—*of gold; *vijjaṁ—*knowledge; *jhatti—*quickly; *ajjhāvido—*are educated; *asi—*You*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: O Kaṇha, You do speak sweetly. You are not at fault here. This must be some unprecedented grace of no one else but the toll gate goddess that even You, the offspring of a noble clan, have quickly acquired an astonishing expertise in gold dealings of the best swindlers.**

**Комментарий:**

This is nothing but another attempt by Lalitā to ridicule Kṛṣṇa.

### Verse 323

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(sāṭopam)*  
*ghaṭṭādhirājam avamatya vivādam eva*  
*yūyaṁ yad ācaratha śulkam aditsamānāḥ*  
*manye vidhitsatha tad atra gires taṭeṣu*  
*durgeṣu hanta viṣameṣu raṇābhiyogam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-āṭopam—*with pride; *ghaṭṭa-adhirājam—*the toll gate superintendent; *avamatya—*defying; *vivādam—*argument; *eva—*only; *yūyam—*you; *yat—*since; *ācaratha—*you create; *śulkam—*the fee; *aditsamānāḥ—*not wishing to give; *manye—*I consider; *vidhitsatha—*you are making; *tat—*for that reason; *atra—*here; *gireḥ—*of the mountain; *taṭeṣu—*on the slopes; *durgeṣu—*that are hard to reach; *hanta—*alas; *viṣameṣu—*uneven; *raṇa—*for the battle; *abhiyogam—*preparation.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (proudly): None of you wishes to offer the fee. Instead, you create only useless arguments, defying the superintendent of the toll station. Alas, I think it must be so, because you wish to get ready to attack us on these uneven and impassable mountain slopes.**

**Комментарий:**

*‘Viṣameṣu’* is also an epithet of Kandarpa, the god of love. He is called so, because the number of his arrows (*iṣu*) is odd (*viṣama*), representing the five sense objects—form, taste, smell, sound, and touch. Compounding *‘viṣameṣu’* with *‘raṇābhiyogam’* would give another meaning to the sentence: “Alas, I think it is so, because you wish to launch an attack of Cupid (*viṣameṣu-raṇābhiyogam*) on these impassable mountain slopes.”

### Verse 324

***rādhā:*** *mohaṇa, kettiaṁ sahissamha? jaṁ ‘atinirmathanād agniś candanād api jāyate’ tti vaaṇaṁ pamāṇaṁ. tā ettha amha-dūsaṇaṁ ṇa deaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mohaṇa—*O bewilderer; *kettiam—*how much; *sahissamha—*we shall tolerate; *jaṁ—*since; *atinirmathanāt—*from excessive friction; *agniḥ—*fire; *candanāt—*from sandalwood; *api—*even; *jāyate—*appears; *tti—*thus; *vaaṇaṁ—*the statement; *pamāṇaṁ—*the evidence; *tā—*therefore; *ettha—*in this regard; *amha—*us; *dūsaṇaṁ—*blaming; *ṇa deaṁ—*should not be given*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: O deceiver, how much more we shall tolerate this? “Due to excessive friction fire appears even from sandalwood.” The statement is true, so please don’t blame us in case we lose our temper.**

**Комментарий:**

This is Rādhā’s response to Kṛṣṇa’s continuous instigations. Sandalwood is naturally cooling, and is rarely used in connection with fire or heat. In this way, the meaning of the proverb is that sometimes even sober persons lose their temper if continuously agitated.

### Verse 325

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  
*apaṭu-bhrama-kāriṇībhir ābhiḥ*  
*kuṭināṭībhir alaṁ prasīda devi*  
*vitarādya dhanāni vā mad-iṣṭāny*  
*anutiṣṭhātanu-saṅgara-kriyāṁ vā*

**Пословный перевод:**

*apaṭu—*the inexpert; *bhrama-kāriṇībhiḥ—*that confuse; *ābhiḥ—*these; *kuṭināṭībhiḥ—*crooked ways; *alam—*enough of; *prasīda—*show mercy; *devi—*O noble lady; *vitara—*submit; *adya—*now; *dhanāni—*wealth; vā—or; *mat-iṣṭāni—*desired by Me; *anutiṣṭha—*perform; *atanu—*massive; *saṅgara-kriyām—*the military operation; *vā—*otherwise.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O Devī, please show mercy: Stop Your crooked ways that bewilder only the simpletons and submit the wealth I want. Otherwise, start Your massive attack.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa says that Rādhā can’t fool Him with Her diplomacy, for it is only for “the simpletons” (*apaṭu*), or, in other words, fools, who are misled by the outward meanings. With this, Kṛṣṇa suggests: “I am not such a fool, but the wisest one—*parama-paṭu*, the knower of secret meanings. I know well how unsteady You are, and I got enough of Your hints.” Kṛṣṇa offers the gopīs a choice to have it either His or their way—“pay money or fight.” However, He invites them to start an “attack of love,” for the word *‘atanu’* (literally, “not small”) is another name of Cupid, meaning “bodiless.” It refers to an instance of Lord Śiva’s burning Kāmadeva to ashes, when the latter wanted to disrupt Śiva’s meditation.

### Verse 326

***vṛndā:***  
*tvaṁ mahā-sāṁyugīno ’si*  
*saṁyuge khyātim āgataḥ*  
*yoddhuṁ tatas tvayā sārdhaṁ*  
*kṣamantām abalāḥ katham*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tvam—*You; *mahā-sāṁyugīnaḥ—*a great warrior; *asi—*are; *saṁyuge—*on the battlefield; *khyātim—*glory; *āgataḥ—*have attained; *yoddhum—*to fight; *tataḥ—*thus; *tvayā sārdham—*with You; *kṣamantām—*should be able; *abalāḥ—*ladies; *katham—*how.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: You are a great warrior, having attained glory on the battlefied. How then could these fragile girls put up a fight with You?**

**Комментарий:**

“You are a great warrior, having attained glory in the loving union (*saṁyuge*).” The rest of the verse implies the following: “Therefore it is only proud and impudent women who are on par with You, not these fragile gopīs.”

## Verse 327(a-b)

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *kānane-cari, svarūpānabhijñāsi—*  
*paśyonnata-śroṇi-rathā mataṅgaja-*  
*kramojjvalāḥ suṣṭhu padāti-śobhanāḥ*  
*kāmasya cañcat-kaca-bhāra-cāmarāś*  
*camūr amūś cāru-camūru-locanāḥ*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kānane-cari—*O forest dweller; *svarūpa—*of their true identity; *anabhijñā—*not aware; *asi—*You are; *paśya—*look; *unnata—*raised; *śroṇi—*whose hips; *rathāḥ—*chariotlike; *mataṅga-ja—*elephant-like; *krama—*with their power; *ujjvalāḥ—*splendid; *suṣṭhu—*well; *padāti—*with their foot soldiers; *śobhanāḥ—*magnificent; *kāmasya—*of the love god; *cañcat—*moving repeatedly; *kaca—*of their hair; *bhāra—*the mass; *cāmarāḥ—*whisk-like; *camūḥ—*at phalanxes; *amūḥ—*these; *cāru—*beautiful; *camūru—*doe-like; *locanāḥ—*with eyes.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O forest dweller, you don’t know who these ‘fragile girls’ really are. Just look at these phalanxes of Kāmadeva: Raised hips are their chariots and, splendid with the might of an elephant, they look magnificent with their foot soldiers. These phalanxes are further equipped with whisks of heavy waving hair and their beautiful eyes are like those of a doe.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa here refutes Vṛndā’s argument that the gopīs are just some fragile girls. As described in the *Mahaṭī-ṭīkā*, He points at the gopīs’ hips, feet, hair, and eyes with His index finger and shows how these parts of their bodies are actually the phalanxes of the Cupid’s army.

‘*Mataṅgaja-kramojjvalāḥ’* could also mean “splendid with the gait (*krama*) of an elephant,” and *‘padāti-śobhanāḥ’* can be also understood as “extremely beautiful (*ati-śobhanāḥ*) with their feet (*padā*).”

### Verse 328

***rādhā:*** *ṇāara, diṭṭhaṁ kula-bāliā-vimohaṇaṁ idaṁ de ghaṭṭinda-jālaṁ. tā puṇo alaṁ vitthāreṇa. sahī-saṇāhā jaṇṇa-vediaṁ calidamhi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ṇāara—*O suave boy; *diṭṭhaṁ—*is seen; *kula-bāliā—*the noble girls; *vimohaṇaṁ—*bewildering; *idam—*this; *de—*Your; *ghaṭṭa—*of the toll gate; *inda-jālaṁ—*trick; *tā—*therefore; *puṇo—*again; *alaṁ—*enough; *vitthāreṇa—*of elaboration; *sahī—*by My friend; *saṇāhā—*protected; *jaṇṇa-vediaṁ—*to the sacrificial arena; *calidamhi—*I am going.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: O suave boy, we have understood the trick of Your so-called toll gate, that is meant to bewilder the noble girls. So stop repeating it again. I am starting to the sacrificial arena, and My friends are protecting Me.**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā implies: “It is obvious from Your own words that ‘there is no sense of what is good and what is bad when one’s desired object is hard to obtain.’”

By saying that the gopīs’ path is clear of all obstacles, Rādhā actually gives a secret hint: “We, who can’t tolerate being late, should be stopped by You on the pretext of a tax.” The word *indra-jālam* (“trick”), therefore, does not refer to Kṛṣṇa’s pretending to be a tax collector, but to His covert intention to have a love meeting the gopīs.

### Verse 329

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *ghūrṇitākṣi, ghana-ghaṭṭa-kareṇa śīghram āghrātāsi. kathaṁ calane tvaṁ prabhavitāsi?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ghūrṇita-akṣi—*O You with rolling eyes; *ghana—*hard; *ghaṭṭa-kareṇa—*by the toll tax; *śīghram—*quickly; *āghrātā—*will be devoured; *asi—*You; *katham—*how; *calane—*of going; *tvam—*You; *prabhavitā—*will be capable; *asi—*are.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O One with rolling eyes, the hard to pay tax has already devoured You. How will You be able to go?**

**Комментарий:**

This is the inner meaning: “You will be quickly captured (*āghrātāsi*) by My hand (*kareṇa*), which moves (*ghaṭṭa*) very swiftly (*ghana*).” Also, the word *āghrātā*, which literally means “smelt,” implies that Kṛṣṇa’s hand is like the uncontrollable trunk of an elephant, which is also called *‘kara’* in Sanskrit. In essence, Kṛṣṇa informs Rādhā that if She will attempt to proceed, He will get Her back with His own hands.

### Verse 330-331

***campakalatā:*** *kiṁ kkhu bhoa-rāassa ahiārī hosi jaṁ kara-dāṇeṇa ārāhaṇijjo tumaṁ?*  
***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *campakalate, bhoga-rāgasyādhikārī tathyam asmi. tathāpi nātīva tuṣṭir mama kara-dānenārādhane. tataḥ prayatna-nigūḍhān kāñcana-kumbhān eva sparśayantu bhavatyaḥ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kiṁ—*whether; *kkhu—*indeed; *bhoa-rāassa—*of the king of Bhoja dynasty, Kaṁsa; *ahiārī—*an officer; *hosi—*You are; *jaṁ—*that; *kara—*a tax; *dāṇeṇa—*by paying; *ārāhaṇijjo—*should be worshiped; *tumaṁ—*You; *campakalate—*O Campakalatā; *bhoga—*to sensuous enjoyment; *rāgasya—*of the attachment; *adhikārī—*an officer; *tathyam—*truly; *asmi—*I am; *tathā api—*still; *na—*not; *atīva—*excessively; *tuṣṭiḥ—*satisfaction; *mama—*for Me; *kara-dānena—*by paying a tax; *ārādhane—*in honoring; *tataḥ—*therefore; *prayatna—*with an endeavor; *nigūḍhān—*hidden; *kāñcana—*golden; *kumbhān—*pots; *eva—*only; *sparśayantu—*give; *bhavatyaḥ—*you \[gopīs\].

**Перевод:**

**Campakalatā: Are You Kaṁsa’s officer that one has to honor You by paying a tax?**

**Kṛṣṇa: O Campakalatā, it’s a fact: I am an appointed officer of an addict to sensuous pleasures. But still, since I am not very much satisfied by someone’s honoring Me by paying a tax, you gopīs must give me those same golden pots that you have so diligently hid.**

**Комментарий:**

The Prākṛt compound *‘bhoa-rāassa’* from Campakalatā’s speech is ambiguous when translated into Sanskrit. Apart from *‘bhoja-rājasya,’* or ‘of the King of Bhojas(Kaṁsa),’ it could be read as *‘bhoga-rāgasya,’* which could be understood as ‘of \[one who is\] addicted (*rāgasya*) to sensuous pleasures (*bhoga*),’ describing the emperor Mahā-manmatha, or “Cupid The Great.” So Kṛṣṇa, who is “an appointed officer” of such a ruler will not be fully satisfied if the gopīs give Him just their hands (*kara-dānena*). To make Him fully satisfied, Kṛṣṇa demands that they offer Him the golden pots that they are smuggling—their breasts!

### Verse 332

***lalitā:***  *hanta muddhā-viḍambaṇa-cādurī-gavvida, pekkha imāo viaḍḍha-ppavarāo goṭṭha-juadi-vallīo raṇṇo ghaṭṭa-dāṇaṁ kaṭṭia salīlaṁ calanti. tā ujjāṇa-cakkavaṭṭiṇaṁ gadua phuk-karehi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*hanta—*alas; *muddhā—*the silly; *viḍambaṇa—*in cheating; *cādurī—*of Your wits; *gavvida—*O proud; *pekkha—*look; *imāo—*at these; *viaḍḍha—*of the wise; *ppavarāo—*chiefs; *goṭṭha-juadi—*of the cowherd maidens; *ballīo—*the best; *raṇṇo—*to the king; *ghaṭṭa-dāṇaṁ—*the toll tax; *kaṭṭia—*tearing apart; *sa-līlaṁ—*playfully; *calanti—*are walking; *tā—*therefore; *ujjāṇa-cakkavaṭṭiṇaṁ—*to the grove emperor; *gadua—*going; *phuk-karehi—*shout.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: Alas, o Kṛṣṇa, You are proud of being as wise as to fool the simpletons. But just look at these most clever, vine-like cowherd girls: they are walking with ease, having crushed into pieces the payment of the king’s tax. So go and complain to Your ‘emperor of the groves.’**

### Verse 333

***kṛṣṇaḥ :***  
*bhuja-vikramiṇā mayā kathaṁ vā*  
*tava hetor api phūt-kṛtir vidheyā*  
*dvipa-darpa-harasya ko harer vā*  
*hariṇī-vṛnda-vimardane prayāsaḥ*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhuja—*with the arms; *vikramiṇā—*powerful; *mayā—*by Me; *katham—*why; *vā—*indeed; *tava—*for your; *hetoḥ—*sake; *api—*even; *phūt-kṛtiḥ—*the expression of disgust; *vidheyā—*should be shown; *dvipa—*of the elephant; *darpa—*of pride; *harasya—*for the remover; *kaḥ—*what; *hareḥ—*of a lion; *vā—*indeed; *hariṇī—*of does; *vṛnda—*the multitude; *vimardane—*of crushing; *prayāsaḥ—*the labor.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: I am a mighty-armed hero. So why should I go and complain to the emperor just because of you? After all, what is the problem for a lion, who crushes the pride of an elephant, to devastate the hord of does?**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa impresses upon Lalitā that there is no need for Him to go the emperor and complain about them gopīs. Kṛṣṇa Himself can handle them in the same manner and with the same ease as a lion devastates the hord of does. By using the word *hareḥ* (“of a lion”) Kṛṣṇa hints at Himself, since the same word can be understood as “of Hari.”

This verse is the instance of *nivṛtti* (citation of an example)—one of the *bhāṇikā’s* seven sub-elements (see Appendix…).

### Verse 334

***rādhā:*** *kiṁ kādavvaṁ hariṇā jaṁ sarahasaṁ validā purado lalidā?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kiṁ—*what; *kādavvaṁ—*can do; *hariṇā—*a lion; *jaṁ—*that; *sarahasaṁ—*with power; *validā—*has appeared; *purado—*in front; *lalitā—*Lalitā*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: What can a lion do when fierce Lalitā is in front Him?**

**Комментарий:**

Another meaning: “What can a lion do when Lalitā with a *śarabha* (*saraha-saṁvalitā*) beast in front of Him?” *Purāṇas* and the *Mahābhārata* describe *śarabha* as an eight-legged beast capable of killing even a lion.

## Verse 335(a-b)

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *paṅkajākṣi, niṣṭaṅkitam avadhāryatām—*  
*vidyotase kalpa-lateva kāma-dā*  
*dhunoṣi ca vyāyata-cilli-kārmukam*  
*ity artha-puñjaṁ mama dehi puṣkalaṁ*  
*kiṁ vā sakhībhiḥ saha suṣṭhu vigraham*

**Пословный перевод:**

*paṅkaja—*like lotuses; *akṣi—*O One with eyes; *niṣṭaṅkitam—*on the verbal expression; *avadhāryatām—*reflect; *vidyotase—*You are splendid; *kalpa-latā—*a wish-fulfilling creeper; *iva—*like; *kāma-dā—*the bestower of one’s desires; *dhunoṣi—*You shake; *ca—*and; *vyāyata—*wide; *cilli—*of Your eyebrows; *kārmukam—*the bow; *iti—*thus; *artha—*of meanings; *puñjam—*the multitude; *mama—*Me; *dehi—*give; *puṣkalam—*abundant; *kim vā—*or else; *sakhībhiḥ—*the sakhīs; *saha—*with; *suṣṭhu—*well; *vigraham—*a fight.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O lotus-eyed One, please reflect on this: “You look resplendent, granting all one’s desires just like a wish-fulfilling creeper, and You shake the bows of Your elongated eyebrows.” Please explain to Me the rich multitude of meanings contained in these words, or otherwise, along with Your friends, give Me a fight.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa describes Rādhārāṇī as *‘kāma-dā,’* or “one who grants all desires,” which could also mean “one who ignites lusty desires.” The last sentence could alternatively mean “Otherwise, along with Your friends, give Me your bodies (*vigraham*).”

### Verse 336

***lalitā:***  *(kuṭilaṁ vilokya) gobiā-hāra, saṅkhacūḍobamaddaṇeṇa vikkhāda-vikkamo si. tā juttā viggahāhilāsuadā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kuṭilam—*cunningly; *vilokya—*glancing; *gobiā-hāra—*O stealer of the gopīs; *saṅkhacūḍa—*of Śaṅkhacūḍa; *ubamaddaṇeṇa—*due to killing; *vikkhāda—*renowned; *vikkamo—*whose might; *si—*You have become; *tā—*therefore; *juttā—*proper; *viggaha—*for a fight; *ahilāsuadā—*the eagerness*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (glancing cunningly): O kidnapper of the gopīs, You are famous for Your strength, for You have slayed Śaṅkhacūḍa. So it is quite befitting for You to be eager for a fight.**

**Комментарий:**

This is the confidential meaning of Lalitā’s words: “…You are famous for Your strength, for You crush (*upamardanena*) the gopīs’ necklaces (*gopikā-hāra*) and diadems made of conchshells (*śaṅkha-cūḍa*). So it is quite befitting for You to be eager for their bodies (*vigraha*).”

### Verse 337

***rādhā:*** *hanta sahīo, kidaṁ parissamābaṇodaṇaṁ. tā turiaṁ ukkhibadha ṇiaṁ ṇiaṁ gaggariaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*hanta—*alas; *sahīo—*O sakhīs; *kidaṁ—*done; *parissama—*of the exhaustion; *avaṇodaṇam—*the driving away; *tā—*therefore; *turiaṁ—*hastily; *ukkhibadha—*lift up; *ṇiaṁ ṇiaṁ—*your personal; *gaggariaṁ—*pots.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: All right sakhīs, we took enough rest. Quickly take your pots.**

### Verse 338

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *sakhe subala, sambhṛtenāvaṣṭambha-kumbhikā-pañcakena satvaraṁ ghaṭṭa-catvaraṁ pariṣkriyatāṁ tvayā. mayā paścād atra pañcamī dhārayiṣyate yataḥ punnāga-priyāsau.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakhe subala—*O friend Subala; *sambhṛtena—*gathered together; *avaṣṭambha—*golden; *kumbhikā—*pots; *pañcakena—*by five; *sa-tvaram—*with haste; *ghaṭṭa-catvaram—*the toll crossing; *pariṣkriyatām—*should be decorated; *tvayā—*by You; *mayā—*by Me; *paścāt—*afterward; *atra—*here; *pañcamī—*the *pañcamī tithi*; *dhārayiṣyate—*will be observed; *yataḥ—*because; *pum-nāga—*to the great snakes; *priyā—*dear; *asau—*it*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: My friend Subala, hurry up, gather these five golden pots and decorate with them the toll gate crossing. After that, I shall observe here the pañcamī tithi, for it is the favorite day of the great snakes.**

**Комментарий:**

The five golden pots that Subala is supposed to gather are the pots with ghee that the gopīs are just about to take from the ground. Kṛṣṇa says He wants to use them as decorations for the celebration of *nāga-pañcamī. Nāga-pañcamī* is the fifth *tithi* in the bright half of the *Śrāvaṇa* month (July-August), on which the Hindus all over India worship snakes according to their local customs. Some people commemorate the pastime of Kṛṣṇa’s chastising Kāliya, which, according to some, occurred on this day. Viśvanātha Cakravartī glosses *punnāga* as *mahāsarpa*, which may refer to the eight great snakes described in the Vedic literature—Ananta, Vāsuki, Śeṣa, Padmanābha, Kambala, Śaṅkhapāla, Dhārtarāṣṭra, Takṣaka, and Kāliya.

Kṛṣṇa’s last sentence has a hidden meaning: “After that, I shall keep the fifth girl (*pañcamī*) here, for She is the favorite of the best among men (*punnāga*).” By the “fifth” girl Kṛṣṇa means Rādharāṇī. Kṛṣṇa’s idea is that after giving away the pots the gopīs would go, and He would keep Rādhā to Himself.

### Verse 339-340

***subalaḥ:***  *(parikramya) lalide, bhāra-dukkha-ppadāo ghaṭṭa-gharaṁ lambhemi tumha-kumbhiāo. tā suhaṁ calantu hodīo.*  
***lalitā:***  *(sāvajña-smitam avalokya) haddhī haddhī! are cillāa-cāuriā-cora-cakkavaṭṭi-līlāmacca! sa{i}dāe bi lalidāe ko kkhu ṇīsajjhaso tiṇaṁ bi parihariduṁ ajjhavassadi?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*parikramya—*walking about; *lalide—*O Lalitā; *bhāra—*of heavy weight; *dukkha-ppadāo—*that give suffering; *ghaṭṭa-gharam—*to the toll booth; *lambhemi—*I will deliver; *tumha—*your; *kumbhiāo—*pots; *tā—*thus; *suham—*comfortably; *calantu—*please go; *hodīo—*you; *sa-avajña—*with neglect; *smitam—*with a smile; *avalokya—*looking; *haddhī haddhī—*fie, fie; *are—*O; *cillāa—*of Cillāta; *cāuriā—*having wisdom; *cora-cakkavaṭṭi—*of the thief emperor; *līlā—*of entertainment; *āmacca—*the minister; *sa{i}dāe—*from sleeping; *bi—*even; *lalidāe—*Lalitā; *ko—*who; *kkhu—*indeed; *ṇīsajjhaso tiṇaṁ—*a blade of grass; *bi—*even; *parihariduṁ—*to take away; *ajjhavassadi—*will attempt.

**Перевод:**

**Subala (pacing about): O Lalitā, these pots must be tormenting you with their heavy weight. I will take them to the toll booth, so that you may proceed with ease.**

**Lalitā (looking with a disdainful smile): To hell with you, the entertainment minister of the king of thieves who are smart like Cillāta! Where is that brave person who will attempt to snatch even a blade of grass from Lalitā even when she is asleep?**

**Комментарий:**

Cillāta is a name of a famous thief.

### Verse 341-342

***subalaḥ:***  *(sa-śaṅkaṁ parāvṛtya) pia-vaassa, ekko haṁ kadhaṁ kumbha-pañcaaṁ āharissaṁ? tā imehiṁ vaassehiṁ saddhaṁ saaṁ ccea saṇṇihehi.*  
***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *sakhe, subalo ’pi suṣṭhu durbalo ’si yad eṣa lalitāyāḥ phalgubhir eva maṭa-sphaṭibhis tvam uccāṭitaḥ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-śaṅkam*—with apprehension; *parāvṛtya*—returning; *pia-vaassa*—O dear friend; *ekko—*alone; *haṁ—*I; *kadhaṁ—*how; *kumbha-pañcaaṁ—*five pots; *āharissaṁ—*will bring; *tā—*therefore; *imehiṁ—*these; *vaassehiṁ—*companions; *saddhaṁ—*these; *saaṁ—*personally; *ccea—*only; *saṇṇihehi—*put down; *sakhe—*O friend; *subalaḥ—*Subala; *api—*though; *suṣṭhu—*quite; *durbalaḥ—*weak; *asi—*you are; *yat—*because; *eṣaḥ—*this; *lalitāyāḥ—*of Lalitā; *phalgubhiḥ—*petty; *eva—*only; *maṭa-sphaṭibhiḥ—*by bragging; *tvam—*you; *uccāṭitaḥ—*taken aback*.*

**Перевод:**

**Subala (turning around, says apprehensively): Dear friend, how will I fetch all the five pots alone? You should bring together the pots Yourself with Your friends.**

**Kṛṣṇa: My friend, although known as Subala, you are quite a weakling. You have retreated just by hearing the petty bragging of Lalitā.**

**Комментарий:**

*‘Subala’* literally means “very strong.” Kṛṣṇa teases Subala in a way a boy would tease his friend.

### Verse 343

***subalaḥ:***  *(sa-narma-smitam apavārya) pia-vaassa, alaṁ vaaṇa-metta-sulaheṇa dappa-rāsiṇā. suṭṭhu paccakkhī-kida-vikkamo si jaṁ tassiṁ diahe rāhiāe saddhaṁ vaṭṭantammi kīlā-jjūe kūḍeṇa jaaṁ ugghusia lalidāe āaḍḍhida-muralīo tumaṁ kotthuha-saṅgobaṇaṁ kuṇanto sumhera-muhīhiṁ sahīhiṁ kaḍakkhijjanto saṅkāulo tuṇṇaṁ saṁvutto āsi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-narma—*with a joke; *smitam—*a smile; *apavārya—*hiding; *pia-vaassa—*O dear friend; *alaṁ—*enough; *vaaṇa-metta—*just by words; *sulaheṇa—*easily achieved; *dappa—*of pride; *rāsiṇā—*of the abundance; *suṭṭhu—*well; *paccakkhī-kida—*made visible; *vikkamo—*the One whose valor; *si—*You are; *jaṁ—*because; *tassiṁ—*on that; *diahe—*day; *rāhiāe—*Rādhā; *saddhaṁ—*with; *vaṭṭantammi—*was going on; *kīlā—*playful; *jjūe—*when gambling; *kūḍeṇa—*by trick; *jaam—*the victory; *ugghusia—*announcing; *lalidāe—*by Lalitā; *āaḍḍhida—*taken away; *muralīo—*whose flute; *tumaṁ—*You; *kotthuha—*of the Kaustubha jem; *saṅgobaṇaṁ—*the concealing; *kuṇanto—*doing; *sumhera—*smilimg charmingly; *muhīhiṁ—*whose faces; *sahīhiṁ—*by sakhīs; *kaḍakkhi—*the object of their sidelong glances; *jjanto—*made; *saṅkā—*by fear; *āulo—*perturbed; *tuṇṇaṁ—*quickly; *saṁvutto—*became; *āsi—*You*.*

**Перевод:**

**Subala (hiding his playful smile): My dear friend, stop being so much puffed up. It is very easy to increase one’s pride by speaking empty words. Everyone witnessed Your valor very well on that day when You gambled for fun with Rādhikā and announced Your unfair victory. Lalitā then snatched Your flute. And when You hid Your Kaustubha jem and charmingly smiling gopīs leered at You, You became terrified in a moment.**

**Комментарий:**

In response to Kṛṣṇa’s humiliating him, Subala challenges Kṛṣṇa that all His proud statements are simply false boasting, for practically, He turns timid on the spot when the matter comes to real action, like at the gambling match with Rādhikā.

Description of gambling contests between Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa is found in the Gauḍīya-vaiṣṇava literature. Subala, however, does not refer to any of the contests that are described in the accounts currently available to us.

### Verse 344-345

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(smṛtvā) mṛṣā-vāda-vaijñānika, maunaṁ bhaja. mahā-samīraṇasya me vikrama-dhāṭī-parispandanārambhe rambheva keyaṁ varākī lalitā.*  
***lalitā:***  *visāhe, taṁ amha-pia-sahī-bhāduaṁ siridāma-joindaṁ vandehi jeṇa parāṇāāmeṇa baliṭṭhaṁ mārudaṁ ṇijjidī-kadua mahārambhāṇaṁ goīṇaṁ goṭṭhī upphullī-kidā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smṛtvā—*remembering; *mṛṣā-vāda—*in false arguments; *vaijñānika—*O expert; *maunam—*silence; *bhaja—*observe; *mahā-samīraṇasya—*of great storm; *me—*My; *vikrama—*of power; *dhāṭī—*by the assault; *parispandana—*of shaking; *ārambhe—*in the beginning; *rambhā—*the plantain; *iva—*like; *kā—*who; *iyam—*this; *varākī—*wretch; *lalitā—*Lalitā; *visāhe—*O Viśākhā; *taṁ—*therefore; *amha—*our; *pia-sahī—*of dear sakhī; *bhāduaṁ—*the brother; *siridāma—*to Śrīdāmā; *joindaṁ—*the chief of yogīs; *vandehi—*pray; *jeṇa—*by whom; *parāṇāāmeṇa—*by his breath control; *baliṭṭhaṁ—*the strongest; *mārudaṁ—*wind; *ṇijjidī-kadua—*conquered; *mahā-ārambhāṇaṁ—*possessed of great pride; *goīṇaṁ—*of the gopīs; *goṭṭhī—*the assembly; *upphullī-kidā—*gladdened.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (recollecting): You, expert in false arguments, keep your mouth shut! Who is this wretch Lalitā? I am a hurricane and she is a banana tree that trembles when I begin My violent outburst.**

**Lalitā: O Viśākhā, you must pray to Śrīdāmā, the brother of our dear sakhī, who is the best of yogīs. He subdued the wildest storm by prāṇāyāma and thereby gladdened the assembly of the proud gopīs.**

**Комментарий:**

As Kṛṣṇa used figurative language by calling himself a hurricane and Lalitā a banana tree, Lalitā speaks in the same manner by calling Śrīdāmā the best of yogīs, who had defeated Kṛṣṇa, the wildest storm, by *prāṇāyāma*. *‘Prāṇāyāma,’* although conventionally referring to the yogic practice of controlling breath, could literally mean “expansion (*āyāma*) of strength (*prāṇa*).” With this, Lalitā indirectly states that Śrīdāmā, by his greater physical strength, had in the past defeated Kṛṣṇa, which brought pleasure to the gopīs.

Śrīdāmā is the chief of *priya-sakhās*. *Priya-sakhās* are Kṛṣṇa’s friends of the same age as Him. Their friendship is unmixed with any other mood, and they please Kṛṣṇa by wrestling, stick fighting, and other similar activities. Śrīdāmā’s appearance is described as follows:

*vāsaḥ piṅgaṁ bibhrataḥ śṛṅga-pāṇiṁ*  
*baddha-spardhaṁ sauhṛdān mādhavena*  
*tāmroṣṇīṣaṁ śyāma-dhāmābhirāmaṁ*  
*śrīdāmānaḥ dāma-bhājaṁ bhajāmi*

**“I worship Śrīdāma, who is dressed in yellow garments and holds a horn in his hand. His complexion is dark blue, and he wears a copper colored turban and a flower garland. Out of friendship, he competes with Mādhava.” (*****Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu*** **3.3.41)**

Therefore, if the gopīs at all want to see Kṛṣṇa defeated and His pride smashed, it would be appropriate, as Lalitā suggests, to pray to Śrīdāmā, who will make a more competent combatant than Subala.

Lalitā’s speech has yet another sense: “O Viśākhā, resort to the revered (*śrī*), best means (*yogi-indram*) of subduing the enemy(*dāma*). By its superior strength (*prānāyāmena*) it gladdened the assembly of the proud gopīs by subduing the strongest person (*baliṣṭham*), who was loudly crying (*rutam*) ‘Please don’t (*mā*) \[do it\]!’” In other words, Lalitā implies that the gopīs can subdue Kṛṣṇa’s bragging by punishing Him, as they did in the past.

Although, being a *priya-sakhā*, Śrīdāmā does not directly participate in Kṛṣṇa’s conjugal pastimes with the gopīs, he nevertheless assists them indirectly, as a *pīṭha-marda*. *Pīṭha-marda* is defined as an assistant in Kṛṣṇa’s conjugal pastimes who is equal to Kṛṣṇa in virtues and, out of fraternal love, is always on Kṛṣṇa’s side.

## Verse 346(a-b)

***viśākhā:***  *(sa-smitam) lalide, sāhu kimpi sumāridaṁ tue—*  
*(iti saṁskṛtena)*  
*śrīdāma-candrāṅkita-pūrva-kāyaṁ*  
*bhāṇḍīra-pūrvācalam āśrayantam*  
*kṛṣṇāmbudaṁ tatra vilokya vṛndā*  
*smereyam āsīc capalā sakhī naḥ*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-smitam—*with a smile; *lalide—*O Lalitā; *sāhu—*aptly; *kimpi—*something; *sumāridaṁ—*is reminded; *tue—*by you; *iti—*having said so; *saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *śrīdāma—*Śrīdāmā; *candra—*by the moon; *aṅkita—*marked; *pūrva—*the upper part; *kāyam—*of His body; *bhāṇḍīra—*of the Bhāṇḍīra tree; *pūrva-acalam—*of the Himālaya Mountains; *āśrayantam—*taking shelter; *kṛṣṇa—*Kṛṣṇa; *ambudam—*at the cloud; *tatra—*there; *vilokya—*looking; *vṛndā—*Vṛndā; *smera—*smiling; *iyam—*this; *āsīt—*became; *capalā—*unsteady; *sakhī—*friend; *naḥ—*our.

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā (smiling): O Lalitā, you have aptly reminded me of something:**

**(says in Sanskrit): Our unsteady friend Vṛndā started smiling, as she stared at the cloud Kṛṣṇa that took shelter of the Himālaya-like Bhāṇḍīra tree, and whose upper part was marked with the Moon of Śrīdāmā.**

**Комментарий:**

The following passage from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (10.18.19-24) describes the cowherd boys’s playing at the Bhāṇḍīra tree, when Śrīdāmā had defeated Kṛṣṇa.

*tatropāhūya gopālān*  
*kṛṣṇaḥ prāha vihāra-vit*  
*he gopā vihariṣyāmo*  
*dvandvī-bhūya yathā-yatham*  
*tatra cakruḥ parivṛḍhau*  
*gopā rāma-janārdanau*  
*kṛṣṇa-saṅghaṭṭinaḥ kecid*  
*āsan rāmasya cāpare*  
*ācerur vividhāḥ krīḍā*  
*vāhya-vāhaka-lakṣaṇāḥ*  
*yatrārohanti jetāro*  
*vahanti ca parājitāḥ*  
*vahanto vāhyamānāś ca*  
*cārayantaś ca go-dhanam*  
*bhāṇḍīrakaṁ nāma vaṭaṁ*  
*jagmuḥ kṛṣṇa-purogamāḥ*  
*rāma-saṅghaṭṭino yarhi*  
*śrīdāma-vṛṣabhādayaḥ*  
*krīḍāyāṁ jayinas tāṁs tān*  
*ūhuḥ kṛṣṇādayo nṛpa*  
*uvāha kṛṣṇo bhagavān*  
*śrīdāmānaṁ parājitaḥ*  
*vṛṣabhaṁ bhadrasenas tu*  
*pralambo rohiṇī-sutam*

**“Kṛṣṇa, who knows all sports and games, then called together the cowherd boys and spoke as follows: “Hey cowherd boys! Let's play now! We'll divide ourselves into two even teams.”**  
**The cowherd boys chose Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma as the leaders of the two parties. Some of the boys were on Kṛṣṇa's side, and others joined Balarāma.**  
**The boys played various games involving carriers and passengers. In these games the winners would climb up on the backs of the losers, who would have to carry them.**  
**Thus carrying and being carried by one another, and at the same time tending the cows, the boys followed Kṛṣṇa to a banyan tree known as Bhāṇḍīraka.**  
**My dear King Parkṣit, when Śrīdāmā, Vṛṣabha and the other members of Lord Balarāma’s party were victorious in these games, Kṛṣṇa and His followers had to carry them.**  
**Defeated, the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa carried Śrīdāmā. Bhadrasena carried Vṛṣabha, and Pralamba carried Balarāma, the son of Rohiṇī.”**

With her present statement, Viśākhā describes this very scene—defeated Kṛṣṇa carrying on His shoulders the winner Śrīdāmā, at the Bhāṇḍīra tree. Viśākhā’s actual point, however, is different. *Mahaṭī-ṭīkā* reveals that Viśākhā is sharing her thoughts with Lalitā about Vṛndā’s behavior. Viśākhā’s smile suggests the following: “O Lalitā, the point you made is excellent, but you haven’t described the best part of that incident.” She then goes on to elaborate by speaking the Sanskrit verse, which carries the following import: “Vṛndā is always convinced that it is only Kṛṣṇa who embodies the cream of everything. But during that incident, she saw that it was more Śrīdāmā than Kṛṣṇa, to embody the best of everything. So, in the same way we see that when our unsteady sakhī deems Kṛṣṇa the best she sides with Him, and when she deems us the best she sides with us.” According to Viśākhā, this is the reason why Vṛndā changes her affiliation time to time—it all depends on whom she is more impressed with at a particular moment. This is the import of the verse.

Bhāṇḍīra is a huge banyan tree in Vraja, which is still standing there. The forest in Vraja where it stands is named after it—Bhāṇḍīravana. Here, the Bhāṇḍīra tree is compared to the Himālaya Mountains. *Śrī Harivaṁśa* (*Viṣṇu-parva* 11.18-22) describes that Bhāṇḍīra tree:

*dadarśa vipulodagra-*  
*śākhinaṁ śākhināṁ varam*  
*sthitaṁ dharaṇyāṁ meghābhaṁ*  
*nibiḍaṁ dala-sañcayaiḥ*  
*gaganārdhocchritākāraṁ*  
*parvatābhoga-dhāriṇam*  
*nīla-citrāṅga-varṇaiś ca*  
*sevitaṁ bahubhiḥ khagaiḥ*  
*phalaiḥ pravālaiś ca ghanaiḥ*  
*sendracāpa-ghanopamam*  
*bhavanākāra-viṭapaṁ*  
*latā-puṣpa-sumaṇḍitam*  
*viśāla-mūlāvanataṁ*  
*pāvanāmbhoda-dhāriṇam*  
*ādhipatyam ivānyeṣāṁ*  
*tasya deśasya śākhinām*  
*kurvāṇaṁ śubha-karmāṇaṁ*  
*nirāvarṣam anātapam*  
*nyagrodhaṁ parvatāgrābhaṁ*  
*bhāṇḍīraṁ nāma nāmataḥ*

**“They \[the cowherd boys\] saw that best of all trees, which had many long branches. With its dense covering of leaves, it resembled a cloud sitting on the earth. Indeed, its form was so large that it appeared like a mountain covering half the sky. Many birds with charming blue wings frequented that great tree, whose dense fruits and leaves made it seem like a cloud accompanied by a rainbow or like a house decorated with creepers and flowers. It spread its broad roots downward and carried upon itself the sanctified clouds. That banyan tree was like the lordly master of all other trees in that vicinity, as it performed the all-auspicious functions of warding off the rain and the heat of the sun. Such was the appearance of that** ***nyagrodha*** **tree known as Bhāṇḍīra, which seemed just like the peak of a great mountain.”**

### Verse 347

***arjunaḥ:*** *(saṁskṛtena)*  
*atra naḥ savayasāṁ gaṇāntare*  
*jīyate jayati vā na kaḥ sakhā*  
*tatra goṣṭha-sudṛśāṁ kim āgataṁ*  
*kāraṇaṁ vadata vo madāplutāḥ*

**Пословный перевод:**

*saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *atra—*in these instances; *naḥ—*our; *savayasām—*of friends; *gaṇa-antare—*amidst the group; *jīyate—*is conquered; *jayati—*conquers; *vā—*or; *na—*not; *kaḥ—*which; *sakhā—*friend; *tatra—*in that matter; *goṣṭha—*of the cowherd village; *su-dṛśām—*for the beautiful eyed girls; *kim—*which; *āgatam—*is understood; *kāraṇam—*cause; *vadata—*tell; *vaḥ—*your; *mada—*with pride; *plutāḥ—*filled.

**Перевод:**

**Arjuna (in Sanskrit): Who amidst us friends does not lose or win in on such occasions? But please, o arrogant girls, tell us the reason that the beautiful-eyed gopīs see as to why one of us sometimes wins and sometimes loses.**

**Комментарий:**

In the opinon of Viśvanātha Cakravartī, Arjuna implies: “The actual reason for someone of us to win or lose is friendship of other boys among ourselves, and not the connection with you as a brother. So your pride has no grounds whatsoever!”

Arjuna’s statement should be understood in the context of the present conversation. In response to Kṛṣṇa’s ridicule, Subala seriously questioned Kṛṣṇa’s bravery by reminding Him of how once He was scared of the gopīs after an unfair gambling match (text 343). Kṛṣṇa boasts by calling Himself a hurricane (text 344), but Lalitā says that Śrīdāmā is still stronger, having been able to defeat Kṛṣṇa (text 345). Arjuna picks up on Lalitā’s words “the brother of our dear sakhī,” feeling that Lalitā spoke in that way to proudly establish the gopīs as the source of Śrīdāmā’s strength, for Śrīdāmā is a brother to Rādhā. Arjuna doesn’t accept Lalitā’s conclusion and offers another reason—it is friendly support of other cowherd boys that determines a particular boy’s success or failure. That is why Kṛṣṇa lost the gambling match and Śrīdāmā was able to defeat Him.

### Verse 348

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(rādhām avalokya)*  
*dātum icchasi na kāñcanāni cec*  
*cāturīṁ manasi kāñcana śritā*  
*gauri gairika-vicitritodarīṁ*  
*tvaṁ tataḥ praviśa bhū-bhṛto darīm*  
*(iti rādhām āvṛṇoti)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*rādhām—*at Rādhā; *avalokya—*glancing; *dātum—*to give; *icchasi na—*do not wish; *kāñcanāni—*gold; *cet—*if; *cāturīm—*wits; *manasi—*in Her mind; *kāñcana—*of some; *śritā—*took shelter; *gauri—*O golden complexioned One; *gairika—*with red pigment; *vicitrita—*beautified; *udarīm—*whose interior; *tvam—*You; *tataḥ—*then; *praviśa—*enter; *bhū-bhṛtaḥ—*of the mountain; *darīm—*the cavity; *iti—*thus; *rādhām—*Rādhā; *āvṛṇoti—*embraces.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (looks at Rādhā): O girl with a golden complexion, if You don’t wish to give us gold and are instead resorting to Your wits, then enter the mountain cave, whose interior is beautiful with red clay.**

**(about to embrace Rādhā)**

**Комментарий:**

As the alternative to not paying the tax, Kṛṣṇa invites Rādhā to enter a cave on Govardhana hill, so that He could enjoy Her there.

### Verse 349-350

***lalitā:***  *(madhyam āsādayantī) raa-ṇārīa, suṇāhi māhurī-māhappehiṁ pāa-pauma-pphaṁsa-sohagga-bhāattaṇaṁ appaṇo amaṇṇia saṅkāuleṇa ṇia-ghara-āvaṭṭhidi-mettaa-kidattha-maṇṇeṇa bhattuṇā bi durādo sādaraṁ vandijjantīe…*  
*(ity ardhokte rādhikā sābhyasūyaṁ lalitāṁ paśyati)*  
*(smitvā) akomāraṁ pūrisa-jaṇa-parimala-kāṇiāṇahiṇṇa-savvaṅgāe jāe amha-pia-sahīe aṅgāiṁ sevā-pajjussueṇābi mañjuṇā ṇaa-jovvaṇeṇa sajjhasādo via mantharī-huvia ajja bi ṇibbharaṁ ṇa parisīlidāiṁ tāe mahā-sadī-ula-cakkavaṭṭiṇīe imāe aṅgassa pekkhaṇe bi kida-jjhavasāo jaṇo mahā-sāhasiāṇaṁ dhurandharo bhaṇīadi. kiṁ uṇa pphaṁsaṇe? tā avehi!*  
*(sarvāḥ smitaṁ kurvanti)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*madhyam—*between; *āsādayantī—*reaching; *raa-ṇārīa—*O woman-hunter; *suṇāhi—*listen; *māhurī—*of Her sweetness; *māhappehim—*with glorification; *pāa-pauma—*Her lotus feet; *pphaṁsa—*of touching; *sohagga—*the fortune; *bhāattaṇaṁ—*the state of having received; *appaṇo—*his; *amaṇṇia—*not considering; *saṅkā—*with fear; *āuleṇa—*overwhelmed; *ṇia—*in his own; *ghara—*house; *āvaṭṭhidi-mettaa—*just by the residence; *kidattha—*successful; *maṇṇeṇa—*who thinks himself; *bhattuṇā—*by Her husband; *bi—*even; *durādo—*from a distance; *sa-ādaraṁ—*with reverence; *vandijjantīe—*of One who is praised; *iti—*thus; *ardha-ukte—*when a half of the statement was said; *rādhikā—*Rādhikā; *sa-abhyasūyam—*with jealousy; *lalitām—*at Lalitā; *paśyati—*looks; *smitvā—*smiling; *ākomāraṁ—*from Her childhood; *pūrisa-jaṇa—*of a male; *parimala—*of the fragrance; *kāṇia—*a minute particle; *aṇahiṇṇa—*not knowing; *savva-aṅgāe—*whose all limbs; *jāe—*whose; *amha—*our; *pia-sahīe—*dear sakhī; *aṅgāim—*the limbs; *sevā—*for service; *pajjussueṇa—*eager; *abi—*even; *mañjuṇā—*lovely; *ṇaa-jovvaṇeṇa—*fresh youth; *sajjhasādo—*out of fear; *via—*as if; *mantharī-huvia—*becoming slow; *ajja—*today; *bi—*even; *ṇibbharaṁ—*completely; *ṇa parisīlidāiṁ—*not contacted; *tāe imāe—*of that very; *mahā-sadī—*of great chaste ladies; *ula—*of the clan; *cakkavaṭṭiṇīe—*queen; *aṅgassa—*of the body; *pekkhaṇe—*to behold; *bi—*even; *kida-jjhavasāo—*attempting; *jaṇo—*a person; *mahā-sāhasiāṇaṁ—*of the very daring; *dhurandharo—*the leader; *bhaṇīadi—*is said; *kiṁ uṇa—*what to speak of; *pphaṁsaṇe—*touching; *tā—*therefore; *avehi—*go away*; sarvāḥ—*all; *smitam kurvanti—*smile.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (coming in between): You rake, listen to this: Even Her intimidated husband reverently prays to Her from a distance with odes to Her sweetness. He thinks himself lucky to simply have Her live in his house, and considers himself bereft of the good fortune to be touched by Her lotus feet…**

**(Rādhikā looks at Lalitā with resentment, as Lalitā is half way through her statement. Lalitā continues with a smile.)**

**…Her limbs haven’t yet smelt even a whiff of any male. Fresh and lovely youthful age, though eager to serve Her, is stunned, as if struck with fear; It hasn’t fully touched Her limbs even to this day. Our dear sakhī is the empress of all chaste ladies. One who attempts to even look at Her form must be the most daring person. What then to speak of someone trying to touch Her? So, You better get away!**

**(All the gopīs smile.)**

**Комментарий:**

Despite the fact that Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s pleasure potency Rādhārāṇī is officially the wife of a cowherd Abhimanyu, he is always kept physically separated from Her by *yogamāyā. Bhakti-ratnākara* 5.1092-1093 explains: “By the influence of *yogamāyā* there is no question of Abhimanyu’s touching even the shadow of Rādhārāṇī. He always remains with his cows and cowherd men…”

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.33.37 gives further insight into this subject:

*nāsūyan khalu kṛṣṇāya*  
*mohitās tasya māyayā*  
*manyamānāḥ sva-pārśva-sthān*  
*svān svān dārān vrajaukasaḥ*

**“The cowherd men, bewildered by Kṛṣṇa’s illusory potency, thought their wives had remained home at their sides. Thus they did not harbor any jealous feelings against Him.”**

Since in the pastimes of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa Abhimanyu is often jealous of Kṛṣṇa, this statement from the *Bhāgavatam* gives a hint that Abhimanyu is not just another cowherd of Vraja, but a special creation of the Lord’s *yogamāyā*, in the likeness of Rādhā’s husband. The same verse also suggests that whenever the gopīs had to meet Kṛṣṇa, they were replaced by *yogamāyā* with their illusory models. Rūpa Gosvāmī adds that it is these replicas of the gopīs, and not the actual gopīs, that were perceived by their husbands at home. ElsewhereViśvanātha Cakravartī states that those husbands didn’t develop any lust toward their wives, what to speak of enjoying them physically. This is because those illusory replicas of the gopīs exactly resembled their originals, and it would be inappropriate for anybody to have intimate physical contact even with them. Also, the husbands of the gopīs never really manifested possessiveness toward their wives, as most of the time those husbands were sent at night by their mothers to sleep in the fields, and not in their own homes.

With Her stern glance, Rādhā reproaches Lalitā as follows: “Glorifying Me and belittling My husband, in My very presence, evinces My shyness and Your aggressiveness. But both of these shouldn’t be here!” Lalitā responds to Rādhā with a smile: “Only You see it like this. But I am Your friend, after all, and so, must speak the truth: If there is something that is being evinced at all, it is the fulfillment of Your actual desire. When His Majesty Eagerness is in full power, could robbers like Shyness do any harm?”The meaning of this is that Rādhā feels that Her shyness and Lalitā’s bold attack create an impediment for Her to fully give Herself to Kṛṣṇa. At this point, Lalitā’s meaningful smile assures Rādhā that Her desire to be with Kṛṣṇa will surely bear fruit. Rādhā’s strong eagerness for Kṛṣṇa will surely remove any remaining impediments like Her shyness and Lalitā’s angry temper.

Surendranath Shastri points out how, with her description of Rādhā, Lalitā subtly suggests to Kṛṣṇa that Rādhā is meant for Him only. The words “Her own intimidated husband” describe Rādhā’s exalted position, and the phrase “reverently prays to Her from a distance” suggests that even though She is married, Her husband does not enjoy Her body. “Odes to Her sweetness” suggest Her unparalleled beauty, and “Her limbs haven’t yet smelt even a whiff of any male” imply that there is no any other male apart from Kṛṣṇa, who desires to obtain Rādhā. Finally, “it \[youthful age\] hasn’t fully touched Her limbs even to this day” describes the most attractive to Kṛṣṇa condition of Rādhā’s transcendental body—Her budding youth.

At the end all the gopīs smile. Their intention is to increase Kṛṣṇa’s eagerness and at the same time restrain Him.

### Verse 351

***madhumaṅgalaḥ:*** *(vihasya) lalide, alaṁ imiṇā gavva-bhareṇa. pucchijja{u} appaṇo pia-sahī gandhavviā jāe duvvāsassa muṇiṇo muhādo amha-pia-vaassassa ākomāraṁ accaria-bamhacaria-māhurī saaṁ ccea āaṇṇidā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vihasya—*laughing; *lalide—*O Lalitā; *alaṁ—*enough; *imiṇā—*of this; *gavva—*of pride; *bhareṇa—*abundance; *pucchijja{u}—*should be asked; *appaṇo—*Your; *pia-sahī—*dear sakhī; *gandhavviā—*Gāndharvikā (a name of Rādhā); *jāe—*by who; *duvvāsassa muṇiṇo—*of Durvāsa Muni; *muhādo—*from the mounth; *amha—*of our; *pia-vaassassa—*dear friend; *ākomāraṁ—*since His childhood; *accaria—*amazing; *bamhacaria-māhurī—*sweetness of celibacy; *saaṁ—*personally; *ccea—*only; *āaṇṇidā—*was heard.

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala (laughing): Stop being so much proud, Lalitā. Ask your dear sakhī Gāndharvikā, who heard directly from the mouth of Durvāsa Muni about our dear friend’s astonishing life of brahmacarya since His childhood.**

**Комментарий:**

Madhumaṅgala vindicates Kṛṣṇa, who just now was “unfairly” blamed for trying to touch Rādhā.

### Verse 352-353

***subalaḥ:***  *lalide, saccāvedi eso muṇi-puttao. tā pattiāehi jaṁ mahā-vīra-vvado bi pia-vaasso ṇārīṇaṁ gaṇādo bhāedi.*  
***arjunaḥ:*** *āṁ viṇṇādaṁ. ado ccea imāṇaṁ kaḍaa-sadda-metteṇa sambhamāulo bhavia kampanto pulaidaṅgo mae vāraṁ vāraṁ diṭṭho thi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*lalide—*O Lalitā; *saccāvedi—*speaks the truth; *eso—*this; *muṇi-puttao—*son of the muni; *tā—*therefore; *pattiāehi—*You should know; *jaṁ—*that; *mahā-vīra—*greatly heroic; *vvado—*observing the vow; *bi—*even; *pia-vaasso—*our dear friend; *ṇārīṇaṁ—*of women; *gaṇādo—*of the class; *bhāedi—*afraid; *ām—*yes; *viṇṇādaṁ—*understood; *ado—*therefore; *ccea—*only; *imāṇaṁ—*of their; *kaḍaa—*bracelets; *sadda-metteṇa—*just from the sound; *sambhama-āulo—*bewildered; *bhavia—*becoming; *kampanto—*shivering; *pulaida-aṅgo—*His hairs stand on end; *mae—*by me; *vāraṁ vāraṁ—*again and again; *diṭṭho—*seen; *thi—*is.

**Перевод:**

**Subala: Lalitā, the son of the sage speaks the truth. Just see, even though our dear friend is fixed in this heroic vow, He is still afraid of women.**

**Arjuna: Oh yes, I understand. That’s exactly why He becomes confused, His hairs stand on end and He trembles at hearing only the tinkling of their bracelets.**

### Verse 354

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(smitaṁ kṛtvā) lalite, nātra vipratipattis te śreyasī yataḥ samāna-dharmaṇoḥ sādhakayoḥ sahavāsa-sauhārda-mādhurī jhaṭiti mahā-vrata-siddhaye sādhu kalpate.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smitam kṛtvā—*smiling; *lalite—*O Lalitā; *na—*not; *atra—*in this matter; *vipratipattiḥ—*doubt; *te—*your; *śreyasī—*the best thing; *yataḥ—*because; *samāna-dharmaṇoḥ—*having the same nature; *sādhakayoḥ—*of the practitioners; *sahavāsa—*of staying together; *sauhārda—*and friendship; *mādhurī—*the sweetness; *jhaṭiti—*quickly; *mahā-vrata—*of the great vow; *siddhaye—*to the perfection; *sādhu—*well; *kalpate—*serves*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (smiling): O Lalitā, it would not be the best for you to doubt this, but when two practitioners of the same nature live together in friendship, the sweetness of their relationship brings their severe vows to perfection quickly and effectively.**

**Комментарий:**

The “two practitioners” are Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

### Verse 355

***rādhā:*** *(dṛg-añcalaṁ sāci vikṣipantī sāvahelaṁ kiñcit parāvṛtya) ṇāara, sāra-sesā ṇa kkhu tumha-cabala-cādurī. tā alaṁ piṭṭha-peseṇa.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dṛg-añcalam—*Her sidelong glance; *sāci—*to the left; *vikṣipantī—*while casting; *sa-avahelam—*with disregard; *kiñcit—*slightly; *parāvṛtya—*turning; *ṇāara—*O suave boy; *sāra—*in actual meaning; *sesā—*that end; *ṇa—*not; *kkhu—*indeed; *tumha—*Your; *cabala—*fickle; *cādurī—*wits; *tā—*therefore; *alaṁ—*enough; *piṭṭha—*the husk; *peseṇa—*of grinding*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (slightly turning away in disregard, while casting Her sidelong glance to the left): O suave boy, Your fickle wits never get the main point. Enough of grinding the husk.**

**Комментарий:**

“Grinding the husk” (*piṣṭa-peṣaṇa*) is an often-quoted example that illustrates useless endeavor. By saying this, Rādhā means that Kṛṣṇa is simply waisting His time trying to fabricate some reason for Their union.

## Verse 356(a-b)

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(apavārya) vṛnde, paśya paśya—*  
*narmoktau mama nirmitoru-paramānandotsavāyām api*  
*śrotrasyānta-taṭīm api sphuṭam anādhāya sthitodyan-mukhī*  
*rādhā lāghavam apy asādara-girāṁ bhaṅgībhir ātanvatī*  
*maitrī-gauravato ’py asau śata-guṇaṁ mat-prītim evādadhe*  
*(rādhā kiñcid vihasya lalitāyāḥ karṇa-mūle lapati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*apavārya—*aside; *vṛnde—*O Vṛndā; *paśya paśya—*look, look; *narma—*joking; *uktau—*to the statement; *mama—*My; *nirmita—*which creates; *uru—*great; *parama—*and supreme; *ānanda—*of bliss; *utsavāyām—*the festival; *api—*even; *śrotrasya—*of Her ear; *anta-taṭīm—*the tip; *api—*even; *sphuṭam—*clearly; *anādhāya—*not focusing; *sthitā—*is standing; *udyat—*rising; *mukhī—*with Her face; *rādhā—*Rādhā; *lāghavam—*dishonor; *api—*even; *a-sa-ādara—*without respect; *girām—*words; *bhaṅgībhiḥ—*by indirect statements; *ātanvatī—*creating; *maitrī—*friendly; *gauravataḥ—*more than reverence; *api—*even; *asau—*She; *śata-guṇam—*hundred times; *mat-prītim—*My love; *eva—*only; *ādadhe—*received; *rādhā—*Rādhā; *kiñcit—*slightly; *vihasya—*laughing; *lalitāyāḥ—*of Lalitā; *karṇa-mūle—*in the earhole; *lapati—*speaks.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (aside): Vṛndā, look, look: Rādhā’s face is blooming, even though She obviously does not heed even slightly My joking words, which produce supreme bliss, and slanders Me by speaking rashly in a roundabout way. Nevertheless, by behaving so Rādhā has attracted My love hundred times more than by respecting Me in a favorable attitude.**

**(Mildly laughing, Rādhā whispers something in Lalitā’s ear.)**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā’s face blossoms—it beams with a concealed smile, which betrays Her inner joy, even though She wants to conceal it. This emotion is called *vivvoka*. It is one of the twenty *alaṅkāras*, or feminine moods that “decorate” the young gopīs of Vraja by unveiling their inner feelings. *Vivvoka* is defined as follows: *‘vivvoko māna-garvābhyāṁ syād abhīṣṭe ’py anādaraḥ’*—“*Vivvoka* is disregard for the beloved out of anger or pride.”

There are gopīs headed by Candrāvalī, whose love for Kṛṣṇa is pervaded with a sense of belonging to Him—“I am Kṛṣṇa’s.” This naturally makes them submissive and agreeable to Kṛṣṇa. Such love is called *ghṛta-sneha*, or “ghee-like affection.”

*ātyantikādaramayaḥ*  
*sneho ghṛtam itīryate*  
*bhāvāntarānvito gacchan*  
*svādodrekaṁ na tu svayam*  
*ghanī-bhaven nisargāti-*  
*śītalān mitha ādarāt*  
*gāḍhādaramayas tena*  
*snehaḥ syād ghṛtavad ghṛtam*

**“Love permeated with extreme adoration is called ‘ghee.’ Just like ghee, such love is not very delectable by itself, but becomes so when endowed with other ingredients. It thickens due to naturally very cool sense of respect between two lovers. For this reason, love that is saturated with a sense of deep respect is the ‘ghee-love.’”**

The love of Rādhārāṇī and the gopīs in Her group is different. It is called *madhu-sneha*, or “honey-like affection,” and it is infused with a strong feeling of possessiveness toward the lover—“Kṛṣṇa is mine.” This naturally gives rise to more passionate and unpredictable exchanges.

*madīyatātiśaya-bhāk*  
*priye sneho bhaven madhu*  
*svayaṁ prakaṭa-mādhuryo*  
*nānā-rasa-samāhṛtiḥ*  
*mattatoṣma-dharaḥ sneho*  
*madhu-sāmyān madhūcyate*

**“Love endowed with extreme sense of possessiveness toward the lover is called ‘honey,’ for it shares with honey the following characteristics: It is an assortment of various** ***rasas*****, it manifests sweetness by itself, and it contains heating and inebriating properties.”**

In the present verse Kṛṣṇa admits Himself that Rādhā with Her contrary attitude to Him is the recepient of His love in much greater degree than any sweetly behaving gopī.

## Rādhā The Queen

### Verse 357-359

***lalitā:***  *kaṇha, ‘goule vikkhāda-guṇo tumaṁ juarāo si’ tti amhe tuṇṇiṁ vaṭṭamha. ja{i} sāmpadaṁ sīmullaṅghaṇe pa{u}tto si tado amhe kīsa appaṇa-kajjaṁ ubekkhissamha?*  
***arjunaḥ:***  *kiṁ taṁ kajjaṁ tumhāṇaṁ jaṁ ubekkhadha?*  
***lalitā:*** *gobehinto ṇia-vundāvaṇassa saṁrakkhaṇaṁ jebba. ido abaraṁ kiṁ kajjaṁ?*  
*(arjunaḥ sāvahelaṁ vihasya huṅkṛtiṁ kurvan mūrdhānam ādhunoti)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kaṇha—*O Kṛṣṇa; *goule—*in Gokula; *vikkhāda—*are well known; *guṇo—*Your good qualities; *tumaṁ—*You; *juarāo—*the prince; *si—*are; *tti—*thus; *amhe—*we; *tuṇṇiṁ—*silent; *baṭṭamha—*are; *ja{i}—*if; *sāmpadaṁ—*at present; *sīma—*the limit; *ullaṅghaṇe—*in transgressing; *pa{u}tto—*engaged; *si—*are; *tado—*then; *amhe—*we; *kīsa—*why; *appaṇa—*our own; *kajjaṁ—*duty; *ubekkhissamha—*will neglect; *kiṁ—*what; *taṁ—*that; *kajjaṁ—*duty; *tumhāṇaṁ—*your; *jaṁ—*which; *ubekkhadha—*You are neglecting; *gobehinto—*from cowherds; *ṇia—*of our own; *vundāvaṇassa—*Vṛndāvana; *saṁrakkhaṇaṁ—*the protection; *jebba—*only; *ido abaraṁ—*than this; *kiṁ—*what; *kajjaṁ—*duty; *arjunaḥ—*Arjuna; *sa-avahelam—*with disregard; *vihasya—*smiling; *huṅkṛtim—*the roaring sound; *kurvan—*making; *mūrdhānam—*his head; *ādhunoti—*shakes.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: Kaṇha, we have no objection against the fact that You, with Your renowned virtues, are the prince of Gokula. But if now You are about to cross the limit, why shall we neglect our duty?**

**Arjuna: What is that duty of yours which You could neglect?**

**Lalitā: To protect our Vṛndāvana from cowherds. That’s all. What else could be our duty?**

**(Arjuna shakes his head as he laughs with disdain and roars)**

### Verse 360-361

***viśākhā:***  *(sa-smitam upasṛtya) lalide, goula-juadī-ula-cakkavaṭṭiṇī amha-pia-sahī āṇabedi.*  
***lalitā:*** *kiṁ taṁ?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-smitam—*with a smile; *upasṛtya—*approaching; *lalide—*O Lalitā; *goula-juadī—*of young girls of Gokula; *ula—*of the clan; *cakkavaṭṭiṇī—*the queen; *amha—*our; *pia-sahī—*dear sakhī; *āṇabedi—*is ordering; *kim—*what; *taṁ—*that.

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā (approaching with a smile): Lalitā, our dear sakhī, the queen of young girls of Gokula, is ordering us.**

**Lalitā: What’s that?**

**Комментарий:**

That “dear sakhī” is Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī.

### Verse 362

***viśākhā:*** *savve gavvidā gobā ladaṅkura-puñja-bhañjaṇa-dakkhāṇaṁ gāiṇaṁ lakkha-koḍī rakkhanto phalehiṁ kukkhim-bhariṇo puppha-pallavehiṁ midho ṇevaccha-āriṇo suiraṁ amha-vundāvaṇassa viddhaṁsaṇaṁ kuṇanti. tā ṇiṭṭaṅkidaṁ kadhehi ede ido vā viramentu kiṁvā karaṁ dentu.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*savve—*all; *gavvidā—*proud; *gobā—*cowherds; *lada—*of plants; *aṅkura—*and creepers; *puñja—*lots; *bhañjaṇa—*in eating; *dakkhāṇaṁ—*experts; *gāiṇaṁ—*of cows; *lakkha-koḍī—*many billions; *rakkhanto—*protecting; *phalehiṁ—*with fruits; *kukkhim-bhariṇo—*filling their stomachs; *puppha-pallavehiṁ—*with flower buds; *midho—*mutually; *ṇevaccha-āriṇo—*decorating; *suiraṁ—*for a very long time; *amha-vundāvaṇassa—*of our Vṛndāvana; *viddhaṁsaṇaṁ—*the destruction; *kuṇanti—*they perform; *tā—*therefore; *ṇiṭṭaṅkidaṁ—*conclusively; *kadhehi—*tell; *ede—*they; *ido—*from this; *vā—*or; *viramentu—*should desist; *kiṁvā—*or else; *karaṁ—*the tax; *dentu—*should pay*.*

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā: Already for a long time all the impudent cowherds, filling their bellies with fruits, have been destroying our Vṛndāvana. They are doing that by tending their millions of cows, who know very well how to devour heaps of plants and creepers, and by adorning each other with flower buds. Tell them once and forever that they must stop it, or else, they have to pay a tax.**

### Verse 363

***madhumaṅgalaḥ:*** *(sāmarṣam) vibarīda-vādiṇi, muṇi-vvadiṇī hohi! adha vā thoaṁ pi tumhāṇaṁ dūsaṇaṁ ṇatthi. pia-vaassassa kāruṇiadā ccea ettha aṇattha-āriṇī saṁvuttā jāe ḍāhiṇāe aḍāhiṇāṇaṁ tuārisīṇaṁ tassa vundāvaṇe paveso pasādī-kido. tā ṇa kkhu ajutto-palābo.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-amarṣam—*with anger; *vibarīda—*in contradiction; *bādiṇi—*O one who talks; *muṇi-vvadiṇī—*one who observes the vow of silence; *hohi—*be; *adha vā—*otherwise; *thoaṁ—*a little; *pi—*even; *tumhāṇaṁ—*your; *dūsaṇaṁ—*fault; *ṇatthi—*there is no; *pia-vaassassa—*of our dear friend; *kāruṇiadā—*merciful nature; *ccea—*only; *ettha—*in this regard; *aṇattha-āriṇī—*useless; *saṁvuttā—*has become; *jāe—*due to which; *ḍāhiṇāe—*kindness; *aḍāhiṇāṇaṁ—*who do not offer presents; *tuārisīṇaṁ—*for people like you; *tassa—*into His; *vundāvaṇe—*Vṛndāvana; *paveso—*entry; *pasādī-kido—*is granted; *tā—*that (kindness); *ṇa—*not; *kkhu—*indeed; *ajutto—*improper; *palābo—*talk.

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala (angrily): You twist the real fact! So take up the vow of silence! On the other hand, you are not offensive at all. It is the soft heart of our dear friend that is the cause of the problem. Out of His kindness, He has granted entrance into His Vṛndāvana to people like You, who never give anything in return. This is not an empty talk.**

**Комментарий:**

According to Madhumaṅgala, Lalitā speaks contrary to the fact that it is Kṛṣṇa, not Rādhā and Her gopīs, who owns the Vṛndāvana forest. By saying “Take up the vow of silence,” Madhumaṅgala asks Viśakhā to stop talking nonsence.

### Verse 364-365

***campakalatā:*** *ajja, aṇajjo si tumaṁ. apajjāloia mohaṁ jappasi.*  
***lalitā:*** *(saṁskṛtena)*  
*śṛṇoti nāyaṁ śataśo ’pi ghuṣṭaṁ*  
*na ca smaraty ātma-dṛśāpi dṛṣṭam*  
*śruti-smṛtibhyāṁ nija-locanābhyāṁ*  
*hīnaṁ tatas tvaṁ sakhi nākṣipāmum*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ajja—*O respected sir; *aṇajjo—*not an ārya; *si—*are; *tumaṁ—*you; *apajjāloia—*not considering; *mohaṁ—*uselessly; *jappasi—*you are talking; *saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *śṛṇoti na—*does not hear; *ayam—*this; *śataśaḥ—*hundred times; *api—*even; *ghuṣṭam—*the said; *ca—*and; *na smarati—*doe not remember; *ātma-dṛśā—*with Her own eyes; *api—*even; *dṛṣṭam—*the seen; *śruti-smṛtibhyām—*of śruti and smṛti; *nija—*his own; *locanābhyām—*eyes; *hīnam—*bereft; *tataḥ—*therefore; *tvam—*you; *sakhi—*O friend; *na ākṣipa—*do not scold; *amum—*him.

**Перевод:**

**Campakalatā: O respected sir, You are not a civilized person, because you are talking nonsence without thinking.**

**Lalitā (in Sanskrit): Don’t blame him, my friend. He is ignorant of śruti and smṛti śāstras, which are supposed to be his eyes. He doesn’t hear what had been said hundred times and doesn’t remember what he saw directly.**

**Комментарий:**

Lalitā means to say “Idiots are beyond reproach”.

*Śruti* and *smṛti śāstras* are said to be the eyes of a *brāhmaṇa*:

*śruti-smṛtī ubhe netre viprāṇāṁ parikīrtite*  
*ekena vikalaḥ kāṇo dvābhyām andhaḥ prakīrtitaḥ*

**“*****Śruti*** **and** ***smṛti*** **are said to be the eyes of a** ***brāhmaṇa*****. If a** ***brāhmaṇa*** **lacks one of them, he is blind on one eye, and if he lacks both—he is totally blind.”**

### Verse 366

***viśākhā:***  *sahi ṇandīmuhi, abi kiṁ so kira pia-sahīe mahāhisea-mahūsavo tue sumarīadi?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sahi ṇandīmuhi—*O Nāndīmukhī sakhī; *abi kiṁ—*whether; *so—*that; *kira—*indeed; *pia-sahīe—*of the dear sakhī; *mahā-ahisea—*of the great ceremonial bathing; *mahā-usavo—*the grand festival; *tue—*by you; *sumarīadi—*remembered.

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā: My friend Nāndīmukhī, do you remember that festival of our dear sakhī’s coronational bathing?**

**Комментарий:**

In Vedic culture, a person who accepts a throne is ceremonially bathed with various auspicious ingredients. Once, Kṛṣṇa also made Rādhā the queen of Vṛndāvana. The gopīs now bring out this fact, for it would reverse the present situation and force Kṛṣṇa and his friends to pay tax to Rādhā.

### Verse 367

***nāndīmukhī:*** *visāhe, ko kkhu so parāṇī bhuaṇe hodi jeṇa mahā-mahūsavo bi visumariduṁ pārīadi?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*visāhe—*O Viśākhā; *ko—*who; *kkhu—*indeed; *so—*that; *parāṇī—*living being; *bhuaṇe—*in the creation; *hodi—*is; *jeṇa—*by whom; *mahā-mahūsavo—*the great festival; *bi—*even; *visumariduṁ—*forgotten; *pārīadi—*may be.

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: O Viśākhā, is there somebody within this creation who can forget even that great festival?**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā’s sovereignty over Vṛndāvana is described in the Vedic literature:

*vṛndāvanādhipatyaṁ ca*  
*dattaṁ tasyāḥ pratuṣyatā*  
*kṛṣṇenānyatra devī tu*  
*rādhā vṛndāvane vane*

**"Being very pleased with Her, Lord Kṛṣṇa gave Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī sovereignty over the forest of Vṛndāvana. As Durgā Devī presides over other places, Rādhā presides over Vṛndāvana." (*****Kārttika-māhātmya*** **of** ***Padma Purāṇa*****, conversation between Śaunaka and Nārada)**

*varāṇāsyāṁ viśālākṣī*  
*vimalā puruṣottame*  
*rukmiṇī dvāravatyāṁ ca*  
*rādhā vṛndāvane vane*

**"Viśālākṣī \[Durgā\] rules over Vārāṇasī, Vimalā presides over Puruṣottama-kṣetra \[Jagannātha Purī\], Rukmiṇī is the queen of Dvārakā, and Rādhā is the queen of Vṛndāvana forest." (*****Skanda Purāṇa*****)**

The above-quoted puranic verses hint at the coronational *abhiṣeka* of Rādhārāṇī, which Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī has revealed in *Dāna-keli-kaumudī*. On his order, Śrī Jīva Gosvāmī wrote a book named *Mādhava-mahotsava*, in which he has elaborated in great length on the current portion of *Dāna-keli-kaumudī* that describes Rādhā’s *abhiṣeka*. The following is a brief summary of the first four chapters of *Mādhava-mahotsava* describing the events preceding the Rādhā’s *abhiṣeka*:

Afflicted by separation, Rādhā was deeply longing to meet Kṛṣṇa. Nandīmukhī delivered to Her Kṛṣṇa’s love message which revealed how eager He was too to meet Rādhā. In Rādhā’s assembly Lalitā joyfully shared her dream in which she saw Rādhā bathed by many goddesses. Then the sakhīs, anticipating some wonderful *abhiṣeka* of Rādhārāṇī in the future, sent Her to Her garden to meet Kṛṣṇa on the pretext of collecting flowers.

Arriving at the meeting place Rādhā found that all flowers were already picked by someone. She thought it was Kṛṣṇa, and started searching for Him in all directions along with Her friends. At that time, an associate of Vṛndā, Mālatī, came to the spot and delivered to Rādhā the news that it was the associates of Candrāvalī who had stolen all the flowers. Mālatī directly saw this, but couldn’t stop Candrāvalī’s friends, who were all the time speaking about the upcoming celebration of Candrāvalī’s *abhiṣeka.*

Having heard the news, Rādhā became hot with anger, for She had understood that Kṛṣṇa, on the way to meet Her, must have been allured by Candrāvalī and is now going to make the latter the queen of Vṛndāvana. In this way, Rādhā, depressed and frustrated, went home.

Kṛṣṇa, however, was innocent. He didn’t appear at the meeting place, for He had to satisfy Candrāvalī, whom he met on the way, with sweet words, although His mind was totally absorbed in Rādhā only.

Learning from Vṛndā about the condition of Rādhā, Kṛṣṇa tried to appease Her by sending His cloth and ornaments, but it failed. Viśākhā then went to meet Kṛṣṇa to chastise Him. Upon arrival, however, she saw Kṛṣṇa’s anguish and had melted. She indirectly suggested to Kṛṣṇa that He would be able to make Rādhā happy by arranging an *abhiṣeka* that would establish Her as the queen of Vṛndāvana. Paurṇamāsī then met Rādhā and gladdened Her by relating Kṛṣṇa’s long-standing intentions to make Her the queen.

Rūpa Gosvāmī starts his summarized description of the *abhiṣeka* from this point of the story.

### Verse 368

***citrā:***  *ṇandīmuhi, acchihiṁ paccakkhī-kido bi so mahūsavo louttaradāe kaṇṇaṁ me uttaraledi. tā suṇāvīadu.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ṇandīmuhi—*O Nāndīmukhī; *acchihiṁ—*to the eyes; *paccakkhī-kido—*made visible; *bi—*even; *so—*that; *mahūsavo—*great festival; *louttaradāe—*by being supernatural; *kaṇṇaṁ—*ears; *me—*my; *uttaraledi—*makes tremble; *tā—*therefore; *suṇāvīadu—*narrate.

**Перевод:**

**Citrā: Nāndīmukhī, even though I saw that great festival with my own eyes, my ears are still excited to hear of its heavenly grandeur. So please, describe it.**

### Verse 369-370

***nāndīmukhī:*** *sahi citte, suṇāhi: imāe vundāe gadua bhaavadī viṇṇattā ‘hanta joesari, vundāvaṇa-rajje ahisiñcajja{u} rāhī jaṁ āgāse asarīriṇī vāṇī evvaṁ paaḍaṁ amhāṇaṁ purado ādiṭṭhavadi’ tti.*  
***vṛndā:***  *(sva-gatam) mukundasya nideśād ākāśa-vāg-apadeśenāham āryām udayojayam.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sahi citte—*O Citrā sakhī; *suṇāhi—*listen; *imāe—*by this; *vundāe—*Vṛndā; *gadua—*having gone; *bhaavadī—*the saintly lady (Paurṇamāsī); *viṇṇattā—*was requested; *hanta—*alas; *joesari—*O mistress of yoga; *vundāvaṇa-rajje—*in the kingdom of Vṛndāvana; *ahisiñcajja{u}—*should be coronated; *rāhī—*Rādhā; *jaṁ—*because; *āgāse—*in the sky; *asarīriṇī—*the unembodied; *vāṇī—*voice; *evvaṁ—*likewise; *paaḍaṁ—*openly; *amhāṇaṁ—*of us; *purado—*in front; *ādiṭṭhavadi—*instructed; *tti—*thus; *sva-gatam—*to herself; *mukundasya—*of Mukunda; *nideśāt—*on the order; *ākāśa-vāk—*of the heavenly voice; *apadeśena—*in the disguise; *aham—*I; *āryām—*the revered lady (Paurṇamāsī); *udayojayam—*induced.

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: O Citrā sakhī, please listen. Vṛndā went to Bhāgavatī and requested her as follows: ‘O Yogeśvarī, Rādhā should be made the queen of this Vṛndāvana kingdom, for the voice from the sky has directly instructed me so.”**

**Vṛndā (to herself): It is on the order of Mukunda that I employed Her Grace in this task, speaking to her as voice from the sky.**

**Комментарий:**

Viśvanātha Cakravartī states that the fact that it was Mukunda who had instructed Vṛndā in that way, points to Vṛndā’s unsurpassable good fortune.

*Mādhava-mahotsava* 4.13 describes that Vṛndā felt shy to come in front of the Vrajavāsīs. She felt unqualified to assist them in their loving dealings. For this reason she had requested Paurṇamāsī to make the arrangements for the *abhiṣeka.*

### Verse 371-372

***nāndīmukhī:*** *tado mahā-tābasīe bhaavadīe āhūdāo pañca-deīo samāadāo.*  
***arjunaḥ:***  *kāo kkhu tāo?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tado—*then; *mahā-tābasīe—*by the greatly austere; *bhaavadīe—*Bhāgavatī (Paurṇamāsī); *āhūdāo—*the invoked; *pañca-deīo—*five goddesses; *samāadāo—*had assembled; *kāo—*who; *kkhu—*indeed; *tāo—*they.

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: Then the most austere Bhāgavatī had invoked and assembled the five goddesses.**

**Arjuna: Who were they?**

## Verse 373(a-b)

***vṛndā:*** *devī prasiddhā bhuvi devakī-sutā*  
*yā kaṁsam ākṣipya jagāma kevalam*  
*bhānoḥ kalatre tanayā ca pañcamī*  
*gaṅgā tu yā mānasa-pūrvikā smṛtā*  
***citrā:*** *tado tado?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*devī—*the goddess; *prasiddhā—*famous; *bhuvi—*in the world; *devakī-sutā—*the daughter of Devakī; *yā—*who; *kaṁsam—*Kaṁsa; *ākṣipya—*after deriding; *jagāma—*went; *kevalam—*to the absolute spiritual existence; *bhānoḥ—*of the sun; *kalatre—*the two wives; *tanayā—*the daughter; *ca—*also; *pañcamī—*the fifth; *gaṅgā—*Gaṅgā; *tu—*but; *yā—*which; *mānasa-pūrvikā—*preceded by the word ‘*mānasa*’; *smṛtā—*is remembered; *tado tado—*then, then;

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: The first one is the goddess famous all over the world as the daughter of Devakī. She ascended to the spiritual realm after condemning Kaṁsa. The second, third, and fourth are the two wives and the daughter of the sun god, and the fifth is Mānasa-gaṅgā.**

**Citra: Then, then?**

**Комментарий:**

The sun god’s two wives are Saṁjñā and Chāyā, and Yamunā is his daughter.

## Verse 374-375(a-b-c)

***nāndīmukhī:*** *tado kaṇiṭṭhāe mattaṇḍa-mahisīe bhaṇidaṁ, ‘bhaavadi, aṇadikkamaṇijjaṁ tumha-sāsaṇaṁ ṇiccidaṁ kkhu amhehiṁ sire gahīdaṁ. kintu kahiṁ mahiṭṭhā esā vacchā rāhī kahiṁ vā solaha-koha-metta-vitthiṇṇaṁ edaṁ vundāvaṇa-rajjaṁ’ tti ṇa suṭṭhu pasīda{i} me hiaaṁ. tado bhaavadīe viloida-muhī deī ekkāṇaṁsā saṁsiduṁ pa{u}ttā:*  
*(iti saṁskṛtena) sakhi savarṇe, samākarṇaya—*  
*āmnāyādhvara-tīrtha-mantra-tapasāṁ svargākhila-svargiṇāṁ*  
*siddhīnāṁ mahatāṁ dvayor api tayoś cic-chakti-vaikuṇṭhayoḥ*  
*vīryaṁ yat prathate tato ’pi gahanaṁ śrī-māthure maṇḍale*  
*dīvyat tatra tato ’pi tundilataraṁ vṛndāvane sundari*  
***citrā:*** *tado tado?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tado—*after that; *kaṇiṭṭhāe—*by the younger; *mattaṇḍa—*of the sun; *mahisīe—*queen; *bhaṇidaṁ—*was said; *bhaavadi—*O Bhagavatī \[Paurṇamāsī\]; *aṇadikkamaṇijjaṁ—*unsurpassable; *tumha—*your; *sāsaṇaṁ—*order; *ṇiccidaṁ—*definitely; *kkhu—*indeed; *amhehiṁ—*by us; *sire—*on our head; *gahīdaṁ—*is accepted; *kintu—*but; *kahiṁ—*where; *mahiṭṭhā—*greatest; *esā—*this; *vacchā—*child; *rāhī—*Rādhā; *kahiṁ—*where; *vā—*indeed; *solaha-koha—*for sixteen krośas; *metta—*only; *vitthiṇṇaṁ—*spread; *edaṁ—*this; *vundāvaṇa-rajjaṁ—*kingdom of Vṛndāvana; *tti—*thus; *ṇa—*not; *suṭṭhu—*well; *pasīda{i}—*is content; *me—*my; *hiaaṁ—*heart; *tado—*then; *bhaavadīe—*of Bhāgavatī; *viloida-muhī—*who was looking at the face of; *deī ekkāṇaṁsā—*Ekānaṁśā Devī; *saṁsiduṁ--*glorifying; *pa{u}ttā—*started; *iti—*thus; *saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *sakhi—*O friend; *savarṇe—*Savarṇā; *samākarṇaya—*listen attentively; *āmnāya—*of the Vedas; *adhvara—*sacrifices; *tīrtha—*holy places; *mantra—*mantras; *tapasām—*and austerities; *svarga—*of the heaven; *akhila—*and of all; *svargiṇām—*heavenly residents; *siddhīnām—*of the mystic perfections; *mahatām—*the great mystics; *dvayoḥ—*of two; *api—*even; *tayoḥ—*those; *cit-śakti—*spiritual potency; *vaikuṇṭhayoḥ—*and Vaikuṇṭha; *vīryam—*the potency; *yat—*which; *prathate—*is famous; *tataḥ api—*than that; *gahanam—*concentrated; *śrī-māthure-maṇḍale—*in the holy land of Mathurā;*—*in the tract of land; *dīvyat—*splendid; *tatra—*there; *tataḥ api—*than that; *tundilataram—*more great; *vṛndāvane—*in Vṛndāvana; *sundari—*O beautiful one; *tado tado—*then, then.

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: After that, the younger queen of the sun god said, ‘O Bhāgavatī, we definitely accept on our head your unsurpassable order. But what is this Vṛndāvana kingdom of just sixteen krośas to this supremely glorious child Rādhā?’ Hearing this, my heart was not quite satisfied. Ekānaṁśā Devī \[Durgā\], looking at Bhagavatī, then started glorifying Vṛndāvana:**

**(in Sanskrit): My friend Savarṇā, please listen carefully: “The celebrated potencies found in the Vedas, sacrifices, holy places, mantras, austerities, the heavenly abode and its residents, the great mystic perfections of yoga and those who possess them, and even in both the spiritual energy and the world of Vaikuṇṭha, are even more concentrated in the sacred region of Mathurā. And in Vṛndāvana they are resplendent even to a greater degree, o beautiful lady.”**

**Citrā: Then, after that?**

**Комментарий:**

The younger queen of the sun god is Chāyā, also known as Savarṇā. From her speech we understand that she knew the greatness of Rādhārāṇī to some degree, but wasn’t aware of the true glory of Vṛndāvana. She indirectly proposed that “Rādhārāṇī should be made the queen of the whole universe, why only of this small Vṛndāvana?”

Nāndīmukhī wasn’t happy with Savarṇā’s statement. She quotes the Sanskrit verse spoken at that time by Durgā Devī, Kṛṣṇa’s sister, who was also one of the five goddesses present there. Prompted by Paurṇamāsī, Durgā described to Savarṇā the superexcellence of Vṛndāvana by comparing its potency to the potency of the Vedas and so on.

In *Mahaṭī-ṭīkā*, Viśvanātha Cakravartī elaborates on the verse 375b: “The Vedas are empowered to explain the reality, the sacrifices like *jyotiṣṭoma* can produce specific results, the holy places can purify, the power of mantras can accomplish the impossible, and the austerities are potent to grant any boon one might desire. By executing Vedic sacrifices, visiting holy places, chanting mantras, or performing austerities, one attains heavenly planets that can yield all sorts of sense enjoyment. The enjoyers of those heavenly abodes have the ability to remain intoxicated with happiness. The mystic perfections, like the ability to become microscopic in size, easily enable one to control nature, and the great yogīs have the power to exert those perfections. The spiritual energy is the Lord’s transcendental internal potency (*cit-śakti*), consisting of eternity, knowledge, and bliss. This internal potency is ever auspicious beyond estimation, for it has the potency to appear as the whole range of objects of its own spiritual substance—like the Vaikuṇṭha world, the potency of which is in its being the highest manifestation of *cit-śakti*. Now, the sacred region of Mathurā contains all these mentioned potencies, but to even higher concentration and quality. And finally, all those potencies exist in still greater extant in Vṛndāvana, that is visibly only sixteen *krośas* in size. Knowing all that, what is the value of ruling over millions of universes, all of which Brahmā saw in just one corner of Vṛndāvana?”

*Caitanya-caritāmṛta* 2.21.29 describes:

*ṣola-krośa vṛndāvana,—śāstrera prakāśe*  
*tāra eka-deśe vaikuṇṭhājāṇḍa-gaṇa bhāse*

**“According to the revelations of revealed scripture, Vṛndāvana extends only sixteen** ***krośas*** **\[thirty-two miles\]. Nonetheless, all the Vaikuṇṭha planets and innumerable universes are located in one corner of this tract.”**

A.C.Bhaktivedānta Swami Prabhupāda explains in his purport: “In Vraja, the land is divided into various *vanas*, or forests. The forests total twelve, and their extension is estimated to be eighty-four *krośas*. Of these, the special forest known as Vṛndāvana is located from the present municipal city of Vṛndāvana to the village called Nanda-grāma. This distance is sixteen *krośas* (thirty-two miles).”

Although Vṛndāvana may seem to be a place belonging to this earth, Vedic literature describes its true glory of being the supreme transcendental abode of the Lord, non-different from Him:

*evaṁ sapta-purīṇāṁ tu*  
*sarvotkṛṣṭaṁ ca māthuram*  
*śrūyatāṁ mahimā devi*  
*vaikuṇṭho bhuvanottamaḥ*

**“Out of the seven cities that bestow liberation, the district of Mathurā is the topmost. O Devī, listen to its glories: It is the spiritual world, Vaikuṇṭha, the topmost of the worlds.”**

*aho madhu-purī dhanyā*  
*vaikuṇṭhāc ca garīyasī*

**“Ah, how glorious is the city of Mathurā, which is superior even to Vaikuṇṭha.” (*****Pātāla-khaṇḍa*** **of** ***Padma Purāṇa*****)**

*idaṁ vṛndāvanaṁ ramyaṁ*  
*mama dhāmaiva kevalam*  
*…*  
*pañca-yojanam evāsti*  
*vanaṁ me deha-rūpakam*  
*…*  
*sarva-devamayaś cāhaṁ*  
*na tyajāmi vanaṁ kvacit*  
*…*  
*tejomayam idaṁ ramyam*  
*adṛśyaṁ carma-cakṣuṣā*

**“This beautiful Vṛndāvana forest is My exclusive abode…My \[Vṛndāvana\] forest occupies the area of five** ***yojanas*****. It is My very body…I, the embodiment of all** ***devas*****, do not ever leave this forest…This effulgent and beautiful Vṛndāvana is unseen by the material eyes.” (Kṛṣṇa’s speech to Nārada in** ***Bṛhad-gautamīya-tantra*****)**

*gokulaṁ vana-vaikuṇṭham*

**“The land of Gokula (Vṛndāvana on this earth planet) is the spiritual world appearing as a forest.” (*****Kṛṣṇa-tāpanī Upaniṣad*****)**

*sātvatāṁ sthāna-mūrdhanyaṁ*  
*viṣṇor atyanta-durlabham*  
*nityaṁ vṛndāvanaṁ nāma*  
*brahmāṇḍopari-saṁsthitam*  
*pūrṇa-brahma-sukhaiśvaryaṁ*  
*nityam ānandam avyayam*  
*vaikuṇṭhādi-tad-aṁśāṁśaṁ*  
*svayaṁ vṛndāvanaṁ bhuvi*  
*golokaiśvaryaṁ yat kiñcid*  
*gokule tat-pratiṣṭhitam*  
*vaikuṇṭha-vaibhavaṁ yad vai*  
*dvārikāyāṁ pratiṣṭhitam*

**“Viṣṇu’s eternal abode named Vṛndāvana is the topmost residence of His devotees. Situated above the material universe, it is extremely difficult to attain. Goloka Vṛndāvana is filled with the opulence of complete spiritual joy. It is full of eternal bliss and inexhaustible. Vaikuṇṭha world and other abodes are but parts and parcels of Goloka Vṛndāvana, which is directly present on the earth as Vṛndāvana. Whatever opulence is there in Goloka Vṛndāvana, it is present in Gokula, Vṛndāvana on this earth, and the opulence of Vaikuṇṭha is present in Dvārakā.” (*****Padma Purāṇa*****,** ***Pātāla-khaṇḍa*** **69.8-10)**

### Verse 376

***nāndīmukhī****: tado haris-upphulle saala-jaṇe ṇibatanta-divva-kusuma-varisaṁ gaaṇaṁ pekkhia bhāṇu-ṇandiṇīe bhaṇidaṁ ‘bhaavadi, ido āantuṁ pajjussuābi tumha-aṇāmantaṇeṇa saṅkida-hiaā divva-mañjūsiāe aṇugadā pia-sahī esā sarassaī ambare vilamba{i}. tā āārīadu’ tti viṇṇattāe bhaavadīe sādaraṁ āāridā viriñci-puttī tattha pavisia divva-mañjūsiaṁ ugghāḍentī bhaṇiduṁ pa{u}ttā. (ity ardhokte)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tado—*after that; *haris—*the joy; *upphulle—*increased; *saala-jaṇe—*of everyone; *ṇibatanta—*falling down; *divva—*divine; *kusuma—*of flowers; *varisaṁ—*with its shower; *gaaṇaṁ—*at the sky; *pekkhia—*looking; *bhāṇu—*of the sun god; *ṇandiṇīe—*by the daughter; *bhaṇidaṁ—*was said; *bhaavadi—*O great one \[Paurṇamāsī\]; *ido—*here; *āantuṁ—*to come; *pajjussaa—*eager; *abi—*also; *tumha—*your; *aṇāmantaṇeṇa—*by the absence of invitation; *saṅkida—*timid; *hiaā—*her heart; *divva—*by the divine; *mañjūsiāe—*case; *aṇugadā pia-sahī—*dear friend; *esā—*this; *sarassaī—*Sarasvatī; *ambare—*in the sky; *vilamba{i}—*is hesitating; *tā—*therefore; *āārīadu—*she should be drawn near; *tti—*thus; *viṇṇattāe—*by the informed; *bhaavadīe—*great lady; *sa-ādaraṁ—*with respect; *āāridā—*was called; *viriñci-puttī—*the daughter of Viriñcī (Brahmā); *tattha—*there; *pavisia—*after entering; *divva-mañjūsiaṁ—*the divine case; *ugghāḍentī—*while opening; *bhaṇiduṁ—*to speak; *pa{u}ttā—*starte; *iti—*thus; *ardha—*when the half (of this statement); *ukte—*was spoken.

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhi: Then, when everybody was swelling with joy, the daughter of the sun god \[Yamunā\], looking at the sky that showered with heavenly flowers, said, ‘O Bhāgavatī, our dear friend Sarasvatī was also eager to come here, but she didn’t receive your invitation. And now, with a trembling heart, she is standing in the sky and hesitates, keeping with her a divine case.
