So please, bring her here.’ Petitioned thus, Bhagavatī (Paurṇamāsī) then respectfully invited Sarasvatī, the daughter of Brahmā, who entered the assembly and began to speak, opening her divine case… (Nāndīmukhī is interrupted)**

## Verse 377(a-b)

***vṛndā:*** *(nirbharautsukyena nāndīmukhī-vācam ācchādya sa-harṣam)*  
*pādmīṁ padma-bhuvaḥ srajaṁ praṇayinī sauvarṇa-paṭṭaṁ śacī*  
*ratnālaṅkaraṇaṁ kuvera-gṛhiṇī chatraṁ pracetaḥ-priyā*  
*dvandvaṁ cāmarayoḥ prabhañjana-vadhūḥ svāhā dukūla-dvayaṁ*  
*dhūmorṇā maṇi-darpaṇaṁ sa-rabhasaṁ mat-pāṇinā prāhiṇot*  
***citrā:*** *tado tado?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nirbhara—*with excessive; *autsukyena—*enthusiasm; *nāndīmukhī-vācam—*the words of Nāndīmukhī; *ācchādya—*interrupting; *sa-harṣam—*with joy;*pādmīm—*made of lotuses; *padma-bhuvaḥ—*of Brahmā; *srajam—*the garland; *praṇayinī—*the beloved wife; *sauvarṇa—*golden; *paṭṭam—*throne; *śacī—*Śacī, the wife of Indra; *ratna—*of jewels; *alaṅkaraṇam—*the ornament; *kuvera-gṛhiṇī—*the wife of Kuverā; *chatram—*the umbrella; *pracetaḥ-priyā—*the dear wife of Pracetās; *dvandvam—*the pair; *cāmarayoḥ—*of whisks; *prabhañjana-vadhūḥ—*the wife of the wind god; *svāhā—*Svāhā, the wife of Agni; *dukūla-dvayam—*a pair of *dukūla* cloth; *dhūmorṇā—*Dhūmorṇā, the wife of Yamarāja; *maṇi—*ornamented with jewels; *darpaṇam—*the mirror; *sa-rabhasam—*with urge; *mat-pāṇinā—*with my hand; *prāhiṇot—*sent; *tado tado—*then, then.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā (interrupting Nāndīmukhī’s narration out of excessive enthusiasm, says joyfully);**

**\[Sarasvatī said:\] “The beloved wives of Brahmā, Indra, Kuvera, Pracetās, Agni, Yama, and the wind god, urgently sent through me their respective gifts: A lotus garland, a golden throne, a jeweled ornament, an umbrella, two fine garments, a gem-ornamented mirror, and a pair of cāmaras.”**

**Citrā: Then, then?**

**Комментарий:**

Vṛndā quotes Sarasvatī, who is announcing the gifts she has brought in her case. These gift items were previously the property of the demons. The demigods have obtained them as a result of their victory over the demons. Now they are offering the items to Rādhārāṇī, not having used them for themselves.

It is said that Agni’s wife presented a pair of fine clothes made of *dukūla*, a particular plant the inner portion of which is used in cloth making. Some say that *dukūla* refers to silk.

## Verse 378(a-b-c)

***vṛndā:*** *tataś ca—*  
*svar-vādya-dhvani-ḍambare śruti-hare gambhīrayaty ambaraṁ*  
*gāyaty ambuda-bhāji tumburu-mukhe gandharva-vṛnde mudā*  
*nṛtyaty apsarasāṁ gaṇe ca gagane rādhābhiṣekotsavaṁ*  
*kartuṁ tāḥ sura-subhruvaḥ sa-rabhasaṁ bhavyās tam ārebhire*  
*(iti nāndīmukhīm avekṣya sa-lajjam) tatas tataḥ?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tataḥ—*after that; *ca—*and; *svaḥ—*celestial; *vādya—*of music; *dhvani—*of the sound; *ḍambare—*when the tumult; *śruti—*one’s ears; *hare—*captivating; *gambhīrayati—*made to resound deeply; *ambaram—*the sky; *gāyati—*when sang; *ambuda—*in the clouds; *bhāji—*living; *tumburu—*by Tumburu; *mukhe—*headed; *gandharva—*of the Gandharvas; *vṛnde—*the hords; *mudā—*with happiness; *nṛtyati—*when danced; *apsarasām—*of Apsarās; *gaṇe—*the multitude; *ca—*and; *gagane—*in the sky; *rādhā-abhiṣeka—*of Rādhā’s coronational bathing; *utsavam—*the festival; *kartum—*performing; *tāḥ—*those; *sura—*of the demigods; *subhruvaḥ—*wives with beautiful eyebrows; *sa-rabhasam—*with urge; *bhavyāḥ—*beautiful; *tam—*that; *ārebhire—*started; *iti—*thus; *nāndīmukhīm—*at Nāndīmukhī; *avekṣya—*glancing; *sa-lajjam—*with shyness; *tataḥ tataḥ—*then, then.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: After this, the sky loudly resounded with the tumult of captivating celestial music, the hords of cloud living Gandharvas headed by Tumburu sang with joy, and the crowds of Apsarās danced in the sky. Then, the lovely wives of the demigods, their eyebrows nicely shaped, began to conduct the festive occasion of Rādhā’s coronational bathing.**

**(having said this, glances bashfully at Nāndīmukhī) …And what happened after?**

**Комментарий:**

Gandharvas are described as residing in the clouds. As Viśvanātha Cakravartī reasons, this is because it is inappropriate for them to reveal themselves to ordinary humans.

## Verse 379(a-b)

***nāndīmukhī:*** *tado pekkhantammi sāṇandaṁ va{i}nda-ṇandaṇe bhaavadīe ṇideseṇa tāhiṁ bhuaṇa-pāaṇa-taraṅgiṇīhiṁ saṅgiṇīhiṁ deīhiṁ tumhehiṁ sahīhiṁ saddhaṁ puraḍa-paṭṭobari ṇivesidāe rāhie divva-mahosahi-rasāmia-pūrideṇa maṇi-kumbha-ṇiurambeṇa mahāhiseaṁ kuṇantīhiṁ vundāvaṇa-rajjassa āhipaccaṁ appidaṁ.*  
***campakalatā:*** *(sa-romāñcam) tado tado?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tado—*after that; *pekkhantammi—*was watching; *sa-āṇandaṁ—*with bliss; *vainda-ṇandaṇe—*as the son of Vraja’s king; *bhaavadīe—*of the saintly lady (Paurṇamāsī); *ṇideseṇa—*on the order; *tāhiṁ—*those; *bhuaṇa—*the world; *pāaṇa—*purifying; *taraṅgiṇīhiṁ—*the waves; *saṅgiṇīhiṁ—*associates; *deīhiṁ—*goddesses; *tumhehiṁ—*with you; *sahīhiṁ—*sakhīs; *saddhaṁ—*together; *puraḍa—*the golden; *paṭṭa—*cloth; *ubari—*on; *ṇivesidāe—*of the sitting; *rāhie—*Rādhā; *divva—*divine; *mahā-osahi—*of great herbs; *rasa—*of the juice; *amia—*with nectar; *pūrideṇa—*filled; *maṇi—*jeweled; *kumbha—*of jars; *ṇiurambeṇa—*with the multitude; *mahā-hiseaṁ—*the great bathing; *kuṇantīhiṁ—*doing; *vundāvaṇa-rajjassa—*over the kingdom of Vṛndāvana; *āhipaccaṁ—*the sovereignity; *appidaṁ—*was offered; *sa-romāñcam—*with her hairs standing on end; *tado tado—*then, then.

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: Then, while Vrajendra-nandana was blissfully watching what was happening, you sakhīs, Rādhā’s divine companions who are like the waves purifying the whole world, have seated Rādhā on a golden throne and performed the mahā-abhiṣeka from many gem studded jars filled with nectarean essence of most excellent divine herbs. Thus, on the order of Bhagavatī, the sovereignity over the kingdom of Vṛndāvana was conferred upon Rādhā.**

**Campakalatā (with hairs standing on end): And then, then?**

**Комментарий:**

Mānasa-gaṅgā, Yamunā and Sarasvatī, present in person, manifested their sacred waters from their right hands in the form of waterfalls. These waters mixed with the holy waters of Vṛndāvana and together formed a lake near the venue of the *abhiṣeka*. Then the grand elaborate coronational bathing was performed under the guidance of Paurṇamāsī.

## Verse 380(a-b)

***nāndīmukhī:*** *tado hatthaṁ ukkhibia sarassaīe sarassaī paāsidā,‘edaṁ taṁ soandhiaṁ dāmaṁ jaṁ siṇeheṇa ammāe sāvittīe pahidaṁ’ tti suṇia deīe ekkāṇaṁsāe taṁ geṇhia goulāṇandassa kaṇṭhe ṇikkhittaṁ. tado ṇamma-sumhera-muhīe mihira-duhidāe vāharidaṁ ‘ammahe dhamma-vimhāraṇe kammaḍhadā bandhu-jaṇa-siṇehāṇaṁ jehiṁ viakkhaṇā bi aviāria paaṭṭenti’ tti samāaṇṇia viñjha-vāsiṇīe bhaṇidaṁ, ‘jauṇe, kā kkhu tue aviārā diṭṭhā pa{u}ttī?’ taṁ suṇia jauṇāe bhaṇidaṁ, ‘dei, amha-bahiṇīe rāhiāe pesidā sogandhia-mālā kīsa tue appa-bhāduṇo diṇṇā?’ tti suṇia vihasantīe deīe hariseṇa hari-kaṇṭhādo cāru-hāreṇa saddhaṁ uttāria taṁ divva-soandhia-mālaṁ pia-sahī-kaṇṭhe ṇikkhibantīe saṁsidaṁ, ‘a{i}, geṇha appaṇo mālaṁ’ tti.*  
*(kṛṣṇaḥ smitaṁ karoti)*  
***viśākhā:*** *tado tado?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tado—*after that; *hatthaṁ—*her hand; *ukkhibia—*lifting up; *sarassaīe—*by Sarasvatī; *sarassaī—*the speech; *paāsidā—*was made appear; *edaṁ—*this; *taṁ—*that; *soandhiaṁ—*fragrant; *dāmaṁ—*wreath; *jaṁ—*which; *siṇeheṇa—*with affection; *ammāe—*by mother; *sāvittīe—*Sāvitrī; *pahidaṁ—*sent; *tti—*thus; *suṇia—*hearing; *deīe—*by goddess; *ekkāṇaṁsāe—*Ekānaṁśā, a name of Durgā; *tam—*that; *geṇhia—*taking; *goula-āṇandassa—*of Gokulānanda \[Kṛṣṇa\]; *kaṇṭhe—*on the neck; *ṇikkhittaṁ—*placed; *tado—*after that; *ṇamma—*playful; *sumhera—*with a wide smile; *muhīe—*her face; *mihira-duhidāe—*by the daughter of Mihira (the sun god); *vāharidaṁ—*was said; *ammahe—*Ah; *dhamma—*dharma; *vimhāraṇe—*in forgetting; *kammaḍhadā—*cleverness; *bandhu-jaṇa—*of relatives; *siṇehāṇaṁ—*of affection; *jehiṁ—*by which; *viakkhaṇā—*the wise; *bi—*even; *aviāria—*without considering; *paaṭṭenti—*act; *tti—*thus; *samāaṇṇia—*having carefully heard; *viñjjha-vāsiṇīe—*by Vindhyā-vāsinī, a name of Durgā; *bhaṇidaṁ—*was said; *jauṇe—*O Yamunā; *kā—*what; *kkhu—*indeed; *tue—*by you; *aviārā—*without consideration; *diṭṭhā—*was seen; *pa{u}ttī—*activity; *taṁ—*that; *suṇia—*hearing; *jauṇāe—*by Yamunā; *bhaṇidaṁ—*was said; *dei—*O goddess; *amha—*by our; *bahiṇīe—*sister; *rāhiāe—*Rādhikā; *pesidā—*sent; *sogandhia—*fragrant; *mālā—*the garland; *kīsa—*why; *tue—*by you; *appa—*to your; *bhāduṇo—*brother; *diṇṇā—*was given; *tti—*thus; *suṇia—*hearing; *vihasantīe—*by the laughing; *deīe—*goddess; *hariseṇa—*with joy; *hari-kaṇṭhādo—*from the neck of Hari; *cāru—*charming; *hāreṇa—*necklace; *saddhaṁ—*with; *uttāria—*taking off; *tam—*that; *divva—*celestial; *soandhia-mālaṁ—*fragrant garland; *pia-sahī—*of the dear sakhī; *kaṇṭhe—*on the neck; *ṇikkhibantīe—*placing; *saṁsidam—*was said; *a{i}—*O you; *geṇha—*accept; *appaṇo—*Your; *mālaṁ—*garland; *tti—*thus. *kṛṣṇaḥ—*Kṛṣṇa; *smitam karoti—*smiles. *tado tado—*then, then.

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: After that, Sarasvatī lifted her hand up and spoke, ‘My mother Sāvitrī has affectionately sent this fragrant garland.’ The goddess Durgā, after hearing this, grabbed the garland and placed it on the neck of Gokulānanda \[Kṛṣṇa\].**

**Then, the playfully smiling daughter of the sun god \[Yamunā\] said, ‘Ah, this is the trick of the affection for one’s relatives - it makes one forget proper behavior. Even the wise, when they are under the spell of this affection, act without thinking.’ Hearing this, Durgā asked, ‘Yamunā, which inconsiderate action on my part did you notice?’**

**Yamunā replied, ‘O Devī, this fragrant garland was sent to our sister Rādhikā. Why did you offer it to your brother?’ Hearing this, Durgā laughed and joyfully removed that celestial fragrant garland from Hari’s neck along with a beautiful necklace. She then put both the garland and the necklace on the neck of our dear sakhī, saying, ‘Please accept Your garland.’**

**(Kṛṣṇa smiles)**

**Viśākhā: Then, then?**

**Комментарий:**

This incident with the garland happened after Rādhā was bathed, dressed, and worshipped. The garland sent by Sāvitrī (wife of Brahmā) was made of lotuses.

Nāndīmukhī mentions Durgā by two names—Ekānaṁśā and Vindhya-vāsinī. Ekānaṁśā is a form of Durgā, who had appeared as the seventh child of Devakī.

*mṛtām iva punaḥ prāpya*  
*brāhmaṇebhyo dadau dhanam*  
*sā parā bhaginī vipra*  
*kṛṣṇasya paramātmanaḥ*  
*ekānaṁśeti vikhyātā*  
*pārvaty-aṁśa-samudbhavā*

**“As if they \[Vasudeva and Devakī\] had recovered their daughter from the grip of death, they gave charity to the** ***brāhmaṇas*****. O** ***brāhmaṇa*****, that girl, who was named Ekānaṁśā, was a partial incarnation of Goddess Pārvatī. She was the transcendental sister of Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead…”**

*ekānaṁśā harer bhagnī*  
*vasudeva-sutā mune*  
*pārvaty-aṁśa-samudbhūtā*  
*su-śīlā cira-jīvinī*

**“Virtuous Ekānaṁśā is the daughter of Vasudeva and the sister of Lord Hari. She is a partial expansion of Goddess Pārvatī. She is eternal.”**

In *Nārada-pañcarātra*, in the conversation between Śruti and Vidyā, the Lord’s *māyā-śakti* is described as ‘*ekā*’, meaning literally “One.” According to Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, this means ‘*eka-vidhā*’ (“of one kind”), and ‘*ekānaṁśā*’ (“One, without portions”). The significance of any of these names is that the same potency of the Lord acts in two capacities—spiritual (*yogamāyā*, which helps the devotees in their service to the Lord) and material (*mahāmāyā*, which bewilders the materialists).

Vindhya-vāsinī is another name of Durgā, meaning “One who resides on the Vindhya mountains.”

### Verse 381

***nāndīmukhī:*** *tado ‘alaṁ kaṭhiṇa-hiaa-saṅgiṇā amhāṇaṁ imiṇā hāreṇa!’ tti hasantīe haṁsa-puttīe kodueṇa rāhiāe hāro kkhu hāriṇi hari-kaṇṭhe appido.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tado—*then; *alaṁ—*enough; *kaṭhiṇa—*hard; *hiaa—*of One with a heart; *saṅgiṇā—*of associate; *amhāṇaṁ—*to us; *imiṇā—*this; *hāreṇa—*garland; *tti—*thus; *hasantīe—*by the smiling; *haṁsa-puttīe—*daughter of Haṁsa (the sun god); *kodueṇa—*with pleasure; *rāhiāe—*of Rādhikā; *hāro—*the necklace; *kkhu—*indeed; *hāriṇi—*attractive; *hari-kaṇṭhe—*onto the neck of Hari; *appido—*was offered*.*

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: The smiling daughter of the sun god \[Yamunā\] then said, ‘What is the use of this necklace, the associate of One whose heart is made of stone!’ and placed Rādhikā’s necklace onto the attractive neck of Hari.**

**Комментарий:**

After goddess Ekānaṁśā \[Durgā\] had garlanded Rādhā, Yamunā took another, pearl necklace from Rādhikā’s neck and placed it on Kṛṣṇa, whom she called “the stone-hearted One.” In this way, Ekānaṁśā and Yamunā, exchanging joking behavior with each other, made Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa exchange Their necklaces.

### Verse 382

***lalitā:***  *tado ekkāṇaṁsāe kaṁsāri-vakkha-tthalādo gahideṇa kuraṅga-ṇāhiṇā rāhiāe tilaaṁ ṇimmidaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tado—*then; *ekkāṇaṁsāe—*by Ekānaṁśā; *kaṁsāri—*of Kaṁsāri; *vakkha-tthalādo—*from the chest; *gahideṇa—*taken; *kuraṅga-ṇāhiṇā—*by the deermusk; *rāhiāe—*of Rādhikā; *tilaaṁ—*a *tilaka* mark; *ṇimmidaṁ—*was painted*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: And after that, Ekānaṁśā \[Durgā\] removed the deer musk from the chest of Kaṁsāri and painted with it a tilaka mark on Rādhikā.**

**Комментарий:**

*Tilaka* here refers to an ornamental mark. Ekānaṁśā drew *tilaka* on Rādhā’s forehead.

## Verse 383(a-b)-384

***vṛndā:*** *(sānandam) tataś ca bhagavatyā sollāsam abhyadhāyi—*  
*sārdhaṁ vallī-vadhūbhir vilasata sukhinaḥ śākhino bhūri phullā*  
*raṅgaṁ bhṛṅgair vihaṅgāḥ prathayata paśavaḥ prauḍhim āviṣkurudhvam*  
*ālībhir vāhinīnāṁ patibhir upacitā śrīmatī rādhikeyaṁ*  
*vṛndām udyāna-pālīṁ śucim iha sacivī-kṛtya vaḥ śāsti rājyam*  
*(ity ānanda-niṣpandam abhinīya)*  
*jagrāha kunda-latikotkalikā-śatāni*  
*patrāṅkureṇa sumanā virarāja citrā*  
*smerā babhūva lalitā nava-mālikāsau*  
*phullā atra campakalatā ca viśākhikā api*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-ānandam—*with joy; *tataḥ—*after that; *ca—*and; *bhagavatyā—*by Bhagavatī (Paurṇamāsī); *sa-ullāsam—*with happiness; *abhyadhāyi—*was said; *sārdham—*with; *vallī—*vines; *vadhūbhiḥ—*with their young brides; *vilasata—*play; *sukhinaḥ—*happy; *śākhinaḥ—*O trees; *bhūri—*excessively; *phullāḥ—*blossoming; *raṅgam—*your playground; *bhṛṅgaiḥ—*with bees; *vihaṅgāḥ—*O birds; *prathayata—*expand; *paśavaḥ—*O animals; *prauḍhim—*grandeur; *āviṣkurudhvam—*manifest; *ālībhiḥ—*with your female companions; *vāhinīnām—*of Her armies; *patibhiḥ—*with the generals; *upacitā—*thriving; *śrīmatī—*majestic; *rādhikā—*Rādhikā; *iyam—*this; *vṛndām—*Vṛndā; *udyāna-pālīm—*the garden protectress; *śucim—*the noble counsellor; *iha—*here; *sacivī-kṛtya—*making a minister; *vaḥ—*your; *śāsti—*is ruling; *rājyam—*kingdom. *iti—*thus; *ānanda—*due to bliss; *niṣpandam—*her stun; *abhinīya—*expressing; *jagrāha—*accepted; *kundalatikā—*Kundalatā; *utkalikā—*of longings; *śatāni—*hundreds; *patra-aṅkureṇa—*with a decorative pattern of streaks drawn on the face with various colorful and fragrant substances; *su-manā—*satisfied; *virarāja—*beamed; *citrā—*Citrā; *smerā—*smiling; *babhūva—*became; *lalitā—*Lalitā; *nava-mālikā—*with a fresh garland; *asau—*she; *phullā—*smiling; *atra—*here; *campakalatā—*Campakalatā; *ca—*and; *viśākhikā—*Viśākhā; *api—*also.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā (joyfully): And then Bhāgavatī buoyantly said, ‘O trees, be happy, blossom with abundance, and rejoice with the vines, your young brides! O birds, expand your playground along with the bees! O beasts, unfold your grandeur with your female companions! Magnificent Rādhikā, thriving with Her military generals, will now rule your kingdom with noble counsellor Vṛndā as Her minister.’**

**(stunned in bliss, Vṛndā continues): At that time Kundalatā became impassioned; Citrā, decorated with various designs drawn with musk and completely satisfied, was beaming with happiness; Lalitā, wearing a fresh garland, smiled; and Campakalatā and Viśākhā were full of joy.**

**Комментарий:**

After Rādhārāṇī was seated on a throne and given royal insignia, Paurṇamāsī proclaimed Rādhā as the queen of Vṛndāvana to the “citizens” of Vṛndāvana forest, cheering up all of them. Rādhā’s “military generals” are Her close friends like Lalitā and Viśākhā.

Next, Vṛndā describes the happiness that Rādhārāṇī’s sakhīs had experienced at seeing Her as the queen of Vṛndāvana.

The double meanings of the words in the verse 384 amazingly create a description of a garland: “The fresh (*nava*), elegant (*lalitā*) garland (*mālikā*) accepted (*jagrāha*) hundreds of jasmine buds (*kunda-latikā-utkalikā śatāni*). This garland became happy (*su-manāḥ*) with tender young leaves (*patrāṅkureṇa*) strung into it, and in this way, looking surprisingly varied (*citrā*), it appeared splendid (*virarāja*). It bloomed (*phullā*), followed by the campaka creeper (*campaka-latā*), even though it was withered (*viśākhikā*).”

### Verse 385-386

***lalitā:***  *ṇandīmuhi, tadā diṇinda-ṇandiṇīe jaṁ bhaṇidaṁ taṁ ṇūṇaṁ tue visumaridaṁ.*  
***nāndīmukhī:***  *halā, kadhaṁ visumaridavvaṁ jaṁ edāe amhāṇaṁ antie evva mantidaṁ, ‘ajja-pahudī appaṇo kīlā-vaṇe lalidā-pahudio amha-pia-sahīo suheṇa sacchandaṁ kusumāim ociṇṇantu.’ taṁ suṇia viñjha-vāsiṇīe bhaṇidaṁ, ‘ja{u}ṇe, tahabi māhavassa āattā kusumāṇaṁ samiddhi’ tti.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ṇandīmuhi—*O Nāndīmukhī; *tadā—*at that time; *diṇinda-ṇandiṇīe—*by the daughter of the Sun; *jaṁ—*what; *bhaṇidaṁ—*was said; *taṁ—*that; *ṇūṇaṁ—*indeed; *tue—*by you; *visumaridaṁ—*is forgotten. *halā—*O friend; *kadhaṁ—*how; *visumaridavvaṁ—*can be forgotten; *jaṁ—*what; *edāe—*by her; *amhāṇaṁ—*to us; *antie—*in the proximity; *mantidaṁ—*was spoken; *ajja-pahudī—*beginning from today; *appaṇo—*own; *kīlā-vaṇe—*in the pastime grove; *lalidā-pahudio—*Lalitā and others; *amha—*our; *pia-sahīo—*dear friends; *suheṇa—*happily; *sacchandaṁ—*as desired; *kusumāiṁ—*flowers; *ociṇṇantu—*may pick; *tam—*that; *suṇia—*hearing; *viñjhava-vāsiṇīe—*by Vindhyā-vāsinī (Durgā); *bhaṇidaṁ—*was said; *ja{u}ṇe—*O Yamunā; *tahabi—*still; *māhavassa—*on spring; *āattā—*is dependant; *kusumāṇaṁ—*of flowers; *samiddhi—*the abundance; *tti—*thus.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: Nāndīmukhī, you have certainly forgot what the Sun’s daughter said at that time.**

**Nāndīmukhī: How can I forget what was said just near us, my friend? Yamunā said, ‘From today on, my dear sakhīs headed by Lalitā can happily pick flowers in my pastime grove as they like.’ But when Vindhyā-vāsinī \[Durgā\] heard this, she remarked, ‘Still, O Yamunā, the abundance of flowers depends on the spring season.’**

**Комментарий:**

Yamunā hints that since Rādhā is now officially the empress of Vṛndāvana, the gopīs can pick flowers in the groves by the bank of Yamunā unimpeded. It is well known that Kṛṣṇa often obstructs them from doing so by every time claiming the forest to be His property. Kṛṣṇa’s sister, Durgā, is clearly on the side of her mischevious brother. To denote the spring season she uses the word *‘mādhava,’* which is a famous name of Kṛṣṇa. “Still, O Yamunā, the abundance of flowers depends on Mādhava.” Her words are in this way a jealous remark to Yamunā, who thought that all the gopīs’ problems with flower collecting were over.

### Verse 387

***vṛndā:*** *(rādhām avekṣya sautsukyam)*  
*devyā datta-viśeṣakā viracitottaṁsā śaner ambayā*  
*tvaṣṭur nandanayā nibaddha-kavarā svīyālibhir maṇḍitā*  
*cañcac-cāmarayā sakhi dyusaritā sauryā ca saṁvījitā*  
*putryā padmabhuvas tvam ucchrita-maṇi-cchatrā na vismaryase*

**Пословный перевод:**

*rādhām—*at Rādhā; *avekṣya—*glancing; *sa-autsukyam—*with eagerness; *devyā—*by Devī (Ekānaṁśā, or Durgā); *datta—*given; *viśeṣakā—*whose *tilaka* mark; *viracita—*made; *uttaṁsā—*whose wreath; *śaneḥ—*of Śani (Saturn); *ambayā—*by the mother; *tvaṣṭuḥ—*of Tvaṣṭā (Viśvakarmā); *nandanayā—*by the daughter; *nibaddha—*arranged; *kavarā—*whose braid; *svīya—*by Her own; *ālibhiḥ—*female companions; *maṇḍitā—*decorated; *cañcat—*by waving; *cāmarayā—*whisk; *sakhi—*O sakhī; *dyusaritā—*by Gaṅgā; *sauryā—*Yamunā; *ca—*and; *saṁvījitā—*fanned; *putryā—*by the daughter; *padmabhuvaḥ—*of Brahmā; *tvam—*You; *ucchrita—*raised; *maṇi—*with gems; *chattrā—*with an umbrella; *na vismaryase—*are not forgotten.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā (glancing eagerly at Rādhā): O sakhī, nobody can forget how You were beautifully surrounded with Your friends, Ekānaṁśā Devī \[Durgā\] marked You with tilaka, Śani’s mother \[Chāyā\] strung a wreath for Your head, Viśvakarmā’s daughter \[Saṁjñā\] braided Your hair, Gaṅgā whisked You with a cāmara, Yamunā fanned You, and Brahmā’s daughter \[Sarasvatī\] held a gem-encrusted umbrella over You.**

**Комментарий:**

The goddesses got their chance to serve Rādhikā in these various ways during Her dressing before ascending on the throne.

The sun god’s younger wife Chāyā, mentioned recently as Savarṇā, happens to be the mother of Śani (Saturn). Once Saṁjñā, the sun god’s wife, was once unable to tolerate her husband’s fierce radiance and left home to perform austerities. To continue serving her husband, she left behind her maidservant Chāyā, disguised as herself. Vivasvān, the sun god, thinking Chāyā to be his wife, begot in her three sons—Śani, Sāvarṇa Manu, and Tapati.

Another wife of Vivasvān, Saṁjñā, is the daughter of Viśvakarmā.

### Verse 388-389

***rādhā:*** *(sa-lajjam) vunde, viramehi.*  
***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(sva-gatam)*  
*dṛśam adhika-didṛkṣur apy ayuñjaṁ*  
*vibudha-vadhū-trapayā ’ham ānatāsyaḥ*  
*hṛdi vara-maṇi-bimbitāṁ tademāṁ*  
*sapadi vilokya mudā skhalann ivāsam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-lajjam—*with shyness; *vunde—*O Vṛndā; *viramehi—*stop. *sva-gatam—*to Himself; *dṛśam—*My eyes; *adhika—*much; *didṛkṣuḥ—*wishing to look; *api—*also; *ayuñjam—*I fixed; *vibudha-vadhū—*of the wives of demigods; *trapayā—*due to the shyness; *aham—*I; *ānata—*lowered; *āsyaḥ—*My face; *hṛdi—*on My chest; *vara-maṇi —*in the best of jewels (Kaustubha); *bimbitām—*reflected; *tadā—*at that time; *imām—*Her (Rādhā); *sapadi—*at the same time; *vilokya—*glancing; *mudā—*with joy; *skhalan—*tottering; *iva—*as if; *āsam—*happened to be.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (bashfully): Vṛndā, please stop.**

**Kṛṣṇa (to Himself): Wanting to look at Rādhā more, I fixed My eyes on Her, but because I felt embarrassed in front of the goddesses present there, I lowered My face. And at the very moment when I saw Rādhā reflected in the Kaustubha gem on My chest, I found Myself tottering out of joy.**

### Verse 390

***subalaḥ:***  *(apavārya) ajjuṇa, sā kira mahāhisea-tthalī rāhīe uggada-ppamadattaṇeṇa ‘ummada-rāhi’ tti jaṇehiṁ ugghusijja{i}.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*apavārya—*aside; *ajjuṇa—*O Arjuna; *sā—*that; *kira—*indeed; *mahāhisea-tthalī—*place of *mahābhiṣeka*; *rāhīe—*of Rādhā; *uggada—*increasing; *ppamadattaṇeṇa—*because of happiness; *ummada-rāhi—*Unmada-rādhā; *tti—*as; *jaṇehiṁ—*by people; *ugghusijja{i}—*is announced.

**Перевод:**

**Subala (aside): Arjuna, people call the site of Rādhā’s coronation as ‘Unmada-rādhā’, because there Rādhā’s joy had no bounds.**

**Комментарий:**

*‘Unmada-rādhā’* means “Maddened with joy Rādhā,” and it refers to the royal assmbly hall specially furnished by Vṛndā Devī for the coronation ceremony. There, Rādhārāṇī became overwhelmed with the bliss at meeting again Her beloved Kṛṣṇa after unexpected separation. After the ceremony was over, Paurṇamāsī sent the goddesses and elders back to their homes, so that Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa could freely enjoy the happiness of Their meeting in the company of Their intimate friends.

This place is mentioned in *Vraja-vilāsa-stava* 61:

*sārdhaṁ mānasa-jāhnavī-mukha-nadī-vargaiḥ saraṅgotkaraiḥ*  
*sāvitry-ādi-surī-kulaiś ca nitarām ākāśa-vāṇyā vidhoḥ*  
*vṛndāraṇya-vareṇya-rājya-viṣaye śrī-paurṇamāsī mudā*  
*rādhāṁ yatra siṣeca siñcatu sukhaṁ sonmatta-rādhā-sthalī*

**“In accordance with Kṛṣṇa’s order, which He spoke \[to Vṛndā Devī\] from the sky, Śrī Paurṇamāsī joyfully bathed and coronated Rādhā as the empress of the supreme kingdom of Vṛndāvana. On that occasion, the demigoddesses such as Sāvitrī accompanied Paurṇamāsī, and the personified prominent rivers like Mānasa-gaṅgā lively sang and danced. May that sacred place, known as Unmatta-rādhā-sthalī\[lit. “the place where Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī became overwhelmed with happiness”\], sprinkle me with happiness.”**

*Bhakti-ratnākara* 5.1278-1279 mentions that this place is in the sub-forest Chatravāna, which is located in Kāmyavāna, one of the twelve main forests of Vraja.

Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī prays to Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī for a chance to witness Her grand coronational ceremony:

*prītyā maṅgala-gīta-nṛtya-vilasad-vīṇādi-vādyotsavaiḥ*  
*śuddhānāṁ payasāṁ ghaṭair bahu-vidhaiḥ saṁvāsitānāṁ bhṛśam*  
*vṛndāraṇya-mahādhipatya-vidhaye yaḥ paurṇamāsyā svayaṁ*  
*dhīre saṁvihitaḥ sa kiṁ tava mahā-seko mayā drakṣyate*

**“O sober One, will I see Your grand** ***abhiṣeka*** **that Paurṇamāsī personally conducted out of love? In that festival, which was accompanied by auspicious singing, dancing, and playing of musical instruments like vīṇā, You were installed as the empress of Vṛndāvana forest by being bathed from various vessels filled with abundantly scented pure liquids. ”**

### Verse 391

***madhumaṅgalaḥ:***  *(apavārya) pia-vaassa, savvaṁ sumaranto mhi. ṇāsaccaṁ pagabbhanti goiāo.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*apavārya—*aside; *pia-vaassa—*O dear friend; *savvaṁ—*everything; *sumaranto—*remember; *mhi—*I; *ṇa—*not; *asaccaṁ—*falsely; *pagabbhanti—*feel proud; *goiāo—*gopīs*.*

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala (aside): I remember everything, dear friend. The gopīs aren’t falsely proud.**

### Verse 392

***rādhā:*** *sahi vunde, gaṇīadu aṭṭha-vārisio kāṇaṇa-karo.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sahi vunde—*O sakhī Vṛndā; *gaṇīadu—*should be counted; *aṭṭha-vārisio—*for eight years; *kāṇaṇa—*forest; *karo—*tax.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: O Vṛndā sakhī, please count the forest tax for eight years.**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā now takes advantage of the fact that everybody has heard the narration of Her *abhiṣeka* and behaves as a rightful queen.

Viśvanātha Cakravartī gives an exhaustive answer on the question why does Rādhā demand a tax for eight years in particular.

Somebody might argue that at this point of time eight years have elapsed since Rādhārāṇī’s coronation, and, as the queen of Vṛndāvana, Rādhārāṇī now rightfully demands from Kṛṣṇa and the cowherd boys Her dues. This opinion doesn’t make sense, however, for the following reason. If Rādhā’s coronation occured in Her *madhya-kaiśora* age, Kṛṣṇa at that time should have been in His eternal *śeṣa-kaiśora* age (beyond which His body doesn’t change its appearance anymore). So, if it was true that Rādhā is demanding the tax for eight years because this much time has elapsed after Her becoming the queen of Vṛndāvana, by this time Kṛṣṇa would have been around seventeen (which is eight years after His *śeṣa-kaiśora* period which He reaches at around nine—following the pattern of Kṛṣṇa’s bodily development given in *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu*). This makes the present tax pastime impossible, since according to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*Kṛṣṇa spent in Vraja only eleven years, and so, He would have already left Vraja.

On the contrary, some say that it is quite possible for the tax pastime to happen eight years after Rādhārāṇī’s coronation. According to this opinion, *dāna-keli-līlā* is one of the unmanifest pastimes (*aprakaṭa-līlā*—pastimes that eternally occur beyond the ordinary time-space-bound perception) that hadn’t taken place during the eleven years spent by Kṛṣṇa in Vraja visibly. So, even though the “visible” Kṛṣṇa had left, the “invisible” one stayed back and continued to play as an ordinary cowherd boy. *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.90.48 supports the eternal presence of Kṛṣṇa in Vraja:

*jayati jana-nivāso devakī-janma-vādo*  
*yadu-vara-pariṣat svair dorbhir asyann adharmam*  
*sthira-cara-vṛjina-ghnaḥ su-smita-śrī-mukhena*  
*vraja-pura-vanitānāṁ vardhayan kāma devam*

**“Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the ultimate resort of all living entities, is glorious. He is said to have been born from the womb of Devakī. He is the guide of the Yadu dynasty, dispelling with His mighty arms everything inauspicious. He destroys the miseries of all living entities, moving and inert. His blissful smiling face always increases the lusty desires of the gopīs of Vṛndāvana and the ladies of Mathurā and Dvārakā cities.”**

In this verse, by using the verb ‘*jayati*’ (“is glorious”) in the present tense, as well as the present participles ‘*vardhayan*’ (“increasing”) and ‘asyan’ (“dispelling”), Śukadeva Gosvāmī implies that Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes in His transcendental abodes of Vṛndāvana, Mathurā, and Dvārakā, are going on eternally.

*Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta* 1.5.461 quotes another *śāstra* that confirms the existence of the unmanifest eternal pastimes in Vṛndāvana:

*kṛṣṇo ‘nyo yadu-sambhūto*  
*yaḥ pūrṇaḥ so ‘sty ataḥ paraḥ*  
*vṛndāvanaṁ parityajya*  
*sa kvacin naiva gacchati*

**“The Kṛṣṇa born among the Yadus is someone other than Kṛṣṇa in His complete form. The original Kṛṣṇa never leaves Vṛndāvana to go anywhere else.”**

Coming back the point about the eight-year tax, there are still others who say that the pastimes of swinging and tax collection are a part of the *prakaṭa-līlā* (pastimes visible in worldly space-time frame). In support of this, they quote *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.18.15:

*kvacic ca dardura-plāvair*  
*vividhair upahāsakaiḥ*  
*kadācit syandolikayā*  
*karhicin nṛpa-ceṣṭayā*

**“They \[cowherd boys along with Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma\] would sometimes jump around like frogs, sometimes play various jokes, sometimes ride in swings and sometimes imitate monarchs.”**

The same party says that *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.2.26 (see footnote 84) rather supports the view that Kṛṣṇa was actually eight years old at the time of the tax pastime. They say that *‘gūḍhārciḥ’* (“covered flame”) is supposed to suggest that Kṛṣṇa stayed in Vraja for eleven years like a covered flame, and after that, like an open flame. This would mean that before Kṛṣṇa turned twelve, His pastimes in Vraja were humanlike, and after turning twelve that He, as an open flame, had openly displayed His power until the age of fifteen(when a common person completes the *kaiśora* age).

In conclusion, an alternative and simpler way to understand why Rādhā demands the tax for eight years is because Kṛṣṇa was eight years old at the time of Her coronation. Thus, Rādhā would imply, “Exacting the tax from them is just, for no one has levied it before My coronation.”

### Verse 393

***vṛndā:*** *(sa-smitam) vṛndāvaneśvari, vṛndaśaḥ prātisvikīr dhavalāḥ pālayatām asaṅkhyānāṁ gosaṅkhyānām ananta eva kānana-karaḥ. tat kiṁ gaṇanā-prayāsena.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-smitam—*with a smile; *vṛndāvana-īśvari—*O queen of Vṛndāvana; *vṛndaśaḥ—*numbering one billion; *prātisvikīḥ—*owned; *dhavalāḥ—*cows; *pālayatām—*tending; *asaṅkhyānām—*for numberless; *go-saṅkhyānām—*cowherds; *anantaḥ—*unlimited; *eva—*certainly; *kānana-karaḥ—*the forest tax; *tat—*therefore; *kim—*what; *gaṇanā—*of counting; *prayāsena—*by the endeavor*.*

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā (smilingly): O queen of Vṛndāvana, the forest tax for numberless cowherds, each tending his own billion of cows, is indeed unlimited. So what is the use trying to calculate it?**

**Комментарий:**

As Rādhā has started acting like the formal queen of Vṛndāvana, Vṛṇdā supportively acts as the queen’s minister, the post given to her at the coronation (text 383b). Vṛndā’s suggests to Rādhā the following: “You have literally purchased Kṛṣṇa and His friends, by confiscating all their property as the tax payment.”

### Verse 394-395

***lalitā:***  *visāhe, vundāvaṇa-cakkavāṭṭanī āṇabedi ‘geṇha haḍheṇa paḍhamaṁ paḍum-maṇṇassa baḍuṇo maṇi-maṇḍaṇaṁ.’*  
***madhumaṅgalaḥ:*** *(apavārya) bho pia-vaassa, ṇiccidaṁ ettha samāhāṇaṁ dukkaraṁ tā palāaṇaṁ ccea amhāṇaṁ saraṇaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*visāhe—*O Viśākhā; *vundāvaṇa-cakkavāṭṭanī—*the empress of Vṛndāvana; *āṇabedi—*orders; *geṇha—*take; *haḍheṇa—*by force; *paḍhamaṁ—*firstly; *paḍummaṇṇassa—*who thinks himself an expert; *baḍuṇo—*of a brāhmaṇa boy; *maṇi-maṇḍaṇaṁ—*the jeweled ornament*. apavārya—*aside; *bho—*O; *pia-vaassa—*dear friend; *ṇiccidaṁ—*certainly; *ettha—*in this matter; *samāhāṇaṁ—*settling; *dukkaraṁ—*hard to make; *tā—*therefore; *palāaṇaṁ—*escape; *ccea—*only; *amhāṇaṁ—*for us; *saraṇaṁ—*shelter*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: Viśākhā, the empress of Vṛndāvana orders to forcibly remove the jeweled ornament from the brāhmaṇa boy who thinks himself very clever.**

**Madhumaṅgala (aside): My dear friend, it is certainly difficult to settle this matter peacefully. The only recourse left for us is to flee from here.**

### Verse 396

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *haṁho goṣṭha-śva, lalitā-laguḍa-tāḍanam āśaṅkya mā saṅkuca. purastād eṣa sudarśano ’smi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*haṁho—*Hey; *goṣṭha-śva—*a cow-pen dog; *lalitā—*of Lalitā; *laguḍa—*with the stick; *tāḍanam—*punishment; *āśaṅkya—*fearing; *mā—*do not; *saṅkuca—*shrink; *purastāt—*in front; *eṣa—*this; *sudarśanaḥ—Sudarśana-cakra*; *asmi—*I am.

**Перевод:**

**Hey cow-pen dog, don’t shrink in fear of being punished by Lalitā’s stick. Here I am, the Sudarśana-cakra, standing in front of the gopīs.**

**Комментарий:**

‘*Su-darśana*,’ if understood literally, means “one with a beautiful appearance.” In this way Kṛṣṇa implies that just as no one can resist the power of *sudarśana-cakra*, the gopīs won’t be able to resist Him, who is the most handsome.

A dog that stays in its master’s cow-pen may appear very brave by barking on everyone passing by. In truth, however, such dogs are cowards, for they at once get afraid by not seeing their master nearby. Kṛṣṇa calls His friend with such a humiliating epithet, and reminds Madhumaṅgala that He is here, and so there is nothing to fear.

### Verse 397

***rādhā:***  *(kṛṣṇam apāṅgena darāliṅgya) suala, alaṁ vilajjideṇa! kāṇaṇa-karo ubaṇīadu!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kṛṣṇam—*Kṛṣṇa; *apāṅgena—*with a sidelong glance; *dara—*gently; *āliṅgya—*embracing; *suala—*O Subala; *alaṁ—*enough; *vilajjideṇa—*of being shameless; *kāṇaṇa-karo—*the forest tax; *ubaṇīadu—*should be brought near*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (gently embracing Kṛṣṇa with a sidelong glance): Subala, stop being so shameless! Submit the forest tax!**

## Whose Forest?

### Verse 398

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(sa-smitam avekṣya)*  
*ekasya tvaṁ prabhur asi sa tu dvādaśānāṁ vanānām*  
*etac cālpaṁ nikhila-jagatī-vartinām eva devaḥ*  
*sāmanta-śrīs tvam iti sa mahā-manmathaś cakravartī*  
*pathyaṁ rādhe śṛṇu haṭham amuṁ mā kṛthās tasya śulke*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-smitam—*with a smile; *avekṣya—*glancing; *ekasya—*of one; *tvam—*You; *prabhuḥ—*the master; *asi—*are; *saḥ—*He; *tu—*however; *dvādaśānām—*of twelve; *vanānām—*forests; *etat—*this; *ca—*and; *alpam—*small thing; *nikhila—*of all; *jagatī—*worlds; *vartinām—*of the inhabitants; *eva—*indeed; *devaḥ—*the Lord; *sāmanta-śrīḥ—*a landlord; *tvam—*You; *iti—*thus; *saḥ—*He; *mahā-manmathaḥ—*Mahā-manmatha; *cakravartī—*the emperor; *pathyam—*the proper thing; *rādhe—*O Rādhā; *śṛṇu—*listen; *haṭham—*pressure; *amum—*that; *mā—*do not; *kṛthāḥ—*do; *tasya—*to him; *śulke—*in the matter of paying the tax.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (glancing with a smile): You are the master of but one forest—He is the master of twelve. And this is to say the least, for He is the Lord of all creatures in the whole world. You are only a vassal, but He is the great emperor Mahā-manmatha. Therefore, O Rādhā, please listen to what is the best for You: do not dare to force the emperor pay the tax.**

**Комментарий:**

There are twelve forests in the land of Vraja. Vṛndāvana, although the biggest, is but one of the twelve.

### Verse 399

***viśākhā:*** *bho suala, evvaṁ hodu. tadhābi tassa ujjāṇa-cakkavaṭṭiṇo ṇideśa-vaṭṭiṇā tuha ghaṭṭa-jjhakkheṇa vundāvaṇa-cakkavaṭṭiṇīe amha-pia-sahīe kāṇaṇa-karo kadhaṁ moa{i}davvo?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bho suala—*O dear Subala; *evvaṁ—*like this; *hodu—*let it be; *tadhābi—*still; *tassa—*of that; *ujjāṇa-cakkavaṭṭiṇo—*grove emperor; *ṇideśa-vaṭṭiṇā—*by the order carrier; *tuha—*your; *ghaṭṭa-jjhakkheṇa—*toll gate supervisor; *vundāvaṇa-cakkavaṭṭiṇīe—*of the empress of Vṛndāvana; *amha—*our; *pia-sahīe—*dear sakhī; *kāṇaṇa-karo—*the forest tax; *kadhaṁ—*how; *moa{i}davvo—*could be released.

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā: Dear Subala, let it be so. But there is still a question: how will your toll gate supervisor, the emperor’s order carrier, will be absolved from the forest tax of our dear sakhī, the empress of Vṛndāvana?**

### Verse 400

***subalaḥ:*** *(sābhyasūyam) visāhe, ṇiccidaṁ khaṭṭārūḍhāsi jaṁ samūḍha-dummada-ghummidā tumaṁ tattaṁ ajāṇantī palabasi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-abhyasūyam—*with indignation; *visāhe—*O Viśākhā; *ṇiccidaṁ—*certainly; *khaṭṭārūḍhā—*presumptuous; *asi—*you are; *jaṁ—*because; *samūḍha—*accumulated; *dummada—*with pride; *ghummidā—*dizzy; *tumaṁ—*you; *tattaṁ—*the truth; *ajāṇantī—*not knowing; *palabasi—*are talking*.*

**Перевод:**

**Subala (indignantly): Viśākhā, you are dizzy due to your thick arrogance and are blabbering on and on, not knowing the truth. You are indeed presumptuous…**

**Комментарий:**

The word *‘khaṭṭārūḍha’* (presumptuous) is derived from *‘khaṭṭām ārūḍha,’* or “one who has climbed a bed.” In his translation of *Siddhānta-kaumudī* (2.1.26)*,* Śrīśa Chandra Vasu explains the usage of this word: “All *brahmacārīs* were bound to sleep on the ground and not on *khaṭvā* or beds, so long as they were *brahmacārīs.* A person who without completing his studies, and without obtaining the permission of his teacher, entered into matrimony, was originally called, in reproach, *khaṭvārūḍhaḥ* (“one who ascended the bed in an improper way”). Then the term was extended to all persons guilty of vile action.”

### Verse 401-402

***viśākhā:*** *(sotprāsa-smitam) kiṁ ettha tattam? kadhehi suṇissaṁ.*  
***subalaḥ:*** *kiṁ vitthareṇa? saṁkkhitta-sāraṁ suṇāhi: jo kkhu mahā-mammaha-cakkavaṭṭī so jevva ṇiccidaṁ pia-vaassa-rūbeṇa vaṭṭanto jāṇīadu. doṇaṁ kira paramatthado bhiṇṇadā ṇatthi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa—*with; *utprāsa-smitam—*a wild laughter; *kiṁ—*what; *ettha—*in this regard; *tattam—*the truth; *kadhehi—*speak; *suṇissaṁ—*I will listen. *kim—*what; *vitthareṇa—*by elaboration; *saṁkkhitta—*abridged; *sāraṁ—*essence; *suṇāhi—*listen; *jo—*who; *kkhu—*indeed; *mahā-mammaha-cakkavaṭṭī—*the emperor Mahā-manmatha; *so—*He; *jevva—*only; *ṇiccidaṁ—*certainly; *pia-vaassa—*of our dear friend; *rūbeṇa—*in the form; *vaṭṭanto—*is present; *jāṇīadu—*should be known; *doṇaṁ—*of the both; *kira—*indeed; *parama-atthado—*in the ultimate sense; *bhiṇṇadā—*difference; *ṇatthi—*there is no*.*

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā (wildly laughing): What is that truth? Speak on, I am listening.**

**Subala: What is the use of speaking too much? Just hear the gist in brief: His Majesty Mahā-manmantha has indeed assumed the form of our dear friend. Ultimately, there is no difference between the two.**

**Комментарий:**

His Majesty Mahā-manmatha fully trusts Kṛṣṇa, his representative in collecting taxes. They are non-different from this practical, administrative way, and this is what Viśākhā is supposed to understand. On the other hand, Subala’s statement is philosophically significant. Kṛṣṇa is non-different from Mahā-manmatha, or “Cupid the Great,” for this amorous side of Kṛṣṇa’s personality is the function of His internal potency, which is one with its source. This is the explanation of Viśvanātha Cakravartī.

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.32.2 states:

*tāsām āvirabhūc chauriḥ*  
*smayamāna-mukhāmbujaḥ*  
*pītāmbara-dharaḥ sragvī*  
*sākṣān manmatha-manmathaḥ*

**“Then Lord Kṛṣṇa, a smile on His lotus face, appeared before the gopīs. Wearing a garland and a yellow garment, He directly appeared as one who can bewilder the mind of Cupid, who himself bewilders the minds of ordinary people.”**

The business of Cupid is to enchant person’s mind through sensual attraction. Kṛṣṇa, however, does this to the highest degree. He is attractive to all, including Cupid and even Himself. So Kṛṣṇa is the original, transcendental Cupid, as stated in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, Madhya 8.138-139:

*vṛndāvane ‘aprākṛta navīna madana’*  
*kāma-gāyatrī kāma-bīje yāṅra upāsana*  
*puruṣa, yoṣit, kibā sthāvara-jaṅgama*  
*sarva-cittākarṣaka, sākṣāt manmatha-madana*

**“In the spiritual realm of Vṛndāvana, Kṛṣṇa is the spiritual, ever-fresh Cupid. He is worshiped by the chanting of the** ***Kāma-gāyatrī*** **mantra, with the spiritual seed** ***klīm*****. The very name Kṛṣṇa means that He attracts even Cupid. He is therefore attractive to everyone—male and female, moving and inert living entities. Indeed, Kṛṣṇa is known as the all-attractive one.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda explains in the purport: “Because Kṛṣṇa appears very much sexually inclined, He is called the new Cupid in the spiritual world. There is no material inebriety in such desire, however.”

### Verse 403

***arjunaḥ:***  *visāhe, idaṁ bi thoaṁ ccea. tā suṇāhi: so kira assuda-ara-sāhammo sammohaṇa-māhurī-bhara-ṇavvo savvobari virehanto pia-vaassassa saala-goula-ba{i}ttaṇeṇa goindāhisea-mahūsavo kassa vā gavvaṁ ṇa kkhu khavvedi?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*visāhe—*O Viśākhā; *idaṁ—*this; *bi—*even; *thoaṁ—*a small matter; *ccea—*only; *tā—*therefore; *suṇāhi—*hear; *so—*that; *kira—*indeed; *assuda—*with what is unheard of; *ara—*previously; *sāhammo—*having the sameness; *sammohaṇa-māhurī—*of fascinating sweetness; *bhara—*with the abundance; *ṇavvo—*fresh; *savva-ubari—*above all; *virehanto—*reigning; *pia-vaassassa—*of our dear friend; *saala—*of the whole; *goula—*Gokula; *ba{i}ttaṇeṇa—*as the Lord; *goinda-ahisea—*of Govinda-abhiṣeka; *mahūsavo—*the great festival; *kassa—*whose; *vā—*indeed; *gavvaṁ—*pride; *ṇa—*not; *kkhu—*indeed; *khavvedi—*will diminish.

**Перевод:**

**Arjuna: And even that is a trifle, Viśākhā. Hear this: Whose pride will not diminish the unprecedented great festival of Govinda-abhiṣeka, when our dear friend was coronated as the Lord of the whole Gokula? This festival was novel with the abundance of fascinating sweetness and it outstanded all other celebrations.**

**Комментарий:**

This is a response from the cowherd boys to the gopīs who claimed Rādhārāṇī the queen of Vrindāvana. ‘After all,’ they say, ‘Kṛṣṇa is the king.’

Kṛṣṇa’s coronation happened after the pastime of lifting Govardhana hill, when Kṛṣṇa saved all the Vrajavāsīs from the devastating rainfall arrnged by furious Indra. When Indra understood his mistake, he remorsefully approached Kṛṣṇa in a secluded place and begged forgiveness. At that time he brought Surabhi, the heavenly mother of the cows, who bathed Kṛṣṇa with her milk. Indra himself bathed Kṛṣṇa with the waters of celestial Gaṅgā brought by Indra’s elephant Airāvata. In this way, on the request of mother Surabhi, who was frustrated with her current master who had offended the Lord, Kṛṣṇa became the new Indra (king) of the cows, and the name Govinda, signifying this fact, was conferred upon Him.

It is all right that Kṛṣṇa is the Lord of the cows, but how did it make Him the Lord of Gokula? Arjuna, who now tries his best to protect the interests of the head tax collector, may argue that if Kṛṣṇa is the Lord of the cows, He autimatically becomes the Lord of the place where the cows stay (*go-kula*, “a cow pen”). After all, this is the literal meaning of the word Gokula, apart from its being the name of Nanda Mahrājā’s village, which is full of cows.

### Verse 404

***madhumaṅgalaḥ:***  *(sollāsam) hanta lalide, suṭṭhu bhaṇādi ajjuṇo jaṁ uaṇisadāhiṁ vaṇaṁ kkhu edaṁ kaṇha-vaṇaṁ vaṇṇijja{i}.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-ullāsam—*with joy; *hanta—*alas; *lalide—*O Lalitā; *suṭṭhu—*well; *bhaṇādi—*speaks; *ajjuṇo—*Arjuna; *jaṁ—*because; *uaṇisadāhim—*by Upaniṣads; *vaṇaṁ—*forest; *kkhu—*indeed; *edaṁ—*this; *kaṇha-vaṇaṁ—*as Kṛṣṇa’s forest; *vaṇṇijja{i}—*is described*.*

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala (buoyantly): Too bad, Lalitā. Arjuna speaks right: Upaniṣads indeed describe this forest as Kṛṣṇa-vana.**

**Комментарий:**

*Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad* 2.31 describes how the twelve forests of Vraja are divided: *‘dve vane staḥ kṛṣṇa-vanaṁ bhadra-vanam. tayor antar dvādaśa vanāni puṇyāni puṇyatamāni,’*—“There are two forests—Kṛṣṇavana and Bhadravana. Within these two forests there are other twelve, some of which are holy and some the most holy.” In their commentaries to this statement, Prabodhānanda Sarasvatī and Jīva Gosvāmī explain that the four forests—Bhadravana, Śrīvana, Lohavana, and Bṛhadvana—are included within Bhadravana (the forest of Balarāma, not to be confused with one of the twelve forests by the same name, that is a part of this bigger Bhadravana). The other eight forests—Bhāṇḍīravana, Madhuvana, Tālavana, Kāmyavana, Bahulāvana, Kumudavana, Khadiravana, and Vṛndāvana—are included within Kṛṣṇavana (the forest of Kṛṣṇa). Viśvanātha Cakravartī includes Bṛhadvana in Kṛṣṇavana and Bhāṇḍīravana in Bhadravana.

So, according to this authoritative evidence from the scripture, Vṛndāvana is a part of Kṛṣṇavana, the forest of Kṛṣṇa.

## The Strange Ways Of His Majesty

### Verse 405

***vṛndā:*** *niradhāri pūrva-parayor*  
*nyāya-vidagdhaiḥ para-vidhir balavān*  
*rājani nave ’bhiṣikte*  
*purātane kasya vā gaṇanā*

**Пословный перевод:**

*niradhāri—*was ascertained; *pūrva—*between previous; *parayoḥ—*and later; *nyāya—*of logic; *vidagdhaiḥ—*by the experts; *para-vidhiḥ—*later rule; *balavān—*stronger; *rājani—*when a king; *nave—*new; *abhiṣikte—*is coronated; *purātane—*in the matter of old; *kasya—*whose; *vā—*indeed; *gaṇanā—*consideration.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: The expert logicians have ascertained that between two rules the later one is stronger. Likewise, when a new ruler is installed, who cares for the old one?**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa’s coronational bathing happened right after the pastime of lifting Govardhana hill, and Kṛṣṇa was seven years old at that time. According to Vṛndā’s present statement it is clear that Rādhārāṇī’s coronation happened after Kṛṣṇa’s, and thus Kṛṣṇa has no claim over Vṛndāvana forest from that point of time. Vṛndā supports her conclusion with the widely known principle of logic that the later injunction is stronger than the previous one.

### Verse 406

***madhumaṅgalaḥ:***  *muñcadha vāālattaṇaṁ. amha-pia-vaassassa ccea kantārāhīsadā. ado kara-hārīhiṁ tumhehiṁ rāa-ula-purisā amhe jhatti khaṇḍa-kuṇḍaliāhiṁ sammāṇijjamha.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*muṣṇadha—*give up; *vāālattaṇaṁ—*talkativeness; *amha—*of our; *pia-vaassassa—*dear friend; *ccea—*only; *kantāra—*over the forest; *ahīsadā—*the presidency; *ado—*therefore; *kara-hārīhiṁ—*who withhold the tax; *tumhehiṁ—*you; *rāa-ula—*royal; *purisā—*people; *amhe—*we; *jhatti—*quickly; *khaṇḍa-kuṇḍaliāhiṁ—*with sugar candies in the form of a coil; *sammāṇijjamha—*we will be honored*.*

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala: Stop talking a lot! Our dear friend is the sole sovereign of this forest. And so you, who are trying to escape paying the tax, must quickly honor us, the royal entourage, with some jalebis.**

**Комментарий:**

Another meaning of the second sentence: “Our dear friend is the sole sovereign (*īsadā*) of His beloved (*kantā*) Rādhā (*rāhī*).”

### Verse 407

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *sakhe subala, śyāmala-maṇḍapikāṁ maṇḍaya yad atra sāmprataṁ śulka-dāsikāḥ praveśanīyāḥ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakhe subala—*O friend Subala; *śyāmala—*dark; *maṇḍapikām—*the pavilion; *maṇḍaya—*decorate; *yat—*because; *atra—*here; *sāmpratam—*now; *śulka—*the tax payment; *dāsikāḥ—*the maidservants; *praveśanīyāḥ—*should be brought inside*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: My friend Subala, decorate the dark pavilion. These purchased maidservants should be taken there now.**

**Комментарий:**

The dark pavilion Kṛṣṇa is speaking about here is the place where He was hiding before intercepting the gopīs (see text 39).

### Verse 408

***rādhikā:*** *hanta takkara-cakkabaṭṭi-sāmanta suala, maha pia-sahīe sāmalāe vvada-veī sāmala-maṇḍabiā esā kīsa tumhehiṁ ghaṭṭa-ghaḍaṇeṇa dūsijja{i}?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*hanta—*alas; *takkara-cakkabaṭṭo—*of the emperor of thieves; *sāmanta—*the general; *suala—*O Subala; *maha—*of my; *pia-sahīe—*dear friend; *sāmalāe—*Śyāmalā; *vvada—*for sacred observances; *veī—*the altar; *sāmala-maṇḍabiā—*dark pavilion; *esā—*this; *kīsa—*why; *tumhehiṁ—*by you; *ghaṭṭa—*toll station; *ghaḍaṇeṇa—*by the disturbance; *dūsijja{i}—*is polluted.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhikā: Alas, o Subala, the general of the emperor of thieves! For what reason do you desecrate the dark pavilion by turning it into this nonsence toll station? It belongs to my dear Śyāmalā sakhī, and is the site her various sacred observances.**

**Комментарий:**

Here Rādhā boldly states that the dark (*sāmala*) pavilion where Kṛṣṇa wants to forcibly take the gopīs to, belongs to Her friend Śyāmalā (*sāmalā*). *Dvārakā-māhātmya* of *Skanda Purāṇa* mentions Śyāmalā as one of the most prominent eight gopīs. She belongs to the gopīs of the *suhṛt-pakṣa* (a party friendly to Rādhā’s wing), and is sometimes referred to as Śyāmā.

### Verse 409

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *vakrāṇāṁ cakravartini, kṛtaṁ rādhā-cakrasya caṅkramaṇayā yad eṣa tarasā durlakṣyaṁ mano ’pi bhindatā dhanvināṁ mūrdhanyena mahā-manmathenādhiṣṭhito mahā-ghaṭṭa-raṅgaḥ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vakrāṇām—*of the cunning; *cakravartini—*O empress; *kṛtam—*enough of; *rādhā-cakrasya—*of the wheel of Rādhā’s chariot; *caṅkramaṇayā—*the rotation; *yat—*because; *eṣaḥ—*this; *tarasā—*with speed; *durlakṣyam—*hard to notice; *manaḥ—*mind; *api—*even; *bhindatā—*by the destroyer; *dhanvinām—*of the wealthy; *mūrdhanyena—*the topmost; *mahā-manmathena—*Mahā-manmatha; *adhiṣṭhitaḥ—*governed; *mahā—*vast; *ghaṭṭa-raṅgaḥ—*the toll station ground*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O empress of the crooked, the time has come for the wheels of Rādhā’s royal chariot to stop circling around, because the vast ground of this toll station is now presided over by the wealthiest Mahā-manmatha. He pierces even the mind, whose ways are hard to spot even if one is very brisk.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa addreses Rādhā as *‘vakrāṇāṁ cakravartini’* (“the empress of the crooked”). The word *‘cakra-vartin’* denotes the emperor, for it literally means “one who rolls (*vartin*) the wheels (*cakra*),” suggesting the unobstructed movements of the emperor’s chariot all over the earth.

Kṛṣṇa’s words imply the following: “If Mahā-manmatha (Cupid, who is Kṛṣṇa) can pierce even the flickering mind with his arrows, it will certainly not be difficult for him to pierce the rotating wheels of the chariot.” In other words, Kṛṣṇa threatens to subdue Rādhā and Her rulership claims with His amorous powers.

### Verse 410

***rādhikā:***  *(saṁskṛtena)*  
*vakras tridhā tvam ādau*  
*madhye cānte ca vaṁśikā-rasika*  
*kala-kṛta-jagatī-pralayo*  
*vakreśvara eva devo ’si*

**Пословный перевод:**

*saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *vakraḥ—*crooked; *tridhā—*in three places; *tvam—*You; *ādau—*in the beginning; *madhye—*middle; *ca—*and; *ante—*end; *ca—*and; *vaṁśikā-rasika—*O flute conoisseur; *kala—*with sweet notes; *kṛta—*performing; *jagatī—*of the world; *pralayaḥ—*destruction; *vakreśvaraḥ—*Vakreśvara (Śiva); *eva—*certainly; *devaḥ—*the lord; *asi—*You are.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhikā (in Sanskrit): O flute conoisseur, You are bent in three places: down, in the middle, and on the top, and you destroy the world with Your sweet flute music. You are indeed Lord Vakreśvara!**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā says, “You call me ‘the empress of the crooked,’ but just look at Yourself: You are Lord Vakreśvara (literally, ‘the crooked God’)!” and She describes Kṛṣṇa as a flute connoisseur (*vaṁśikā-rasika*), whose body is bent in three places—knees, waist, and neck—when He plays His flute.

On the other hand, Vakreśvara is the name of a famous *śiva-liṅga*, meaning “the lord of the crooked one (Aṣṭāvakra Muni).” Rādhārāṇī compares Kṛṣṇa to Vakreśvara (Śiva) in His ability to show affection. Once in Satya-yuga, Aṣṭāvakra Muni came to Kāśī (Vārāṇasī) for worshipping Lord Śiva. The sage was told, however, that Lord Śiva won’t accept his worship unless he performed it in a spot known as Gupta Kāśī (“hidden Kāśī”) in the land of Gauḍa (Bengal). The sage then travelled eastward to that place and worshipped Lord Śiva, who, pleased with Aṣṭāvakra’s devotion, promised to grant his blessings to those who worship Aṣṭāvakra Muni before worshiping him. The heavenly architect Viśvakarma then constructed a temple at that spot.

Like Lord Vakreśvara (Śiva), Kṛṣṇa also destroys the universe. Kṛṣṇa’s destruction of the universe, however, is special. *‘Jagatī’* can refer not only to the world around us, but also to the living entities inhabiting it. So, for those who are fortunate to hear Kṛṣṇa’s flute, He destroys not only their material bodies and the world aroung them, but their whole material existence. Or else, taking other meanings of the word *‘pralaya,’* those fortunate souls turn devastatedby the transcendental ecstasy of hearing that sweet music, or their minds become fully absorbedin it.

Another reading of the third line is *‘kala-kṛta-jagatī-praṇayo,’* which means “with Your sweet flute music You show Your love (*praṇayo*) for the world.”

### Verse 411

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(kiñcid vihasya)*  
*vāci kace bhruvi dṛṣṭau*  
*smite prayāṇe ’vaguṇṭhane hṛdi ca*  
*tvām ity aṣṭasu vakrām*  
*aṣṭāvakrāyitāṁ vande*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kiñcit—*slightly; *vihasya—*laughing; *vāci—*in speech; *kace—*in hair; *bhruvi—*in eyebrows; *dṛṣṭau—*in glance; *smite—*in smile; *prayāṇe—*in movements; *avaguṇṭhe—*in covering with a veil; *hṛdi—*in heart; *ca—*and; *tvām—*You; *iti—*thus; *aṣṭasu—*in eight; *vakrām—*bent; *aṣṭāvakrāyitām—*One who acts like Aṣṭāvakra Muni; *vande—*I praise.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (laughing softly): You are crooked in eight places: Your speech, hair, eyebrows, glance, smile, movements, veiling, and heart. I thus praise You, who behave like Aṣṭāvakra Muni.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa retorts: “You call me crooked, but look at Your own self!” He outsmarts Rādhā by noticing in Her not just three, but eight twists. “All right, as you say, I am Lord Vakreśvara. You are then Aṣṭāvakra, my devotee. Just look at Yourself!”

Rādhā is “not straight” from eight angles: She speaks in a roundabout way, Her hair is wavy, Her eyebrows and smile are of a curved shape, She glances sideways, Her movements are…, She covers Her face with a veil, and finally, She doesn’t easily open Her heart .

### Verse 412-414

***campakalatā:*** *alakkha-vaṅkimābi tumaṁ lakkha-vaṅkimāsi. tā appaṇo samāṇa-dhammiṇā jaṇeṇa kīlehi. visuddha-dhammāṇaṁ amhāṇaṁ ido juttā gadī.*  
***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *puṇyavati, mahā-dānaṁ vinā gatir durlabhā.*  
***campakalatā:*** *santa-jaṇāṇaṁ savva-padhīṇā gadī pasiddhā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*alakkha—*unnoticed; *vaṅkimā—*who crookedness; *abi—*though; *tumaṁ—*You; *lakkha-vaṅkimā—*who has hundred thousand crooks; *asi—*are; *tā—*therefore; *appaṇo—*Your own; *samāṇa—*same; *dhammiṇā—*whose nature; *jaṇeṇa—*with a person; *kīlehi—*play; *visuddha—*pure; *dhammāṇaṁ—*whose nature; *amhāṇaṁ—*for us; *ido—*from here; *juttā—*proper; *gadī—*the going. *puṇyavati—*O pious one; *mahā-dānam—*the big tax; *vinā—*without; *gati-durlabhā. santa-jaṇāṇaṁ—*for honest people; *savva-padhīṇā—*on all roads; *gadī—*the movement; *pasiddhā—*well known*.*

**Перевод:**

**Campakalatā: Even though Your crookedness is unnoticed, You have thousands of crooks. Therefore, You go and play with someone of the same feather. We are pure-hearted, and so we better go from here.**

**Kṛṣṇa: O pious girl, it will be difficult for you to go without paying the big tax.**

**Campakalatā: Everyone knows that honest people can travel by any road.**

### Verse 415

***citrā:*** *purisuttama, puṇṇa-siloo si. tā dhamma-kamma-pa{u}ttāṇaṁ amhāṇaṁ kuṇa mokkhaṇaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*purisuttama—*O Puruṣottama; *puṇṇa-siloo—*glorified with auspicious verses; *si—*You are; *tā—*therefore; *dhamma-kamma—*in religious activities; *pauttāṇam—*engaged; *amhāṇam—*for us; *kuṇa—*do; *mokkhaṇam—*the deliverance*.*

**Перевод:**

**Citrā: O Supreme Person, since You are glorified with auspicious verses, please deliver us, who tread the path of religious activities!**

**Комментарий:**

The gopīs are carrying ghee to the sacrifice. This is undoubtedly a religious activity (*dharma-karma*).

### Verse 416-417

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *citre, vicitreyam asya cakravartinaḥ prakriyā yatra dharmeṇa durlabho mokṣaḥ. kintu kāma-prayogeṇa dhruvaṁ labhyate.*  
***nāndīmukhī:*** *sattha-ārāṇaṁ muṇīṇaṁ bi avisaṁvādiṇī esā rīdī jaṁ ede kāmassa aṇantaraṁ ccea mokkhaṁ paḍhanti. dhammaṁ kira dūrado paḍhama-kakkhārohaṇe.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*citre—*O Citrā; *vicitrā—*strange; *iyam—*this; *asya—*of this; *cakravartinaḥ—*emperor; *prakriyā—*the manner; *yatra—*in which; *dharmeṇa—*by dharma; *durlabhaḥ—*hard to obtain; *mokṣaḥ—*liberation; *kintu—*but; *kāma-prayogeṇa—*by the employment of kāma; *dhruvam—*for sure; *labhyate—*is obtained. *sattha-ārāṇaṁ—*authors of *śāstras*; *muṇīṇaṁ—*for sages; *bi—*also; *avisaṁvādiṇī—*not contradictory; *esā—*this; *rīdī—*manner; *jaṁ—*because; *ede—*they; *kāmassa—*kāma; *aṇantaraṁ—*after; *ccea—*only; *mokkhaṁ—*mokṣa; *paḍhanti—*study; *dhammaṁ—*dharma; *kira—*indeed; *dūrado—*far; *paḍhama-kakkhā—*to the first position; *arohaṇe—*in elevating.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O Citrā, the ways of His Majesty are very strange. In dealings with him it is hard to attain liberation by following dharma. Liberation is rather obtained by engaging in kāma.**

**Nāndīmukhī: This path does not contradict even the sages who compile śāstras, because they study about mokṣa only after passing through the stage of kāma. Indeed, dharma is far from promoting one to the first-class situation in mokṣa.**

**Комментарий:**

The generally prescribed by the Vedas way of liberation from material bondage has four consequent stages: *dharma* (religious duty), *artha* (economic development), *kāma* (sense enjoyment), and finally *mokṣa* (liberation). Nāndīmukhī fortifies Kṛṣṇa’s statement by logic: if we look at the order of the four stages, *kāma* is in immidiate proximity to *mokṣa*, unlike *dharma*, which is on the “opposite end.”

### Verse 418-419

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(smitvā rādhāṁ paśyan) hanta śulka-krīti, prītir eva nāthasya tavādya gatiḥ. tad ānandaya mahā-dānīndram enam abhīṣṭa-sevayā!*  
***lalitā:*** *mohaṇa, bhūriṇā tabo-hareṇa ccea ghaṭṭī-pālassa dāsīttaṇaṁ sijjhadi. edāe uṇa maha-sahīe taṁ dullahaṁ jaṁ atabassiṇī esā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smitvā—*smiling; *rādhām—*at Rādhā; *paśyan—*looking; *hanta—*alas; *śulka—*for the toll; *krīti—*purchased; *prītiḥ—*love; *eva—*only; *nāthasya—*of the master; *tava—*Your; *adya—*today; *gatiḥ—*destination; *tat—*therefore; *ānandaya—*gladden; *mahā-dānīndram—*the chief of tax collectors; *enam—*this; *abhīṣṭa—*with desired; *sevayā—*service*. mohaṇa—*O trickster; *bhūriṇā—*abundant; *tabo—*of austerity; *hareṇa—*weight; *ccea—*only; *ghaṭṭī-pālassa—*to the tollgate man; *dāsīttaṇaṁ—*the slavery; *sijjhadi—*is accomplished; *edāe—*this; *uṇa—*again; *maha—*my; *sahīe—*for sakhī; *taṁ—*that; *dullahaṁ—*hard to achieve; *jaṁ—*because; *atabassiṇī—*not austere; *esā—*She*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (smiling and looking at Rādhā) :O girl purchased in place of the tax! Sorry, but today Your destination is nothing but the pleasure of My master. So gladden Me, the chief of His tax collectors with a service that He covets the most!**

**Lalitā: O tricky boy, servitorship to the tax collector is attained only after heaps of austerities, and so, it is unattainable for my sakhī. She is not at all austere.**

### Verse 420-421

***nāndīmukhī:*** *ṇiuñja-līlā-kuñjarinda, lalidā bhaṇādi ‘solukkieṇa tue ccea seā-ulehiṁ ubāsaṇijjā amha-sahī jaṁ esā saala-jovvaṇavadī-maṇḍala-cakkavaṭṭiṇī.’ tā imiṇā vibarīdeṇa alaṁ jappideṇa.*  
***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(sa-harṣam) nāndīmukhi, duratikrameyaṁ lalitā-kṛtājñā. tad eva sevitu-kāmaḥ prathamaṁ hṛdayaṅgame śātakumbha-kumbhe pañca-śākha-pallavam arpayāmi. (iti rādhām āsādayati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ṇiuñja-līlā—*of grove pastimes; *kuñjara-inda—*the king of elephants; *lalidā—*Lalitā; *bhaṇādi—*is saying; *solukkieṇa—*the tax superintendant; *tue—*by You; *ccea—*only; *seā-ulehiṁ—*with many services; *ubāsaṇijjā—*worshipable; *amha-sahī—*our sakhī; *jaṁ—*because; *esā—*She; *saala—*of all; *jovvaṇavadī—*young girls; *maṇḍala—*of the assembly; *cakkavaṭṭiṇī—*the empress; *tā—*therefore; *imiṇā—*of this; *vibarīdeṇa—*contrary; *alaṁ—*enough; *jappideṇa—*talking*. sa-harṣam—*with joy; *nāndīmukhi—*O Nāndīmukhī; *duratikramā—*hard ot transgress; *iyam—*this; *lalitā-kṛtā—*made by Lalitā; *ājñā—*order; *tat—*therefore; *eva—*only; *sevitu-kāmaḥ—*desiring to serve; *prathamam—*firstly; *hṛdayam-game—*on the charming; *śātakumbha—*golden; *kumbhe—*pot; *pañca-śākha—*five-branched; *pallavam—*twig; *arpayāmi—*I shall place; *iti—*thus; *rādhām—*Rādhā; *āsādayati—*approaches.

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: O chief elephant of the amorous sports in the groves, what Lalitā means to say is this: ‘It is You, the tax superintendant, who should worship our sakhī with various services, for She is the empress of all young girls.’ And no need of saying anything contrary to this.**

**Kṛṣṇa (buoyantly): O Nāndīmukhī, Lalitā’s order is hard to neglect. I wish to serve Rādhā! So first of all let me place the five-branched creeper on that attractive golden pot. (coming near Rādhā)**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa wants to touch Rādhārāṇī’s breast with his hand.

### Verse 422-423

***lalitā:***  *(sa-bhrū-bhaṅgam upakramya) ṇāara-mmaṇṇa, jhilladu esā dullīladā-vallarī.*  
***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *kṛpaṇe, vipaṇāyitāsau sva-vaśā bhavatyā śulkena. tad atra ‘vikrīte kariṇi kim aṅkuśe vivādaḥ?’ (iti mandaṁ mandaṁ padaṁ spandayati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-bhrū-bhaṅgam—*with a frown; *upakramya—*coming near; *ṇāara-mmaṇṇa—*O One who thinks himself a sophisticated person; *jhilladu—*may rest; *esā—*this; *dullīladā—*of mischief; *vallarī—*creeper*. kṛpaṇe—*O miserly girl; *vipaṇāyitā—*is sold; *asau—*this; *sva-vaśā—*dependent One; *bhavatyā—*by You; *śulkena—*with a fee; *tat—*thus; *atra—*in this matter; *vikrīte—*is purchased; *kariṇi—*an elephant; *kim—*what; *aṅkuśe—*about a goad; *vivādaḥ—*the argument; *iti—*thus; *mandam mandam—*very slowly; *padam—*His foot; *spandayati—*shakes.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (coming close to Kṛṣṇa and frowning): You, who think Yourself to be very smart, put the creeper of Your mischief at rest.**

**Kṛṣṇa: O miser, You have given away this subordinate to you girl as the tax. So, as the proverb goes, ‘why quarreling about the goad when the elephant has already been purchased?’ (tapping very slowly with His foot)**

**Комментарий:**

If one has sold an elephant, one cannot hold the goad back. In other words, if one has given away a property, one cannot hold on to things that naturally go along with the property, or argue about how the property is to be used. Quoting the proverb, Kṛṣṇa conveys the following: “Why do you interfere when I want to touch what you have already sold to Me?” Kṛṣṇa has chosen the appropriate proverb, because another meaning of *‘sva-vaśā’* (“the girl subordinate to you”) is “a female elephant.”

### Verse 424-425

***lalitā:*** *kaṇha, aṇahiṇṇo ṇāsi lalidā-dullāliccāṇaṁ. tā kiṁtti appaṇo māhappaṁ pekkhāviduṁ pa{u}ttāsi?*  
***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *suvīram-manye, paśya vikramiṇāṁ cakravartī purastād eva caṅkramīti. tad alaṁ klība-bhaṅga-kāriṇā kṛtrima-bhujaṅgamenaivāmunā mudhāṭopa-taraṅgeṇa. tūrṇaṁ viśrāṇaya ghaṭṭa-śulkāni!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kaṇha—*O Kṛṣṇa; *aṇahiṇṇo—*unaware; *ṇāsi—*You are not; *lalidā—*of Lalitā; *dullāliccāṇaṁ—*of rude ways; *tā—*therefore; *kiṁtti—*why; *appaṇo—*Your; *māhappaṁ—*glory; *pekkhāviduṁ—*to show; *pa{u}ttāsi—*You are proceeding. *suvīram-manye—*O one who thinks herself very brave; *paśya—*look; *vikramiṇām—*of the mighty; *cakravartī—*the emperor; *purastāt—*in front; *eva—*only; *caṅkramīti—*is walking about; *tat—*thus; *alam—*enough; *klība—*weaklings; *bhaṅga-kāriṇā—*which brakes; *kṛtrima—*artificial; *bhujaṅgamena—*snake; *eva—*only; *amunā—*of this; *mudhā—*vain; *āṭopa—*of arrogance; *taraṅgeṇa—*wave; *tūrṇam—*quickly; *viśrāṇaya—*submit; *ghaṭṭa-śulkāni—*the fees of the toll station*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: O Kaṇha, You aren’t unaware of Lalitā’s rude manners. And if so, why are You trying to show off?**

**Kṛṣṇa: O girl who think youself very brave, look: the emperor of the mighty heroes is strolling right in front of you. So stop gushing forth the waves of your vain arrogance, which is like a fake snake that can scare only weaklings. Pay the tax at once!**

### Verse 426

***lalitā:*** *hanta ghaṭṭī-ghaṇṭā-ghosaṇa, ja{i} sulukka-ggahe dīhāggaho si tadā sañjhā-osare amha-duāraṁ āacchehi suṭṭhu ghaṇaṁ gholaṁ dāissamha.*  
*(kṛṣṇaḥ smitaṁ kṛtvā nāndīmukhī-mukham avalokate)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*hanta—*alas; *ghaṭṭī-ghaṇṭā—*of the toll gate bell; *ghosaṇa—*O ringer; *jai—*if; *sulukka-ggahe—*in exacting the tax; *dīha-āggaho—*persistent; *si—*You are; *tadā—*then; *sañjhā-osare—*at dusk; *amha-duāraṁ—*to our door; *āacchehi—*come; *suṭṭhu—*well; *ghaṇaṁ—*thick; *gholaṁ—*cream; *dāissamha—*I will give; *kṛṣṇaḥ—*Kṛṣṇa; *smitam kṛtvā—*smiling; *nāndīmukhī-mukham—*at Nāndīmukhī’s face; *avalokate—*looks.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: Alas, o toll gate bellman, if You are so persistent to get Your tax, then come to my door at dusk and I shall give You lots of fat cream.**

**(Kṛṣṇa smiles and looks at Nāndīmukhī’s face.)**

**Комментарий:**

This is not an invitation for a cream party, but a slap in the face. The cream Lalitā is talking about is a fatty residue that in the end of the day forms on the walls of a churning pot. This “cream” is a by-product that is usually distributed to the beggars or servants. Lalitā is saying: ‘Come at dusk, for even though nobody would take the stale “cream” at that time, you will like a lot its sour taste with some salt.’

### Verse 427

***nāndīmukhī:*** *lalide, kāma-heṇu-vinda-ba{i}ṇo ballavindassa ghare kiṁ ghaṇaṁ gholaṁ bi ṇatthi jaṁ tassa kide tumhāṇaṁ ghare gantavvaṁ?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*lalide—*O Lalitā; *kāma-heṇu—*of wish-fulfilling cows; *vinda—*of the multitude; *ba{i}ṇo—*the master; *ballava-indassa—*of the cowherd king; *ghare—*in the house; *kiṁ—*whether; *ghaṇaṁ—*thick; *gholaṁ—*cream; *bi—*even; *ṇatthi—*there is no; *jaṁ—*that; *tassa kide—*for its sake; *tumhāṇaṁ—*to your; *ghare—*house; *gantavvaṁ—*should go.

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: Do you think, Lalitā, that in the house of the cowherd king who has plenty of wish-fulfilling cows there is no thick cream, and that Kṛṣṇa has to go and get it from your house?**

### Verse 428

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *sudīrghākṣi rādhike, phalguni lalitā-pralāpārabhaṭī-paṭale tvaṁ nibaddha-pratyāśā mā khalu śulka-niryātanāya vaimukhyam apekṣiṣṭhāḥ. tad eṣa sannikṛṣṭo rahasyaṁ varṇayāmi. (ity urasi pāṇim ādhitsati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sudīrgha-akṣi—*O One with very elongated eyes; *rādhike—*O Rādhikā; *phalguni—*insignificant; *lalitā-pralāpa—*of Lalitā’s talk; *ārabhaṭī—*of courage; *paṭale—*in the abundance; *tvam—*You; *nibaddha-pratyāśā—*who fixed Your hope; *mā—*do not; *khalu—*indeed; *śulka-niryātanāya—*for giving the tax; *vaimukhyam—*the aversion; *apekṣiṣṭhāḥ—*expect; *tat—*therefore; *eṣaḥ—*this; *sannikṛṣṭaḥ—*drawn near; *rahasyam—*the mystery; *varṇayāmi—*I shall describe; *iti—*thus; *urasi—*on the chest; *pāṇim—*His hand; *ādhitsati—*wishes to place.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Your eyes are very elongated, o Rādhikā. You have put Your all hopes in the extreme courage of Lalitā’s prattling. But don’t expect that I will desist from forcing You to pay the tax. So let Me come close to reveal the secret. (wants to put his hand on Rādhā’s breast)**

**Комментарий:**

Revealing the secret means exposing the golden pot that Rādhā and the other gopīs are accused of smuggling. For Kṛṣṇa, it is, of course, nothing but the excuse to touch Rādhā.

### Verse 429

***lalitā:*** *dullaha-phale haṭhilla, lalidāe purado bhuaṇa-parāṇo gandha-vāho bi rāhīe tthaṇ-ambar-añcalassa pphaṁsaṇaṁ bi ṇa kāduṁ pahavedi. ettha hatthaṁ ṇikkhebiduṁ kida-jjhavasāassa de muddhadā ccea pajjavassadi!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dullaha—*hard to get; *phale—*in the matter of the fruit; *haṭhilla—*obstinately persisting; *lalidāe—*of Lalitā; *purado—*in front; *bhuaṇa-parāṇo—*the life of the world; *gandha-vāho—*the wind; *bi—*even; *rāhīe—*of Rādhā; *tthaṇa-ambara—*of cloth covering breast; *añcalassa—*the end; *pphaṁsaṇaṁ—*touching; *bi—*even; *ṇa—*not; *kāduṁ—*to do; *pahabedi—*able; *ettha—*on this place; *hatthaṁ—*Your hand; *ṇikkhebiduṁ—*to put; *kida-jjhavasāassa—*who undertakes; *de—*Your; *muddhadā—*foolishness; *ccea—*only; *pajjavassadi—*results*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: Kṛṣṇa, You are so stubborn to get the unreachable fruit! In front of Lalitā even the wind, which is the life of the world, cannot even touch the border of Rādhikā’s blouse. By trying to put Your hand on Rādhikā’s breasts, You only proved that You are indeed a fool!**

**Комментарий:**

Describing the wind as *‘bhuvana-prāṇa’* (“the life of the world”) and *‘gandha-vāha’* (“the aroma-carrier”), Lalitā wants to intimidate Kṛṣṇa with the fact that even the wind, despite being so closely related to every living entity and all-pervading, does not dare to do what Kṛṣṇa is now attempting at.

*‘Muddhadā’* (*‘mugdhatā’—* “foolishness”) could also mean “a blissful state of oblivion.” Lalitā hints that it is due to such blissful state that Kṛṣṇa has acted so daringly.

### Verse 430

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  
*kṛṣṇa-kuṇḍalinaś caṇḍi*  
*kṛtaṁ ghaṭṭanayānayā*  
*phut-kṛti-kriyayāpy asya*  
*bhavitāsi vimohitā*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kṛṣṇa-kuṇḍalinaḥ—*of the black snake; *caṇḍi—*O fierce one; *kṛtam—*enough; *ghaṭṭanayā—*of chasing; *anayā—*this; *phut-kṛti-kriyayā—*by doing hissing; *api—*also; *asya—*its; *bhavitā—*can become; *asi—*you; *vimohitā—*bewildered.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O passionate girl, don’t chase away the black snake, lest it will spellbind you with its hissing.**

**Комментарий:**

*‘Kṛṣṇa-kuṇḍalinaḥ’* (“the black snake”) also means “Kṛṣṇa, who wears earrings.” *‘Phut,’* taken to mean “hissing” in relation to snakes, is originally onomatopoetic sound that is supposed to imitate the sound of blowing into a liquid by touching it with one’s lips. With this, Kṛṣṇa indirectly “threatens” Lalitā that He could kiss her.

## Verse 431(a-b)

***lalitā:***  *(saṁskṛtena) kṛtaṁ vibhīṣikayā, yataḥ*  
*vilasati susiddha-mantre*  
*maṇḍita-matir āhi-tuṇḍikī lalitā*  
*sukaraṁ mūrdhonnamanaṁ*  
*na jihmagasyātra kṛṣṇasya*

**Пословный перевод:**

*saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *kṛtam—*enough; *vibhīṣikayā—*of scaring; *yataḥ—*because; *vilasati—*is illustrious; *susiddha-mantre—*in \[chanting\] of the magical mantra; *maṇḍita—*decorated; *matiḥ—*whose mind; *āhi-tuṇḍikī—*the snake catcher; *lalitā—*Lalitā; *sukaram—*easy to perform; *mūrdha-unnamanam—*the raising of its hood; *na—*not; *jihmagasya—*for the snake; *atra—*in this matter; *kṛṣṇasya—*black.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (in Sanskrit): Enough of scaring me: The smart snake-catcher Lalitā is illustrious in the chanting of the magical mantra. This being so, it will not be easy for the black snake to even raise its hood.**

**Комментарий:**

The second part of the verse can be understood as follows: “…it will not be easy for cunning Kṛṣṇa (*jihma-gasya kṛṣṇasya*) to even raise His face (*mūrdhonnamanam*), \[so what to speak of kissing me.\]”

### Verse 432

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *nāndīmukhi, ghaṭṭādhikāriṇām abhirūpāyām apy anṛta-vṛttau parāṅ-mukhī me dakṣiṇasya rasanā. pāṇiś ca haṭha-ceṣṭāyāṁ pṛṣṭha-dāyī. tad atra kiṁ dūṣaṇam āsāṁ rāmāṇāṁ vipratipattau?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nāndīmukhi—*O Nāndīmukhī; *ghaṭṭa-adhikāriṇām—*of tollgate supervisors; *abhirūpāyām—*pleasing; *api—*though; *anṛta-vṛttau—*to dishonest ways; *parāṅ-mukhī—*averse; *me—*of Me; *dakṣiṇasya—*who is kind; *rasanā—*the tongue; *pāṇiḥ—*My hand; *ca—*also; *haṭha-ceṣṭāyām—*to violent action; *pṛṣṭha-dāyī—*shows its rear side; *tat—*therefore; *atra—*in this case; *kim—*what; *dūṣaṇam—*fault; *āsām—*of these; *rāmāṇām—*beautiful women; *vipratipattau—*in disagreeing*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O Nāndīmukhī, as I am a kind person, My tongue is averse to although pleasant but dishonest ways of tax collectors, and My hands refrain from any violence. Then what is My fault that these beauties are disagreeing with Me?**

**Комментарий:**

According to Kṛṣṇa, He is honest and nonviolent. He therefore thinks: “The gopīs are refusing to pay the tax, taking advantage of My kind and truthful nature. This being so, My kindness and truthfulness simply give Me trouble.”

### Verse 433

***lalitā:***  *(sa-narma-smitaṁ saṁskṛtena) –*  
*mithyā jalpatu te kathaṁ nu rasanā sādhvī-sahasrasya yā*  
*bimboṣṭhāmṛta-sevanād agha-ripo puṇyā prayatnād abhūt*  
*kasmād eva balāt karotu ca karaḥ soḍhuṁ kṣamaḥ subhruvāṁ*  
*raktaḥ suṣṭhu na nīvi-bandham api yaḥ kā vānya-bandhe kathā*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-narma-smitam—*with a playful smile; *saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *mithyā—*in vain; *jalpatu—*should talk; *te—*Your; *katham—*why; *nu—*at all; *rasanā—*tongue; *sādhvī—*of pure-hearted girls; *sahasrasya—*to a thousand; *yā—*which; *bimba-oṣṭha—*of bimba-color lips; *amṛta—*the nectar; *sevanāt—*by tasting; *agha-ripo—*O enemy of sin; *puṇyā—*pure; *prayatnāt—*by endeavor; *abhūt—*became; *kasmāt—*why; *eva—*only; *balāt—*by force; *karotu—*should act; *ca—*also; *karaḥ—*Your hand; *soḍhum—*to tolerate; *kṣamaḥ—*able; *su-bhruvām—*of the girls with beautiful eyebrows; *raktaḥ—*affectionate; *suṣṭhu—*well; *na—*not; *nīvi—*of a knot of a garment; *bandham—*the confinement; *api—*even; *yaḥ—*which; *kā—*what; *vā—*at all; *anya-bandhe—*about another confinement; *kathā—*the talk.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (with a playful smile): O enemy of sin, Your tongue has become pure by painstakingly tasting the ambrosia from the bimba-colored lips of pure-hearted girls. Why then it should speak lies? And also, why should Your hands be violent? Full of affection, they are unable to tolerate when the gopīs are confined by the fetters of their dress knots. What to speak then if the gopīs are confined by some other fetters?**

**Комментарий:**

This verse appears in *Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi* 14.107, cited as an example of *narma-lalita-māna*, or “loving anger (*māna*), gracefully expressed (*lalita*) with humor (*narma*).”

The definitions of *māna* and *lalita-māna* are as follows:

*snehas tūtkṛṣṭatāvāptyā*  
*mādhuryaṁ mānayan navam*  
*yo dhārayaty adākṣiṇyaṁ*  
*sa māna iti kīrtyate*

**“*****Māna*** **(loving anger) is a type of love when a woman becomes refractory in order to intensify her loving affection by experiencing a new kind of sweetness.”**

*madhu-snehas tu kauṭilyaṁ*  
*svātantrya-hṛdayaṅgamam*  
*bibhran narma-viśeṣaṁ ca*  
*lalito’yam udīryate*

**“The woman’s crooked attitude touches the heart of the lover, for she considers her lover to be subordinate to her. When, out of** ***madhu-sneha*** **(honey-like affection—see commentary to verse 356), the beloved displays eitherthat crooked attitude or a special humorous mood, it is called** ***lalita-māna*** **(graceful loving anger).”**

In the present verse of *Dāna-keli-kaumudī*, Lalitā indirectly exposes Kṛṣṇa’s so-called truthfulness and kindness. Viśvanātha Cakravartī paraphrases her statement: “Your tongue drinks nectar, *amṛta*, from the lips of pure-hearted girls. Therefore, it can’t speak lies, for the demigods, who are also known for drinking *amṛta*, are truthful. Also, when you see the garments tied around the gopīs’ waists, You think, ‘Ah, the gopīs’ waists suffer in these fetters. Let Me free them!’ With this in mind You then quickly approach the gopīs. Even though reproached, cursed, or even slapped by them, You don’t see it as an insult and ultimately “free” their waists.”

The last sentence beginning “what to speak of” implies that apart from the knots on their dress, the gopīs are confined into the prison of their bodices, braided hair, and so on. So, if Kṛṣṇa liberates the gopīs waists, He liberates their hair, breasts, and other bodily parts as well from their respective confinements. Moreover, He does it first of all merely by His glance!

Thus, in this mocking manner Lalitā actually states that Kṛṣṇa is a cheater, a cruel person, and an avowed libertine.

### Verse 434

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(kiñcid vihasya) lalite, satyaṁ bhavatyaḥ kṛta-puṇya-puñjānāṁ śiromaṇayaḥ yāsāṁ bhāgadheya-siddhauṣadhinākṛṣṭā bhagavatyāḥ pāripārśvikā nāndīmukhīyaṁ pratyupasthitā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kiñcit—*slightly; *vihasya—*laughing; *lalite—*O Lalitā; *satyam—*truly; *bhavatyaḥ—*you (plural); *kṛta—*of those who performed; *puṇya-puñjānām—*heaps of pious acts; *śiromaṇayaḥ—*the hcrest jewels; *yāsām—*of whose; *bhāgadheya—*good fortune; *siddha-auṣadhinā—*by the perfect medicinal herb; *ākṛṣṭā—*attracted; *bhagavatyāḥ—*of Bhagavatī (Paurṇamāsī); *pāripārśvikā—*the attendant; *nāndīmukhī—*Nāndīmukhī; *iyam—*this; *pratyupasthitā—*has arrived.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (softly laughing): Lalitā, you gopīs are truly the crest jewels of those who have performed heaps of pious acts. Attracted by the miraculous medicinal herb of your good fortune, Nāndīmukhī, the attendant of Bhagavatī, has arrived here.**

**Комментарий:**

With His mild laughter Kṛṣṇa shows that He was pleased with Lalitā’s resentful words.

From the words “Nāndīmukhī…has arrived here” we shouldn’t conclude that for some time Nāndīmukhī wasn’t present on the stage. She was there already for quite some time (text 185). Kṛṣṇa has simply called Nāndīmukhī closer. He speaks of her as “the attendant of Paurṇamāsī,” proposing to Lalitā that Nāndīmukhī become a mediator to abait the current dispute. Of course, Kṛṣṇa only expects her to more efficiently help Him achieve the desired goal.

### Verse 435

***lalitā:***  *ṇandīmuhi, bhaavadī-pāeahiṁ sāvidāsi! turiaṁ ido dūrī-hohi! pekkhamha kiṁ eso amhāṇaṁ karedi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ṇandīmuhi—*O Nāndīmukhī; *bhaavadī-pātrahiṁ—*by the revered Bhagavatī (Paurṇamāsī); *sāvidā—*cursed; *asi—*you are; *turiaṁ—*quickly; *ido—*from here; *dūrī-hohi—*go far away; *pekkhamha—*we shall see; *kiṁ—*what; *eso—*He; *amhāṇaṁ—*to us; *karedi—*will do*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: Nāndīmukhī, you are cursed by the revered Bhagavatī! Go far away from here at once! Let’s see what He will do to us.**

**Комментарий:**

Without any diplomacy, Lalitā rejects the so-called mediator outright.

### Verse 436

***nāndīmukhī:***  *lalide, mahā-saṅkaḍaṁ edaṁ tumhāṇaṁ jaṁ haṭhilla-cakkavaṭṭiṇo hatthe paḍidāo ttha. tā ettha samae pariccāo ṇa kkhu siṇehassa aṇurūbo.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*lalide—*O Lalitā; *mahā-saṅkaḍaṁ—*great danger; *edaṁ—*this; *tumhāṇaṁ—*for you; *jaṁ—*that; *haṭhilla-cakkavaṭṭiṇo—*of the oppressive emperor; *hatthe—*into the hand; *paḍidāo—*have fallen; *ttha—*you; *tā—*thus; *ettha—*at this; *samae—*time; *pariccāo—*leaving; *ṇa—*not; *kkhu—*indeed; *siṇehassa—*for love; *aṇurūbo—*suitable*.*

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: Lalitā, it is a great danger that you have fallen into the hands of this emperor of thugs. I can’t leave you at this moment, for it would go against my love for you.**

**Комментарий:**
