*Mahaṭī-ṭīkā* elaborates on the mood of Nāndīmukhī’s speech: “Even if you curse me, I am unable to leave you. It is out of love, and not out of formality, for we practically see that the bond of love is stronger than that of duty.”

### Verse 437

***arjunaḥ:*** *pia-vaassa, ‘jāo pagabbhāo sulukke vippaḍibajjanti tāo tuṇṇaṁ amha-purado āṇijjantu’ tti ujjāṇa-cakkavaṭṭiṇo sāsaṇaṁ kahaṁ tue visumaridaṁ?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*pia-vaassa—*O dear friend; *jāo—*who; *pagabbhāo—*arrogant; *sulukke—*in paying the fee; *vippaḍibajjanti—*object; *tāo—*them; *tuṇṇaṁ—*quickly; *amha-purado—*in front of Me; *āṇijjantu—*bring; *tti—*thus; *ujjāṇa-cakkavaṭṭiṇo—*of the grove emperor; *sāsaṇaṁ—*order; *kahaṁ—*how; *tue—*by You; *visumaridaṁ—*forgotten.

**Перевод:**

**Arjuna: My dear friend, how could You forget the emperor of the groves, who ordered, ‘bring before Me those arrogant girls who refuse to pay the tax?’**

**Комментарий:**

Arjuna brings Kṛṣṇa, diverted by the “side issues,” back on the track by reminding about the emperor’s order (verse 257)

### Verse 438

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(harṣam abhinīya) sādhu smāritam arjunena. hanta lalite, sakhāyo me sakhyaś ca te ghaṭṭam evādhitiṣṭhantu. tvaṁ tu mayā sārdham ekākinī svayaṁ prasthāya niṣkuṭa-cakravartinaḥ suṣṭhu goṣṭhī-gaṅgāvagāhanena khelaya nija-locana-mīnayor mithunam.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*harṣam—*joy; *abhinīya—*expressing; *sādhu—*well; *smāritam—*reminded; *arjunena—*by Arjuna; *hanta—*alas; *lalite—*O Lalitā; *sakhāyaḥ—*friends; *me—*My; *sakhyaḥ—*girlfriends; *ca—*and; *te—*your; *ghaṭṭam—*at the toll station; *eva—*only; *adhitiṣṭhantu—*may stay; *tvam—*you; *tu—*however; *mayā—*Me; *sārdham—*with; *ekākinī—*alone; *svayam—*personally; *prasthāya—*leaving; *niṣkuṭa-cakravartinaḥ—*of the grove emperor; *suṣṭhu—*well; *goṣṭhī—*of the assembly; *gaṅgā—*into the Gaṅgā; *avagāhanena—*by plunging; *khelaya—*make play; *nija—*your; *locana-mīnayoḥ—*of fishlike eyes; *mithunam—*the couple*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (showing joy): Well reminded, Arjuna. Alas, o Lalitā. Let our companions stay at the toll station, and you alone go with Me and make play the fish couple of your eyes by plunging them into the Gaṅgā of the grove emperor’s assembly.**

### Verse 439

***lalitā:***  *dhamma-dhurīṇa, tāe ccea kulaṅgaṇāe appaṇo do-ulaṁ rakkhidaṁ jāe puṇṇa-siloa-ma{u}liṇā tue saddhaṁ ekāgiṇīe ṇijjaṇe calidaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dhamma—*of dharma; *dhurīṇa—*O bearer; *tāe—*by that; *ccea—*only; *kula-aṅgaṇāe—*virtuous lady; *appaṇo—*own; *do-ulam—*two families; *rakkhidaṁ—*protected; *jāe—*with whom; *puṇṇa-siloa—*of those glorified by auspicious verses; *ma{u}liṇā—*the best; *tue—*You; *saddhaṁ—*with; *ekāgiṇīe—*alone; *ṇijjaṇe—*to a seclusion; *calidaṁ—*goes*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: O upholder of dharma, You are the best of all glorious personalities. Only that virtuous girl who goes alone with You to the secluded place can secure the reputation of her mother’s and father’s families.**

**Комментарий:**

Lalitā ironically implies that a girl who would go with Kṛṣṇa to a secluded place is low-class, and by doing so she would stain the reputation of her family.

### Verse 440

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *kiṁ vānayā kṣepiṣṭhe ghaṭṭa-karmaṇi dīrgha-sūtratā-prastāvanayā. prasahya tarasā śulkam evāṅgī-karavāṇi. (iti rādhikām anusādhayati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kim—*what (is the need); *vā—*at all; *anayā—*of this; *kṣepiṣṭhe—*fastest; *ghaṭṭa-karmaṇi—*in the toll gate work; *dīrgha-sūtratā—*of procrastination; *prastāvanayā—*introduction; *prasahya—*forcibly; *tarasā—*speedily; *śulkam—*the tax; *eva—*only; *aṅgī-karavāṇi—*I should accept; *iti—*thus; *rādhikām—*Rādhikā; *anusādhayati—*advances.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: What is the need for this long introduction to the brisk affairs of the toll gate? Let Me quickly take the tax by force. (advances toward Rādhikā)**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa has been trying a lot to settle the matter by diplomacy. Now His patience has reached its end.

### Verse 441

***lalitā:***  *(solluṇṭhaṁ vihasya) hanta somma-suumāra, attaṇo ṇetta-cattare rāhiā-taṇu-pphaṁsa-sāhasiadā tuammi vaṭṭadi tti savvadhā ṇa pattiāedi esā lalidā tti pasiddhā siddhāṇusāsaṇā ballavī tā pekkhiduṁ kida-kodūhalā ciṭṭhadi. suṭṭhu vittārehi appaṇo vikkama-savvassaṁ!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-ulluṇṭham—*with irony; *vihasya—*laughing; *hanta—*alas; *somma—*gentle; *suumāra—*O delicate boy; *attaṇo—*my own; *ṇetta-cattare—*on the plaza of eyes; *rāhiā-taṇu—*the body of Rādhā; *pphaṁsa—*in touching; *sāhasiadā—*the boldness; *tuammi—*in You; *vaṭṭadi—*exist; *tti—*thus; *savvadhā—*in all respects; *ṇa pattiāedi—*does not believe; *esā—*this; *lalidā—*Lalitā; *tti—*thus; *pasiddhā—*famous; *siddha-aṇusāsaṇā—*whose orders are accomplished; *ballavī—*a cowherd girl; *tā—*that; *pekkhiduṁ—*to see; *kida-kodūhalā—*curious; *ciṭṭhadi—*is standing; *suṭṭhu—*well; *vittārehi—*distribute; *appaṇo—*of Your; *vikkama—*valor; *savvassaṁ—*the entirety*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (ironically laughing): O gentle delicate boy, this gopī is famous as Lalitā, whose orders never fail. And she just can’t believe that in front of her eyes You are brave enough to touch Rādhā’s body. So this Lalitā is here, curious to see how You will accomplish that. Come on, unleash the whole of Your courage!**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā actually craves for Kṛṣṇa’s forceful grab on the pretext of Him seizing the tax. Lalitā, however, wants to make sure that her friend maintains Her refractoriness by keeping on flatly rejecting Kṛṣṇa’s advances. That is the actual purpose of Lalitā’s tirade. Otherwise, if Lalitā doesn’t prompt Rādhā in this way, Rādhā could even go mad in the loving ecstasy that would soften Her heart. After all, Kṛṣṇa stood near Rādhārāṇī already for quite long, and She directly relished His form, features, and jokes enough that Her eagerness for Kṛṣṇa could swallow the heaps of Her sobriety in a single moment. If Rādhārāṇī softened toward Kṛṣṇa in this way, it would certainly put an obstacle to the dispute over the tax. This is Viśvanātha Cakravartī’s justification of Lalitā’s vehement behavior.

In her speech, Lalitā tries to intimidate Kṛṣṇa by calling Him *‘somma’* (*saumya—*“gentle”) and *‘suumāra’* (*su-kumāra—*“delicate”), which implies that by nature He is not violent and unable to perform a daring act.

Lalitā calls herself a gopī, “whose orders never fail.” By saying this, she shows herself to be a *prakharā* (stern) sakhī:

*durlaṅghya-vākyā prakharā*  
*prakhyātā gauravocitā*

**“A respectable sakhī is called** ***prakharā*****, and nobody dares to transgress her orders.”**

### Verse 442

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(kiñcid vihasya) namas tubhyaṁ mahācaṇḍi. cāmuṇḍe, namas tubhyam. nūnaṁ muṇḍa-mālākhyam ātmano maṇḍanaṁ vimucya durvāra-māra-saṁhārāya gopikā-rūpeṇopasthitāsi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kiñcit—*slightly; *vihasya—*laughing; *namaḥ—*obeisances; *tubhyam—*to you; *mahā-caṇḍi—*o goddess Mahācaṇḍī ; *cāmuṇḍe—*O Cāmuṇḍā; *namaḥ tubhyam—*obeisances to you; *nūnam—*indeed; *muṇḍa-mālā—*the head wreath; *ākhyam—*called; *ātmanaḥ—*your; *maṇḍanam—*decoration; *vimucya—*removing; *durvāra—*hard to restrain; *māra—*god of love; *saṁhārāya—*for destroying; *gopikā-rūpeṇa—*in the form of a gopī; *upasthitā—*are present; *asi—*you*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (mildly laughing): Obeisances to you, o Mahācaṇḍī. O Cāmuṇḍā, obeisances to you. Leaving your garland of skulls, here you are indeed, in the form of a gopī, to destroy the unbridled god of love.**

**Комментарий:**

Mahācāṇḍī, or Cāmuṇḍā, are the names of goddess Kālī, who wears a garland of fifty human skulls and is the ghastly epitome of violent fury.

Light laughter generally shows that one is aware of something. With His laughter, Kṛṣṇa also assures Lalitā of the following: “I just want to crush to pieces your friend’s pride and thus conquer Her by making Her amorous restlessness visible to everybody. I do not intend to do anything by force.”

### Verse 443

***viśākhā:***  *sahi lalide, vija{i}ṇī hohi!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sahi lalide—*O sakhī Lalitā; *vija{i}ṇī—*victorious; *hohi—*be.

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā: Sakhī Lalitā, be victorious!**

## Lalitā In Trouble

### Verse 444-445

***lalitā:***  *(smitaṁ kṛtvā sva-gatam) kidaṁ suṭṭhu saṁlāba-vilasidaṁ. tā doṇam ahiṭṭhāmia-pūrassa ogāhaṇe titthaṁ ārambhissaṁ.*  
*(prakāśam) visāhe, tattha gadua ṇivedehi bhaavadīṁ amhāṇaṁ bāḍhaṁ edaṁ viḍambaṇaṁ!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smitam kṛtvā—*smiling; *sva-gatam—*to herself; *kidam—*is done; *suṭṭhu—*well; *saṁlāba—*of talking; *vilasidaṁ—*the pastime; *tā—*of them; *doṇam—*both; *ahiṭṭha—*desired; *amia—*of nectar; *pūrassa—*of the flood; *ogāhaṇe—*for plunging; *titthaṁ—*the holy pond; *ārambhissaṁ—*I shall start; *prakāśam—*audibly; *visāhe—*O Viśākhā; *tattha—*there; *gadua—*going; *ṇivedehi—*inform; *bhaavadīṁ—*Bhagavatī (Paurṇamāsī); *amhāṇaṁ—*about our; *bāḍhaṁ—*strong; *edam—*this; *viḍambaṇaṁ—*abuse.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (smiling, says to herself): The pastime of verbal fight is now successful. Now, let me start digging a holy pond to plunge both of Them into the flood of nectar They both are after.**

**(audibly): Viśākhā, go and inform Bhagavatī that they are abusing us so badly!**

### Verse 446-447

***nāndīmukhī:***  *(sva-gatam) esā bhaavadī māhavī-maṇḍabantaridā pekkhantī savvaṁ suṇādi. (prakāśam) lalide, bhaavadī kkhu goulesarīe pāse vaṭṭadi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sva-gatam—*to herself; *esā—*this; *bhaavadī—*Bhagavatī (Paurṇamāsī); *māhavī-maṇḍaba—*in the bower of *mādhavī* flowers; *antaridā—*hidden; *pekkhantī—*looking; *savvaṁ—*everything; *suṇādi—*hears; *prakāśam—*audibly; *lalide—*O Lalitā; *bhaavadī—*Bhagavatī (Paurṇamāsī); *kkhu—*indeed; *goulesarīe—*the queen of Gokula; *pāse—*near; *vaṭṭadi—*is*.*

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī (to herself): Bhagavatī hears everything. She watches this pastime by hiding in the mādhavī bower.**

**(audibly): Lalitā, surely Bhagavatī is now with Yaśodā.**

### Verse 448

***rādhikā:*** *(sa-parihāsaṁ vihasya janāntikam) halā lalide, appaṇo bi bhūiṭṭho amhesu tumha-siṇeho ajja ccea suṭṭhu paccakkhī-kido jaṁ ghaṭṭa-ālado jādaṁ amhāṇaṁ jādaṇaṁ tattha appa-samappaṇeṇa moābiduṁ ubakkamantī tumaṁ iṅgideṇa lakkhīasi. tā dhaṇṇāsi!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-parihāsam—*with ridicule; *vihasya—*laughing; *jana-antikam—*to the side; *halā lalide—*O Lalitā; *appaṇo—*than for yourself; *bi—*also; *bhūiṭṭho—*deeper; *amhesu—*for us; *tumha-siṇeho—*your love; *ajja—*now; *ccea—*only; *suṭṭhu—*well; *paccakkhī-kido—*is shown; *jaṁ—*because; *ghaṭṭa-ālado—*from the tollgate man; *jādaṁ—*coming; *amhāṇaṁ—*us; *jādaṇam—*from torture; *tattha—*there; *appa-samappaṇeṇa—*by offering yourself; *moābiduṁ—*to free; *ubakkamantī—*attacking; *tumaṁ—*you; *iṅgideṇa—*with trembling; *lakkhīasi—*are noticed; *tā—*thus; *dhaṇṇā—*glorious; *asi—*you*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (Laughs mockingly and speaks to the side): O Lalitā, only now it is obvious that you love us more than yourself: we see how you tremble as you are sacrificing yourself by attacking this tax collector in order to set us free from His tortures. All glories to you for that!**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā already blamed Lalitā before in putting all the gopīs into trouble (text 147 & 163). Viśvanātha Cakravartī brings out Rādhā’s hidden message: “Lalitā, you are trembling! This is because you have suddenly lost your boldness. And also, when Kṛṣṇa told you ‘Here you are indeed, in the form of a gopī, to destroy the unbridled god of love’ (text 442), Viśākhā said ‘Sakhī Lalitā, be victorious’ (text 443). Actually, Viśākhā mocked you by indirectly saying ‘Be victorious in destroying the god of love,’ but you only smiled, without saying anything in response. From this I can understand your true desire.”

### Verse 449

***lalitā:***  *a{i} sūra-vvadekka-vissude, asama-vāṇī-samare ccea saccaṁ kusalamhi. tumaṁ uṇa asama-bāṇa-samare jaṁ puṇo puṇo diṭṭha-purisaāra-soṭṭhava-sārāsi. tā pasīda kaḍakkha-jimbhaṇa-bāṇeṇa ṇaṁ mahā-vīram-maṇṇaṁ jimbhaantī kkhaṇaṁ ettha ciṭṭha. amhe thoaṁ aggado gadua paḍibālemha tumaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*a{i}—*O; *sūra—*to the sun god; *vada-ekka—*Your sole vow; *vissude—*famous One; *asama—*rough; *vāṇī—*of speech; *samare—*in the battle; *ccea—*only; *saccaṁ—*truly; *kusalamhi—*I am an expert; *tumaṁ—*You; *uṇa—*again; *asama-bāṇa—*with Kāmadeva; *samare—*in the battle; *jaṁ—*because; *puṇo puṇo—*again and again; *diṭṭha—*\[You\] see; *purisaāra—*of acting like a man in conjugal enjoyment; *soṭṭhava—*the excellence; *sārā—*the essence; *asi—*You; *tā—*thus; *pasīda—*have mercy; *kaḍakkha—*Your sidelong glance; *jimbhaṇa—*of casting; *bāṇeṇa—*with the arrow; *ṇaṁ—*this; *mahāvīram-maṇṇaṁ—*who thinks himself a great hero; *jimbhaantī—*devouring; *kkhaṇaṁ—*for a moment; *ettha—*here; *ciṭṭha—*stay; *amhe—*we; *thoaṁ—*a little; *aggado—*further; *gadua—*after going; *paḍibālemha—*will wait; *tumaṁ—*for You.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: O You, famous for Your solemn vow to Sūryadeva, it is right that I am expert in the battle of rough words. You, however, are expert in the battle of Kāmadeva, for at every moment You are aware of the secret, which is the advantage of acting like a man in conjugal enjoyment. Please be kind, therefore, stay here for a moment more and burst apart with the arrow of Your sidelong glance this youth, who thinks Himself a great hero. We will go a little further and wait for You.**

**Комментарий:**

The reason for Rādhā to be “expert in the battle of Kāmadeva” is that She is aware of the secret of winning such battles—the advantage of *puruṣakāra*, which is, as Viśvanātha Cakravartī explains, assuming masculine position in conjugal enjoyment. This means “winning the battle,” or taking up the dominant role in such enjoyment. Apart from this, *puruṣakāra* means attaining the goal of human life.

By addressing Rādhā as ‘famous for Her solemn vow to Sūryadeva,’ Lalitā implies the following: “We all know it well for what purpose You took up Your vow to the sun god.”

One could also understand *‘sūra-vadekka-vissude’* as “One famous for Her vow to the hero (*sūra*).” It is noteworthy, because later Lalitā calls Kṛṣṇa “One who thinks Himself a great hero.”

*‘Asama-bāṇa’* (“having odd number of arrows”) denotes Kāmadeva, who carries five arrows.

### Verse 450

***rādhikā:*** *(sa-praṇayābhyasūyam) avehi appaṇo āāra-saṅgovaṇekka-dakkhe avehi! dāṇiṁ ccea pekkhissam!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-praṇaya—*with love; *abhyasūyam—*indignantly; *avehi—*go away; *appaṇo—*Your; *āāra—*appearance; *saṅgovaṇa—*in hiding; *ekka—*best; *dakkhe—*O expert; *avehi—*go away; *dāṇiṁ—*now; *ccea—*only; *pekkhissaṁ—*I will glance*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (with loving anger): Go, You topmost expert in hiding Your true self, go! I shall glance at Him right now!**

**Комментарий:**

“If this is what you had in mind all the way, then just see!” Rādhā speaks indignantly as if She feels betrayed by Her friend, who has been plotting to leave Her alone with Kṛṣṇa.

### Verse 451-452

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(sva-gatam) samprati mantharita-caṇḍimeva saṁlakṣyate lalitā.*  
*(prakāśam) sādhu sādhu lalite, samayābhijñāsi yad adya mudhā vivāda-ghaṭāṁ vighaṭayya ghaṭṭam adhitiṣṭhasi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sva-gatam—*to Himself; *samprati—*now; *mantharita—*curtailed; *caṇḍimā—*whose vehemence; *iva—*like; *saṁlakṣyate—*is noticed; *lalitā—*Lalitā; *prakāśam—*openly; *sādhu sādhu—*well done; *lalite—*O Lalitā; *samaya—*of a proper time; *abhijñā—*knower; *asi—*you are; *yat—*because; *adya—*today; *mudhā—*pointless; *vivāda—*of arguments; *ghaṭām—*the series; *vighaṭayya—*breaking; *ghaṭṭam—*at the toll station; *adhitiṣṭhasi—*you are present*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (to Himself): It seems Lalitā’s steams have been blown off.**

**(audibly): Very good, Lalitā, excellent. You know how to behave according to time: here you are at My toll station, having now stopped the cascade of your pointless arguments.**

### Verse 453-455

***lalitā:***  *chala-keli-cha{i}lla, eso haṭhilladā-la{u}ḍī-metta-oṭṭambhaṇo ballavāṇaṁ gaṇo via ṇa kkhu sāra-ggāhiṇīṇaṁ ballavīṇaṁ gaṇo.*  
***viśākhā:***  *(sambhramam abhinīya) lalide, mahā-pamādo mahā-pamādo!*  
***lalitā:***  *kīdiso eso?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*chala—*deceitful; *keli—*in games; *cha{i}lla—*O expert; *eso—*this; *haṭhilladā—*of obstinacy; *la{u}ḍī—*the stick; *metta—*only; *oṭṭambhaṇo—*whose support; *ballavāṇaṁ—*of the cowherds; *gaṇo—*group; *via—*like; *ṇa—*not; *kkhu—*indeed; *sāra-ggāhiṇīṇam—*of the essence takers; *ballavīṇaṁ—*gopīs; *gaṇo—*group*. sambhramam—*confusion; *abhinīya—*expressing; *lalide—*O Lalitā; *mahā-pamādo mahā-pamādo—*great blunder, great blunder. *kīdiso—*what kind; *eso—*this.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: O expert in deceitful games, it is definitely the gopās, and not the honest gopīs, who support themselves exclusively with the stick of stubbornness.**

**Viśākhā (expressing confusion): O Lalitā, what a blunder, what a blunder!**

**Lalitā: What blunder?**

### Verse 456-457

***viśākhā:***  *a{i} kalaha-loluhadā-vimhārida-dhamme, viramehi! tehiṁ jaṇṇiehiṁ saṁdisia pesidaṁ amhāṇaṁ jaṁ havaṇijjaṁ heaṅgavīṇaṁ harantīhiṁ tumhehiṁ kulaṅgaṇāṇaṁ kula-kaṇṇaāṇaṁ vā dūsaṇa-āriṇi kāmi-jaṇe diṭṭhi-kkhebo bi savvadhā ṇa kāddavvo.*  
*(iti nāsikāgre tarjanīm āsajya) haddhī haddhī! tue uṇa ummattāe moheṇa mohaṁ jevva jappantīe bahu-ālaṁ vaaṇaṁ bi sammīsidaṁ!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*a{i}—*O; *kalaha—*for quarrel; *loluidā—*due to greed; *vimhārida—*who forgot; *dhamme—*duty; *viramehi—*stop; *tehiṁ—*by those; *jaṇṇiehiṁ—*priests; *saṁdisia—*having been ordered; *pesidaṁ—*sent; *amhāṇaṁ—*our; *jaṁ—*that; *havaṇijjaṁ—*sacrificial; *heaṅgavīṇaṁ—*ghee; *harantīhiṁ—*carrying; *tumhehiṁ—*by you; *kula-aṅgaṇāṇaṁ—*noble ladies; *kula-kaṇṇaāṇaṁ—*noble girls; *vā—*or; *dūsaṇa-āriṇi—*on an abuser; *kāmi-jaṇe—*lusty person; *diṭṭhi-kkhebo—*a glance; *bi—*even; *savvadhā—*by all means; *ṇa kaddavvo—*should not be cast; *iti—*thus; *nāsikā—*of the nose; *agre—*on the tip; *tarjanīm—*her index finger; *āsajya—*fixing; *haddhī haddhī—*alas, alas; *tue—*by you; *uṇa—*again; *ummattāe—*insane; *moheṇa—*due to bewilderment; *mohaṁ—*uselessly; *jevva—*only; *jappantīe—*who is speaking; *bahu-ālaṁ—*for a long time; *vaaṇaṁ—*speech; *bi—*even; *sammīsidaṁ—*was mixed*.*

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā: Please stop, quarrelsome Lalitā! You forgot about our duty! The priests have ordered us that when we carry the ghee sent for the offering we cannot at any cost even look at a lusty fellow who abuses either noble ladies or girls.**

**(pointing her index finger at the tip of Lalitā’s nose) But on the contrary, you have not only looked at such a lusty fellow, but also passed so much time in useless talk, under the spell of insanity! To hell with You, to hell!**

### Verse 458

***lalitā:***  *(viṣādam abhinīya) visāhe, suṭṭhu kadhesi. savvaṁ muddhāe mae visumaridaṁ. tā cintehi ettha kiṁpi ṇikkidaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*viṣādam—*despair; *abhinīya—*expressing; *visāhe—*O Viśākhā; *suṭṭhu—*well; *kadhesi—*you speak; *savvaṁ—*all; *muddhāe—*bewildered; *mae—*by me; *visumaridaṁ—*forgotten; *tā—*thus; *cintehi—*think; *ettha—*in this case; *kiṁpi—*of some; *ṇikkidaṁ—*atonement*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (showing depair): Viśākhā, you are right. I was bewildered and forgot everything. But then think of some atonement for this.**

### Verse 459

***vṛndā:***  *(vihasya) yajña-puruṣasya viṣṇor anusmaraṇam eva munayaḥ sarvāgha-vidhvaṁsanaṁ vyāharanti. tataḥ smaryatām asau.*  
*(lalitā ‘viṇṇu viṇṇu’ iti kīrtayantī nāsikām abhispṛśya karṇaṁ spṛśati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vihasya—*laughing; *yajña-puruṣasya—*the personality of sacrifice; *viṣṇoḥ—*of Viṣṇu; *anusmaraṇam—*constant remembrance; *eva—*only; *munayaḥ—*sages; *sarva-agha—*of all sins; *vidhvaṁsanam—*the annihilator; *vyāharanti—*describe; *tataḥ—*therefore; *smaryatām—*should be remembered; *asau—*He; *lalitā—*Lalitā; *viṣṇu viṣṇu—*O Viṣṇu, Viṣṇu; *iti—*thus; *kīrtayantī—*exclaiming; *nāsikām—*her nose; *abhispṛśya—*touching; *karṇam—*her ear; *spṛśati—*touches.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā (laughing): Sages say that one can get rid of all sins only by constantly thinking of Viṣṇu. So think of Him!**

**(Touching her nose and then pulling her ears, Lalitā exclaims, ‘Viṣṇu, Viṣṇu!’)**

**Комментарий:**

Touching one’s nose and pulling ears is the gesture of repentance.

### Verse 460

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(sa-smitam) lalite, satyaṁ vidūṣitāsi! tad atra tarasā sannidhehi. sadya eva doṣāspṛṣṭāṁ karavāṇi bhavatīm. (ity anusṛtya bhujāśleṣaṁ nāṭayati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-smitam—*with a smile; *lalite—*O Lalitā; *satyam—*truly; *vidūṣitā—*polluted; *asi—*you are; *tat—*thus; *atra—*here; *tarasā—*with haste; *sannidhehi—*come close; *sadyaḥ—*immidiately; *eva—*only; *doṣa—*by fault; *aspṛṣṭām—*untouched; *karavāṇi—*let me make; *bhavatīm—*you; *iti—*thus; *anusṛtya—*approaching; *bhuja—*with His hands; *āśleṣam—*the embrace; *nāṭayati—*acting out.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (smiling): O Lalitā, you are really polluted! So come close quickly, and I shall instantly make you faultless. (Approaches Lalitā and embraces Her)**

**Комментарий:**

The second sentence also means: “So come close quickly, and I shall instantly touch (*spṛṣṭām*) you with My hands (*doṣā*).”

### Verse 461-462

***lalitā:***  *(sa-sādhvasam apasṛtya sa-nirvedam iva) hanta hanta, para-kalatta-milābaṇa-vilāsa-sāhasieṇa kula-bāliāhaṁ pphaṁsaṇe dūsidamhi!*  
***rādhikā:*** *(smitaṁ kṛtvā) lalide, amha-saṅgādo tuṇṇaṁ abehi jaṁ rada-hiṇḍaa-pphaṁsa-kalaṅkidāsi!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-sādhvasam—*with panic; *apasṛtya—*backing off; *sa-nirvedam—*with despair; *iva—*as if; *hanta hanta—*alas, alas; *para-kalattā—*others’ wives; *milābaṇa—*making dejected; *vilāsa—*whose sport; *sāhasieṇa—*with an aggressive rake; *kula-bāliā—*a nobel girl; *ahaṁ—*I; *pphaṁsaṇe—*in contact; *dūsidamhi—*I am polluted*. smitam kṛtvā—*smiling; *lalide—*O Lalitā; *amha—*from our; *saṅgādo—*assembly; *tuṇṇaṁ—*quickly; *abehi—*go away; *jaṁ—*because; *rada-hiṇḍaa—*of the women seducer; *pphaṁsa—*by the touch; *kalaṅkidā—*stained; *asi—*you are*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (backing off in panic, speaks as if with despair): O my God! I am a noble girl, and I am polluted by touching an aggressive rake who makes fun by tormenting others’ wives!**

**Rādhikā (smiling): Lalitā, leave us gopīs at once! You are stained by the touch of this rake!**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā smiles with this in mind: “Although you wanted Kṛṣṇa to touch My body, you nevertheless have to accept defeat by being touched yourself first.”

### Verse 463

***lalitā:***  *a{i} viṇodaṁ kuṇantīe aliaṁ ccea edaṁ bhaṇidaṁ mae. kudo mādisīe pa{i}-vvadā-sihaṇḍiṇīe pphaṁsa-mahā-sāhase eso bhua-bhuaṅgamo utthāduṁ pahabedu?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*a{i}—*O; *viṇodaṁ—*fun; *kuṇantīe—*making; *aliaṁ—*falsely; *ccea—*only; *edaṁ—*this; *bhaṇidaṁ—*was said; *mae—*by me; *kudo—*how; *mādisīe—*of one like myself; *pa{i}-vvadā—*of chaste women; *sihaṇḍiṇīe—*the peahen; *pphaṁsa—*of touching; *mahā-sāhase—*in the great boldness; *eso—*this; *bhua—*hand; *bhuaṅgamo—*snakelike; *utthāduṁ—*raise; *pahabedu—*could*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: Ah, what I said is not true. I was just joking. How dare the snake of His hand raise to touch me, the peahen of chaste women?**

**Комментарий:**

Peafowl and snake are natural enemies, and the latter is always killed if a fight occurs. Thus, comparing Kṛṣṇa’s hand to a snake and Herself to a peahen, Lalitā indirectly states that Kṛṣṇa has no chances in defeating her. She denies the fact that Kṛṣṇa has actually touched her body.

*‘Pa{i}-vvadā-sihaṇḍiṇīe’* (*‘pati-vratā-śikhaṇḍinyāḥ’*) could also mean “the crown (*śikhaṇḍinyāḥ*) of chaste women.”

### Verse 464

***rādhā:*** *a{i} asacca-bhāsiṇi, viṇṇādaṁ viṇṇādaṁ! ciṭṭha ciṭṭha! kida-bbhutthāṇāiṁ tuha taṇu-ruhāiṁ ccea sakkhittaṇaṁ taṇṇanti!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*a{i}—*O; *asacca—*of untruth; *bhāsiṇi—*speaker; *viṇṇādaṁ viṇṇādaṁ—*understood, understood; *ciṭṭha ciṭṭha—*stop, stop; *kida-bbhutthāṇāim—*standing on end; *tuha—*your; *taṇu-ruhāīṁ—*hairs; *ccea—*certainly; *sakkhittaṇaṁ—*evidence; *taṇṇanti—*spread*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: Hey lier, stop! Don’t say anything! I see, I see: Your very hairs bear witness against you by standing on end!**

**Комментарий:**

Hairs standing on end (*romāñca*), which is one of *sāttvika-bhāvas*, disclose Lalitā’s involuntary ecstasy of being touched by Kṛṣṇa.

### Verse 465

***lalitā:*** *raa-ṇārīa, suṭṭhu kkhuhidamhi jaṁ tue dūsidaṁ maṁ sahīo ṇa pphaṁsanti. ado ‘na duḥkhaṁ pañcabhiḥ saha’ tti bhaṇidī jadhā paaḍī-hodi tadhā karehi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*raa-ṇārīa—*O libertine; *suṭṭhu—*well; *kkhuhidamhi—*I am agitated; *jaṁ—*because; *tue—*by you; *dūsidaṁ—*polluted; *maṁ—*me; *sahīo—*sakhīs; *ṇa pphaṁsanti—*do not touch; *ado—*therefore; *na—*no; *duḥkham—*misery; *pañcabhiḥ—*five; *saha—*with; *tti—*thus; *bhaṇidī—*saying; *jadhā—*as; *paaḍī-hodi—*manifests; *tadhā—*so; *karehi—*act*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: You libertine, I am very disturbed, for my friends do not touch me now, as you have polluted me. So please, fulfill the meaning of the statement: ‘There is no suffering if the misery is shared by five persons.’**

**Комментарий:**

Lalitā prompts Kṛṣṇa to embrace the other gopīs as well.

### Verse 466

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *campakalate, payodharollekhi-dīrgha-śākho ’yaṁ tamālaḥ. tad enam ālambya pariphullā bhava.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*campaka-late—*O campaka creeper; *payodhara—*clouds; *ullekhi—*scratching; *dīrgha—*long; *śākhaḥ—*whose branches; *ayam—*this; *tamālaḥ—*tamāla tree; *tat—*therefore; *enam—*it; *ālambya—*leaning; *pariphullā—*blossoming; *bhava—*become*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: O campaka creeper, this tamāla tree can scratch clouds with its long branches. So, cling to it and blossom.**

**Комментарий:**

Instigated by Lalitā, Kṛṣṇa first approaches Campakalatā. This is the double meaning of Kṛṣṇa’s speech: “O Campakalatā, this *tamāla* tree (refers to Himself) scratches breasts (*payodhara*) with its long branches. So, cling to it, and have your hairs stand on end (*pariphullā*).” This analogy is based on the fact that creepers grow, develop, and prosper if they take shelter of a tree trunk.

### Verse 467

***campakalatā:*** *(sa-kampaṁ kiñcid apasṛtya) cha{i}lla, puṇo bi lalidaṁ jevva milāṇaṁ karehi jaṁ ‘na śayānaḥ pataty adhaḥ’ tti vaaṇaṁ suṇīadi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-kampam—*with trembling; *kiñcit—*slightly; *apasṛtya—*backing off; *cha{i}lla—*O clever One; *puṇo—*again; *bi—*also; *lalidaṁ—*Lalitā; *jevva—*only; *milāṇaṁ—*sad; *karehi—*make; *jam—*because; *na—*not; *śayānaḥ—*a lying person; *patati—*falls; *adhaḥ—*down; *tti—*thus; *vaaṇaṁ—*the statement; *suṇīadi—*is heard*.*

**Перевод:**

**Campakalatā (trembling, backs off slightly): O clever One, you better batter the same Lalitā again, for, as the statement goes, ‘A person lying on the ground can’t fall further.’**

**Комментарий:**

In other words, “If Lalitā is already polluted, what is the difference for her if You touch her again? I am, however, still pure.”

The statement ‘A person lying on the ground can’t fall further’ (*na śayānaḥ pataty adhaḥ*) comes from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 11.21.17.

### Verse 468

***lalitā:***  *abi ṇāma pia-sahīṁ visāhaṁ kaḍhiṇoru-pañca-sāhobasohidaṁ a{i}rādo pekkhissaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*abi ṇāma—*it is likely; *pia-sahīṁ—*my dear friend; *visāhaṁ—*Viśākhā; *kaḍhiṇa—*firm; *uru—*large; *pañca-sāha—*with five twigs; *ubasohidaṁ—*decorated; *a{i}rādo—*soon; *pekkhissaṁ—*I will see*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: It is likely that soon I will see my dear friend Viśākhā adorned with five firm and large twigs.**

**Комментарий:**

*‘Pañca-śākha’* (“five branches”) couls also mean “a palm of a hand.” Lalitā now hopes to see Viśākhā embraced by Kṛṣṇa. She describes Kṛṣṇa’s hands as firm, for firm hands is a good attribute for a male.

With her statement, Lalitā makes a pun on Viśākhā’s name, which literally means “branchless” (*‘vi’—*without; *‘śākhā’—*branches).

### Verse 469

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *viśākhike, taruṇāliṅgitā succhāyā bhava. tad itaś campakalateva mā bhaṅgam upayāsīḥ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*viśākhike—*O Viśākhikā; *taruṇā—*by the tree; *āliṅgitā—*embraced; *succhāyā—*having a good shade; *bhava—*become; *tat—*thus; *itaḥ—*from it (tree); *campakalatā—*Campakalatā; *iva—*like; *mā—*do not; *bhaṅgam—*to bending; *upayāsīḥ—*resort*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: My dear heart Viśākhā, let the tree embrace you and you stay in its pleasant shade. Don’t bend away from this tree, like that campaka creeper.**

**Комментарий:**

Alternative meaning: “My dear Viśākhā, let this youth (*taruṇa*) embrace you and you become beautiful (*su-cchāyā*). Don’t avoid (*bhaṅgam upayāsīḥ*) Him (*itaḥ*) like Campakalatā.”

### Verse 470

***viśākhā:***  *(tūrṇam apasarpantī) kalaṅkiṇi lalide, kadhaṁ ‘vidūṣayati nirlajjaḥ svayaṁ duṣṭaḥ parān api’ tti vaaṇa-pamāṇaṁ kāduṁ pa{u}ttāsi? tā suppaaḍaṁ jevva de āūdaṁ. alaṁ alieṇa vilakkhaṇa-bhaveṇa. diṭṭhiā cintāulāsu amhesu atakkidaṁ sulukkassa yoggā-kidāsi tumaṁ devveṇa. (lalitā kiñcid apasṛtya taṁ prekṣya dṛg-añcalaṁ kuñcayati.)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tūrṇam—*quickly; *apasarpantī—*backing off; *kalaṅkiṇi—*stained; *lalide—*O Lalitā; *kadhaṁ—*how; *vidūṣayati—*pollutes; *nirlajjaḥ—*shyless; *svayam—*oneself; *duṣṭaḥ—*wicked; *parān—*others; *api—*also; *tti—*thus; *vaaṇa-pamāṇaṁ—*verbal testimony; *kāduṁ—*to fulfill; *pa{u}ttāsi—*you are ready; *tā—*thus; *suppaaḍaṁ—*very evident; *jevva—*only; *de—*your; *āūdaṁ—*purpose; *alaṁ—*enough; *alieṇa—*of false; *vilakkhaṇa—*strange; *bhaveṇa—*mood; *diṭṭhiā—*fortunately; *cintā—*by anxiety; *āulāsu—*overwhelmed; *amhesu—*among us; *atakkidaṁ—*undisputably; *sulukkassa—*for the fee; *yoggā—*suitable; *kidāsi—*are made; *tumaṁ—*you; *devveṇa—*by the providence. *lalitā—*Lalitā; *kiñcit—*slightly; *apasṛtya—*moving back; *tam—*at Him; *prekṣya—*looking; *dṛg-añcalam—*the corners of her eyes; *kuñcayati—*contracts.

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā (backing off briskly): Dirty Lalitā, are you about to fulfill the meaning of the words ‘A dirty and shyless person pollutes others along with himself?’ If so, then your purpose is pretty obvious. Enough of putting on a false mask. We were plunged in anxiety, but luckily, the providence has chosen you among all of us to be a suitable substitution for the fee.**

**(Lalitā, moving back a little, squints at Kṛṣṇa.)**

**Комментарий:**

By *‘devveṇa’* Viśākhā could also mean Kṛṣṇa. Although conventionally referring to a divine personality, the word *‘deva,’* derived from the verbal root *‘div,’* (“to shine, play”) could be also understood as “playful,” referring in this context to Kṛṣṇa.

### Verse 471

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(smitvā rādhāṁ didhīrṣan) vilolākṣi, lalitā-locana-bhaṅgī-vātyayā bāḍham āndolita-pāṇi-pallavo ’smi. tad adya nāropaya sabhye mayi sābhyasūyaṁ cakṣuḥ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smitvā—*while smiling; *rādhām—*of Rādhā; *didhīrṣan—*wishing to catch hold; *vilola-akṣi—*O One with unsteady eyes; *lalitā-locana—*of Lalitā’s eyes; *bhaṅgī—*of the crooked behavior; *vātyayā—*by the storm; *bāḍham—*certainly; *āndolita—*shaken; *pāṇi-pallavaḥ—*the twigs of My hands; *asmi—*I; *tat—*therefore; *adya—*now; *na āropaya—*do not place; *sabhye—*gentleman; *mayi—*at Me; *sa-abhyasūyam—*with indignation; *cakṣuḥ—*Your glance.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (smiling, wants to catch hold of Rādhā): O One with restless eyes, the branches of My arms are swaying due to the hurricane of Lalitā’s crooked glance. So now do not look at Me, a refined gentleman, with anger.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa justifies his behavior: “I am just doing what your sakhī has suggested. And that must have been Your idea in the first place. Keep this in mind!”

### Verse 472

***rādhā:***  *(sa-sādhvasaṁ viśākhām anusarantī) sahi, parittāhi attāṇaaṁ ccea jaṁ rāhāe māliṇṇe visāhā maliṇā bhaṇīadi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-sādhvasam—*with fear; *viśākhām—*Viśākhā; *anusarantī—*going after; *sahi—*O sakhī; *parittāhi—*save; *attāṇaaṁ—*yourself; *ccea—*certainly; *jaṁ—*because; *rāhāe—*of Rādhā; *māliṇṇe—*in pollution; *visāhā—*Viśākhā; *maliṇā—*polluted; *bhaṇīadi—*is said*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā (fearfully hides behind Viśākhā): My friend, save your own self, for if Rādhā is polluted, Viśākhā will be impure too.**

**Комментарий:**

By protecting Rādhā’s dignity, Viśākhā will protect her’s too. Expressing in this way Her unity with Viśākhā, Rādhā gives a sign to Lalitā that the latter is out of their friendly bond.

### Verse 473

***lalitā****: a{i} gandhavvie, dhutta-ma{u}liṇā luddhaeṇa aṇuddudā tumaṁ kīsa ṇaṁ pañca-muhīṁ mukkia ekkaṁ kuraṅgiaṁ saraṇaṁ gacchasi? tā maha aṅkam alaṅkarehi turiaṁ saṅkāulo bhavia palāedu eso.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*a{i}—*O; *gandhavvie—*female antelope; *dhutta—*of cheaters; *ma{u}liṇā—*the chief; *luddhaeṇa—*by hunter; *aṇuddudā—*are chased after; *tumaṁ—*You; *kīsa—*why; *ṇaṁ—*this; *pañca-muhīṁ—*lioness; *mukkia—*leaving; *ekkaṁ—*alone; *kuraṅgiaṁ—*of the doe; *saraṇaṁ—*to the shelter; *gacchasi—*are going; *tā—*therefore; *maha—*my; *aṅkaṁ—*lap; *alaṅkarehi—*decorate; *turiaṁ—*quickly; *saṅkā—*with fear; *āulo—*overwhelmed; *bhavia—*becoming; *palāedu—*will escape; *eso—*He*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: Ah female antelope, chased after by the most cunning hunter, why have You left the lioness and are now seeking shelter of this doe? Just sit on my lap, and this hunter will quickly run away, full of fear.**

**Комментарий:**

The intended meaning: “Ah Gāndharvikā (the name of Rādhā), if You are chased after by this most cunning rake (*lubdhakena*), why are You seeking shelter of this doe (referring to Viśākhā), leaving the leader of our quintet (*pañca-mukhīm—*speaking of herself)? Just sit on my lap (stay close to me), and this rake will quickly run away, full of fear.”

### Verse 474

***rādhikā:*** *(kutukena bhūriṇā bhrū-bhaṅgenādhikṣipantīva sa-narma-smitaṁ saṁskṛtena)*  
*viśrambha-ghātini cirād uparudhya śuddhā*  
*viśrambhatas tvam iha naḥ sva-gṛhād anaiṣīḥ*  
*lobhād vrataṁ yadi nijaṁ vyadhunos tad astu*  
*kiṁ dūṣayanty api satīs trapase na devi*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kutukena—*with eagerness; *bhūriṇā—*big; *bhrū-bhaṅgena—*with a frown; *adhikṣipantī—*vilifying; *iva—*as if; *sa-narma—*playful; *smitam—*with a smile; *saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *viśrambha—*of faith; *ghātini—*O the breaker; *cirāt—*for long time; *uparudhya—*halting; *śuddhā—*pure; *viśrambhataḥ—*out of trust; *tvam—*you; *iha—*here; *naḥ—*us; *sva-gṛhāt—*from our homes; *anaiṣīḥ—*brought; *lobhāt—*out of greed; *vratam—*vow; *yadi—*if; *nijam—*your; *vyadhunoḥ—*gave up; *tat—*that; *astu—*let be; *kim—*whether; *dūṣayanti—*disgracing; *api—*also; *satīḥ—*the chaste girls; *trapase na—*not feel ashamed; *devi—*O devi.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhikā (As if vehemently vilifying Lalitā with Her big frown, speaks in Sanskrit with a playful smile): You traitor, you led us trustful pure-hearted girls from our homes to this place, halting us here for such a long time! Never mind if you gave up your vow out of greed, but do you want to also disgrace us, chaste girls? Don’t you feel ashamed, o devi?**

**Комментарий:**

The vow that, according to Rādhā, Lalitā has given up, is not to even look at a lusty person while carrying ghee to the sacrifice (text 456). The gopīs have broken the vow because Lalitā was too oblivious in quarreling with Kṛṣṇa.

Calling Lalitā *‘devi’* (“an exalted lady”), Rādhā sarcastically implies the opposite.

### Verse 475-476

***lalitā:***  *haddhī haddhī! sahi vunde, bhaṇāhi kadhaṁ suddhā huvissaṁ.*  
***vṛndā:***  *lalite, kṛtam etayā cintā-caryayā. nikuñja-mahā-tīrthe rati-vallabha-jāgaryā-vrate prārabdhe kā tāvad aghasya sambhāvanāpi?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*haddhī haddhī—*alas, alas; *sahi vunde—*O sakhī Vṛndā; *bhaṇāhi—*tell; *kadhaṁ—*how; *suddhā—*pure; *huvissaṁ—*I will become*. lalite—*O Lalitā; *kṛtam—*done; *etayā—*with this; *cintā-caryayā—*worrisome conduct; *nikuñja—*forest grove; *mahā-tīrthe—*in the great holy place; *rati-vallabha—*of the Rati’s beloved (Kāmadeva); *jāgaryā—*of awakening; *vrate—*when the religious observance; *prārabdhe—*is commenced; *kā—*which; *tāvat—*at all; *aghasya—*for sin; *sambhāvanā—*the possibility; *api—*even.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: Alas, o Vṛndā, my friend, alas! Tell me how can I become purified.**

**Vṛndā: No worries, Lalitā. Is there at all even a chance to commit a sin in this sacred forest grove, when the sacred all-night festival of Kāmadeva has just begun?**

**Комментарий:**

Vṛndā hints that this so-called quarrel over the tax is a precursor to the all-night amorous pastimes between Kṛṣṇa and the gopīs.

## The Ring, Necklace, and Hungry Brāhmaṇa

### Verse 477-479

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *keli-kutūhalitayā kutaḥ sva-karmaṇi mantharo ’smi yad adya śulkārtham udyamaḥ khalu sādhīyān?*  
***nāndīmukhī:***  *lalide, pekkha paccāsīdadi majjhaṇṇo. tā kadhīadu kettio sulukko tumhāṇaṁ sammado.*  
***lalitā:***  *hanta dāṇinda, ja{i}bi amhāṇaṁ pañca-pāiā ccea ettha juttā tahabi tumha-muhaṁ avekkhia esā maṇi-muddiā ubaṇīdā. (iti citrāṅguler ākṛṣya mudrikāṁ purastād upanyasyati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*keli—*for the pastime; *kutūhalitayā—*due to the eagerness; *kutaḥ—*why; *sva-karmaṇi—*in My own work; *mantharaḥ—*slow; *asmi—*I; *yat—*if; *adya—*today; *śulka-artham—*for the sake of the fee; *udyamaḥ—*the undertaking; *khalu—*indeed; *sādhīyān—*better*. lalide—*Lalitā; *pekkha—*look; *paccāsīdadi—*is near; *majjhaṇṇo—*noon; *tā—*thus; *kadhīadu—*should be said; *kettio—*how much; *sulukko—*fee; *tumhāṇaṁ—*your; *sammado—*approved. *hanta—*alas; *dāṇi-inda—*O chief of tax-collectors; *ja{i}bi—*even if; *amhāṇaṁ—*our; *pañca-pāiā—*five *pādikās*; *ccea—*only; *ettha—*in this matter; *juttā—*appropriate; *tahabi—*still; *tumha-muhaṁ—*your faces; *avekkhia—*considering; *esā—*this; *maṇi-muddiā—*jeweled ring; *ubaṇīdā—*brought forward;*iti—*thus; *citrā-aṅguleḥ—*from Citrā’s finger; *ākṛṣya—*having pulled; *mudrikām—*the ring; *purastāt—*in front; *upanyasyati—*places.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: If it is better to exact the tax now, why I became so slow in My real business, eager after flirting?**

**Nāndīmukhī: Lalitā, look, it’s almost noon. Tell, then, how much you have agreed to pay.**

**Lalitā: O chief tax collector, though in this case it is proper for us to give only five pādikās, still, after looking at your faces, we submit this jeweled ring. (Pulling the ring off from Citrā’s finger, brings it in front of Kṛṣṇa.)**

**Комментарий:**

A sum of fourty *kapardikās* (cowrie shells) is called *pādikā.*

### Verse 480

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(sa-vyājāmarṣam) sakhe, kṣipraṁ kṣipyatām adri-mūrdhani kṣudreyaṁ mudrikā. (subalaḥ prakṣepa-mudrām abhinīya mudrikāṁ kare saṅgopayati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-vyāja—*with pretense; *āmarṣam—*angrily; *sakhe—*O friend; *kṣipram—*quickly; *kṣipyatām—*should be thrown; *adri—*of the hill; *mūrdhani—*on the top; *kṣudrā—*petty; *iyam—*this; *mudrikā—*ring; *subalaḥ—*Subala; *prakṣepa—*of throwing; *mudrām—*the gesture; *abhinīya—*showing; *mudrikām—*the ring; *kare—*in his hand; *saṅgopayati—*hides.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (pretends to be angry): My friend, throw at once this worthless ring to the top of the hill.**

**(Subala makes the throwing movement, but hides the ring in his hand)**

### Verse 481-482

***lalitā:*** *(sa-roṣam) vunde, diṭṭhaṁ tue jaṁ dullahā maṇi-muddiā pakkhittā?*  
***nāndīmukhī:***  *halā, jaha tassa ṇaa-ṇihīṇaṁ ahiba{i}ṇo kuberassa mahā-cintāmaṇi-maṇīsideṇa sādara-pasāride hatthe phuṭṭa-kapaddiā-ṇikkhebo taha jevva eso tumha-vavahāro.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-roṣam—*with anger; *vunde—*O Vṛndā; *diṭṭhaṁ—*seen; *tue—*by you; *jaṁ—*that; *dullahā—*hardly obtainable; *maṇi-muddiā—*jeweled ring; *pakkhittā—*was thrown. *halā—*O; *jaha—*as; *tassa—*this; *ṇaa-ṇihīṇaṁ—*of the nine treasures; *ahiba{i}ṇo—*the master; *kuberassa—*of Kuvera; *mahā-cintāmaṇi—*for great *cintāmaṇi* jewel; *maṇīsideṇa—*out of desire; *sa-ādara—*respectfully; *pasāride—*is stretched; *hatthe—*when the hand; *phuṭṭa—*of holed; *kapaddiā—*a *kapardikā*; *ṇikkhebo—*rejection; *taha—*likewise; *jevva—*only; *eso—*this; *tumha—*your; *vavahāro—*behavior*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (angrily): Vṛndā, did you see how they threw away this rare jeweled ring?**

**Nāndīmukhī: O sakhī, you behaved just as a person who throws a holed kapardikā into the respectfully outstretched hands of Kuvera, the master of the nine treasures, who desires to receive a priceless cintāmaṇi jewel.**

**Комментарий:**

Speaking to Lalitā, Nāndīmukhī means that Citrā’s jeweled ring is not at all a worthy object to give to Kṛṣṇa, who definitely expects something more valuable.

### Verse 483-485

***lalitā:***  *(sva-gatam) doṇaṁ suṭṭhu ukkaṇṭhidāṇaṁ āsāsaṇaṁ bhaṅgīe karissaṁ.*  
*(iti parikramya janāntikam) halā rāhi, jadhārihaṁ dāṇaṁ viṇā dullahaṁ amhāṇaṁ ido patthāṇaṁ. tā tuha kaṇṭha-ṭṭhidaṁ hāraṁ ccea sulukkī-karemha. (iti balād iva hāram uttārya sa-narma-smitam)*  
*ukkaṇṭhide, kīsa adhīrāsi? esā ṇisiṭṭhatthā mottiāvalī dūdī kaṇhaṁ alaṅkāduṁ calidā. tā ahisāre sajjā hohi!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sva-gatam—*to herself; *doṇaṁ—*of the both; *suṭṭhu—*well; *ukkaṇṭhidāṇaṁ—*who are eager; *āsāsaṇaṁ—*the encouragement; *bhaṅgīe—*by tortuous talk; *karissaṁ—*I will do; *iti—*thus; *parikramya—*walking around; *jana-antikam—*to the side; *halā rāhi—*O Rādhā; *jadhārihaṁ—*suitable; *dāṇaṁ—*paying; *viṇā—*without; *dullahaṁ—*hard to achieve; *amhāṇaṁ—*for us; *ido—*from here; *patthāṇaṁ—*departure; *tā—*thus; *tuha—*Your; *kaṇṭha-ṭṭhidaṁ—*situated on Your neck; *hāraṁ—*the necklace; *ccea—*only; *sulukkī-karemha—*we will convert into the fee; *iti—*thus; *balāt—*with force; *iva—*slight; *hāram—*the necklace; *uttārya—*removing; *sa-narma—*playful; *smitam—*with a smile; *ukkaṇṭhide—*O longing One; *kīsa—*why; *adhīrā—*restless; *asi—*You are; *esā—*this; *ṇisiṭṭha-atthā—*a kind of female messenger (*nisṛṣṭārthā*); *mottiā—*of pearls; *āvalī—*necklace; *dūdī—*a messenger; *kaṇhaṁ—*Kṛṣṇa; *alaṅkāduṁ—*to decorate; *calidā—*gone; *tā—*thus; *ahisāre—*for a rendezvous; *sajjā—*prepared; *hohi—*be*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (to herself): Let me cheer up both of them, who are so eager for each other, with some ambiguous words.**

**(after walking around, speaks to the side) He Rādhe, it will be difficult for us to leave from here without paying a suitable amount. So let’s give Your necklace as the fee.**

**(removes the necklace with a slight force and speaks with a playful smile) O longing girl, why are You so restless? This pearl necklace is a messenger that has departed to decorate Kaṇha. So be ready for a rendezvous!**

**Комментарий:**

Lalitā calls Rādhā’s pearl neclace a *nisṛṣṭārthā* messenger:

*vinyasta-kārya-bhārā syād*  
*dvayor ekatareṇa yā*  
*yuktyobhau ghaṭayed*  
*eṣā niṛṣṭārthā nigadyate*

**“*****Nisṛṣṭārthā*** **is a type of female messenger, who thoughtfully arrange for a meeting of a boy and a girl, being entrusted with this task by either one of them.”**

### Verse 486

***rādhikā:*** *a{i} sambhoa-saṁrambhiṇi, alaṁ imiṇā dambha-gambhīrimārambheṇa! ettha vivāda-mahāmahe adakkhiṇābi tumaṁ dakkhiṇāsi ṇimmidā paṇaeṇa sahīhiṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ai sambhoa-saṁrambhiṇi—*O coveter of union; *alam—*enough; *imiṇā—*of this; *dambha—*deceitful; *gambhīrimā—*of gravity; *ārambheṇa—*undertaking; *ettha—*in this; *vivāda—*of argument; *mahā-mahe—*great celebration; *adakkhiṇā—*clumsy; *abi—*though; *tumaṁ—*you; *dakkhiṇāsi—*expert; *ṇimmidā—*are maid; *paṇaeṇa—*out of love; *sahīhiṁ—*by your friends*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhā: Hey, you only want that we unite! Enough of this show of sobriety! It is your friends, who out of love made you expert in this great dispute festival, even though you are clumsy.**

**Комментарий:**

The intended meaning of the last sentence: “It is your friends, who out of love made you agreeable (*dakkhiṇā—‘dakṣiṇā’*) in this great dispute festival, even though you are refractory (*adakkhiṇā—‘adakṣiṇā’*).” Rādhā makes a point that although Lalitā is a typical *vāma* girl, she now behaves with Kṛṣṇa in an unusually soft manner, for she is repeatedly rebuked by her friends, who seemingly expel her from their circle for being a betrayer, “polluted” by Kṛṣṇa’s touch.

*asahā māna-nirbandhe*  
*nāyake yukta-vādinī*  
*sāmabhis tena bhedyā ca*  
*dakṣiṇā parikīrtitā*

**“A girl who cannot bear to persist with her resentment, who speks to her lover in a pleasing manner, and is pliable by his gentle words, is called** ***dakṣiṇā*****.”**

Rādhā addresses Lalitā *‘sambhoa-saārambhiṇi’* (*‘sambhoga-samrambhiṇi’—*“desirous of the union”)*,* which also suggests that the latter has changed her *vāma* disposition out of the desire to bring this tax pastime to fruition in the form of the loving union (*sambhoga*) of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

### Verse 487

***lalitā:***  *(kṛṣṇam avekṣya) ghaṭṭa-ṇāha, esā aṇagghā mottiāvalī mae upaṇihī-kidā. ado padose suvaṇṇobaṇaeṇa puṇo tuatto moāva{i\]davvā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kṛṣṇam—*at Kṛṣṇa; *avekṣya—*looking; *ghaṭṭa-ṇāha—*O toll station master; *esā—*this; *aṇagghā—*priceless; *mottiā-āvalī—*pearl necklace; *mae—*by me; *upaṇihī-kidā—*is deposited; *ado—*therefore; *padose—*in the evening; *suvaṇṇa—*gold; *ubaṇaeṇa—*by bringing; *puṇo—*again; *tuatto—*from You; *moāva{i}davvā—*should be taken out*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (looking at Kṛṣṇa): O toll gate supervisor, since this priceless pearl necklace is a collateral, I should bring gold in the evening and take the necklace from You back.**

**Комментарий:**

*‘Suvaṇṇobaṇaeṇa’* (*‘suvarna-upanayena’—* “by bringing (*upanayena*) gold (*suvarṇa*)”) could also be read as *‘suvaṇṇā-ubaṇaeṇa’* (*‘suvarṇā-upanayena’*)*,* and understood as “by bringing the golden girl (*‘suvarṇā’*),” which refers to Rādhārāṇī. Thus Lalitā states: “…I should bring golden Rādhā in the evening and take the necklace from You back.”

### Verse 488

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(sa-harṣaṁ hāram ādāya sva-gatam) so ’yaṁ śaṅkhacūḍasya cūḍā-maṇir eva nāyakī-kṛto ’sti yaḥ sānukampam āryeṇa pralamba-ripuṇā rādhikāyai prasādī-kṛtaḥ. tad anena mamādhunā pratyāśā-bījasyāṅkurāvasthatā vistṛtā.(iti hāreṇa sva-kaṇṭhaṁ prasādhayati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-harṣam—*with joy; *hāram—*the necklace; *ādāya—*taking; *sva-gatam—*to Himself; *saḥ ayam—*that very; *śaṅkhacūḍasya—*of Śaṅkhacūḍa; *cūḍā-maṇiḥ—*crest jewel; *eva—*only; *nāyakī—*a central jewel; *kṛtaḥ—*made; *asti—*is; *yaḥ—*which; *sa-anukampam—*with compassion; *āryeṇa—*by the noble; *pralamba-ripuṇā—*enemy of Pralamba; *rādhikāyai—*to Rādhikā; *prasādī-kṛtaḥ—*gifted; *tat—*thus; *anena—*by it; *mama—*My; *adhunā—*now; *pratyāśā—*of hope; *bījasya—*of seed; *aṅkura—*of being a creeper; *avasthatā—*the condition; *vistṛtā—*is expanded; *iti—*thus; *hāreṇa—*with the necklace; *sva-kaṇṭham—*His neck; *prasādhayati—*adorns.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (joyfully taking the necklace, speaks to Himself): That same crest jewel of Śaṅkhacūḍa, which the noble enemy of Pralamba has presented to Rādhikā with sympathy, has become a central jewel in this necklace. In this way, it has now expanded the creeper of My hope.**

**(adorns His neck with the necklace)**

**Комментарий:**

“The noble enemy of Pralamba” is Balarāma, since it was He who killed the demon.

After killing Śaṅkhacūḍa, Kṛṣṇa removed the jewel from the head of the demon and presented it to Balarāma, who, in turn, gave it to Rādhikā. The jewel then was placed in Rādhikā’s necklace.

### Verse 489

***rādhikā:*** *(janāntikam) lalide, pekkha bhāadheaṁ tavassiṇīe mottiāvalīe!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*jana-antikam—*to the side; *lalide—*O Lalitā; *pekkha—*look; *bhāadheaṁ—*at the fortune; *tavassiṇīe—*of an ascetic; *mottiā-āvalīe—*the pearl necklace*.*

**Перевод:**

**Rādhikā (to the side): O Lalitā, look at the fortune of this ascetic, the pearl necklace!**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā means that Her pearl necklace must have undergone severe austerities to have the fortune of clinging to Kṛṣṇa’s neck. No doubt, She craves to be there too.

### Verse 490

***lalitā:***  
*tuha ṇisevia uṇa rāhi*  
*tthaṇa-sambhuṁ mottiāvalī suddhā*  
*hariṇo vihara{i} hiae*  
*tuha kahaṇijjo kahaṁ mahimā*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tuha—*Your; *ṇisevia—*having served; *uṇa—*again; *rāhi—*O Rādhā; *tthaṇa—*of breast; *sambhuṁ—*Śambhu-like; *mottiā-āvalī—*the pearl necklace; *suddhā—*pure; *hariṇo—*of Hari; *viharai—*sports; *hiae—*on the chest; *tuha—*Your; *kahaṇijjo—*could be described; *kahaṁ—*how; *mahimā—*glory.

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: O Rādhe, after serving Your Śambhu-like breast this spotless pearl necklace is now playing on the chest of Hari. How then can we describe Your glory?**

**Комментарий:**

“If this is the glory of Rādhikā’s pearl necklace, that it has attained the neck of Hari by hanging on Your breast, what is then the extent of *Your* glory? It is simply undescribable.” In this way Lalitā cheers up Rādhā, who feels jealous of the pearl necklace.

Lord Śiva is worshiped in the form of *liṅga*—a thick stone cylinder with a rounded top. Rādhā’s breast is compared to a *śiva-liṅga* for it shares some of its qualities—it is also firm, thick and round in shape.

Another meaning of *‘mottiāvalī’* (*‘mauktikāvalī’*) is “the hords (*āvalī*) of liberation-seekers (*mauktika*).” Lalitā compares the “journey” of Rādhā’s necklace from Her breast to Hari’s neck to the journey of some transcendentalists, who, in their pursuit of liberation, worship Lord Śiva. Some of such devotees of Śiva are especially fortunate—by the mercy of Lord Śiva they become purified (*suddhā*) to the extent that they finally take shelter of the Supreme Lord Hari. This is confirmed by Jīva Gosvāmī:

*janmāntara-sahasreṣu*  
*samārādhya vṛṣa-dhvajam*  
*vaiṣṇavatvaṁ labhed dhīmān*  
*sarva-pāpa-kṣaye sati*

**“By worshipping Lord Śiva \[Vṛṣa-dhvaja\] for many thousands of births one becomes free from all sins. Then one can become a** ***vaiṣṇava*****.”**

### Verse 491-492

***rādhikā:*** *kuḍile, alaṁ palābeṇa. pekkhīadu ido bi pa{u}raṁ bhaṅgurāe bhamara-kalaṅkidāe vaṇa-mālāe sohagga-līlāidaṁ! (iti saṁskṛtam āśritya)*  
*viśuddhābhiḥ sārdhaṁ vraja-hariṇa-netrābhir aniśaṁ*  
*tvam addhā vidveṣaṁ kim iti vana-māle racayasi*  
*tṛṇī-kurvaty asmān vapur agha-ripor āśikham idaṁ*  
*pariṣvajyāpādaṁ mahati hṛdaye yā viharasi*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kuḍile—*O cunning one; *alaṁ—*enough; *palābeṇa—*of talking; *pekkhīadu—*may be seen; *ido—*than that; *bi—*even; *pa{u}raṁ—*greater; *bhaṅgurāe—*curved; *bhamara—*by the bees; *kalaṅkidāe—*stained; *vaṇa-mālāe—*of the forest garland; *sohagga—*fortunate; *līlāidaṁ—*dalliance; *iti—*thus; *saṁskṛtam—*Sanskrit; *āśritya—*turning to; *viśuddhābhiḥ—*pure; *sārdham—*with; *vraja—*of Vraja; *hariṇa-netrābhiḥ—*with the doe-eyed girls; *aniśam—*incessantly; *tvam—*You; *addhā—*directly; *vidveṣam—*enmity; *kim iti—*why; *vana-māle—*O garland of forest flowers; *racayasi—*you create; *tṛṇī-kurvati—*makes into grass; *asmān—*us; *vapuḥ—*body; *agha-ripoḥ—*of the enemy of Agha; *āśikham—*up to the top of His head; *idam—*this; *pariṣvajyā—*embracing; *āpādam—*down to His feet; *mahati—*big; *hṛdaye—*on the chest; *yā—*who; *viharasi—*are sporting.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhikā: O cunning girl, enough of your talk. Look at that crooked forest garland stained by the bees! It is gracefully dallying even more than My pearl necklace!**

**(in Sanskrit): O forest garland, why do you openly make enemies with the innocent doe-eyed maidens of Vraja all the time? You play on the broad chest of Hari and hang down to His feet. By embracing the body of Agha’s enemy from His feet up to the top of His head, You turn us into straw!**

**Комментарий:**

Rādhā reproaches Lalitā by describing Kṛṣṇa’s garland as more fortunate than Her pearl necklace: “Just look at that lucky forest garland! I have no any relationship with it. Therefore, you simply hurt Me by praising My so-called good fortune!”

From the way Rādhā describes Kṛṣṇa’s garland—“crooked” and “stained” by the bees—Rādhā seems to be extremely envious of it. This envy, however surprising it may appear, indicates *mādana-mahābhāva*—the topmost grade of love for Kṛṣṇa that only Rādhā could possess. *Madana-mahābhāva* is defined as follows:

*sarva-bhāvodgamollāsī*  
*mādano’yaṁ parāt paraḥ*  
*rājate hlādinī-sāro*  
*rādhāyām eva yaḥ sadā*

**“*****Mādana(-mahābhāva)*** **is the topmost stage of love for Kṛṣṇa, surpessing even** ***mohana(-mahābhāva)*****. It arises when all other** ***bhāvas*** **are manifest. The essence of** ***hlādinī*** **potency, it always brilliantly appears exclusively in Rādhā.”**

Envy is one of the distinctive symptoms of *mādana-mahābhāva*: *‘atrerṣyāyā ayogye ’pi prabalerṣā-vidhāyitā’*—*“*In *mādana-mahābhāva*, the beloved displays extreme envy to an otherwise non-enviable object.”

The Sanskrit verse beginning *‘viśuddhābhiḥ’* in which Rādhā blames the garland is quoted in *Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi* 14.222 as an example of envy that springs from *madana-mahābhāva*. Rādhā’s words are paraphrased by the *ācāryas*as follows: “O forest garland, why do you make enemies with us, pure-hearted girls, who do not deserve to be so treated? After all, we never make enemies with you. You, however, are not only inimical to us, but also show your enmity openly and all the time by embracing the body of Agha’s enemy from His feet up to the top of His head. Wherever He may go, You keep on dallying on His broad chest, not leaving it for a moment. In this way you scorn us, who have given up everything just for His sake.”

### Verse 493

***madhumangalaḥ:***  *kallāṇi lalide, mahā-ghaṭṭa-bālindo tumhehiṁ āṇandido tā eso buhukkhido ekkāe heaṅgavīṇa-gabbhāe gurua-gaggarīe kāa-ttho bi kāa-ttho kijja{u}.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kallāṇi—*auspicious; *lalide—*O Lalitā; *mahā-ghaṭṭa-bāla—*of the greatest of tax collectors; *indo—*the chief; *tumhehiṁ—*by you; *āṇandido—*made happy; *tā—*thus; *eso—*this; *buhukkhido—*hungry person; *ekkāe—*one; *heaṅgavīṇa—*butter; *gabbhāe—*in whose belly; *gurua—*very heavy; *ggagarīe—*of a pot; *kāa-ttho—*situated in the body; *bi—*though; *kāa-ttho—*situated in his body; *kijja{u}—*should be*.*

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala: O auspicious Lalitā, you made the supreme toll gate officer very happy. Therefore, even though this hungly person is now inside the body of your very heavy pot of ghee, please bring his soul back to his own body.**

**Комментарий:**

“…even though I am now inside the body (*kāa-ttho—‘kāya-sthaḥ,’* or literally, “situated in the body”) of your very heavy pot of ghee” means that the ghee in Lalitā’s pot is Madhumaṅgala’s life and soul. And by saying “…please bring his soul back to his own body,” Madhumaṅgala requests Lalitā to feed him some of her ghee, which would bring his soul back to his body.

Madhumaṅgala is playing with the word *kāyastha*, which conventionally means “clerk.” This is what Madhumaṅgala is in Kṛṣṇa’s toll station staff, as he himself declares in text 270. Also in text 314 Kṛṣṇa calls Madhumaṅgala *‘kāyasthikā-rasavatī-pauragavaḥ,’* or “the chef of burocratic ‘kitchen.’”

### Verse 494

***viśākhā:***  *haṁho loluha, mā kkhu evvaṁ bhaṇāhi. satta-tantuṇo kira heaṅgavīṇaṁ edaṁ.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*haṁho loluha—*O greedy one; *mā—*do not; *kkhu—*indeed; *evvaṁ—*like this; *bhaṇāhi—*speak; *satta-tantuṇo—*for the sacrifice; *kira—*indeed; *heaṅgavīṇaṁ—*ghee; *edaṁ—*this*.*

**Перевод:**

**Viśākhā: O greedy boy, don’t speak like this. This ghee is for the sacrifice.**

**Комментарий:**

Vedic fire sacrifice is called *‘sapta-tantu’* (*’satta-tantuṇo’* in the text), or “seven-threaded,” because such sacrifice has seven parts, which are like its threads. Also, the names of the seven worlds are chanted—*bhūr*, *bhuvaḥ*, *svaḥ*, *mahaḥ*, *janaḥ*, *tapaḥ*, and *satya*. Another reason for this name is because fire, in which an oblation is offered, has seven tongues.

### Verse 495

***madhumangalaḥ:***  *visāhe, dhaṇṇāo kkhu jaṇṇia-bamhaṇīo jāhiṁ appa-gharassa bi taṁ aṅgirasa-sattaṁ ubekkhia tassa suṭṭhu miṭṭhaṇṇehiṁ savve ballaā bhuñjāvidā. tumhehiṁ uṇa para-gharassa satta-tantuṇo joggehiṁ bi ṇaaṇīdehiṁ ṇava-tantuo bi ekkalo eso bi ṇa bhuñjāvīadi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*visāhe—*O Viśākhā; *dhaṇṇāo—*pious; *kkhu—*indeed; *jaṇṇia—*of the priests; *bamhaṇīo—*the wives of *brāhmaṇas*; *jāhiṁ—*by whom; *appa—*own; *gharassa—*for house; *bi—*even; *taṁ—*that; *aṅgirasa-sattaṁ—āṅgirasa* sacrifice; *ubekkhia—*neglecting; *tassa—*its; *suṭṭhu—*well; *miṭṭhaṇṇehiṁ—*with sweets; *savve—*all; *ballaā—*cowherd boys; *bhuñjāvidā—*were fed; *tumhehiṁ—*by you; *uṇa—*again; *para—*of another; *gharassa—*for the house; *satta-tantuṇo—*for the sacrifice; *joggehiṁ—*suitable; *bi—*also; *ṇaaṇīdehiṁ—*with ghee; *ṇava-tantuo—brāhmaṇa*; *bi—*even; *ekkalo—*one; *eso—*this; *bi—*even; *ṇa bhuñjāvīadi—*not being fed.

**Перевод:**

**Madhumaṅgala: Viśākhā, indeed pious are those wives of brāhmaṇas, who neglected the āṅgirasa sacrifice, even though it was performed for their benefit, and fed all the cowherd boys with sacrificial sweets. You, on the contrary, haven’t fed even this one brāhmaṇa with ghee which is meant for somebody else’s sacrifice.**

**Комментарий:**

Madhumaṅgala refers to a pastime of Kṛṣṇa’s showing favor to the wives of the *brāhmaṇas*. It is described in the twenty third chapter of the tenth canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.*

As if echoing Viśākhā, who described the fire sacrifice as “that which has seven threads,” Madhumaṅgala calls himself “one who has nine threads” (*‘ṇava-tantuo’—‘nava-tantukaḥ’*), a term referring to a person who wears a sacred thread, a *brāhmaṇa*. A sacred thread is composed of different number of strands that signifies the stage of life a *brāhmaṇa* is going through. Three stands are worn by a *brahmacārī* who is undergoing his studies. Three other are added when a *brahmacārī* is directly engaged in performing *arcaṇa* or *yajñas*. After completing the formal education, a *brahmacārī* is called a *snātaka* (“a graduate”). At that stage three more stands are added to his thread (making the total number nine), signifying his gradual movement toward the *gṛhastha-āśrama*, or family life. So, by using here the word *‘nava-tantukaḥ,’* Madhumaṅgala defines himself to be a *snātaka.*

### Verse 496

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *lalite, yad eṣa mahā-ghaṭṭeśvarasya mamopahārāya hāro nisṛṣṭas tad atīva samyag anuṣṭhitam. sāmpratam udyāna-cakravartino ’py abhīṣṭa-śulkena saparyā paryālocyatām.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*lalite—*O Lalitā; *yat—*because; *eṣa—*this; *mahā—*great; *ghaṭṭa-īśvarasya—*the toll gate supervisor; *mama—*to Me; *upahārāya—*for presenting; *hāraḥ—*the necklace; *nisṛṣṭaḥ—*given up; *tat—*then; *atīva—*very much; *samyak—*correctly; *anuṣṭhitam—*done; *sāmpratam—*now; *udyāna-cakravartinaḥ—*the grove emperor; *api—*also; *abhīṣṭa—*desired; *śulkena—*with a fee; *saparyā—*honoring; *paryālocyatām—*should be considered.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Lalitā, it is very correct that you have chosen to present the necklace to Me, the toll gate supervisor. Now please think of how to honor the emperor of forest groves with a fee that he desires the most.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa acknowledges Rādhā’s necklace that Lalitā gave Him. However, He doesn’t consider this to be the tax. Instead, He now demands for something else that would really please His Majesty Mahā-manmatha.

### Verse 497

***lalitā:***  *(sa-praṇaya-roṣam) tue jāṇia pattamhi. tā ṇa kkhu ajuttā esā viḍambaṇa-kadatthaṇā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-praṇaya—*with love; *roṣam—*angrily; *tue—*by You; *jāṇia—*aware; *pattamhi—*I am obtained; *tā—*thus; *ṇa—*not; *kkhu—*indeed; *ajuttā—*improper; *esā—*this; *viḍambaṇa—*by abusing; *kadatthaṇā—*torture*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (with loving anger): I knowingly allowed You to deceive me, and so, it is not at all strange that You took advantage and are now harassing us.**

**Комментарий:**

“I gave You the necklace with the idea that it was the total tax. You, however, took it as Your share only. In this way, You have cheated me so easily.”

## Paurṇamāsī Arrives

### Verse 498

***nāndīmukhī:***  *mahā-dāṇinda, appaṇo ahimadaṁ dāṇaṁ diḍhaṁ kahijja{u} jahā majjhathī-bhavia mae paricchijja{i}.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mahā-dāṇi-inda—*O chief of the greatest tax collectors; *appaṇo—*for You; *ahimadaṁ—*approved; *dāṇaṁ—*tax; *diḍhaṁ—*firmly; *kahijja{u}—*tell; *jahā—*as; *majjhathī-bhavia—*being neutral; *mae—*by me; *paricchijja{i}—*will be divided.

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: O supreme tax collector, tell us once and forever the amount You approve of, and I, as a neutral person, shall divide it.**

## Verse 499(a-b)

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *nāndīmukhi, samākarṇyatām*  
*vaditum adhikam āryā-pāriṣadyās tavāgre*  
*katham ucitam atheṣṭaṁ kevalaṁ me parārdham*  
*iha yadi tad-abhāvaṁ vakṣi kiṁ tatra kuryāṁ*  
*bhavatu mayi parārdhyāṁ nyasya śiṣṭāḥ prayāntu*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nāndīmukhi—*O Nāndīmukhī; *samākarṇyatām—*listen; *vaditum—*to speak; *adhikam—*a lot; *āryā—*of the revered lady; *pāriṣadyāḥ—*of the councelor; *tava—*of you; *agre—*in front; *katham—*how; *ucitam—*is it proper; *atha—*then; *iṣṭam—*desired; *kevalam—*only; *me—*by Me; *parārdham—*100,000,000,000,000,000 golden *taṅkas*; *iha—*here; *yadi—*if; *tat—*its; *abhāvam—*of absence; *vakṣi—*you speak; *kim—*what; *tatra—*in that case; *kuryām—*should I do; *bhavatu—*let it be; *mayi—*to Me; *parārdhyām—*the sum of one *parārdha*; *nyasya—*depositing; *śiṣṭāḥ—*the noble girls; *prayāntu—*may proceed.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Nāndīmukhī, please listen: Is it proper for Me to speak in front of you, Paurṇamāsī’s councelor? Anyway, I only want one parārdha, and if you say that they haven’t got it, what can I do? So let the matter be settled: The noble girls may proceed on, after handing over to Me the sum of one parārdha of golden ṭaṅkas.**

**Комментарий:**

One *parārdha* of *ṭaṅkas* equals 200,000,000,000 tons.

The confidential meaning of the verse, however, is that Kṛṣṇa asks Nāndīmukhī that Rādhā be handed over to Him. *Mahaṭī-ṭīkā* states that with the word *‘parārdhyā’* in the last line, Kṛṣṇa actually describes Rādhārāṇī, His eternal consort. Surendranath Shastri elaborates that apart from meaning “a sum of one *parārdha*,” *‘parārdhyā’* means “one who is considered to be a better (*para*) half (*ardha*).” Thus, Kṛṣṇa says, “All right, I do not need one *parārdha* of golden *ṭaṅkas* if you don’t have it. I want the most excellent Rādhā—She alone, and not gold, will fulfill My purpose. The rest (*ṣiṣṭāḥ*) of the gopīs may proceed on.”

### Verse 500

***nāndīmukhī:***  *raṅgilla-puṅgava, cittā tumha cittāṇuvaṭṭiṇī. tā esā ccea sulukkāidā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*raṅgilla-puṅgava—*O best of the passionate; *cittā—*Citrā; *tumha—*to Your; *citta—*wishes; *aṇuvaṭṭiṇī—*agreeable; *tā—*thus; *esā—*she; *ccea—*only; *sulukkāidā—*fit for the fee*.*

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: O most passionate boy, Citrā is agreeable to Your wishes. So, she is the only one fit to be the fee.**

**Комментарий:**

Nāndīmukhī can say this about Citrā, because she is the youngest of the five gopīs (Rādhā, Lalitā, Viśākhā, Campakalatā, and Citrā). Nāndīmukhī wouldn’t dare to say this about Lalitā, for example. In this way, Nāndīmukhī observes the subtle etiquette even in the midst of this hilarious pastime.

Citrā is a gopī of an extremely gentle character. That is why Nāndīmukhī says that she is agreeable to Kṛṣṇa’s wishes.

### Verse 501

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *hastopakaṇṭha-vartinī citrā. tad asau nātidurlabhā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*hasta-upakaṇṭh —*at hand; *vartinī—*standing; *citrā—*Citrā; *tat—*thus; *asau—*she; *na atidurlabhā—*not very difficult to obtain*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Citrā is just at hand. So it is not very difficult to get her.**

### Verse 502

***paurṇamāsī:*** *(praviśya) nāgara nāgarī-mūrdhābhiṣikta, yatra nibaddha-mahā-spṛho ’si tāṁ kila parārdhenāpi durlabhām anarghām eva jānīhi.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nāgara—*O suave boy; *nāgarī—*of beautiful girls; *mūrdha—*on the head; *abhiṣikta—*installed; *yatra—*in whom; *nibaddha—*fixed; *mahā-spṛhaḥ—*whose great desire; *asi—*You; *tām—*Her; *kila—*indeed; *parārdhena—*with a *parārdha*; *api—*even; *durlabhām—*hard to obtain; *anarghām—*priceless; *eva—*only; *jānīhi—*know*.*

**Перевод:**

**Paurṇamāsī (entering the stage): O suave boy, though all smart girls carry You in their hearts, know for sure that One whom You covet so much is truly priceless and unobtainable for the price of even one parārdha of golden ṭaṅkas.**

### Verse 503

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(sāpatrapam abhivādya) bhagavati, kevalaṁ śulka-vittānām upalabdhaye gṛhītāgraho ’smi. na tu kākinī-pāda-mūlyānāṁ bhavad-gopīnām.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-apatrapam—*with impudence; *abhivādya—*adressing; *bhagavati—*O Bhagavatī (Paurṇamāsī); *kevalam—*merely; *śulka-vittānām—*wealth for the tax; *upalabdhaye—*for obtaining; *gṛhīta—*took up; *āgrahaḥ—*persistence; *asmi—*I; *na—*not; *tu—*but; *kākinī-pāda—*quarter of a *kākinī*; *mūlyānām—*whose value; *bhavat-gopīnām—*your gopīs.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (impudently): Your Holiness, I am stubborn to get the tax and not your gopīs, who cost but a quarter of a kākinī.**

**Комментарий:**

*Kākinī* equals twenty cowrie coins (commentary to text 180). Kṛṣṇa is intimidated by the sudden appearance of Paurṇamāsī on the scene. It is to impress upon her that the tax, and not the gopīs, is His only concern, he says that the value of the five gopīs headed by Rādhikā is merely a quarter of a *kākinī*. In other words, they are of no interest to Him.

### Verse 504-505

***rādhikā:*** *bhaavadi, diṭṭhiā viḍambaṇambu-rāsiṇo pāraṁ amhehiṁ diṭṭhaṁ jaṁ saaṁ ettha tattha-hodī samāadā.*  
***paurṇamāsī:*** *(janāntikam)*  
*dānīndrasya prasabham anurī-kṛtya mānoddhurāṇāṁ*  
*dānaṁ viśva-prakaṭam aṭavī-maṇḍalākhaṇḍalasya*  
*saṁrabdhānāṁ kalaha-laharī-lubdhatā-durmukhīṇāṁ*  
*pātaḥ śātodari na bhavitā kiṁ viḍambāmbudhau vaḥ*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhaavadi—*O Bhagavatī; *diṭṭhiā—*luckily; *viḍambaṇa—*of deception; *ambu-rāsiṇo—*of vast waters; *pāraṁ—*the opposite shore; *amhehiṁ—*by us; *diṭṭhaṁ—*seen; *jaṁ—*because; *saaṁ—*yourself; *ettha—*here; *tattha-hodī—*your grace; *samāadā—*has arrived*. janāntikam—*to the side; *dānī-indrasya—*for the chief tax collector; *prasabham—*vehemently; *anurī-kṛtya—*not agreeing; *māna—*due to pride; *uddhurāṇām—*from the wild; *dānam—*with the payment; *viśva-prakaṭam—*obvious to all; *aṭavī-maṇḍala—*of the forest area; *ākhaṇḍalasya—*the king; *saṁrabdhānām—*furious; *kalaha—*of strife; *laharī—*after the waves; *lubdhatā—*due to greediness; *durmukhīṇām—*foul-mouthed; *pātaḥ—*fall; *śāta-udari—*O One with slender waist; *na bhavitā—*will not be; *kim—*whether; *viḍamba—*of deception; *ambudhau—*into the ocean; *vaḥ—*your.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhikā: O Bhagavatī, with your grace having arrived here, we can luckily see the other shore of this ocean of deception.**

**Paurṇamāsī (to the side): O slender-waisted girl, you gopīs are unbridled due to your arrogance, furious and foul-mouthed, eager to play in the waves of strife. How then you won’t fall into this ocean of deception by vehemently refusing to pay the obvious to all tax to the chief collector, the king of the woods?**

**Комментарий:**

Paurṇamāsī now scolds Rādhā by saying that it is the gopīs themselves who cause their own trouble by refusing the tax collector His dues.

### Verse 506

***lalitā:***  *bhaavadi, pekkha dullaho hāro amhehiṁ diṇṇo tahabi ṇa mukkiamha.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhaavadi—*O Bhagavatī; *pekkha—*look; *dullaho—*rare; *hāro—*the necklace; *amhehiṁ—*by us; *diṇṇo—*given; *tahabi—*still; *ṇa mukkiamha—*not released*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā: Bhagavatī, please look at this: we are still not released despite having given away the rare necklace.**

**Комментарий:**

Lalitā, having somehow overheard the accusation from Paurṇamāsī, defends herself and the other gopīs by stating that they have actually paid the fee.

### Verse 507

***paurṇamāsī:*** *lalite, paśya: bhavatīnāṁ kalaha-kūṭa-kaṣāyeṇa pāṭalita-citta-dukūlaḥ pratikūla iva śikhaṇḍa-cūḍas tiṣṭhati. tad adya vinā priyopahāram ahārya-saṁrambha-ḍambharo ’sau manasvī.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*lalite—*O Lalitā; *paśya—*look; *bhavatīnām—*your; *kalaha—*of quarreling; *kūṭa—*of the abundance; *kaṣāyeṇa—*by the red color; *pāṭalita—*pink; *citta—*of whose mind; *dukūlaḥ—*the fine cloth; *pratikūlaḥ—*hostile; *iva—*as if; *śikhaṇḍa—*peacock feather; *cūḍaḥ—*on whose head; *tiṣṭhati—*stands; *tat—*thus; *adya—*today; *vinā—*without; *priyā—*the favorite; *upahāram—*gift; *ahārya—*not to be given up; *saṁrambha—*of anger; *ḍambaraḥ—*having a lot; *asau—*He; *manasvī—*clever*.*

**Перевод:**

**Paurṇamāsī: Lalitā, just think about it: The red dye of your loud quarrel has tinged the fine silk of Kṛṣṇa’s mind pink. And so, this clever boy with a peacock feather in His head is seemingly hostile to you gopīs. He will not be able to curb the storm of His wrath if you will not offer Him His favorite thing.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa’s pure mind is like white color. Because it was agitated by the gopīs’ red-hot quarrel it has become pink.

*‘Priya-upahāram’* (“the favorite gift”) interpreted eight as “the gift from the beloved (*priyā*),” or “the gift which is the beloved,” indicates Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. So, unless Kṛṣṇa receives something from Her or Herself, He will not calm down.

### Verse 508

***nāndīmukhī:***  *bhaavadi, āṇabedu imāṇaṁ majjhe edaṁ bhāraṁ kā kkhu vahissadi?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhaavadi—*O Bhagavatī; *āṇabedu—*notice; *imāṇaṁ—*them; *majjhe—*among; *edaṁ—*this; *bhāraṁ—*weight; *kā—*who; *kkhu—*indeed; *bahissadi—*will speak*.*

**Перевод:**

**Nāndīmukhī: O Bhāgavatī, please tell me who among these gopīs is capable of carrying such a weight?**

**Комментарий:**

Nāndīmukhī refers to the recent Kṛṣṇa’s statement (verse 499), whereby He demanded the tax of one *parārdha* of golden *ṭaṅkas* (200,000,000,000 tons).

### Verse 509

***paurṇamāsī:***  
*yā pañcasu sarojākṣī*  
*paramārādhikā bhavet*  
*dhīrā saivāsya vijñeyā*  
*dhurīṇārādhane dhuri*  
*(lalitā manāg iva smitvā rādhikāṁ paśyantī dṛg-antaṁ kūṇayati. rādhikā cintām iva nāṭayantī vṛndā-karṇa-mūle lagati.)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*yā—*who; *pañcasu—*among five; *saroja-akṣī—*lotus eyed; *paramā—*the topmost; *ārādhikā—*worshipper; *bhavet—*fit to be; *dhīrā—*steadfast; *sā—*She; *eva—*only; *asya—*His; *vijñeyā—*to be understood; *dhurīṇā—*the bearer of the weight; *ārādhane—*in \[His\] worship; *dhuri—*ahead; *lalitā—*Lalitā: *manāk—*slightly; *iva—*as if; *smitvā—*smiling; *rādhikām—*at Rādhikā; *paśyantī—*looking; *dṛk-antam—*the eye corners; *kūṇayati—*shrinking; *rādhikā—*Rādhikā; *cintām—*worry; *iva—*as if; *nāṭayantī—*expressing; *vṛndā—*of Vṛndā; *karṇa-mūle—*to the ear; *lagati—*clings*.*

**Перевод:**

**Paurṇamāsī: Of the five of them—the One with lotuslike eyes, the supreme worshipper—She indeed is soon to be recognized as the steadfast bearer of the weight of serving Kṛṣṇa.**

**(Slightly smiling, Lalitā squints while looking from the corner of her eyes at Rādhikā. The latter, slightly anxious, speaks something into Vṛndā’s ear.)**

**Комментарий:**

*‘Paramā ārādhikā’* (“the topmost worshipper”) implies that the girl under description is fit to offer topmost worship. The purpose of Rādhikā’s exclusive worship of Kṛṣṇa is to make Him happy. The same words could also be split as *‘paramā rādhikā,’* which directly points at Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, or as *‘para-māra-adhikā,’* meaning “\[the girl, who\] excels (*adhikā*) \[the other four\] in supreme love (*para-māra*).” The “supreme love” here refers to *kṛṣṇa-prema.*

Lalitā can understand that it is Rādhā whom Paurṇamāsī describes as the topmost worshipper of Kṛṣṇa. That is why Lalitā smiles and indirectly glances at Rādhā.

### Verse 510

***vṛndā:***  *bhagavati, vedi-madhyameyaṁ nivedayati ‘hanta viśva-vedini, prapañcita-cāru-cāturī-camatkṛtiṁ lalitām evātra mahā-saṅkaṭe niraṭaṅkayad āli-maṇḍalam. kevalam asau pratīkṣāyās tavānujñāṁ pratīkṣamāṇā samakṣam avatiṣṭhate.’*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhagavati—*O Bhāgavatī; *vedi—*like a sacrificial altar; *madhyamā—*whose waist; *iyam—*this; *nivedayati—*tells; *hanta—*alas; *viśva—*of all; *vedini—*O knower; *prapañcita—*who has expanded; *cāru—*charming; *cāturī—*of wisdom; *camatkṛtim—*the wonder; *lalitām—*Lalitā; *eva—*only; *atra—*in this; *mahā-saṅkaṭe—*great trouble; *niraṭaṅkayat—*have misled; *āli—*of girlfriends; *maṇḍalam—*the group; *kevalam—*only; *asau—*she; *pratīkṣāyāḥ—*respected; *tava—*your; *anujñām—*for order; *pratīkṣamāṇā—*waiting; *samakṣam—*in front of your eyes; *avatiṣṭhate—*is standing*.*

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: O Bhagavatī, this girl with a waist like sacrificial altar, says the following: ‘Alas, o knower of everything. Actually, we girls have put Lalitā, who has increased the wonder of Her attractive wits, into this great trouble. She is now in front of you, simply awaiting orders from your grace.’**

**Комментарий:**

Here Rādhā’s waist is compared to *vedi*, the altar in a Vedic sacrifice. *Vedi* is a place next to the sacrificial fire for keeping the oblations, and it is a shallow pit in the shape of an elongated trapezia which is narrow in the middle and has curved sides. In this way it reminds the shape of a beautiful woman with a thin waist.

As if quoting what Rādhā has just whispered in her ear, Vṛndā impresses upon Paurṇamāsī the following: “Lalitā is very clever in her speech. It is she who is fit to worship Kṛṣṇa. That is why we gopīs have brought her here by trick. Now, ready for surrendering herself, Lalitā is awaiting for your order.”

### Verse 511

***lalitā:***  *(smitaṁ kṛtvā) hiaa-raaṇassa vijaammi saṁvutte alaṁ imāe haṭha-raṅga-rakkhāe.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smitam kṛtvā—*smiling; *hiaa—*of (Rādhā’s) heart; *raaṇassa—*of the jewel; *vijaammi—*when the victory; *saṁvutte—*happened; *alaṁ—*enough; *imāe—*of this; *haṭha—*of violence; *raṅga—*of the arena; *rakkhāe—*protection*.*

**Перевод:**

**Lalitā (smiling): If the jewel of Her heart has already gained victory, there is no need of keeping the battle going on.**

**Комментарий:**

Lalitā smiles—she can understand that Vṛndā didn’t quote Rādhā’s actual words. Lalitā tells Vṛndā to stop continuing the tax dispute, for it is now clear that Kṛṣṇa, the jewel of Rādhā’s heart, is the winner in this dispute.

### Verse 512

***paurṇamāsī:*** *nāyuktam uktaṁ lalitayā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*na ayuktam—*not improperly; *uktam—*said; *lalitayā—*by Lalitā.

**Перевод:**

**Paurṇamāsī: What Lalitā says is not wrong.**

**Комментарий:**

Paurṇamāsī agrees with Lalitā.

### Verse 513-514

***rādhikā:*** *(kiñcid uccair iva) bhaavadi, pasīda pasīda! imassiṁ duranta-vvasaṇe kaḍhora-ghaṭṭī-bāla-hatthe mā kkhu kādaro pa{i}di-dakkhiṇo eso jaṇo sullukkīadu! (iti saṁskṛtena)bhrāmyaty eṣa gireḥ kuḍaṅga-kuhare kṛṣṇo bhujaṅgāgraṇīḥ*  
*spṛṣṭo yena janaḥ prayāti viṣamāṁ kām apy asādhyāṁ daśām*  
*nābhadraṁ na ca bhadram ākalayituṁ śaktāsmi dṛṣṭi-cchaṭā-*  
*mātreṇāsya hatāham icchasi kutaḥ prakṣeptum atrāpi mām*  
*(iti līlayā śuṣkaṁ rudatī pādopakaṇṭhe luṭhati)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kiñcit—*slightly; *uccaiḥ—*loudly; *iva—*as if; *haavadi—*O Bhagavatī; *pasīda pasīda—*be kind, be kind; *imassiṁ—*in this; *duranta—*infinite; *vvasaṇe—*whose wrongdoing; *kaḍhora—*rough; *ghaṭṭī-bāla—*of the tollgate man; *hatthe—*in the hands; *mā—*should not; *kkhu—*indeed; *kādaro—*afflicted; *pa{i}di—*by nature; *dakkhiṇo—*kind; *eso—*this; *jaṇo—*person; *sullukkīadu—*be made into the fee; *iti—*thus; *saṁskṛtena—*in Sanskrit; *bhrāmyati—*wanders; *eṣa—*this; *gireḥ—*of the hill; *kuḍaṅga—*of the bower; *kuhare—*in the cave; *kṛṣṇaḥ—*black; *bhujaṅga-agraṇīḥ—*the chief of serpents; *spṛṣṭaḥ—*touched; *yena—*by whom; *janaḥ—*a person; *prayāti—*proceeds; *viṣamām—*terrible; *kām api—*some; *asādhyām—*incurable; *daśām—*condition; *na abhadram—*not inauspicious; *na—*not; *ca—*and; *bhadram—*auspicious; *ākalayitum—*to consider; *śaktā—*able; *asmi—*I; *dṛṣṭi—*glances; *chaṭā—*by many; *mātreṇa—*just; *asya—*His; *hatā—*hurt; *aham—*I; *icchasi—*you wish; *kutaḥ—*why; *prakṣeptum—*to abandon; *atra—*here; *api—*also; *mām—*Me. *iti—*thus; *līlayā—*as a show; *śuṣkam—*artificially; *rudatī—*crying; *pāda—*of her feet; *upakaṇṭhe—*in the proximity; *luṭhati—*rolls.

**Перевод:**

**Rādhikā (a bit louder): O Bhagavatī, have mercy, have mercy! This gentle girl is desperate! Please don’t give Her away in place of the tax into most sinful and rough hands of this tax collector!**

**(in Sanskrit): The black cobra lurks in the cave-like bower on this hill. If it bites somebody, that person develops some terrible, incurable disease, and I can’t say if it’s good or not. I myself am bitten by this cobra’s many glances. Why do you want to abandon me here? (Pretending to cry, She rolls at the feet of Paurṇamāsī.)**

**Комментарий:**

Outwardly speaking of the black cobra, Rādhā, of course, describes Kṛṣṇa: “This dark (*kṛṣṇaḥ*) chief of lovers (*bhujaṅga-agraṇīḥ*)…”

With the words “some terrible incurable disease” and “by His many glances,” Rādhārāṇī indirectly confesses Her love for Kṛṣṇa. At the same time, with Her artificial crying, she pretends to be unwilling to surrender to Kṛṣṇa.

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu describes *kṛṣṇa-prema* in a similar way:

*ei premā-āsvādana, tapta-ikṣu-carvaṇa,*  
*mukha jvale, nā yāya tyajana*  
*sei premā yāṅra mane, tāra vikrama sei jāne,*  
*viṣāmṛte ekatra milana*

**“If one tastes such love of Godhead, he can compare it to hot sugarcane. When one chews hot sugarcane, his mouth burns, yet he cannot give it up. Similarly, if one has but a little love of Godhead, he can perceive its powerful effects. It can only be compared to poison and nectar mixed together.”**

There is also a similar statement in Rūpa Gosvāmī’s another drama *Vidagdha-mādhava* (2.18), where Paurṇamāsī speaks to Nāndīmukhī:

*pīḍābhir nava-kāla-kūṭa-kaṭutā-garvasya nirvāsano*  
*nisyandena mudāṁ sudhā-madhurimāhaṅkāra-saṅkocanaḥ*  
*premā sundari nanda-nandana-paro jāgarti yasyāntare*  
*jñāyante sphuṭam asya vakra-madhurās tenaiva vikrāntayaḥ*

**“My dear beautiful friend, if one develops love of Godhead, love of Kṛṣṇa, the son of Nanda Mahārāja, all the bitter and sweet influences of this love will manifest in one’s heart. Such love of Godhead acts in two ways. The poisonous effects of love of Godhead defeat the severe and fresh poison of the serpent. Yet there is simultaneously transcendental bliss, which pours down and defeats the pride of nectar and diminishes its value. In other words, love of Kṛṣṇa is so powerful that it simultaneously defeats the poisonous effects of a snake, as well as the happiness derived from pouring nectar on one’s head. It is perceived as doubly effective, simultaneously poisonous and nectarean.”**

### Verse 515

***paurṇamāsī:*** *(bhujābhyām āśliṣya) vatse, mā rodanaṁ kṛthāḥ. sarvam idaṁ te śubhodarkaṁ bhaviṣyati.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhujābhyām—*with her hands; *āśliṣya—*embracing; *vatse—*O my child; *mā—*do not; *rodanam kṛthāḥ—*cry; *sarvam—*all; *idam—*this; *te—*for Your; *śubha—*auspicious; *udarkam—*for future; *bhaviṣyati—*will be*.*

**Перевод:**

**Paurṇamāsī (embracing Rādhā with both hands): My child, do not cry. All this is for Your bright future.**

## The Complete Kṛṣṇa

### Verse 516-517

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *bhagavati, satyaṁ bhāgadheya-bhāg asmi yad atra sādhīyasi samaye tavopasthitir*  
*babhūva. tataḥ svīkṛta-śulkam evātmānam avadhārayāmi.*  
***paurṇamāsī:*** *(janāntikam) rāmaṇīyaka-nidhe, ramaṇī-maṇir eva tavopakaṇṭha-sthala-śobhanī-babhūva. kim anyena phalgunā śulkena.*

**Пословный перевод:**
