*bhagavati—*O saintly lady; *satyam—*truly; *bhāgadheya—*of the fortune; *bhāk—*the enjoyer; *asmi—*I am; *yat—*because; *atra—*in this; *sādhīyasi—*most appropriate; *samaye—*time; *tava—*your; *upasthitiḥ—*presence; *babhūva—*occured; *tataḥ—*thus; *svī-kṛta—*who has obtained; *śulkam—*the fee; *eva—*certainly; *ātmānam—*Myself; *avadhārayāmi—*I ascertain*. jana-antikam—*to the side; *rāmaṇīyaka—*of the handsome; *nidhe—*O treasure; *ramaṇī—*of the lovely girl (Rādhā); *maṇiḥ—*the jewel; *eva—*very; *tava—*Your; *upakaṇṭha-sthala—*of neck; *śobhanī—*the splendor; *babhūva—*became; *kim—*what is the use; *anyena—*of another; *phalgunā—*petty; *śulkena—*fee*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa: Your Holiness, I am really fortunate, for at this most appropriate time you have graced us with your presence. I therefore consider that I have exacted the tax.**

**Paurṇamāsī (to the side): O treasure among the handsome, the jewel from this lovely girl has already adorned Your neck. What need is there for another insignificant fee?**

**Комментарий:**

Paurṇamāsī speaks of the jewel of the demon Śaṅkhacūḍa, which ended up being the central jewel in Rādhā’s necklace. Just a few moments before Lalitā transferred this necklace from Rādhā’s neck to Kṛṣṇa’s (texts 483-485).

Paurṇamāsī’s statement, however, carries another meaning: “…this best of lovely girls (*ramaṇī-maṇir*) has already adorned Your neck.” Paurṇamāsī says “has adorned,” in order to impress upon Kṛṣṇa that even though Rādhā has not yet embraced Him at this point, it is beyond any doubt that She will.

### Verse 518-519

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(sānandam ātma-gatam) diṣṭyā mad-abhīṣṭā śulkī-kṛtā bhagavatyā rādhikā.*  
*(prakāśam) bhavagati, satyam asya medura-mahārāga-kaumudī-ḍambara-karambitasya ramaṇī-ratnasya labdhaye bhavatyāḥ prasāda-vīthim antareṇa nānyā yuktir abhivartate.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-ānandam—*with joy; *ātma-gatam—*to Himself; *diṣṭyā—*luckily; *mat—*by Me; *abhīṣṭā—*most desired; *śulkī-kṛtā—*made as the fee; *bhagavatyā—*by Bhagavatī; *rādhikā—*Rādhikā; *prakāśam—*audibly; *bhavagati—*O Bhagavatī; *satyam—*truly; *asya—*this; *medura—*soft; *mahā-rāga—*golden; *kaumudī—*of moonrays; *ḍambara—*with the abundance; *karambitasya—*mixed; *ramaṇī—*of beautiful girls; *ratnasya—*the jewel; *labdhaye—*for obtaining; *bhavatyāḥ—*of your; *prasāda—*mercy; *vīthim—*the series; *antareṇa—*without; *na—*not; *anyā—*another; *yuktiḥ—*arrangement; *abhivartate—*is victorious*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (joyfully to Himself): Luckily Paurṇamāsī made the fee My most cherished Rādhikā.**

**(audibly): O Your Holiness, this lovely girl’s jewel is dazzling like the pleasant, golden moon. It is true: Without the waves of your mercy I couldn’t have obtained it by any means.**

**Комментарий:**

Another meaning of the first sentence: “…this lovely girl is a jewel (*ramaṇī-ratnasya*) that is dazzling with the moonlight of soft *prema* (*mahā-rāga*).” This shows that Kṛṣṇa has understood the hidden meaning of Paurṇamāsī’s words.

### Verse 520

***paurṇamāsī:*** *(sa-narma-smitam) nāgarendra, mayā cintāmaṇir iyaṁ prastutā. bhavatā tu kāntā-maṇir avadhāritā!*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-narma—*playful; *smitam—*with a smile; *nāgara-indra—*O best of suave men; *mayā—*by me; *cintāmaṇiḥ—cintāmaṇi* jewel; *iyam—*this; *prastutā—*was extolled; *bhavatā—*by You; *tu—*but; *kāntā—*the beloved; *maṇiḥ—*jewellike; *avadhāritā—*was considered*.*

**Перевод:**

**Paurṇamāsī (with a playful smile): O best of the elegant, when I was praising this cintāmaṇi jewel, You took it to mean Your jewellike beloved!**

**Комментарий:**

Paurṇamāsī is joking with Kṛṣṇa as if He has become silly due to His love-sickness. It is clear, however, that in text 519 Kṛṣṇa shrewdly picked up on Paurṇamāsī’s ambiguous words in text 517.

### Verse 521

***kṛṣṇaḥ:*** *(sa-lajja-smitam) bhagavati, mad-girām apy atraiva viśrāntiḥ. yad etasya lalanā-lalāmasya saṅgame bhavat-pāriṣadyāḥ sācivya-vidyā-sampad eva hetur āsīt.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-lajja—*bashful; *smitam—*with a smile; *bhagavati—*O Bhagavatī; *mat—*for My; *girām—*words; *api—*also; *atra—*in this regard; *eva—*only; *viśrāntiḥ—*retirement; *yat—*because; *etasya—*with this; *lalanā—*of the beautiful girl; *lalāmasya—*the ornament; *saṅgame—*for obtaining; *bhavat—*of your; *pāriṣadyāḥ—*associate; *sācivya—*in assistance; *vidyā—*of expertise; *sampat—*the wealth; *eva—*only; *hetuḥ—*the cause; *āsīt—*was*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (bashfully smiling): Your Holiness, when it comes to this jewel, even I am dumbfounded. And it is only due to the expert assistance of your associate that I have obtained the ornament from this beautiful girl.**

**Комментарий:**

The “jewel” that even Kṛṣṇa is incapable of describing is Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. In the second sentence Kṛsṇa is still indirect: the compound *‘lalanā-lalāmasya,’* (“the ornament of this beautiful girl”) referring to the *cintāmaṇi* jewel in Rādhā’s necklace, can also be understood as “the ornament among all beautiful girls,” referring to Rādhā.

By Paurṇamāsī’s associate Kṛṣṇa means Nāndīmukhī. It was she who in the first place informed Kṛṣṇa that Rādhā and the other gopīs’ will pass by the Govardhana hill (text 19).

### Verse 522

***paurṇamāsī:*** *cāturī-vidyā-mahopādhyāya, kṛtaṁ vailakṣya-vaibhavena. cintāmaṇi-lābha evāvaśyaṁ kāntā-maṇi-lābhāya kalpate. na hi pratyūṣa-śobhāyām upasthitāyāṁ bhānujāyāḥ śriyo viṣṇupada-sevāyāṁ vyabhicariṣṇutā ghaṭate. tatas tvam adya pūrṇo ’si.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*cāturī—*of wisdom; *vidyā—*of the art; *mahā-upādhyāya—*O great teacher; *kṛtam—*done; *vailakṣya—*of bewilderment; *vaibhavena—*with the greatness; *cintāmaṇi—*of the *cintāmaṇi* jewel; *lābhaḥ—*the obtainment; *eva—*certainly; *avaśyam—*necessarily; *kāntā—*of the beloved; *maṇi—*jewellike; *lābhāya—*in the obtainment; *kalpate—*results; *na—*not; *hi—*indeed; *pratyūṣa—*of dawn; *śobhāyām—*when the beauty; *upasthitāyām—*there is; *bhānu-jāyāḥ—*of the Sun; *śriyaḥ—*of the splendor; *viṣṇu-pada—*on the sky; *sevāyām—*in dwelling; *vyabhicariṣṇutā—*deviance; *ghaṭate—*happens; *tataḥ—*thus; *tvam—*You; *adya—*today; *pūrṇaḥ—*complete; *asi—*are*.*

**Перевод:**

**Paurṇamāsī: O Kṛṣṇa, You are the great teacher in the art of wisdom. So stop feeling so much perplexed. Getting that cintāmaṇi jewel is the guarantee that You will obtain Your jewellike beloved, for when the dawn breaks with all its beauty, the Sun with its splendor won’t fail to arise in the sky. So, today You have become complete.**

**Комментарий:**

Paurṇamāsī assures Kṛṣṇa that He will certainly obtain Rādhārāṇī if He has already got the wish-fulfilling *cintāmaṇi* jewel from Her necklace. To make it clear, Paurṇamāsī gives an appropriate simile with the daybreak and the Sun: The beauty of the dawn is the sure sign that the Sun will rise soon. With the same sentence Paurṇamāsī speaks about Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa: “When the dawn breaks with its splendor, the beautiful daughter of Vṛṣabhānu (*bhānu-jāyāḥ śriyaḥ*) won’t fail to serve (*sevāyām*) the feet of Viṣṇu (*viṣṇu-pada*).”

Kṛṣṇa has become complete in the sense that He has attained Rādhā. This statement of Paurṇamāsī concludes the plot of *Dāna-keli-kaumudī*. Such concluding statement—or *saṁhāra*—is the seventh element of one-act *bhāṇikā* play. (See Appendix…)

### Verse 523

***vṛndā:***  *pūrṇimāyām upasthitāyāṁ ko nāma kalā-nidher apūrṇatāvakāśaḥ?*

**Пословный перевод:**

*pūrṇimāyām—*when the full moon; *upasthitāyām—*is present; *kaḥ—*where; *nāma—*indeed; *kalānidheḥ—*of the Moon; *apūrṇatā—*for incompleteness; *avakāśaḥ—*the chance.

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: Is there a chance at all that the Moon will remain incomplete on the day of pūrṇimā?**

**Комментарий:**

Because *paurṇamāsī* and *pūrṇimā* are synonims, and Kalā-nidhi (“the storehouse (*nidhi*) of fine arts (*kalā*)”) is another name of Kṛṣṇa, the intended meaning of Vṛndā’s statement is this: “Is there a chance at all that Kṛṣṇa, the storehouse of fine arts, will remain incomplete in the presence of Paurṇamāsī?”

### Verse 524

***paurṇamāsī:*** *vṛnde, rādhām anurudhyamānena vidhunaiva madhurī-kṛteyaṁ mādhavīyā paurṇamāsī.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vṛnde—*O Vṛndā; *rādhām—*the Rādhā star; *anurudhyamānena—*covering; *vidhunā—*by the Moon; *eva—*only; *madhurī-kṛtā—*made pleasant; *iyam—*this; *mādhavīyā—*of the month of Mādhava; *paurṇamāsī—*the full moon day*.*

**Перевод:**

**O Vṛndā, it is rather the Moon itself, that, by adhering to the Rādhā star, makes the night its appearance in the month of Mādhava so pleasant.**

**Комментарий:**

Paurṇamāsī says that it is not the full-moon night that makes the moon glorious, but it is the full moon that makes the night of its appearance very pleasant. With this, Paurṇamāsī, as a humble devotee of Kṛṣṇa, wants to impress upon Vṛndā that she, Paurṇamāsī, is not to be praised as the cause behind the union of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. It is rather Kṛṣṇa, who has delighted Paurṇamāsī by coming together with Rādhā in the end of the tax pastime. This is supported by the words’ double meanings: “O Vṛndā, it is Kṛṣṇa (*vidhunā*) Himself, who delights (*mādhurī-kṛtā*) His devotee (*iyam mādhavīyā*) Paurṇamāsī by clinging to (*anurudhyamānena*) Rādhā.”

The month of Mādhava (April-May), is also called Vaiśākha. The reason is that in this month, the moon, on the day when it is full (*pūrṇimā*), passes through the region of the ecliptic where Rādhā star (also called Viśākhā) is situated. From this speech of Paurṇamāsī we also understand that this tax pastime took place on that day.

### Verse 525

***vṛndā:***  *tad enaṁ pūrṇam eva pūrṇimā samakṣam upalakṣayatu viśākhā-sakhyā.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tat—*therefore; *enam—*this; *pūrṇam—*full (Moon); *eva—*only; *pūrṇimā—*the full moon night; *samakṣam—*in front the eyes; *upalakṣayatu—*let behold; *viśākhā—*with Viśākhā star; *sakhyā—*the friend*.*

**Перевод:**

**Vṛndā: So let the full-moon night behold directly the full Moon with its friend, the Viśākhā star.**

**Комментарий:**

The inner meaning: “So let Paurṇamāsī (*pūrṇimā*) behold directly this complete person (*pūrṇam*) with the friend of Viśākhā (*viśākhā-sakhyā*).” *‘Pūrṇam’* refers to Kṛṣṇa, for just recently Paurṇamāsī has stated that Kṛṣṇa has become complete—*‘tvam adya pūrṇo ’si’* (text 522). Viśvanātha Cakravartī describes how by saying this sentence, Vṛndā has indicated that at this point Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa should enter the secluded pastime bower along with Lalitā and the other gopīs. Also, Vṛndā has again hinted at the influence of Paurṇamāsī in bringing together the divine couple. Soon after that, the divine couple comes back, adorned with divine ornaments. Looking at Them, Paurṇamāsī says the following:

### Verse 526

***paurṇamāsī:*** *vaidagdhī-candrikā-candra, bāḍham atra pratibhūr abhūvam. sāyaṁ tavābhīṣṭam eva śulkam arpayiṣyāmi. tad anumanvasva. sāmpratam amūr adhvara-vedī-prasādhanāya sādhayantu.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vaidagdhī—*of wisdom; *candrikā—*(shining with) the rays; *candra—*O Moon; *bāḍham—*indeed; *atra—*in this matter; *pratibhūḥ—*a bail; *abhūvam—*I was; *sāyam—*in the evening; *tava—*by You; *abhīṣṭam—*cherished; *eva—*certainly; *śulkam—*the fee; *arpayiṣyāmi—*I will offer; *tat—*thus; *anumanvasva—*please permit; *sāmpratam—*now; *amūḥ—*these (gopīs); *adhvara—*of sacrifice; *vedī—*the altar; *prasādhanāya—*for decorating; *sādhayantu—*let (them) arrive*.*

**Перевод:**

**Paurṇamāsī: O Kṛṣṇa, You are the moon shining with rays of wisdom. I surely promise You that I shall give You Your cherished fee in the evening. But for now, let the gopīs go and decorate the sacrificial arena.**

**Комментарий:**

For Paurṇamāsī’s delight, Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa are now standing in front of her together, as a couple, beautifully surrounded by Their closest friends. It is noon, however, which is not the most suitable time for Kṛṣṇa to enjoy His cherished fee, the conjugal pastimes with Rādhikā. Also, at this point the gopīs have to really haste to the sacrificial arena to perform their duties—they are already quite late. For these reasons, Kṛṣṇa wouldn’t be able to relish the tax in the form of His beloved with a free mind. Paurṇamāsī tactfully communicates this by addressing Kṛṣṇa as “the moon, shining with rays of wisdom.” And just to assure Kṛṣṇa that putting off His amorous sports till evening wouldn’t make the grand endeavor of this tax pastime futile, Paurṇamāsī herself guarantees that later on she will reunite Him with Rādhikā.

### Verse 527

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(sāpatrapam) yathājñāpayati bhagavatī.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-apatrapam—*with embarrasment; *yathā—*as; *ājñāpayati—*orders; *bhagavatī—*the saintly lady*.*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (with embarrasment): As Your Holiness orders.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa humbly accepts Paurṇamāsī’s judgement.

### Verse 528-529

***paurṇamāsī:*** *sarvānanda-kadamba-mūrte, yadyapi bāḍham etayā hṛdayaṅgamayā te līlayā kṛtārthāsmi tathāpi kim apy abhyarthayitum icchāmi.*  
***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(sa-harṣam) bhagavati, śīghram ājñāpaya kiṁ te bhūyaḥ priyaṁ karavāṇīti.*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sarva—*of all; *ānanda—*bliss; *kadamba—*of the abundance; *mūrte—*O form; *yadi api—*even though; *bāḍham—*indeed; *etayā—*this; *hṛdayaṅgamayā—*heart-touching; *te—*Your; *līlayā—*by pastime; *kṛta-arthā—*satisfied; *asmi—*I am; *tathā api—*still; *kim api—*for something; *abhyarthayitum—*to ask; *icchāmi—*I wish*. sa-harṣam—*with joy; *bhagavati—*O Bhagavatī; *śīghram—*quickly; *ājñāpaya—*order; *kim—*what; *te—*for you; *bhūyaḥ—*again; *priyam—*pleasing; *karavāṇi—*I should do; *iti—*thus.

**Перевод:**

**Paurṇamāsī: O personification of all bliss, even though I am quite satisfied with Your heart-touching tax pastime, I still wish to ask for something.**

**Kṛṣṇa (gladly): Your Holiness, order Me quickly, please. What more shall I do to please you?**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa is totally surrendered to His devotee, Paurṇamāsī. Following her advice, He has already put off relishing His fee for later. Now He is completely ready to do anything else she might ask.

### Verse 530-532

***paurṇamāsī:*** *niravadya-keli-mādhurī-sudhā-sindho, sādhīyasi prasaṅge kṛtā hi prārthanā niścitam eva phala-garbhinī bhaved ity adhunā nivedayāmi:*  
*sahacarī-kula-saṅkulayā guṇair*  
*adhikayā saha rādhikayānayā*  
*tam iha narma-suhṛn-militaḥ sadā*  
*ghaṭaya mādhava ghaṭṭa-vilāsitām*  
*kiṁ ca –*  
*rādhā-kuṇḍa-taṭī-kuṭīra-vasatis tyaktānya-karmā janaḥ*  
*sevām eva samakṣam atra yuvayor yaḥ kartum utkaṇṭhate*  
*vṛndāraṇya-samṛddhi-dohada-pada-krīḍā-kaṭākṣa-dyute*  
*tarṣākhyas tarur asya mādhava phalī tūrṇaṁ vidheyas tvayā*

**Пословный перевод:**

*niravadya—*of immaculate; *keli—*pastimes; *mādhurī—*of sweetness; *sudhā—*of nectar; *sindho—*O ocean; *sādhīyasi—*at the best; *prasaṅge—*opportunity; *kṛtā—*made; *hi—*indeed; *prārthanā—*prayer; *niścitam—*definitely; *eva—*indeed; *phala-garbhinī—*producing effect; *bhavet—*should become; *iti—*therefore; *adhunā—*now; *nivedayāmi—*speak out; *sahacarī-kula—*of Her group of companions; *saṅkulayā—*mixed with a large gathering; *guṇaiḥ—*by Her qualities; *adhikayā—*superior; *saha—*with; *rādhikayā—*Rādhikā; *anayā—*this; *tam—*that; *iha—*in this place; *narma—*with confidential; *suhṛt—*friends; *militaḥ—*accompanied; *sadā—*always; *ghaṭaya—*perform; *mādhava—*O Mādhava; *ghaṭṭa—*of a tollgate; *vilāsitām—*the pastime; *kim ca—*moreover; *rādhā-kuṇḍa—*of Rādhā-kuṇḍa; *taṭī—*on the bank; *kuṭīra—*in a hut; *vasatiḥ—*whose residence; *tyakta—*who has left; *anya-karmā—*other activities; *janaḥ—*a person; *sevām—*service; *eva—*only; *samakṣam—*directly; *atra—*here; *yuvayoḥ—*of the two youths; *yaḥ—*who; *kartum—*to do; *utkaṇṭhate—*longs; *vṛndā-araṇya—*of Vṛndāvana forest; *samṛddhi—*of opulence; *dohada-pada—*the cherished object; *krīḍā—*playful; *kaṭākṣa—*whose sideling glance; *dyute—*the shine; *tarṣa—*desire; *ākhyaḥ—*called; *taruḥ—*the tree; *asya—*of this person; *mādhava—*O Mādhava; *phalī—*fruitful; *tūrṇam—*quickly; *vidheyaḥ—*may be made; *tvayā—*by You.

**Перевод:**

**Paurṇamāsī: O Kṛṣṇa, You are an ocean of nectar born from the sweetness of Your own immaculate pastimes. Since a prayer offered at a most appropriate moment will surely be answered, I am now requesting You of the following:**

**O Mādhava, may You, in the company Your most confidential friends, conduct this tax pastime near Govardhana hill again and again with exalted due to Her transcendental qualities Rādhikā, who is surrounded by Her sakhīs.**

**And, O Mādhava, the radiance of whose playful sidelong glance is sought after by the opulence of Vṛndāvana forest and all its inhabitants, I have yet one more wish: If someone has left all material activities, lives on the bank of Rādhā-kuṇḍa, and yearns to directly serve the divine youthful couple there, then please, induce the tree of such devotee’s aspirations bear its fruit very soon.**

**Комментарий:**

By addressing Kṛṣṇa as “an ocean of nectar born from the sweetness of His own immaculate (*niravadya*) pastimes,” Paurṇamāsī definitively establishes Kṛṣṇa’s tax pastime as divine and transcendental, untinged by faults of the material world like lust and greed. The progressive *sādhaka*, who strives to attain the eternal service of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and is at least theoretically aware that Their pastimes are absolutely on the spiritual platform, has to be repeatedly reminded of this.

Every Sanskrit drama must end with a blessing, and *Dāna-keli-kaumudī* is not an exception from this rule. Kṛṣṇa, who by the end of this pastime has become most complete by fulfilling His desire, is now in a very liberal mood. He is especially inclined to fulfill the desires of His devotees. It is indeed the most appropriate moment for Paurṇamāsī to take advantage of Kṛṣṇa’s benevolence.

Paurṇamāsī’s first wish is that Kṛṣṇa should coninue to perform the tax pastime again and again in the future. This is her general request. Her second wish, however, which is expressed in the second verse, is more specific. She is asking Kṛṣṇa to fulfill the desires of some renounced devotee who lives on the banks of Rādhā-kuṇḍa, full of eagerness to attain the service of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Whom is she talking about? Bearing in mind the reason why Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī wrote *Dāna-keli-kaumudī*, we may say almost for sure that Paurṇamāsī is talking about Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī. Through this concluding prayer of Paurṇamāsī, Rūpa Gosvāmī expresses his love and care for his dear and dependent friend. Kṛṣṇa, who by His playful sidelong glance makes all the trees of Vṛndāvana brim with fruits, will certainly not neglect a single tree of His humble devotee’s earnest aspirations.

### Verse 533

***kṛṣṇaḥ:***  *(sa-harṣābhyupagatam) bhagavati, tathāstu. tad ehi prātisvikābhīṣṭa-kṛtyāya prayāma.(iti niṣkrāntāḥ sarve)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-harṣa—*with joy; *abhyupagatam—*consentingly; *bhagavati—*O Bhagavatī; *tathā—*so; *astu—*may it be; *tat—*therefore; *ehi—*come on; *prātisvika—*each others; *abhīṣṭa—*favorite; *kṛtyāya—*for activities; *prayāma—*let us depart; *iti—*thus; *niṣkrāntāḥ—*gone off the stage; *sarve—*all.

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa (with a happy consent): Let it be so, Your Holiness. Come on all, let’s leave and attend to each others favorite business.**

**(everyone goes off the stage)**

*(iti śrī-śrī-dāna-keli-kaumudī nāma bhāṇikā samāptā.)*

**Пословный перевод:**

*iti—*thus; *śrī-śrī-dāna-keli-kaumudī—*Śrī Śrī Dāna-keli-kaumudī; *nāma—*by the name; *bhāṇikā—*play; *samāptā—*completed.

**Перевод:**

**Thus ends the play entitled Śrī Śrī Dāna-keli-kaumudī.**

### Verse 534

*grathitā sumanaḥ-sukhadā*  
*yasya nideśena bhāṇikā-srag iyam*  
*tasya mama priya-suhṛdaḥ*  
*kaṇṭha-taṭīṁ kṣaṇam alaṅkurutām*

**Пословный перевод:**

*grathitā—*strung; *sumanaḥ—*to the flowers; *sukha-dā—*the bestower of happiness; *yasya—*on whose; *nideśena—*order; *bhāṇikā—*of play; *srak—*the garland; *iyam—*this; *tasya—*his; *mama—*of my; *priya—*dear; *suhṛdaḥ—*friend; *kaṇṭha—*of his throat; *taṭīm—*the side; *kṣaṇam—*for a moment; *alaṅkurutām—*may decorate.

**Перевод:**

**The garland of this bhāṇikā play bestows happiness to its flowers. I have strung it on the order of my dear friend, and so, may it decorate his voice for a moment.**

**Комментарий:**

Another meaning of the first sentence: “The garland of this *bhāṇikā* play bestows happiness to the pure-hearted devotees (*su-manaḥ*).” The dear friend that Rūpa Gosvāmī speaks of is Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī.

### Verse 535

*gate manu-śate śāke*  
*candra-svara-samanvite*  
*nandīśvare nivasatā*  
*bhāṇikeyaṁ vinirmitā*

**Пословный перевод:**

*gate—*in the passed; *manu-śate—*fourteen hundredth; *śāke—*year of the Śāka era; *candra—*by one; *svara—*and seven; *samanvite—*accompanied; *nandīśvare—*of Nandīśvara; *nivasatā—*by the resident; *bhāṇikā—*play; *iyam—*this; *vinirmitā—*was composed.

**Перевод:**

**This bhāṇikā play was composed by a resident of Nandīśvara in the year 1471 of the Śāka era.**

**Комментарий:**

The place of Rūpa Gosvāmī’s *bhajana-kuṭira* at Nandīśvara hill is called Ṭera-kadamba. It is located two kilometers east of Nandgaon town in the midst of the fields behind the temple of Āśeśvara Mahādeva. The place is originally associated with the pastime of Kṛṣṇa’s calling His cows by playing the flute.

1471 of the Śāka era corresponds to 1549 CE.

Viśvanātha Cakravartī concludes his *Mahaṭī-ṭīkā* with the following verse:

*dāna-keli-kaler ante*  
*rādhā-mādhavyor yugam*  
*kāma-lobha-madākrāntam*  
*ekākāram ahaṁ bhaje*

**“I worship the united forms of Rādhā and Mādhava, as They appear at the end of the tax pastime, overwhelmed with love, greed, and madness after each other.”**
